# Continuity State

## Handover chain, in order. Do not duplicate these blocks.

1. `state/volume-02-close.md` — the authoritative handover **out of** Volume 02, written before Chapter 101.
2. **The "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 101–110" block at the END of this file** — the authoritative handover **into** Batch 0002, and it supersedes the Volume 02 STATUS block above wherever the two differ.
3. `state/open-threads.md` — the live threads, at the end of the file.

Everything between here and the Volume 03 block is working detail for Chapters 1–100, current to the end of Chapter 100.

---

## Stable series canon

- Nacre is a contemporary estuary city of roughly 1.3 million people, with eight official districts, nine Lattice service districts including Lowglass, and the Old Ward civic core.
- The Civic Lattice is a physical and social permission network woven into civic infrastructure. It is not a simulation, a portal network, or a mind-reading computer.
- A valid permission has a holder, scope, witness, term, claim, and precedence. A missing part creates a seam.
- The Lattice can authorize access, duties, service claims, and compatible rules. It cannot create matter, force a choice, read intent, rewrite memory, resurrect the dead, or grant skill unrelated to an existing service.
- The story begins three months before a twelve-year Reclamation Season that renews root clauses.
- Lowglass is a real neighborhood erased from human maps and municipal indexes eighteen years ago during an off-cycle emergency renewal known as the Quiet Cut; the Lattice retains its underlying witness claims.
- Iona Vey is the first administrator and the original architect of the Continuity Project. Her name, notices, and design influence must be present in Volume 1; her full preservation and proxy role are the final antagonist reveal.
- Evan Mercer disappeared during the Quiet Cut. He did not survive as a secret helper; he died after cutting a lowland node, though the exact record remains hidden in early volumes.
- Jonas Mercer’s access comes from a fault-maintenance credential containing Evan’s counter-amendment and its unresolved second signature. It is not bloodline destiny or a prophecy.

## Chapters 1–10 established canon

- Jonas is a probationary NDC field technician, grade III. His permanent appointment review is still pending, scheduled in ten days as of Chapter 10. He wants the job, full archive clearance, and a more complete record of Evan’s disappearance.
- Stage 0 applies in Chapters 1–2. The first major Chapter 3 bridge reset activates only the limited Stage 1 Fault-reader view. Jonas remains Stage 1 through Chapter 10.
- The Saint Orra bridge has an obsolete **empty crossing** term that conflicts with current ambulance and clinic service claims. Its relay is repaired, but the civic term is not mended.
- The term’s clause text, quoted verbatim only in Chapters 1 and 3, is **EMPTY CROSSING—KEEP THE WEST LANE CLEAR DURING AMBULANCE SERVICE**. It was written when the west lane belonged to freight and ambulances used the east side. The name “empty crossing” is the field’s own; the clause is what defeats a current ambulance.
- The bridge runs east–west. The ambulance enters from the east approach and uses the west lane toward Saint Orra. The failing structure is the **south span**, which also carries the **side service route** and its **side hinge**. Public Works passed the west-lane hinge; the south span and both pedestrian approaches failed.
- In Chapter 3 Jonas names the fault class for the reader: two terms with no order between them. **A seam.** His filed classification remains “unresolved permission conflict.”
- The Chapter 3 ambulance lane was authorized only under the separately inspected Assembly Emergency Service Test. Public Works passed the west lane; the south span and approaches failed separate inspection. Witnessed use did not cure the permission conflict.
- The Chapter 4 unlisted shuttle arrived on a separate Saint Orra service-lane feed. It is not an effect of the bridge reset and does not retroactively give the reset a claim. During its clinic handoff, Jonas does not initiate gate use under the Chapter 2 suspension; Mina’s care claim and the existing attendant procedure open the attended path.
- A route can be present and operational while absent from human maps. The opening batch does not explain the neighborhood’s civic history or the full practice that maintains it.
- The Old Ward public board shows **CENTRAL ROOT—HOLDER: —** and a Reclamation Season maintenance-review line. This is an empty field, not a hidden person, succession offer, or request to find a bearer.
- The bridge plate carries an old **I. Vey** Continuity Project seal. Jonas initially treats it as manufacturer history; Chapter 8 identifies it as a municipal maintenance-series mark.
- A partial lowland service code matches a hardware code in the family copy of Evan’s last file. The location and fault-filer fields are missing or cut off. This is an incomplete seed, not proof about Evan and not the counter-amendment.
- The old-canal shelter is closed. Oren Pell’s public-safety order is valid because the south wall and water line are unsafe; his action remains harmful to residents because they lose an address and local service continuity.
- The old pump remains closed after a pressure inspection found an unsafe physical line and an unresolved shared-maintenance term. Bottled water and a clinic tank cover only the next care shift; reopening has not been approved. The tank is sized by metered consumption, not by headcount: the clinic’s recorded draw for observation four plus the ration the receiving shelter has actually drawn on its bottled supply that month.
- The responsibility bond, the first civic term mending, the seam mark, and the named maintenance claim all occurred in Chapters 11–17. See “Chapters 11–20 established canon” below.

## Service ledger

- River Stacks cabinet 6-R-14: relay replaced in Chapter 1.
- Saint Orra bridge: attended 25-minute bypass in Chapter 1; 90-minute Chapter 3 test; 90-minute Chapter 10 attended-service window. All temporary authority expired and closed.
- West clinic gate: Chapter 2 escort ran 10:56 to 10:57:41, lasting 1 minute 41 seconds; the separate municipal gate-use suspension was not lifted. The local review narrowed Jonas’s access to attended maintenance after the named bearer initiates the route; he cannot initiate or approve gate use alone. The exception applies only while the current watch remains in place, so a later shift needs its own bearer-initiated request. Chapter 9 local pressure reset served an urgent transfer; the shelter route opened at 10:16 and closed unused at 10:19 because the vehicle had already been committed to Selah’s **street route** (her term for the Chapter 9 alternative, longer and not shared with the clinic); Omar Sadiq’s nonurgent shelter return then completed with a recorded 43-minute delay. The Chapter 10 alternative is a different thing: a **taxi line** on the alternate street grid, not a scheduled run and not covered by the shelter’s local claim. Do not merge the two.
- Old-canal shelter: Alma Reyes returned under a short named escort; all residents later relocated to Bellow Market; building sealed at 8:03 in Chapter 6.
- Bellow Market receiving shelter: receipted the relocated residents’ possessions and, in Chapter 6, promised a **return-of-possession route** when the old building reopens. The building has not reopened, so the route is unperformed and unexpired. Custodian: Mara Holt as night steward, then the receiving shelter as an institution.
- Pump house: reopening denied; temporary water and clinic tank arranged; no resident headcount recorded.
- Jonas/Oren scope agreement, Chapter 6: Jonas attends the noon pump inspection **only** to document the access plate. He does not clear the water, certify the building, or take the inspection duty. Oren appears as registrar. Discharged as written in Chapter 7, where Jonas attended, read the plate, and left the reopening decision with Public Works.
- Chapter 6 union transport record: Jonas signed the transport record naming Selah’s receiving shelter, Mina’s clinical duty, and Mara’s night watch, and refused the union form’s blank personal guarantee. This instrument covers transport only. **It is not the visible responsibility bond** Tomas blocks on in Chapters 9 and 10, which additionally carries scope, refusal rights, an expiry, and a named claim. Batch 0002 must not treat the transport record as the bond.
- Chapter 10: the 90-minute attended-service window ran from 10:00 to 11:30. One ambulance lane, clinic entrance, and Bellow Market shelter-to-clinic route remained under their existing duties. The shelter vehicle’s run and handoff leg completed at 10:55 by taxi on the alternate street grid, with a 38-minute delay; the formal shelter-route authorization remained until 11:30. The **side service gate** on the south span was physically stopped before 10:17; the side route was then formally isolated at 10:31 under Oren’s safety-stop authority. Every temporary route closed.
- Chapter 10 crossing fixtures are three separate things and must not be conflated: the **north-bank pedestrian gate** at the tram end (Nora latches it by hand; unrelated to the service span), the **west lane** (the inspected ambulance lane under the Assembly test, not a gate), and the **south-span side service gate** (the vehicle gate on the failed span that drops into its physical stop and is later isolated under Oren’s order). None of them is the west-clinic gate, which is a different structure in a different building.
- Chapter 10 witness extension: Ruth Nwosu’s duty ends at 11:00, but the test expiry is 11:30. Under the Assembly Emergency Service Test a named witness remains until the expiry **or** names a named successor who will take the duty. No successor exists, and the reason is thematic rather than logistical: the local record never carried Ruth as a holder, so the 11:00 morning-watch handover is a signature with nobody to hand the key to. Mina Okafor keeps the paper and the corridor from 11:00; Ruth keeps the key until 11:30. Dr. Sethi’s day team still comes on at noon, and the patient — in observation four since 10:10 — is signed to the named on-call clinician until then, so the clinic route closes at 11:30 on the test’s expiry rather than on the patient’s condition. Ruth signs the extra half hour on a shorter signature line, is unpaid for it because the board has her signed off at six, and is on her second morning since the Chapter 9 night shift.
- Chapter 10 delay attribution: the 38-minute shelter delay has three recorded causes, not two — the underlying permission conflict, the physically stopped side service gate, and Jonas’s own scope. At 9:58 Selah reads the scheduled clinic run back to him and names that it crosses the failed south span. Jonas can price the taxi alternative (38 minutes) and write the risk onto the scope, but he cannot re-book Selah’s local claim, and Selah declines to spend the shelter’s water money on it. He leaves the run where it is. At 10:17 it reaches the stopped gate and pays exactly the price he forecast. This is his scheduling error, not a trap he failed to see.
- No temporary route survives as Jonas’s personal authority.

## Chapters 11–20 established canon

### The responsibility bond

- Signed in the River Stacks shop on the second day of the bridge sequence, read aloud, and stapled to the shop's yellow board. Seven clauses: **scope** (one local term at one named hinge, no citywide, cross-node, or root change), **witness** (at least one named living person who can state from memory what the term permits, when it ends, and what claim follows; the issuer may not be the only witness to his own change), **expiry**, **claim**, **refusal** (any named bearer or witness may stop the change or any later use, without the issuer's agreement, and need not give a reason the issuer accepts), **if the issuer is wrong** (the term returns to its prior state, the issuer's name stays in the record, and the shop steward or the department may suspend his civic repair authority until a review he cannot postpone), and **clause seven**, which Jonas wrote himself: the bond does not affect his civil-service appointment.
- **The named hinge is the Saint Orra west clinic care entrance.** The bond cannot be used anywhere else.
- Ruth Nwosu witnessed and wrote her own condition in the margin: **I CAN STOP IT AND KEEP MY REASON. HE MAY NOT ASK ME FOR IT AFTERWARDS.** Bea Osunde was the second witness. Nina Calder countersigned as records.
- The Chapter 6 union transport record is a different instrument and remains one. It covers transport only.

### The west-clinic care entrance term

- The engraved term on the plate is **NO UNATTENDED ACCESS — WEST CLINIC CARE ENTRANCE**. Below it somebody long ago wrote a pencilled window, **23:00–06:00**, whose author nobody alive knows.
- Ruth Nwosu's real watch is **22:00–11:00**. The board's record has her finishing at 18:00, which is four hours before she starts, so none of her night is in any record. The pencilled window is six hours short of her real one.
- The written term, signed on the back of a shift sheet at 23:38 on the first evening: **holder** the named watch or care worker on duty; **scope** a verified clinic care claim, a verified clinic transfer with a bed and a named receiving shift that does not close the emergency approach, and a return-of-care handoff named by a local steward, from receiving threshold to assigned bed, for that transfer only; **witness** two named people at every handover who have read it and can say when it ends; **precedence** this term answers to NO UNATTENDED ACCESS for those three cases and controls the care entrance against any premises, freight, or opening-hours term, with every other use unchanged; **term** from the first service window at 07:00 to 06:00 six days later, with no self-carry-forward; **claim** the issuer maintains it until a named successor steward accepts that maintenance or the term expires, and if the term fails without a bearer present the route narrows to emergency only and the issuer answers for the delay.
- Signatories: Ruth Nwosu as bearer, Mina Okafor as record, Sanaa Iqbal as an informed witness whose signature covers the scope and **not** the gate's safety, and Selah Mora by telephone for the return-of-care limb only, 22:00–06:00, with Bellow Market's name refused for everything else.
- **The six parts are now established in prose and may be used without re-teaching.** The fault class **seam** is likewise established.

### The first civic repair (Chapter 13) and the second (Chapter 17)

- The Chapter 13 repair restored **one field: precedence**. Nothing else. The plate accepted it and printed a service schedule, not a verdict.
- The schedule's second line reads: **Bearer of record: NWOSU, R., night watch. Window on record: 23:00–06:00. Window declared by the holder: 22:00–11:00.** Ruth's hours remain wrong after the repair. She has told Jonas to fix them.
- The schedule's sixth line was the defect: **IF NO HOLDER IS ON DUTY: the route is held by the issuer. ISSUER FALLBACK — MERCER, J. AVAILABILITY: acknowledged per shift.**
- The Chapter 17 amendment removed the issuer from the fallback and substituted: **IF NO HOLDER IS ON DUTY: THE ROUTE IS CLOSED. An emergency opening must be declared in writing by the named on-call clinician, entered in the log, and written on the public board. Term unchanged. Issuer unchanged. Claim unchanged.** The witness for the second edit was **Ezra Mbeki**, not Bram Osei, and Marta Sowle's request to take the issuer off the door is what triggered it.
- Stage 2 permits one local rule at one hinge at a time. The Chapter 17 work was a separate clause on a plate he had already worked, **witnessed by Ezra Mbeki**, with Marta Sowle present. Jonas stated the limit aloud before he touched the plate.
- **The seam mark** is a grey line along the inside of the right wrist, opened during the Chapter 13 edit, extended past the wrist to below the elbow by the Chapter 17 amendment. The right hand is largely numb and does not reliably close.
- **The maintenance claim is unpaid.** It is discharged only when a named successor steward accepts the maintenance, or the term expires. As of Chapter 20 neither has happened.
- Ruth Nwosu remains bearer. Sanaa Iqbal is a separate informed witness and, since Chapter 17, the named clinician for the declared emergency reduction. Jonas is issuer only.

### The roster (Chapter 17)

> **B. Osei, day porter — 06:00–11:00, Mon–Sat.**
> **E. Mbeki, porter — 13:30–22:00.**
> **R. Nwosu, night watch — 22:00–11:00.**
> **Return-of-care limb only, 22:00–06:00: S. Mora, Bellow Market. Retainer requested in writing of Saint Orra west: not yet agreed.**
> **11:00–13:30 — NO NAMED HOLDER.** The gate is closed in that window unless a named holder is on duty. Emergency care only may be opened by the on-call clinician, in writing, and every such opening is written on the board and entered in the log.

- **The Bellow Market retainer is outstanding, not granted.** Selah Mora asked on the page for Bellow Market to be paid a retainer for the return-of-care hours, refused Jonas's offer to get it out of the clinic budget (*not you, the hospital, in writing, if it comes from a technician it is a favor*), and made Mina read the line aloud before the roster was mounted. As of Chapter 20 the request sits with the Saint Orra west site administration and is unsigned; the line **not yet agreed** stays on the board until the hospital writes the cheque. Do not record this cost as paid, and do not let Jonas fund it.
- **Tomas's fourth prerequisite is unanswered.** He named four things a real day watch would need: a payroll line the department agrees to, a training afternoon somebody pays for, a clinic-side naming authority, and **a written rule for what happens when two people on the rota both refuse** — the least-bad option at four in the morning. The first three exist in part; the fourth does not, and the Chapter 17 roster makes it worse by writing *a refusal of a whole shift ends the shift, not the job*. He raised it once, on the evening of the third day, and did not raise it again. It is a live thread, not a closed question.

- The naming authority is the clinic, not the department. Dr. Sethi named the porters in person on the first day of the meeting and said so.
- The gap is real, named, published, and deliberately not filled with Jonas. It is tested on the page: an eighty-eight-year-old's non-urgent dressing claim is refused at 12:05 and admitted at 12:50 under a written clinical declaration, and both lines go in the log.
- At every handover the outgoing and incoming holders read the term to each other. Where the route is closed between them, the outgoing holder reads it aloud to the clerk on the desk and signs the box, and the incoming holder reads it aloud on his own before taking the key. Any holder may refuse a single use or a whole shift without giving a reason; a refusal of a whole shift ends the shift, not the job. Any patient, family member, or member of staff may ask who is holding the door, may ask for a different holder, and may appeal a refusal to a named person who is not a technician.
- A union or shop member may call a stop on work they believe has been named without understanding the claim.

### The costs that stay on the record

- **Lorna Vessey, sixty-one**, west-clinic observation patient, denied a transfer on the third day at 15:22 with a verified claim, an assigned bed, a named receiving nurse, and a booked cardiac slot. The slot was released at 15:41 to the standby list. **Next available: the fourteenth.** It is entered in the gate log, the conflict record, the review sheet, the public Old Ward board, and the corridor notice board. It is not discharged by the Chapter 17 amendment. The Old Ward strip carries the window and the slot; it does not carry her name, at her daughter's insistence.
- **Marta Sowle, ninety-one** (she/her), was brought back from a scan alone on a chair at 02:40 on the third morning and was found on the corridor floor at 02:53 by a cleaner, eleven minutes after she went down. The safeguarding form records the ward's own contributing factor: *route held on plate record; holder of record not on site; holder of record contacted at 03:05 and did not answer until 03:31.* Ezra Mbeki moved her alone and wrote that down too, including that he knew before he moved her. Marta Sowle agreed to the event being published, not her name, and appears in person in Chapter 17 to ask for the issuer line to be taken off the door. **The times in every state file are 02:40 (brought back) and 02:53 (found).**
- **The registrar's office has an open question.** A letter asks the department to state whether Jonas's two solo shift acknowledgements were continued attended maintenance or breaches of the Chapter 2 review order. Nina Calder has three working days. No answer has been filed.

### The suspension and its breach

- The Chapter 2 gate-use suspension is **still active and was not lifted**. Jonas may service an attended route only when its named bearer initiates it, and he may not initiate or approve gate use alone.
- On the second evening he acknowledged two one-hour gaps in the night window from his kitchen at 21:09. On the third afternoon he acknowledged the day shift at 15:58 and used it to open a route. Both acts are, on the registrar's reading, initiation of gate use by a suspended technician. The second one got a patient through a door; the first one accomplished nothing, because the plate had Ruth's name on the middle of the night and she was asleep.
- Being issuer has never made Jonas bearer, and no later chapter may treat the suspension as restored clearance.

### The series 6 chain and the lowland trail

- The west-clinic care entrance plate is **Continuity Project series 6, plate 41**. Series cards live in **drawer four** of the wall cabinet in the River Stacks shop, which grade III is not cleared to open.
- The chain reads: 6-38 River Stacks cabinet 6-R-14; 6-39 Saint Orra bridge west-lane relay; 6-40 Saint Orra bridge side-hinge service relay; 6-41 Saint Orra west clinic care entrance; and **6-L/14 and 6-L/19, “Canal Lowline,” lowland service nodes, address not indexed.**
- The code **LO/6-L/14** is the same partial lowland service code Jonas found under a bridge plate in Chapter 8 and wrote in a private pad, and it matches a hardware code in the family copy of Evan's last file. **He has told no one at the department that the code may belong to his father.** The trail entered his life only because he issued a term on a series plate; it did not explain the bridge, the shuttle, or the old-canal shelter, and Jonas refuses that explanation on the page.
- The Reclamation Season review request is public. Jonas's appointment review is five days after he reads it; the series response is due nine days after he reads it, four days later than the appointment review.
- He searched the library street index (four volumes, thin lowland sheets), the water authority's standpipe map, and the school catchment office, and was refused a draft catchment sheet for want of a departmental reference, because an unindexed service has no reference to give.

### The Weir

- The Weir is a three-hundred-meter lowland street between the canal embankment and a row of boat sheds, blank on the library index, the water map, the transit map, and the education office's working knowledge. Selah Mora named it to Jonas and refused to open the sealed log.
- **The Weir Street net loft** is a converted net-repair building: childcare exchange in the day, a Wednesday clinic from 10:00 to 14:00 run by **Ottoline “Otie” Beck**, seventy-one, a retired district nurse. **Aurrie Nkemdirim**, sixty-one, runs the exchange. **Margo Doré**, sixty-three, keeps the water rota. **Bo Trimble**, seventeen, does the pipework and is not on the tool rota. **Hector Mbatha**, seventy, lives on the Weir.
- **The Weir stand** is a green pillar with a bucket on a chain and a forty-slot rota board, run by residents, twenty-two minutes a day for six weeks against a former forty, because the lowland main's silt trap packs at every surge and has to be cleared by hand at low water.
- **The loft's civic plate was taken down by a resident and dropped in the canal** because it kept asking who was on duty at two in the morning and nobody ever was. What is left on the kitchen wall is a civic plate with its lower face scraped, carrying the legible fragment: **[scraped] LOWLAND WARDS — WATCH ESTABLISHED FOR THE FLOOD YEARS / ANY HOUSE MAY CALL. THE WATCH ANSWERS. / [scraped]**. Nobody in the loft knows who made it, who scraped it, or why.
- Children on the Weir carry brass tokens marked with three lines of blue tape. Nobody explains them and nobody was asked.
- **The barrier problem, as first described in Chapter 19–20:** a concrete chamber at the bottom of the lowland dip with a hinged iron flap gate whose pintle is rusted solid and whose sill beam is rotten through the middle third, beside an earth-and-gravel bank carrying a boil about two meters wide on its inner face. A proper repair needs the chamber pumped and held dry, four working days inside the tide table, and either a marine crew of six from out of town or six people from the street, who will do it worse and get hurt. Jonas packed the sill with fresh bedding compound on the second night, which buys a season and not a surge. **Repaired on 21 March; see Chapters 21–30 canon.**
- **Three positions, still unresolved and now written on a wall with a name against each:** apply to the city for a recognized culvert and service term and accept a survey, a count, and a number (Aurrie); take a county pump and stone for one tide window and write nothing down (Margo); carry on as an independent local practice with no plate (Ottie). Hector is on no line. Jonas is not asked to choose and has not chosen.
- **Chapter 27 replaced the fault with a repair.** See *Chapters 21–30 established canon* above. The barrier now stands, will not hold a surge, and has no name on it for the next flood.
- Pia Santos's four conditions for Jonas, stated on the towpath: no names, no photographs, no promises without local agreement, and no use of the sealed old-canal log. She is nineteen, she is a courier and caregiver, and she decides what he is allowed to see.
- **Added in Chapters 21–30:** **Wesley Kpomah**, forty-four, tool rota, terminal shifts at a container yard, who tightens the coupling on the 13th because the wrench belongs to the rota; **Perdita Sow**, forty-four, borough road sweeper who is on the sixth seat and who has swept the Weir road on Sundays for eleven unpaid years without ever being given a map with it on it; **Delphine Ofori**, seventy-eight, the holder in the first rota slot.

### Instruments Jonas refused in this batch

Three distinct forms, each refused for a different reason, and each one a way of making him the invisible owner or of making nobody responsible:
1. **Service Continuity Rider — Temporary Maintenance Classification** (department, Chapter 14): would reclassify the gate as equipment and buy a standby technician. Refused because the record would say *equipment* and no name.
2. **Temporary Local Continuity Waiver — Saint Orra West, Care Access** (hospital administration, Chapter 16): would let *the issuer or a designated municipal officer* open the gate in a gap. Refused because a category cannot be told no. Gunnar Halvorsen is the west site's service manager and is a pressured local figure, not a villain.
3. **Jonas's own fourteen-day private rota** (Chapter 16): found by Bea Osunde, who kept it. Refused because it named one person and no one who could call a doctor.

## Chapters 21–30 established canon

### Calendar (authoritative for the rest of Volume 1)

- **The volume's calendar is anchored at Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March. Every weekday in Volume 1 follows from that anchor and must be computed from it. The dates are the dates; the weekdays are not the real-world calendar and must never be reconciled against it.**
- Ch 21 = **Thursday 13 March**. Ch 22 = **Friday 14 March**. Ch 23 = **Saturday 15 March**. Ch 24 = **Sunday 16 March**. Ch 25 = **Monday 17 March**. Ch 26 = Monday 17th night, nine o'clock, into the small hours of **Tuesday 18 March** — the kitchen meeting breaks up at about 03:40, and the first bank work is at low water 05:05 on the 18th. Ch 27 = **Friday 21 March**. Ch 28 = **Saturday 22 March**. Ch 29 = **Monday 24 March**. Ch 30 = **Tuesday 25 March into Wednesday 26 March**.
- Chapters 19–20 are **Wednesday 12 March**.
- The series 6 response is due **28 March** (a Friday). The **Reclamation Season maintenance review is Tuesday 1 April**. The Saint Orra west site board sits **the ninth of next month**.

### The barrier on the lowland dip

- The flap gate at the bottom of the dip: a disc of green-painted iron on a cast frame, four feet across. The **pintle is rusted solid**. The **sill beam is rotten through the depth across the middle third**, hollow to the hand.
- **There are two sets of bolt holes in the frame.** The current pintle was fitted into holes forty millimetres out from the original, larger, sound ones, so the flap has never sat square. This is why the fill behind the bank is full.
- The chamber floor is **1.60 m below datum**; the sill's top face is **1.10 m below datum**. **A neap low is 1.5 m; a spring low is 2.4 m.** Therefore *in a neap the chamber never clears the beam* and the work can only be done in a spring. Margo Doré had believed the opposite for twenty-nine years; Jonas corrected her from the almanac on 21 March and she accepted it in one paragraph.
- **Tides:** floods on the 8th, 15th, 22nd, 29th and 6th. Springs 12th–22nd. Neaps 22nd–26th. The usable working window is the **morning ebb: low water between 05:05 and 05:30, everything out of the chamber and gone by 07:30**. The evening ebb is dark and was refused. Four days of tide is **nine hours and ten minutes** of work; the cost is paying people for the waiting. **The 04:00 hour on the loft wall paper is the muster, not the working time** — Jonas corrects the paper on the 17th, and says so on the 21st at 03:30 when he is the one sitting in the kitchen.
- **The centuries-old operating error:** the flap was being shut **by hand on the ebb**, which holds water behind it, which is why the bank boiled. Under the frame, painted on the concrete beneath a lead seal: **DO NOT CLOSE ON THE EBB. CLOSE ON THE FLOOD. 6-L/14 — LOWLANDS — 8·III.**
- **The repair, completed Friday 21 March:** rotten middle third cut out; new seasoned ash beam; new pintle; stops set by Jonas three times; bank cut back two metres and revetted with four tonnes of stone; flap set to shut on the flood. It shut itself at **02:10 on 22 March, unattended**, and the barrier held for four hours and ten minutes.
- **The honest limit, written on the loft wall by Jonas:** it will hold an ordinary tide and a great deal of rain. **It will not hold a surge.**
- **There is no name on the bank for any flood.** A ring of pencil on the loft wall reads **THE 22ND, AT 03:00, ON A BANK. NO NAME.** On the 22nd, Perdita Sow was on it alone with her own lamp, having swept that road on Sundays for eleven unpaid years, and rang the loft at 02:10 to say two words.

### The Weir stand term (6-L/19) — issued and expired

- The stand pillar carries a second unindexed plate: **6-L/19, LOWLAND WARDS, WEIR STAND, DRAWING POINT**, with the duty *water is drawn only by the person named on the rota for that hour* and *if the silt trap is packed the stand is closed*, an empty **HOLDER** field, and an illegible claim.
- The fault on it is a **seam**: a rota claim and a mains premises claim with no order between them.
- Jonas's bounded edit mended **precedence only**. The plate printed:
  - **PRECEDENCE MENDED. THIS POINT ANSWERS TO THE HOLDER ON THE ROTA.**
  - **A CLAIM FROM OUTSIDE THE ROTA DOES NOT OVERRIDE THE HOLDER AND DOES NOT CANCEL THE ROTA.**
  - **AN EMERGENCY DRAW FOR CLINICAL REASON IS PERFORMED AND ENTERED ON THE ROTA.**
  - **THE HOLDER MAY REFUSE ANY DRAW. A REFUSAL ENDS THE DRAW AND NOT THE ROTA.**
  - **CLAIM: THE HOLDER KEEPS THIS POINT TRUE UNTIL THE SILT TRAP IS CLEAR. UNPAID.**
  - **TERM: 14 DAYS FROM THIS READING, OR THE COMPLETION OF THE BARRIER WORK, WHICHEVER IS SOONER. NO CARRYING FORWARD. RENEWED ONLY BY THE HOLDER ON THE ROTA, READING IT ALOUD TO THE NEXT HOLDER ON THE ROTA.**
  - **ISSUER UNCHANGED. THE ISSUER OF THE PREVIOUS TERM IS NOT A HOLDER OF THIS ONE.**
- **Issuer line: THE HOLDER ON THE WEIR STAND ROTA, KEEPER M. DORÉ.** Jonas is **not** the issuer. A plate will not take a role without a name; Margo consented to her name for that reason and said she would rather be found by a rota than by a record.
- **Witnesses:** Margo Doré, **Bea Osunde** (who drove ninety minutes to be one and stopped the work over the holder field), and Ottie Beck.
- **The term EXPIRED at 13:30 on Friday 21 March**, on the second of its two expiry triggers, because the barrier work finished that afternoon. It was not noticed for five days. Since it lapsed the plate no longer asks the rota anything and the tap runs by itself, because when the rota does not answer the mains claim wins.
- **It can only be renewed by the holder on the rota reading it aloud to the next holder on the rota.** Not by Jonas, not by the department, not by the county. Nobody on the rota has done this yet.
- **The term's own recorded failure:** at 02:30 on Friday 21 March, ninety minutes after Delphine Ofori closed the stand correctly, Ottie Beck could not get water for a four-year-old with a fever, because the emergency clause requires the holder to perform the draw and the holder was in bed. The boil's cause and the missing water are two faces of the same seam.

### The Weir numbers

- **Hector Mbatha's door count: 61 in April 1994, 59 last April**, recounted every April for thirty-two years, in a pocket diary with a rubber band and a length of green twine. Fifty-nine is the current number. A door has been bricked up since the flood year that he still counts.
- **The stand's rota board says SLOTS: 40 and 40 is minutes of water a day, not households.** A water authority man entered 40 into *households served* in a report in Saltmouth nine years ago, and the report is still in a library there. Margo knew and did not correct it for nine years.
- The Weir has **59 doors**. Nine of them are named in this canon only as numbers: 12, 22, 31 (Pia's mother's), 40.

### The Weir's money

- All-in job with the marine crew, the pump and the mason: **£7,130** (crew £3,600; pump £1,900; stone £340; skip £410; materials £280; mason £600). Without pump and mason: **£4,630**. Without the marine crew too: **£1,030**. In hand: **£913** (£72 loft tin, £740 water tin, £101 church hall).
- The county form's **box four** was left blank in three people's handwriting: Margo Doré, Hector Mbatha, Aurrie Nkemdirim. **No pump, no mason.**
- The skip was **not hired**, because an unpumped chamber is not cut. **£620 was spent** (stone and materials) plus **£140** for the ash beam, of which **£39 was Aurrie Nkemdirim's own money** out of the exchange tin. **£760 against £913. Nobody on that street has been paid for any of it.**

### The Weir's three positions, now on a wall

- **FOR THE APPLICATION — Aurrie Nkemdirim. FOR THE COUNTY PUMP — Margo Doré. FOR CARRYING ON UNNUMBERED — Ottoline Beck. THE BANK — anyone who will take it, and there is no name on that line because there is no name yet.**
- The refusal that must be respected forever in this series: **a refusal ends the draw or the shift, not the job, and the person refusing does not have to be argued out of it.** Margo refused box four. Hector refused the bank. Aurrie refused the bank and refused to own the street. Wesley Kpomah refused his Wednesday slot in writing on the board. Bo will not carry Jonas's two letters up the path.
- The demand, stated by Pia and not withdrawn: **not a count, not a survey, not a statement — who is the other end of it.**

### The evidence under the sill

- A **lead service seal** on the frame, stamped **6-L/14 · SERVICE · LOWLAND WORKS · XI·17 · S&D IW** — eleven years old, from a district ironworks.
- Painted on the concrete beneath it: **DO NOT CLOSE ON THE EBB. CLOSE ON THE FLOOD. 6-L/14 — LOWLANDS — 8·III.**
- Cold-chiselled into the sound cheek of the rotten beam: **E·M**, and a punch.
- **Jonas wanted E·M to be Evan and it cannot be.** The seal is eleven years old; Evan died eighteen years ago; he was a Continuity Project engineer and did not work for a district ironworks. The two marks are most likely the same fitter's. That is the finding, and it is a question about a district ironworks, not a answer about a father.
- The seal and the beam are in an oilcloth envelope in an **unlabelled tin in the loft, held by Ottoline Beck.** The Weir's rule: nobody on that street tells the county or the department it exists, and the answer if asked is that the loft keeps a tin. **Jonas may not photograph it, write its number in a departmental document, or keep it.**

### The registrar's answer (filed 11:44, 14 March, public)

- **Finding 1: the 21:09 acknowledgement was a breach.** Finding 2: **the 15:58 acknowledgement is ratified as an emergency opening under the standing reduction, and the department, not the individual, accepts the claim.**
- Consequence 1: Jonas remains issuer, on the issuer line; the maintenance claim is **not discharged and not transferred**, because a department cannot be a holder of a plate term.
- Consequence 2: removed from all gate-initiation at 6-41 **and from cabinet 6-R-14** until a review of the review.
- Consequence 3: the maintenance history of 6-L/14 and 6-L/19 **cannot be produced**; a physical inspection of both before the review date is proposed, at the issuer's cost of attendance.
- Consequence 4: **the permanent appointment review is deferred** to the review of the review.
- Annexes, public: the gate log, the 02:40 safeguarding form, the conflict record, the released cardiac slot and next available date, and the 12:05 refusal and 12:50 declaration in the published gap.
- The withdrawn first draft is in the registrar's answer, in the minute, with a name and a time: **Royden Achebe withdrew draft one at 10:09 on 14 March**, because a category cannot be the holder of a plate. It is not an Assembly sitting and no later chapter may put it in the Assembly chamber.

### The non-civic classification (Jonas's own six words)

- Written at **12:41 on 14 March** in the records room, initialled, on a form headed *state the civic function of each node in chain*. Lodged into the series 6 file on **22 March at 14:10**.
- **6-L/14 — NON-CIVIC PLANT. 6-L/19 — NON-CIVIC PLANT.**
- If the review accepts them, both nodes leave the review and the Weir leaves the city's obligations. Nothing is taken away; it simply stops being on any list, and there is nothing to appeal against.
- **One atmosphere seed, not an offer:** Pia says there will be "a woman from CivicCore's press office at the back" of the 1 April review, "because they come to everything now and they have started coming early." **CivicCore has made no offer to Jonas and Rook has not appeared.** The contractor's presence at a public review is a fact about the world, not a bargain, and must not be grown into an offer before Chapter 31.
- **The only cure that does not put his name on a lie**, per Oren Pell: a **refusal to classify** — that the local custodians were asked for a statement of function and declined, that the reporting officer therefore has no basis for a finding, and that in the absence of a finding the node should remain in scope until somebody with standing makes it. **The Weir has refused to help him write it. As of Chapter 30 it is unfiled.**

### The clinic gate, Chapters 21–30

- The term has been **renewed three times at the 22:00 handover, out loud, by the named bearers**: 13 March, 19 March, 24 March. The current term **expires 06:00 on 30 March.** A renewal is a fresh six-day term and a fresh decision; Mina Okafor says so out loud and refuses to be the one who teaches the lines.
- At the 13 March renewal Ruth Nwosu wrote, in the box, in capitals: *my hours on this record say 23:00 and I have read them back twice and they still say 23:00. I have renewed this term with that written down here.*
- **The gap did real work on 24 March.** At 12:02 a stretcher came up the ramp; the plate said HOLDER NOT NAMED; Mina telephoned the on-call clinician; **Sanaa did not answer for nine minutes** because she was in a resuscitation room. Opened 12:12, returned 12:31. The log and the public box carry the nine minutes.
- **Bram Osei refused Thursday** and told Mina the reason, which is his granddaughter's birthday, so that the day would go on the board as a refusal and not as a birthday. **The board now reads 06:00 NO HOLDER on a weekday morning** and the roster does not say who covers a refusal.
- **Ruth's hours are corrected to 22:00–11:00 from 15 March** by a signed rostering exception. The nineteen years are not computed. Ruth refuses the number, has it in a **red diary from a newsagent in her bag**, and asked that the board say only that the hours are right from the fifteenth.
- **The gap is six hours, not two and a half.** Her record carries 23:00–06:00 (seven hours) against a declared 22:00–11:00 (thirteen), so the unrecorded labour is **six hours a night, most nights** — the same six hours Chapter 12 called the pencilled window short. The **two and a half hours** belongs to the 11:00–13:30 gate gap and is a different instrument; do not mix them. The **£118,000** Ruth names on the 24th is the only figure that has ever been spoken, she refuses to give it, and it is what six hours at a night rate across nineteen years comes to. Nobody else may compute it.
- **The retainer is still unsigned.** Halvorsen has entered in his diary: **ninth of next month, Saint Orra west site board, retrospective retainer, return-of-care limb, 22:00–06:00, carried unpaid since the fifteenth of March.** A diary entry is not a cheque. The words **not yet agreed** stay on the board.

### Jonas's confession on the Weir

- The man in the department van in **2019** who told Bo Trimble the gasket was the wrong size, broke the stand for six weeks, and was asked for a cigarette outside a hardware shop and refused, was **Jonas**. He admitted it in a kitchen on the 17th, unprompted, after Bo's father-of-the-moment pressure, and added that Hector had put it to him the day before, at the loft kitchen table, that the man had his face and he had said nothing. Margo Doré's nine years of *I have watched that happen* is now a suspicion confirmed.

## Chapters 31–40 established canon

### Calendar (authoritative for the rest of Volume 1)

- **One decision, applied batch-wide: the calendar is the Ch 21–30 anchor (Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March). Do not recompute any weekday from the real calendar. Where a weekday is needed, count from 13 March.**
- Ch 31 = **Thursday 27 March.** Ch 32 = **Friday 28 March** (series 6 response filed). Ch 33 = **Saturday 29 March** (waiver refused; 22:00 renewal, new expiry 06:00 4 April). Ch 34 = **Sunday 30 March** (the 06:00 expiry is superseded by that renewal; Brine Wharf closed at 18:00). Ch 35 = **Monday 31 March.** Ch 36 = **Tuesday 1 April** (review in the morning, Assembly in the afternoon). Ch 37 = **Wednesday 2 April.** Ch 38 = **Wednesday 2 April** afternoon and night, into **Thursday 3 April** morning. Ch 39 = **Friday 4 April.** Ch 40 = **Saturday 5 April.**
- **1 April is a Tuesday.** 8 April is a **Tuesday**, 9 April a **Wednesday**, 14 April a **Monday**, 15 April a **Tuesday**, 17 April a **Thursday**, 19 April a **Saturday**, 23 April a **Wednesday**.
- Standing dates ahead: **Nora Bell, Monday 14 April, 15:30.** **Ruth Nwosu reads the term at 22:00 on Monday 7 April and has refused the fifteenth**; the term expires 06:00 on **Tuesday 8 April** and the 7 April renewal runs to 06:00 on **Wednesday 15 April**. **Saint Orra west site board: Wednesday 9 April.** **Weir stand term expires 06:00 on Saturday 19 April; a flood is forecast for Thursday 17 April.** **Cooper's Reach appeal panel: Wednesday 23 April.** **Standing Committee report on the continuity contract: 2 June.**

### The contractor's bargain (Chapters 31–33) — spent

- **CivicCore's four-part offer, made by Ceri Vaughan** (continuity counsel, forty-five) in a first-floor room over a tool-hire place on Marsh Lane on 27 March, and recorded by her as **REFUSED** rather than deferred: (1) permanent appointment, grade four equivalent, **£44,000** against Jonas's £31,400, probation closed; (2) **root-level notice access** — every root notice, sent and unsent, as it is sent; (3) **every term Jonas has ever issued continued without expiry**, maintenance carried by an on-call contractor, the maintenance claim discharged; (4) the **blanket continuity waiver** in exchange: no local term, holder, refusal, witness arrangement or standing practice is incompatible with continuity maintenance, and **the refusal of any person, rota or institution does not suspend, delay or condition continuity maintenance.** Expiry of the offer: the twentieth of April. There is a laminated card with a reference on it. He has not signed and has not said no.
- **The participation fund offer**, withdrawn at 10:10 on 29 March: the arrears and the return-of-care retainer retrospective into the west site's own participation fund so that the hospital's service manager administers and countersigns it. Vaughan refused to say Ruth's number in a room because it is not written down anywhere.
- **Alden Rook, CivicCore operations director, forty-six**, from a water authority, carries a ledger, and is right about enough that Jonas cannot dismiss him. He quotes a service floor in Gantry Ward: **240 customers, 41 hinges on the board at a time, 9 of them not his staff.** NDC median first attendance on a failed civic hinge: **nineteen days.** Morrow Fields Plot 6 pump: forty-seven days departmental, four hours forty with CivicCore. **Wren Court, Salter's Yard, Tideglass: thirty-one rooms, no civic address, a named steward, a monthly participation fee, better service than the west clinic, and clause nine (disconnection for non-payment, sixty days' notice, one visit, a photograph of the meter).** Rook refuses to price 6-L/14 or 6-L/19 and says he will not send anybody up that footpath. **Rook's one ask, refused: if the Weir ever becomes a contract, bring the street first, and let him be fourth.**
- **Rook's prediction, delivered on 28 March and correct:** a panel cannot act on the absence of a finding; the cheapest way to clear an open item is to accept the reporting officer. He told Jonas to hold the sentence and let the silence sit, and warned that good news usually arrives in somebody else's coat.
- **The refusal, 29 March, 09:30, department second-floor briefing room, minuted, and the minute published on the public board on Monday 31 March.** Present: Jonas, Royden Achebe, Nina Calder (records), Ceri Vaughan, Tomas Renn, Bea Osunde, Oren Pell. Sanaa Iqbal was telephoned in the room. Jonas could not find any clause in the document that stops a rota keeping its own door, and said so. His stated reasons: the expected one (a category cannot be told no) and two new ones — **he cannot tell whether he is signing as a man who believes it or as a man who needs the cabinet, the appointment and the arrears, and the form has no box for that**; and **the page works by finding whoever is closest and putting their name in, and the nearest responsible person on the lowland chain is him.** Bea Osunde said, in the minute, that he is the only man anybody can point at and say *you were here when it started*.
- **The appointment review was concluded once and only once, on the morning of Thursday 27 March, in eleven minutes**: civic practice sound, the delays in production not attributable to the individual, appointment granted. Vaughan quotes that decision in Chapter 31 and then offers to buy it. **On 29 March at 10:10 the outcome comes out of the tray and is refused by Jonas, not concluded.** Jonas has been through review twice in total: the registrar's review of the review of 14 March and the appointment review of 27 March. Do not date either review to 1 April.
- **Cost of the refusal:** appointment **declined**, not deferred — grade three, probationary, reviewable at the end of the season. **The west-clinic maintenance claim assigned in writing in the series 6 file to the nearest responsible maintenance route, which is Jonas.** Two CivicCore crews off the Saint Orra queue, twelve city jobs back to nineteen days. The participation fund withdrawn.
- **Sanaa Iqbal's minuted position:** the waiver is the best argument for it anybody will make in ten years, it is the clause that would have stopped her being nine minutes late, and she does not want it, because the day the door opens for anybody who claims it she stops being the person who decides. She asked for that minuted so that no one later says she said no out of loyalty to a man she has not certified.

### The non-civic classification — split on 1 April

> **6-L/14: NON-CIVIC PLANT. ACCEPTED. NODE LEAVES THE REVIEW. NO CIVIC CLAIM ATTACHES TO THE CONTENTS OF THE CHAMBER OR THE BANK.**
> **6-L/19: NON-CIVIC PLANT. NOT ACCEPTED. NODE REMAINS IN SCOPE PENDING PHYSICAL INSPECTION. NO INSPECTION SCHEDULED — NO LOCATION OF RECORD.**

- The panel is the Series 6 panel of the Reclamation Season maintenance review, Old Ward committee room two, 09:30, six members, public gallery of **forty-one**, nineteen of whom were still in the gallery when the Assembly sat at two o'clock. Chair **Councillor Fenella Marsh.** Speaker of the Assembly **Councillor Adaeze Farrant.**
- Jonas **declines to maintain, declines to withdraw, and refuses to read the legible condition line on the 6-L/14 plate into a public record**, on the ground that a minute is a list and the only way a street ever gets an address is if somebody living there is made findable in order to produce one. Minuted as a decision at 09:42, not as an oversight. **The line therefore goes into the file unread.** It reads: IF THE FLAP IS NOT SHUT, THE BANK IS NOT A WALL.
- **Oren Pell objects on filing grounds only:** a node with a lapsed holder is not the same instrument as a node with no holder, and that difference is the entire dispute.
- The six words are Jonas's: two node numbers and two two-word findings, initialled 12:41 on the fourteenth of March, lodged by Achebe on the twenty-second under Jonas's reporting officer number. **They must be counted, not paraphrased, in any later chapter.**
- **The Assembly, 2:00, 1 April:** the continuity contract referred back to committee **to report 2 June**, on a show of hands, no division. The Weir's board paper received under the standing order on a member's submission, over Jonas's objection, and is now a document. **Councillor Iven Sore** moves that the Assembly require a named service holder for 6-L/19 and **loses on the standing orders twice over** — the Assembly may not require a person to accept a duty, and the motion needs a service whose existence was not established that morning. She says she would move it again. The Brine Wharf order is **ratified, subject to the 23 April panel.**
- **The interim post.** A motion to appoint an interim lowland maintenance review pending the inspection, to be filled by the reporting officer of record for **series 6 plate 6-41** and the lowland chain hanging off it. **Jonas declines, in the room, minuted.** **Royden Achebe takes it in his own name** on the ground that a department cannot hold a plate and a man can, and that the department would have been thrown out inside a week. Jonas tries to raise the 14 March withdrawal and is ruled out of time.
- **The Weir's paper is now in a public record.** It is a document, not a filing. **It carries no door number and no list of houses, and it names nobody** — it refers to the one technician whose name the department already has and to a woman who paid £39 of her own money. Sore certifies exactly that, and Jonas's only intervention is to point out that the paper does not name him. Sore's warning to Jonas: it will be a file in four weeks and will be produced in a hearing about a plant asset in six months, and he is the only man she has met who kept it out of one.
- **Three different objects, and they are not interchangeable:**
  1. **The loft's paper** (Chapters 22 and 28, received by the Assembly 1 April): one side of a sheet of ruled net-loft paper, unsigned, no letterhead, the work and the cost and the sentence in the middle, pinned to the Old Ward board on 22 March with four paperclips and a piece of tape over the seam. No door numbers and no list.
  2. **Hector Mbatha's pencil map** (Chapter 31): a separate sheet clipped to the same board on 24 March with a bent paperclip, **fifty-nine door marks**, a tree and a number against the tree. It is on the board because it went up on its own, and Jonas does not touch it.
  3. **The folded slip in Jonas's coat** (Chapter 35): **written by Pia Santos herself on the Saturday morning of 29 March**, on the back of his own copy of the series 6 form, at the bottom of the slipway. Four names, three positions, a ring round a blank space, and under the ring, in Jonas's own hand, **THE 22ND, AT 03:00, ON A BANK. NO NAME.** It is the only thing in his coat he may not write into a file.

### Brine Wharf (Chapters 34–35) — new site, new people, new appeal

- **Brine Wharf, Cooper's Reach, at the head of the buried canal.** Five storeys. A ground-floor clinic running a **Thursday session**, list of **forty-one patients, nine over seventy-five, three housebound.** Eleven flats above. In the basement, **Lowland Refuge Store 1, series 6 plate 6-L/21** — foam rolls 60, sandbags 2,400, portable pump, generator, blankets 140 — **HOLDER: —**, three painted initials from 1988, and eleven centimetres of lettering chiselled off below the holder. Its only civic line reads: **IF THIS HOLDER IS EMPTY, THIS STORE IS NOT A STORE.**
- **Yasmin Rohan, fifty-three**, residents' steward for eleven years, holds the register and one of two keys. **Ferdy Oduya, thirty-four**, holds the second key, works nights at the grain terminal, and is the one who gets called at 02:00. **Joseph Anum, eighty-one**, flat nine since 1991.
- **The defect is real and Oren Pell is right.** January substructure survey: thirty-four millimetres of drop at the north-west corner of the ground-floor slab. A remedial notice for a hundred and eighty millimetres of temporary works and a monitoring pin. Freeholder's agent replied on 9 February that the works were not a landlord obligation. The pin was removed. **Jonas measures it himself on 30 March: fifty-four millimetres, three worse than the registrar's fifty-one, in the corner the escape route discharges into. He refuses to conceal it.** The order is served on fifty-one because the figure was measured on the twenty-second and a person may not be moved out on a measurement taken the day the order is made.
- **The renewed flood-safety order: made 17:40, served 17:52, effective 18:00, 30 March.** Eleven flats and 406 possessions out into two borough vans and a hired coach to **the Anchor House hostel in a street off Salt Row, bought out for fourteen nights**, the last of them the night of 12 April. From 13 April nobody has paid for anything.
- **The store goes to a lock-up on Marsh Lane with a contracted keyholder on a seven-to-seven clock**, at a cost of about £94 a week that nobody can pay. CivicCore offered on 27 March, in writing, to hold the store and stand the cost of the Carrow Lane clinic for eleven weeks; **Yasmin Rohan declined in one sentence on 31 March** — *the lowland declines, thank you, because in the fourth year of this we would be a client.* They do not press it.
- **The appeal: filed 31 March, panel 23 April.** Twenty-three days' notice against a fourteen-day minimum. Representation required — solicitor, officer of a recognised civic body, or a person the panel accepts. **£340 deposit, refundable on success. £2,000 continuation bond if the order is upheld.** The panel requires the appellant to state in their own words what the safety position would be if the building stayed open. **The Weir cannot be a party** — a party must be a person or an institution with a name on a register, and there is no address, no classification that survives the review, and no unexpired term. **Nell Ardent takes it for nothing, once; enforcement afterwards is £95 an hour.** Yasmin Rohan wrote one name in the fourth line of the form and read it out to nobody.
- **The Weir and the annex refuse alignment, separately, and arrive there independently.** Yasmin Rohan: *do not put two streets in the same place, because the next thing that happens is a count, and the count will be wrong in the way counts are always wrong.* **Oren Pell forbids the Weir's evidence from the appeal** and says why: the term that broke on 21 March is one he did not write and cannot be produced, the department will call the record unreliable, and the department will be right, because the record is unreliable and the reason is a tin in a loft he is not going to ask about.
- The Thursday session moves to the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane, nine hundred metres and nine steps away, from 2 April.

### The cold store and the brass tag (Chapter 39)

- **Bellow Market cold store, series 3 plate, loading hatch.** A borough food-safety hold issued 23:00 on Thursday 2 April after a compressor trip at six degrees for ninety minutes that afternoon. A collectors' self-collect term, 05:00–09:00, member's own claim. **Its holder is named, and this satisfies the Stage 2 sub-rule: rule eleven of the collectors' rules, written in 2009, puts the market's working chef in the holder field for the day's window and her name in the witness field beside it, because a plate will not take a bare role and because she is the one person who can say what a fish was doing on a pallet and on a table. Fola Adeyemi has stood at that hatch every morning for twenty-two years, holds a borough licence in her own name, and put her name to rule eleven herself, so being named is not a new exposure.** No order between the two terms. The hatch opens and shuts, four seconds open, six shut, and eleven traders are locked out of their own stock.
- The only civic notice in the batch: **WHEN TWO TERMS ANSWER TO EACH OTHER, THE HATCH IS BOTH OPEN AND SHUT.** It is a condition and it does not say what to do.
- Jonas cannot read the fault, let alone mend it, because the holder must instruct it and the only named person refuses. **Fola Adeyemi, fifty-nine, refuses in the room in front of the eleven of them, and then acts anyway — in her own role as the holder, on her own terms, and not for him.** She tells Jonas she is doing it because **rule eleven has her name in the holder field for the day, which is the field that does not let her go home**, and she will be sixty next year and unemployed.
- **The bounded edit: one field, precedence only, issuer the market's working chef, claim unpaid. THE HOLDER OF THIS TERM ANSWERS TO A FOOD-SAFETY HOLD IN FORCE. A HELD STORE IS NOT A COLLECTION POINT. A HOLDER MAY REFUSE ANY COLLECTION. THE HATCH DOES NOT OPEN AND SHUT.** The hold was lifted by **Harminder Bhullar**, borough environmental health officer, at 11:20. The collection happened at 16:20, more than seven hours outside the term's own window. A fish business lost the following Saturday, which is the day a fish business makes its rent.
- **The object.** Under the standoff, in the bottom of the hinge housing, a brass service tag, the size of a two-pound coin, punched, torn from its twin at the crimp. Stamped **C.P. SERIES 6 · LOWLAND WORKS · XI·17**, hand-inked **6-L/14 · 6-L/19 · 6-40 — SET OF THREE — RETURN ALL THREE.** It establishes that 6-40, the Saint Orra bridge side-hinge service relay, was maintained as one service with the two lowland nodes. It is not an answer. **Jonas gives it to Fola Adeyemi, who puts it in an apron pocket.** He does not have it, may not have it, and may not write it anywhere. The Weir's tin is a different tin and is still sealed.

### The two-refusal night (Chapter 38) — one clock, 2–3 April

- **The clock for this chapter is authoritative and must not be varied. Car at Saint Orra west 06:00 on Thursday 3 April; declaration given 06:12; door open 06:19; six minutes up the ramp; baby out 06:31; Mina writing it up 07:10; Tomas telephoned 07:10.** The public number is **nineteen minutes from the car to the door** and it is on the board, the box, the yellow board in the shop, and every state file. There is no second version of this clock.
- **Renewal, Tuesday 1 April, 22:00.** Ezra Mbeki reads the five lines, Ruth Nwosu reads them back, Ruth signs the box, Mina Okafor writes the times. **New term to 06:00 on 8 April.** For about eleven seconds the plate's HOLDER NOT NAMED is untrue.
- **Two refusals on the same night, both on Wednesday 2 April, neither argued out of it.** Ruth Nwosu, in writing on the board at 14:20, for a back, after four nights out of five; she had been on from ten the previous night until eleven that morning, went home, slept until two, and left for Salt Row again. Selah Mora, by telephone at 16:20, because her mother has a chest and her daughter had nobody; Aurrie Nkemdirim took the child at eleven o'clock at night without being asked and is not on any rota and does not want to be in a document.
- **The Wednesday 2 April 22:00 handover has one holder instead of two.** Ezra reads to Mina, signs the outgoing box, puts the black key in the drawer, and asks for the record to read *offered, refused, twice* and not *no cover available*. He goes home at 22:10 with three days left on the term and nobody to hand it to.
- **Dr. Sanaa Iqbal answered on the second ring.** She was at a kitchen table in Kestrel Hill doing a school-holiday week's discharge letters she had volunteered for at 21:00 on Wednesday, not in a resuscitation room. Thirteen minutes against nine on 24 March, and she has said the difference is her and not the design.
- **Marta Sowle, ninety-one, was awake** and came out of bay nine in a cardigan and slippers, and asked who was holding the door, and told Mina to put *holder not named, door opened on a declaration, nineteen minutes* in the public box, and told Jonas to keep his name off the door.
- **Ivo Bamba** held a broken handrail in the dark with his shoulder for the whole way up. **Jonas pushed, on the off side, and could not have opened the door and did not try.**
- **Tomas Renn's question is not answered.** He is asked to write it up in his own hand and to decide in advance whether he will be able to say in four months that it worked because two women said no and a doctor happened to be awake. It is now the fourth time he has asked.
- On **Saturday 5 April** Jonas rebolted the east handrail of the ramp — twenty-five minutes, flat bar and four bolts, no plate, no witness, no permission. **The ramp light is still out and the estates office has the part on order.**

### The Weir stand term — renewed 5 April

- **Renewed at about 21:40 on Saturday 5 April by Delphine Ofori, seventy-eight, reading the five lines aloud to Bo Trimble, seventeen, who has been on that water rota since he was eleven.** A slot was refused in writing by a man of forty-four who works nights and the ten minutes were not made up. **The term runs fourteen days from that reading, to 06:00 on Saturday 19 April**, because the barrier work is complete and the sooner trigger has already fired once. **Jonas was at the bottom of the slipway and was not invited up.** He did not hear the last two lines of the term: the wind took them, and he was not going to be told them again by a woman of seventy-eight on a night when she was tired.
- **The cost is Bo's name.** He is findable now, at a water point, on a street with no address, and he knew it and took it.
- **The trap is packed, the tarp is off the intake grille, the water is the colour of weak tea, the boil is back, and a flood is forecast for 17 April** — two days before the term runs out. Clearing a trap costs two unpaid people on a Wednesday morning at low water, 05:05, and there is no money for it. It is not a civic term and no witness can be put to it.

### The nine witnesses (Chapters 37, 40) — six, and two lines blank

- A **job card** ruled by Jonas himself, nine lines, in pencil, with the services on the left. **First ruled on the Thursday afternoon of 26 March, standing up in a yard at the shop, and written up on the Saturday morning of 5 April.** It is not a file and it is not a document and it lives in his coat. **He wrote a refusal onto it in full.**
- **Six yes.** **Nora Bell**, Saint Orra crossing guard, nineteen years, *Monday 14 April, half past three, at that end of the barrier, bring your own paper* — a standing appointment, not a signature, and she refused to be given the ninety seconds without the week, and she refused any Thursday. **Mina Okafor**, west-clinic transfer clerk, **three conditions in her own hand:** every term she witnesses is read aloud in that corridor at the handover in the same five lines; nothing goes on the public board with her name on it that has not been on the board already; **and she withdraws on 9 April if there is no cheque.** **Emeka Rusk**, pump operator, docks, twenty-six years, read the five lines back twice and said he would rather be named than be the man who is not there. **Teo Vlah**, thirty-four, mobile mechanic, Rook Street, **machines only, and he made Jonas write it in the line before he said yes.** **Roz Idris**, year five, Gantry Fields, **not for anything a child is inside, and not for anything she has been told is failing by the person who wants it fixed.** **Kofi Sarr**, Saint Orra platform dispatcher, twenty-one years, **a platform and never a passenger.**
- **One no, recorded.** **Fola Adeyemi, fifty-nine**, market cook, Bellow Market, twenty-two years at the griddle: *I have a gas account, a supplier who can take the unit out on a Thursday, a lease with a landlord who owns four other cook-houses, a licence with my name on it, and a rota with eleven people on it. That is four things that can be served on me on a Tuesday. You do not need a cook. You need somebody nobody can dismiss.* **She is also, by rule eleven of the collectors' rules, the named holder of the self-collect term and the named witness of record, and she has since taken the brass tag.**
- **Two lines blank. The seventh is a street and the ninth is a hospital.** Jonas has **decided not to ask** Pia Santos or Sanaa Iqbal and has written nothing on either line. He has told nobody. Bea Osunde: a card with a name and a day and a time is fine; a card with a subject on it is a list, and a hearing is the one room in this city where a list is not a list, and the ninth is going to cost somebody. Tomas Renn: *seven lines, and you have written one of them in the wrong direction, and the wrong one is the one you are proudest of.*

## Attended procedures the opening batch relies on

These municipal instruments, not Jonas’s ability, are the legal basis of every temporary route in Chapters 1–10. None of them mends a civic term or creates a holder.

- **Bridge attended bypass (Chapter 1):** requires a person at each end, the dispatcher on the line, and the receiving clinic accepting the arrival. Closing any one of the three ends the window. It is hardware-only; a suppression lead would hide the conflict and is prohibited.
- **Clinic emergency maintenance card (Chapter 2):** permits an attended escort for a patient with a verified care claim, provided the physical route is inspected and the responsible watch remains until the escort ends. It grants no new holder and does not alter the clinic’s night permission.
- **Attended escort procedure (Chapter 4):** opens a narrow attended path from a vehicle to a service entrance, witnessed by the clinic care claim, the clerk, and the vehicle’s custodian. It does not open the vehicle to the public and creates no public transit permission.
- **Assembly Emergency Service Test (Chapters 3 and 10):** the separately inspected clause under which Oren may keep one bounded ambulance lane on a closed crossing, with named witnesses, an expiry, and his right to terminate. Inside a running test, a named witness may not simply leave at their own shift end: they remain until the expiry or name a named successor who accepts the duty on the record. Chapter 10 exercises this and charges Ruth thirty unpaid minutes.

## Places and fixtures established on-page

- The Saint Orra pedestrian bridge carries a tram deck and a westbound trolley signal that Dev Singh can hold for a limited period before rail control objects.
- The Saint Orra transit platform has at least four numbered bays; platform four was the Chapter 4 shuttle stop.
- The Old Ward public notice board stands beneath a glass canopy between the ferry office and the Assembly’s public doors, near the Old Ward bridge.
- The board carries, permanently: **CENTRAL ROOT — HOLDER: —**; the Reclamation Season maintenance-review line; the west-clinic gate's own service log with a tick box; the department's filed response of 14 March; and, since 22 March, the non-civic classification line. A printed **service diagram** pinned over the lower half of the map now shows one filled circle and one open square (*unregistered plate*) four millimetres apart in the bottom-left corner, with no street name. Beside the frame, clipped on by an unnamed hand on 24 March, is **Hector Mbatha's pencil map: THE WEIR. 59 DOORS. STAND, CHAMBER, BANK, AND THE TREE BY NUMBER 12.**
- The **River Stacks shop** has a yellow board (the bond), a wall cabinet with drawer four (series cards, now closed to Jonas), a bench, and a yard with a parapet the rain comes in over.
- The **net loft** kitchen carries, on the wall above the shelf of cleaned cups: a civic plate with its lower face scraped; the three named positions with a name against each; a ring of pencil round a blank space reading **THE 22ND, AT 03:00, ON A BANK. NO NAME.**; the barrier record; and a dated line about the £140 beam. Under the exchange tin is **the tin with the spares**, which is where the lead seal and the beam are, unlabelled.
- The **Weir stand** is a green pillar with a bucket on a chain, a tap at the bottom, and a forty-slot rota board nailed to the fence beside it.

## Custody and privacy

- Selah Mora is the named watch steward who keeps the old-canal shelter’s sealed full return-of-care and route log.
- The outer seal uses anonymous consent marks. Each resident may review or correct their own entry. Selah may use the log only for completed return-of-care and immediate safety follow-up.
- The log expires at the next route review. Any later use requires the affected resident’s consent.
- The NDC report contains only a redacted custody reference. It contains no resident identities, passenger list, full route, or old-canal address.
- Chapter 10’s municipal test record names the recognized Bellow Market handoff, not the sealed route or residents.
- **The Weir's own custody rule, set in Chapter 27 and binding on every later chapter:** the lead service seal and the rotten beam are in an oilcloth envelope in an unlabelled tin in the loft, held by **Ottoline Beck**. Nobody on that street tells the county, the department, or anybody in Saint Orra that it exists. The answer if asked is that the loft keeps a tin. Jonas may not photograph it, may not write 6-L/14's serial or its date in a departmental document, and may not keep it. **Hector Mbatha asked to hold it and refused, on the record, because a thing that is held is a thing that is yours.**

## Supporting cast established

- Nora Bell: Saint Orra crossing guard; physical witness and anchor during the bridge tests.
- Dev Singh: bridge dispatcher; records crossings, test limits, and route closures.
- Ruth Nwosu: existing Saint Orra west-clinic night-watch steward, she/her. Her work is real even though the local record is incomplete. She is the intended bearer for the first bounded gate repair in Chapters 11–18, not Jonas. She is on shift until the eleven o’clock morning-watch handover while the board claims she signed off at six. In Chapter 10 she stays to the 11:30 test expiry as a named witness with no successor, and pays for it unpaid.
- Mina Okafor: west-clinic transfer clerk; records patient dispositions and clinical handoffs.
- Selah Mora: old-canal watch steward, then first-night handoff steward under Mara Holt at Bellow Market. Protects resident consent and local claims.
- Mara Holt: Bellow Market night steward who receives displaced residents and possessions. A separate, unnamed Bellow Market day steward attends the Chapter 7 noon inspection; do not fold the two roles together.
- Kofi Sarr: Saint Orra platform dispatcher. Wants a passenger list for any unregistered service vehicle, yields when a care claim is live, and never receives one. His boundary was established on-page and should be respected rather than reversed.
- Dr. Sethi: Saint Orra day-team physician who takes observation four at noon.
- Emeka Rusk: experienced pump operator; demonstrates that living maintenance practice can outlive its municipal index.
- Hester Vann: Public Works inspector, she/her, who denies pump reopening on physical grounds and sizes the emergency tank by meter rather than headcount.
- Nina Calder: NDC records supervisor; resists both false clean classification and Jonas’s instinct to carry undocumented responsibility.
- Alma Reyes, Rafi Reyes, and Omar Sadiq: completed opening-batch care/service cases; they are not mystery carriers. Selah and the shelter residents use “Raf” for Rafi Reyes.
- Unnamed functional figures: the municipal recorder at the Chapter 7 inspection and the dispatch clerk who asked for a headcount in Chapter 6.
- **Added in Chapters 21–30:** **Royden Achebe**, forty-nine, NDC field services manager, who withdraws draft one at 10:09 and later lodges the non-civic classification; **Delphine Ofori**, seventy-eight, Weir stand rota holder in the first slot; **Wesley Kpomah** and **Perdita Sow** (see the Weir).

## Protagonist and relationship continuity

- Jonas remains observant and practically competent. He is changing from “fix first, explain later” toward recording what remains unresolved and who can refuse responsibility.
- His Stage 1 view shows relationships between active faults and terms, not motive. Sensory bleed can bring metallic taste, pressure, nausea, and nearby emotional residue. A trusted witness and physical anchor are required for safe reading.
- System voice: the ceramic maintenance notice is the Lattice’s only direct speech, and it states a condition, never a solution or a motive. Five panels appear in Chapters 1–10 (Chapters 2, 3, 7, 9, 10), never more than one in a chapter. They do not read intent, do not name Iona, and must not become a menu, a score, or a hint engine.
- Chapters 21–30 use the same discipline. The one plate notice is **6-L/14's IF THE FLAP IS NOT SHUT, THE BANK IS NOT A WALL**, which is a condition, not a solution. The 6-L/19 mend prints one strip and it is a term, not an answer. No System surface in this batch names a person, explains an erasure, or tells anybody what to do.
- Sanaa remains a physician, not a magical authority. Chapter 9 earns limited professional trust because Jonas states the 43-minute cost exactly. She still challenges the moral framing of technically safe outcomes.
- Tomas refuses an undocumented shared-node edit. His visible responsibility bond must be negotiated, witnessed, and consequential before the first civic repair.
- Pia speaks for herself and for the immediate passengers she chose to protect. She gives no passenger list, does not become Jonas’s apprentice, and does not speak for all unlisted residents.
- Oren is a pressured local official enforcing valid safety rules, not the source of the fault or a hidden villain.
- One slow-burn relationship only: Jonas and Sanaa. No romance has begun.

## Power limits observed and remaining

- Before the visible responsibility bond, Jonas may continue Stage 0 repairs and Stage 1 diagnosis only. That limit was observed; the bond was signed before any civic edit.
- The first civic repair was one bounded local term at the Saint Orra west-clinic gate. The second was one fallback clause at the same plate. Neither touched the root, reconciled separate service chains, or made Jonas permanent.
- The existing night-watch steward is the bearer. Sanaa is a separate informed witness and, since Chapter 17, the named clinician for the declared emergency reduction. Jonas is the issuer and holds a named maintenance claim.
- Every repair required a physical anchor, a clear scope, a witness who understood it, and a claim. The Chapter 13 edit was anchored on the care-entrance plate with **Ruth Nwosu** on the probe; the Chapter 17 amendment was anchored on the same plate with **Ezra Mbeki** on it. **Bram Osei is the day bearer named by Dr. Sethi. He is not a witness to either edit.**
- Between the Chapter 13 repair and the Chapter 17 amendment the fallback did follow Jonas's personal availability, and a transfer was denied on-page as a result. **Since Chapter 17 the fallback no longer follows his availability at all**: it is closed by default and openable only by the named on-call clinician's written declaration. The denied transfer stays on the record and is not discharged.
- The seam mark and the claim remain active. The Chapter 17 amendment explicitly did not discharge either.
- The Chapter 2 gate-use suspension is still live and was breached twice on the page, with the breach minuted. It is not restored clearance.
- Remaining limits for Chapters 31+: Jonas still cannot read the root, issue a citywide command, create a permission for a service with no existing civic function, or act without a witness. He cannot make people consent, choose the morally correct fallback, or know in advance which local practice will hold.
- **New limit established in Chapter 25:** where a local holder has standing, Jonas is not the issuer. He can perform the bounded edit at somebody else's instruction, under somebody else's authority, with a witness who can state the cost — and the maintenance that follows belongs to the holder, not to him.
- **New limit established in Chapter 30:** a renewal clause he wrote is binding on him. He cannot renew a term he did not issue, and a term that expires on the *sooner* of two triggers expires whether or not anybody is watching.

## Reserved Volume 1 reveals

- Chapters 21–27 are **written**. They delivered the barrier repair, a bounded term at the water point, the first civic edit Jonas did not issue, and a fragment under the sill that is two cold-chiselled letters and a paint line. They did **not** deliver the old community-watch term's history, the Quiet Cut, or the civic erasure, and must not be retrofitted to.
- **Still reserved:** the Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the community-watch term, the meaning of the three-line brass tokens, and anything that would make the old-canal route, the Chapter 3 reset, or the Chapter 4 shuttle consequences of the first civic repair.
