- Chapters 28–36: **written.** They delivered the direct offer, Alden Rook, the permanent-employment bargain, the blanket continuity waiver and its refusal, the split classification, the Assembly sitting, and the **separate Lowglass clinic-and-housing annex closure at Brine Wharf with its own appeal**. The opening old-canal shelter remains closed and was not reused, and the old pump stays closed. **CivicCore's direct offer, Rook, the waiver and the annex are all spent and must not be re-run.** The contract survives and is referred to committee to report **2 June**.
- **Chapters 37–48: written.** They delivered the nine-line card, five refusals written in full, the surge, the witnessed handover, the map with the blank centre, the public “last working system administrator” title, and **Permission: find the blank bearer.** The coalition was completed as **eight names, five refusals and one line that is a key with no holder**, which is the honest shape of it and not a failure of nerve. **Still unspent: why the root field is blank, why Jonas's ordinary maintenance credential reaches it without making him its bearer, Evan’s counter-amendment and second signature, the Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the community-watch term, the three-line brass tokens, and Iona.**
- Evan’s counter-amendment, second signature, and complete fate remain unspent.
- The full Lowglass history and the nature of Iona’s preserved identity remain unspent.

## Ending constraints

- Jonas loses direct root access in the final volume.
- Iona survives under public oversight and loses command authority; she is neither resurrected nor absolved.
- Evan remains dead; Jonas resolves his relationship to the missing father without resurrection or complete forgiveness.
- Jonas and Sanaa remain together by choice and keep independent professional lives.
- Nacre’s final Civic Commons has nine rotating Lattice service-district steward seats, multiple witnesses, public renewal, explicit claims, a maximum seventy-two-hour emergency term, and no inheritable root office. A six-of-nine vote can activate only a non-coercive common link; dissenting districts keep local terms.
- The final transition has two clocks: a nine-month political window and a conditional seventy-two-hour physical cascade window that begins when the first linked hinge fails.
- The central mystery is answered. Questions about other cities and the first Lattice experiment remain open without introducing a new final threat.

## Chapters 41–48 established canon — VOLUME 01 CLOSE

### Calendar (authoritative; the anchor is unchanged and was not recomputed)

- Ch 41 = **Monday 7 April into Wednesday 9 April.** Ch 42 = **Monday 14 April.** Ch 43 = **Wednesday 15 April.** Ch 44 = **Wednesday 16 April.** Ch 45 = **Thursday 17 April into Friday 18 April, 06:00.** Ch 46 = **Friday 18 April into Saturday 19 April, 07:30.** Ch 47 = **Saturday 19 April, 11:00.** Ch 48 = **Saturday 19 April into Sunday 20 April.**
- **6 April is a Sunday, 7 April a Monday, 8 April a Tuesday, 9 April a Wednesday, 14 April a Monday, 15 April a Tuesday, 16 April a Wednesday, 17 April a Thursday, 18 April a Friday, 19 April a Saturday, 20 April a Sunday, 23 April a Wednesday, 19 May a Monday.** Counted from Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March. Do not reconcile against the real calendar.

### The west-clinic care term — the run of renewals and the forty-hour gap

- Renewals on 13, 19, 24, 29 March, 1 April and 7 April at the 22:00 handover. **The 7 April renewal ran the term to 06:00 Wednesday 15 April.** Ruth Nwosu had said on 5 April that she would do the eighth and not the fifteenth, and on 7 April she added, out loud in the corridor and on the record, **that she would not do the fourteenth either**, and said a third question would stop her telling him in advance.
- **The term therefore EXPIRED at 06:00 Wednesday 15 April and was not renewed that night.** The plate printed **THE DOOR ANSWERS TO NO LIVE TERM. A DECLARATION OPENS IT ONCE.**
- **At 11:52 on Wednesday 15 April a woman of eighty-four was found dead in bay six.** Mortuary transfer slot at the east site, 13:00. **Two refusals:** Bram Osei, off at eleven, whose reason was that his authority is a key and a key is not a permission; and Ruth Nwosu, on her fourth consecutive shift, who had refused the date in advance. Neither was argued out. **The least-bad option was Ivo Bamba, night orderly, twenty-nine, on since 22:00 the previous night, not on the rota, not a holder, carrying a laundry bag.** Declaration given at **11:54**, door at **12:04**, bier up the ramp at 12:20, mortuary van's tail-lift shut at the east site at **12:30** (thirty-six minutes from the declaration, half an hour inside the slot), family told at **13:10 instead of 12:30 — forty minutes.**
- **The single public figure for 15 April is FORTY MINUTES, and the rule behind it was set by Marta Sowle out loud before the number existed:** *the box counts what a family was left not knowing, and it does not count how long a door took.* Mina Okafor wrote the rule in first, declined to enter a figure she had not watched happen, and then entered forty minutes, crediting the rule to Marta Sowle in the box. **Consequence for all later volumes: the nineteen minutes of 3 April and the forty minutes of 15 April are both "time until the family knew", never "time the door took".** The door on the fifteenth took twelve minutes from the body and ten from the declaration. **There is no 48-minute figure anywhere in this account and the number on the public board in Ch 48 is forty.**
- **The term was renewed at 22:00 on Wednesday 16 April** by Ruth Nwosu to **06:00 Tuesday 21 April**, with Ezra Mbeki as incoming holder and **Dr. Sethi reading it back a second time as witness**, because the corridor's own witness had withdrawn. **Gap: 06:00 15 April to 22:00 16 April = forty hours.** This is the fact the contractor used and it is true.
- On the Sunday 20 April, with Bram off (Mon–Sat), Ezra read the five lines to Ruth at 22:00 and **Mina Okafor read them back a second time**, in her job and not as a witness, and said so twice to two different people.

### The Saint Orra service agreement of 2019, paragraph 11 — NEW CONTRACT RULE, now canon

- **Paragraph 11, first half:** where a civic node's term has failed, the party holding the standing continuity authorisation may assume the maintenance and operation of that node pending determination.
- **Paragraph 11, second half, a definition:** **a node is in failure where its civic term has not been continuously in force for a period exceeding twenty-four consecutive hours.** This is a contractual maintenance guarantee. Jonas Mercer read it in March in Rook's office and did not remember it until 02:30 on 18 April, and he did not put the two halves together: a **term can be continuously broken and then renewed, and the break stays on the record.** The renewal on the night of 16 April did not cure the forty hours. **This is the Volume 02 priced-access instrument, and it is a real mechanism: it makes maintenance conditional on continuity, and continuity is a thing people do.**
- The determination against the west-clinic care entrance was made at **11:20 on Thursday 17 April** and cites plate 6-41 and the forty-hour gap. It is in the register. **Ceri Vaughan was correct on the face of her own document and the point is now canon: a contractor's contractual definition of failure is not a civic determination of a node's state.**

### The registrar's record of 18 April — the condition line and the second unclaimed burden

- **The condition line on 6-L/14 — IF THE FLAP IS NOT SHUT, THE BANK IS NOT A WALL — is now in a registrar's record**, entered by **Oren Pell** on the evening of Friday 18 April, at about 19:10, in a car park in the Old Ward, with Jonas present. It was **not** read into a public minute and Jonas did not read it. The distinction was made aloud: **a minute is a list; a registrar's record describes a state of a node.**
- **The record also contains, for the file and not for Jonas: the condition line was available to be recorded on 1 April 2014 and was not**, on a reporting officer's ground, not a registrar's.
- **Cost of reading it in:** a civic work with no holder and no issuer, recorded, is an unclaimed burden, and it attaches to the nearest responsible maintenance route. The department had already assigned the care-entrance claim to Jonas, and a registrar may not attach two unclaimed burdens to one route without a determination. **The determination attaches 6-L/14 — CHAMBER AND BANK — to the interim lowland maintenance post, series 6, holder ROYDEN ACHEBE.** He accepted it by telephone at about 19:06 from a yard in Gantry Ward, in nine words, saying he would rather have had it before he had the post. **Jonas Mercer was right on 1 April and it cost the street a claim on a man seventeen days later, and Achebe is the one paying and nobody in the building can say so in a minute.**

### The register of attachment — the public title, and what it is

- **REGISTER OF ATTACHMENT. Name: MERCER, J. Position: LAST WORKING SYSTEM ADMINISTRATOR, CITY OF NACRE. Appointing resolution: none. Status: civic standing, not departmental. Not subject to departmental review, grade, or probation. Liability: bears unclaimed burdens and maintenance claims attached to the civic chain in his own name, in the city, findable by the city, until released by no person.**
- Created Saturday 19 April in committee room two, Old Ward, at an extraordinary meeting on four days' notice, chair **Fenella Marsh**. Moved by **Nina Calder** as records, drafted twice, on the chair's instruction. **The registrar stated the reason on the minute and it is his reason: the register does not give the man power, it gives him no root access, no precedence, no office, no staff, no vote, no key and no right to refuse an attachment, and it puts his name where the city can find it permanently so that the claims land in the open instead of in a series 6 drawer, and so that the next person who wants to attach a burden must look at the register first and see he is already the one. He said in the room: *it is a bucket under a tap, and the tap is going to run for a long time*, and asked that the minute say he said it.** It did.
- **Jonas was in the gallery as a witness to the 17 April determination and was not asked.** He asked one question: can it be taken off. The answer was no — not by the department, not by a grade, not by a review, not by him.
- **Fenella Marsh refused to minute the title until Oren Pell stated its basis, and then minuted it with the basis stated.** A department cannot hold a plate.
- **Oren Pell tried to describe the three residents an unclaimed burden would otherwise have gone to, in a room with a full gallery, and Councillor Sore stopped him, and he complied, and the minute was corrected to *the nearest person who can be reached other than a resident of an unnamed district*.** He objected on filing grounds. Marsh sustained the objection in part and overruled it in part. **No Weir resident is described in any public minute in Volume 01.**

### The map and the blank centre

- The Lattice's drawing layer produces a **service diagram, four sheets**, showing **nine ordinary service districts** — a borough, a trust, a company, a hospital, a school, a transit operator, a housing association, a market, a utilities district — each with a name, a filled holder square, an expiry and a node number. **A service district is a shape drawn out of the terms in force inside it and is not an administrative ward; the nine are not the city's eight districts and never were.**
- **In the middle of the middle there is nothing: a blank square the size of a thumbnail, no name, no number, no node, and no line in the key.** It is the root field. It is not a printing fault. The basement printer says it has been blank since about 2011 and that root fields are not drawn.
- **Inside that blank square, on the four-sheet print pulled from the basement machine at about 23:00 on Friday 18 April, there is a faint closed outline about forty millimetres across with an empty holder square and a dot where the other nine have a filled square and a node number, and under it one line in the smallest type the printer has: DRAWN FROM A TERM IN FORCE 16:40, 17 APRIL TO 06:00, 18 APRIL. NO HOLDER FIELD. NO NAME. NO CLASSIFICATION.** **The term lasted thirteen hours and twenty minutes and Lowglass is legally unlisted; the outline has been on the layer for at least seventeen hours longer than that and is still on it.**
- **The term that put the shape there is now on the page (Ch 45, 16:40 Thursday 17 April).** Aurrie Nkemdirim, sixty-one, issued it standing up, out loud, in about four minutes, in the net-loft kitchen, for **a door, a stove, a floor and Marigold Adjei, seventy-seven, with a chest, in the room at the top of the stairs.** She said in front of the room that it was **not a service**, that it did nothing for Marigold, and that the duty would fall on herself because she was the only person there not there because of the forecast. She **refused to give her name to a plate** and did not write it down; there is no copy of it in the city. Jonas Mercer was in the doorway with his coat on, holding the plate he had clamped to the shop's own service riser the day before — **A FLOOD IS FORECAST. NO TERM ANSWERS TO A FORECAST** — **and he did not read it out.** He stood and watched her do in four minutes the thing he has spent five weeks failing to do, and put the plate back in his bag unread, because reading it aloud in that room would have told thirty people that the woman who had just spoken is not civic. **Marigold Adjei, seventy-seven, is a new named figure and is distinct from the woman of eighty-four who died in bay six on 15 April.** The term ran to 06:00 on Friday 18 April — **thirteen hours and twenty minutes** — nobody renewed it, and it is the shortest-lived term in the history of the chain.
- **The layer has no delete operation, and that is the corrected mechanism (Ch 47).** The print came out of the basement machine at about 23:00 on Friday 18 April, which is **seventeen hours after the term expired.** The shape was therefore *not* a stale print of an earlier accurate state: at the moment of printing there was no term in force inside the blank centre, and the plate's own rule — **THE MAP IS DRAWN FROM THE TERMS IN FORCE. A DISTRICT WITH NO TERM IS NOT DRAWN** — was true and was being broken by the same machine. The layer drew a shape out of a term that had ended, annotated its own error under it, and left the shape there. **Canon: a shape on this layer is evidence that a term once existed, not evidence that one exists, and a district that loses its term keeps its outline on the drawing layer until the layer is redrawn from scratch — which nothing in this city has authority to do.** Nobody in the building printed a second copy and nobody thought there was anything to print.
- **A child's name is written in biro, pressing hard, in the margin over the blank square, in a hand that is not Jonas's, on a print that lay on a crate in the River Stacks yard in the rain. Jonas does not know whose it is. He folded the sheets with the margin inward and did not ask anybody, because asking a street whose only power is that there is nothing on any list which name is on his map is how a name becomes a record. Nothing else in the city on the twentieth of April is findable by one person and by nobody else.**
- **The Old Ward board, Saturday 19 April:** **CENTRAL ROOT — HOLDER: —** (unchanged) · **6-L/14 — NON-CIVIC PLANT. ACCEPTED. NO CIVIC CLAIM ATTACHES TO THE CONTENTS OF THE CHAMBER OR THE BANK.** · **6-L/19 — NOT ACCEPTED. REMAINS IN SCOPE PENDING INSPECTION. NO INSPECTION SCHEDULED — NO LOCATION OF RECORD.** · **6-L/21, LOWLAND REFUGE STORE 1, COOPER'S REACH — HOLDER: —. NOT REACHED ON 17 APRIL.** · **6-L/14 — UNCLAIMED BURDEN ATTACHED 18 APRIL. SEE HOLDER.** · the new four-sheet service diagram · a new empty box with a card: **a claim may be stated in this box for ninety days; a claim so stated does not establish a service, does not require a name, and may not be used as evidence of occupancy, population, households or persons** · and, on the enamel strip at the frame, unsigned: **Permission: find the blank bearer.**

### The committee of Saturday 19 April

- Extraordinary meeting of the Standing Committee on Civic Continuity, four days' notice, committee room two, six at a table, gallery of forty-one and full at 10:55. Marsh in the chair, Sore, four other members. Pell in attendance as registrar. Nina Calder, records. Achebe, in attendance in his own name. Jonas as a witness to the determination. **Alden Rook and Ceri Vaughan for CivicCore.**
- **Item one, four minutes:** the 17 April determination **suspended pending determination**. *Not a dissolution. CivicCore continues to hold and operate the physical network.* Item three: the unclaimed burden noted, the committee **unable to take any step**, the chair re-reading Sore's lost standing-orders motion from 1 April verbatim, and Achebe giving a date of **29 May**.
- **Item two:** **Councillor Sore moved the adjournment and the committee adjourned to 19 May, on the ground that it cannot report on a contract that turns on *holder* until it has determined what a holder is. She stated in the room that the motion is not in the interest of her own party and that she would not explain it.**
- **Item four:** the district shown unnamed inside the blank centre is recorded as appearing on the diagram; **no holder field is created for it and no name is entered against it**; the ninety-day claims box; and the committee **reserves its position**.
- Alden Rook, in the corridor, in about forty seconds, to four people: the clause comes back on 19 May; the first person who can define *holder* controls every node in the city; and to Jonas — *you are going to be the man who is in the register, and in about two years somebody is going to use that register to say the city has an administrator, and I am going to have been right about that in a corridor at half past two in the afternoon and there is going to be nobody in the world on my side when I say it.* And then: **do not lose the map. Maps are the only thing a contractor cannot re-draw without somebody in a room saying so.**

### The nine witnesses — the finished card

**Eight names, five refusals written in full, and line six is a key with no holder. The card is a job card in his coat, not a file, and it is not clean and it is not a complaint.**

1. **A crossing — NORA BELL.** The barrier end and the gate she latches. Nineteen years. She did not refuse. She narrowed it before she said yes: *I do not say who crosses it, and I want it in the writing that nobody asked me to latch that gate once in nineteen years and that is not going to stop because of this.* **Refused a council flood forum seat, an annual allowance and a badge, twice**; a man with a laminated card on a lanyard has asked her the second question three times. She was relieved of her crossing for the remainder of the night on 18 April by rail control and did not go back on it.
2. **A door — BRAM OSEI.** Mina Okafor withdrew on 9 April and was not asked again. Bram took it in a sentence and asked that **the Thursday he refused in the first week of March stays on the public board where it is, in the same box, with the same words.** He said he did not want his Thursday tidied.
3. **A market — BISI ALADE**, forty-one, fish, five years in Bellow Market. **Fola Adeyemi's four reasons were read out in full** because a roster that showed only consent was a roster that had been cleaned. Bisi's condition, in her own words, twice: *the mended hatch is in the record and it cost me a Saturday, and on the eighteenth of April the collectors' rule eleven is what I say it is, and the Saturday is not a lesson anybody learned.* She is not a spokesperson for eleven traders.
4. **A pump — EMEKA RUSK.** Read the five lines back three times. *I would rather be named than be the man who is not there.* **On 18 April he held the storm overflow for an hour and ten minutes to keep the market dry, and BISI ALADE made him turn it over from fifty feet of cold-store water.**
5. **A machine — TEO VLAH.** *Machines only*, said before Jonas got there, at volume.
6. **A school — A KEY AND NO HOLDER.** **ROZ IDRIS REFUSED IN THE YARD**, in full: she will witness a service she has been told is failing and not one described to her as failing by the person who wants it fixed, and the school had been asked to hold people. **IBRAHIM SESAY, fifty-one, caretaker and portal at Tarn Street Primary, took it as a key**: he opened the school at 02:20 on 18 April on his own key with no instruction, and a teacher who is not on the card told him to shut it, and he shut it, and it opened for one hour fifty minutes and closed on a key. **There is no line for what happens to a man who does that and he said so in the yard.**
7. **A street — YOLANDA FOFANA**, fifty-four, named steward of Wren Court, Salter's Yard, thirty-one rooms, no civic address, a participation fee, and a clause nine. **The Weir refused line seven in four lines read back unaltered, and Jonas said out loud in the yard, at her insistence, that she is not the answer to the Weir.** Her three conditions: her residents are told a card exists before anything else; the answer to any request for a list of people is the address of Wren Court and nothing else; **the fee does not stop, because the day the fee stops is the day somebody does not get water.**
8. **A platform — KOFI SARR.** A platform and never a passenger, said twice, and he would not say what the second question was. He refused a council flood forum dinner on 2 May. **On 18 April he closed bay four and was right, and it was the reason no minibus went under the embankment, and it was also the reason about sixty people walked four and a half miles.**
9. **A hospital, which became a west clinic — DR. SETHI**, the clinic's naming authority. **He required the record to state that he named Bram, Ezra and Ruth on the rota in the first week of March and has not asked them.** He will not witness a hospital: *there is no such thing as a hospital, there are forty of them, and the day a card says a hospital in a room in June it will mean the one the man in the coat wanted.*

**Five refusals, written in full, in the lines they belong in:** Fola Adeyemi (market, her four reasons, her own words). The Weir (street, four lines, read back and not altered, refused by Aurrie Nkemdirim and refused separately and separately by Pia Santos so that it stays one refusal and not two). Dr. Sanaa Iqbal (hospital, refused in a corridor in front of Mina Okafor, because a doctor who both declares emergencies and certifies the term that authorises them is a conflict of interest on the face of a document — *and she told him she has been trying to get a second named clinician on that roster since 24 March, four refusals and one no from the education office, and had not told him because he would have made it a thing about a card*). Mina Okafor (door, withdrawn 15:35 on 9 April, in her own hand, with the reason written: *there is no cheque*). Roz Idris (school, refused in the yard).

**Bea Osunde refused to be on the card in front of everybody:** *a witness who works for the man who is mending the term is not a witness, she is a second pair of hands with a name on it.* She is not the ninth witness and she is not fixed.

#### CANON NOTE — the nine witnesses is an earned deviation from `outline/volume-01.md`, and it is permanent

`outline/volume-01.md` names nine witnesses in three places (L65, L76, L85) as *a crossing guard, a clinic clerk, a market cook, a pump operator, a mechanic, a teacher, **a Lowglass resident**, a transit dispatcher, **and Sanaa**.* **The volume did not deliver that list and must never be described as having done so.** The four differences are all on the page, all refused or narrowed on the page, and all deliberate:

1. **The clinic clerk withdrew** (Mina Okafor, 9 April) and the door went to **Bram Osei**, a porter, not a clerk.
2. **The teacher refused** (Roz Idris, in the yard, in full) and the school went to **Ibrahim Sesay** as *a key with no holder*, not to a person.
3. **Line seven is not a Lowglass resident.** It is **Yolanda Fofana of Wren Court**, and Jonas said out loud, at her insistence and in front of twenty-two people, that she is not the answer to the Weir. **The Weir's refusal stands as one of the five refusals above and it is not a line on the card.**
4. **Sanaa refused line nine in a corridor** and line nine became **a west clinic**, held by **Dr. Sethi**, the naming authority — with his own conflict stated in the yard.

**Canon for Volume 02: the card is eight names, five refusals and a key. It is a job card in a coat, not a file, it is not clean, and it is not a complaint. Do not restore a ninth line, do not put Bea Osunde on it, and do not let any later volume say nine witnesses without stating the five refusals in the same breath.**

### The bridge — the third bounded civic edit, at a plate he can work

- **Saint Orra bridge west-lane hinge**, west approach, the hinge Public Works passed in the first week of March. **The south span stays isolated and the obsolete empty-crossing term is untouched**, and is named as the next fight: on a Tuesday with no water on the deck it is still the reason an ambulance loses eleven minutes at that bridge.
- The seam is two years old: **the barrier end's pedestrian closure term and the ambulance service claim have no order between them.** On 18 April the barrier keeper closed the west lane at 15:56 and the ambulance service claimed it at 03:06 and the deck was both for **fifty-eight minutes**, and Dev Singh wrote *both* in the crossing log and would not be moved off it.
- **Issuer: D. SINGH**, Saint Orra bridge dispatcher, because a plate will not take a role without a name and the west-lane schedule is his. **Maintenance claim: the barrier keeper's, unpaid, and expressly not the issuer's and not the mender's. Witness: Nora Bell** (her scope is the barrier end and the gate she latches, and no other part of the bridge). **Second bearer: YVONNE AMOAH**, forty-one, Saint Orra ambulance duty officer, eleven years, who asked for the wording *at the time* and not in advance.
- The printed term: the lane is used only when the barrier keeper and the ambulance duty officer have both agreed at the crossing; each may stop it; **a handover is a fresh act of both, spoken, in daylight, and the person receiving it answers for it.** **Two handovers are in the log in fifty minutes**, which is the proof that it is not an office.
- **The one plate notice: A WITNESSED HANDOVER IS RECORDED AMONG THE HOLDERS. IT GRANTS NOBODY AN OFFICE.**
- **Dev Singh's own demand, on the record, at the end: the empty-crossing clause is still in the file, it was not read into anything, and it is his, and it is not a flood, and nobody is going to pay him for it. It is on a file and a name and a date and a fight, and it will take eighteen months.** **The next fight, named.**

### The surge of Thursday 17 April — the clock, authoritative

- **High water at Saltmouth 03:10. 910 mm of rise in the eight hours either side of 03:00.** Predicted 2.9 m above spring high at Saltmouth, revised downward three times in five days and wrong three times. **The number on the left comes off the river and not off anybody's policy.**
- **02:10 the flap shuts itself on the flood, unattended, as designed.** The plate prints one line: **THE FLAP IS SHUT.** **04:20 the bank goes** — the inner face takes the top two feet and the side and goes into the dip in a long grey wall, and the dip fills to within a metre of the road in about four minutes.
- **The trap was never cleared.** Jonas Mercer got eighteen inches of silt out of it on the Friday morning alone, with a bucket, in nine minutes, and could not shift a foot and ten that remains, and put the bucket on the step and told nobody, and it is in no record and it bought a fortnight of brown water.
- **03:40 Emeka Rusk turns the storm overflow over**, having held it for an hour and ten minutes, at Bisi Alade's insistence, fifty feet of cold-store water, on a radio, in the dark, alone, at fifty-one. He told nobody until the Monday. **04:10 the dip is over the line where the road meets the embankment and stays over for the best part of two hours. 05:10 the low block's back doors go.** Water down 400 mm by about 09:00.
- **04:11 the Marsh Lane substation trips** and is dark for nine hours. **The cause on the fault report is surge current associated with the storm overflow gate operation, and the operator box is blank**, and the woman at the fault desk has said twice there is nobody to fill it in, and Emeka Rusk has decided that is the correct outcome.
- **PERDITA SOW**, forty-four, borough road sweeper, eleven unpaid years of Sundays on that road, is on the bank at 04:20 and is in the water four minutes. **She is not dead.** Cut across the front of the left thigh, sprained right wrist, dislocated finger, at Saint Orra east from 06:10 on the Friday. **A boy of nineteen on a towpath with a bicycle put the bike in it and went down on his hands and knees; nobody has asked his name and Jonas has not found out.**
- **The school**: Ibrahim Sesay opened it at 02:20 on his own key with no instruction; Roz Idris told him to shut it at 04:00; twenty people spent an hour in the rain on a playing field; **Teresa Nkemelu, about seventy, hired two minibuses with her own money and charged the parish nothing and put it on a form.**
- **Kofi Sarr closed bay four at 02:11 with a bar across it**, on a stated reason, and it is right, and it is also why about sixty people walked four and a half miles. **Nora Bell kept the westbound trolley signal from 15:20 to 15:30 — eleven minutes — was relieved of her post for the rest of the night by rail control, and did not go back on it.** The queue she caused took until about 19:20 to clear.
- **The Methodist hall on Carrow Lane took water under the front door at 04:55** and eleven Brine Wharf households were moved out of it at six in the morning, five nights into not being paid for, with about forty possessions and no vehicle.
- **CivicCore's determination** is made at 11:20 on the Thursday; Ceri Vaughan arrives on the ramp at 01:40 on the Friday with a laminated card and a folder and does not go through the door, and her technician on Marsh Lane cannot get through the flood, and she says two true things and leaves.
- **Oren Pell, from a car on the Salt Row embankment at 02:20, enters two findings in one record, in one hand:** a term that ends on its own terms has not failed, **and** a contractor's contractual definition of failure is not a civic determination of a node's state. He suspends the determination on the second ground. He also enters that the door would have been shut at 03:10 and a woman of ninety-one moved in a flooding corridor on a doctor's own signature, which is worse than anything in the agreement.
- Marta Sowle, ninety-one, moved out of bay nine at 04:04 on a chair, with Ruth Nwosu standing in the door and Ivo Bamba holding it and nobody holding anything else. **Ivo Bamba went home at four.**

### The Weir after the surge

- The term expired 06:00 Saturday 19 April and the plate went quiet. **At about 06:10 on Saturday 19 April Bo Trimble, seventeen, read the five lines aloud to MARGO DORÉ for ONE WINDOW — 06:00 Saturday 19 April to 06:00 Sunday 20 April** — and **read the claim out loud first and said it was not true**: the claim is *the holder keeps this point true until the silt trap is clear*, unpaid, and the trap is not clear. Margo Doré said the plate does not care whether a claim is true and a person does, and that this is the entire argument of the street, and read the window in anyway.
- **The rota for the twenty-four hours is one name and no names under it. Every draw is entered with a time and not with whose. The stated consequence: not one person on that street will be able to prove in June that they had water.** *That is the price of not having a list, and I have known that price since 1994.* No rota of households was written. **Nobody was added to the rota. Bo Trimble was already findable and nothing was added to him.**
- **The three positions are unchanged and the bank line has a ring round a blank space and nothing in it.** Nobody filled it. Nobody volunteered into it in a speech. **Hector Mbatha is on no line and does not appear in Chapters 41–48.**
- **Counting rule, held:** the low block is described as fourteen households in Chapters 45 and 48 because a flood takes back doors, and that number **is expressly refused as a rota and as a list** on the page. **Water is never sized by a headcount.**
- **Councillor Iven Sore went up the towpath on Saturday 19 April at about 07:30 and was let in by Aurrie Nkemdirim, who decided, and who was not asked twice.** Sore got eleven minutes. She asked the question — *who is the other end of it* — and the answer given back to her by Pia Santos is that on the other end of it there is nobody, not because people will not answer, but because a department cannot give a record of a place with no address and if it could, the record would be a form with a number in it and the number would be the wrong one. **Sore did not look at the ring of pencil on the wall. Jonas Mercer understood that not looking was the single most important thing that happened in that kitchen and that he was the only person who could tell it had happened.**
- **Yasmin Rohan declined the CivicCore assistance offer in the same sentence on 18 April that she declined it on 31 March**, in two different rooms, eleven people hearing the second time who had not heard the first.

### Tomas Renn's finding — the fourth question

- **The union's submission to the Standing Committee, filed 09:14 on Friday 18 April, four days early**, in the public papers for the 2 June folder. **Two of four stewards would not sign**: Ada Kwarteng, who reads it as the shop having stopped asking, and Cuthbert Nkemelu, who thinks the last sentence is a provocation, and he is right and it was not taken out. It went in as a minority submission.
- **The finding: *the civic term architecture was not the failure on the third of April and was not the failure on the fifteenth, and the record should not be permitted to suggest otherwise.*** This is Tomas's sentence and he wrote it and it is in his name alone.
- **The cost:** a demand became a finding, and a finding can only be cited, and it will be cited first by a continuity contractor, because it is the only sentence in the world that says nothing in the design caused this. **He is no longer the only person asking in the city and he did it on purpose, and he told nobody at the shop, and Jonas found out on the Saturday from Bea Osunde and did not go and find him.**
- **His own counter-finding, said in the yard and not in the document:** the architecture **was** the problem. Not on the third and not on the fifteenth. On the third the term was live and the door shut at six in the morning because **a renewal clause requires two people to be in a corridor at ten at night and does not require anybody to make them be.** That is not luck. That is a rule that puts a rota in charge of a promise. He has three of four prerequisites and the third is a hospital doctor's good intentions, and he says he is fifty per cent of the problem instead of sixty.
- **He is at fifty per cent of the problem and the question is not answered, and Jonas does not answer it, and the answer is not Jonas.**
- A CivicCore continuity officer telephoned the shop on the Friday afternoon asking whether the union would consider a joint working paper on continuity. **Tomas said no in a sentence.**

SETHI** (already), now line nine · **BRAM OSEI** (already), now line two · **MARTA SOWLE** (already) · **IVO BAMBA**, 29, night orderly, the least-bad option twice · **TERESA NKEMELU**, about 70, hired two minibuses with her own money · **COUNCILLOR FENELLA MARSH**, chair, series 6 review and the extraordinary committee · **NINA CALDER** (she/her, records) · **PERDITA SOW** (already), injured, not dead, three injuries, the dislocated finger is the one she will be angry about for eleven weeks · **ADA KWARTENG** and **CUTHBERT NKMELU**, shop stewards, minority report.

- **The Weir stand term runs 06:00 Saturday 19 April to 06:00 Sunday 20 April, held by Margo Doré, read by Bo Trimble, over a claim that is known to be false.** After Sunday at six there is no term and no holder and the trap is not clear.

## Volume 01 — DIVERGENCES FROM `outline/volume-01.md` (logged, deliberate, carried)

The outline's *Concrete resolution* list is the plan and is **not rewritten**. Four of its items were not delivered as written. They are logged here so that no later phase mistakes an omission for an oversight, and so that Volume 02 inherits them as open rather than closed.

1. **L95 — "Tomas establishes a worker-led inspection requirement for major repairs." NOT DELIVERED. Deferred, and the deferral is the point.** Tomas Renn's submission is explicitly a **finding, not a demand**: *the shop does not have a demand any more. The shop has a finding* (Ch 43). He himself names what is missing — *the second half of it needs a payroll line and a training afternoon and a naming authority and a written rule, and I have got three and a bit of them and the third one is a hospital doctor's good intentions.* **No worker-led inspection exists anywhere in this city at the end of Volume 01, and the string "worker-led" appears only in the outline.** Volume 02 inherits: **Tomas has a finding with a date on it and two of four stewards in a minority report against it.**
2. **L96 — "Sanaa's clinic adopts a consent and appeal process that cannot be overridden invisibly." NOT DELIVERED. Deferred.** Nothing was adopted by anybody. What exists instead is **Marta Sowle's box rule** (Ch 43) — *the box counts what a family was left not knowing, and it does not count how long a door took* — and a public box that records events and discharges none of them. **The clinic's consent architecture is still the one that cost a term forty hours of continuity and a woman of eighty-four her morning.** Volume 02 inherits: **the appeal process is unbuilt, the Ardent appeal sits 23 April, and the Brine Wharf panel will probably fail.**
3. **L93 — transit, shelter, water and flood-gate access. PARTIALLY MET, and the two failures are the volume's argument.**
   - **Transit: met.** Kofi Sarr, bounded and renewed, with an obsolete empty-crossing clause that is still in the file and is his next fight.
   - **Flood gate: met.** Emeka Rusk, named, with a blank operator box on the fault report he has decided is correct.
   - **Water: met only where a metered consumer exists, and deliberately not met on the Weir.** The clinic tank and the Marsh Lane bottled supply cover the next care shift, sized by metered consumption and not by headcount. **The Weir stand runs on a rota with no names under it and is dead after 06:00 Sunday 20 April**, and Margo Doré calls that *the price of not having a list* and refuses to have it written down as a plan. **This is the earned exception, not a failure to note.**
   - **Shelter: NOT MET, and it is the most consequential omission in the volume.** A Methodist hall flooded with eleven people in it; a school opened on a key for one hour fifty minutes and was shut by a teacher; a net-loft kitchen held about thirty people and four children, and the room heard the term read out, with **no term on its door until Aurrie Nkemdirim issued one at 16:40 on the Thursday, for no service, under no plate and with no copy anywhere.** CivicCore's retrospective assistance offer was refused in the same sentence on 18 April as on 31 March. **Volume 02 inherits shelter as the unsolved service.** The old pump also stays closed, the line unsafe, the tape sun-bleached, and Jonas does not lift a corner of it.
4. **L94 — "Jonas receives permanent civil-service status."** The **register of attachment** is not civil-service status and must never be written as a promotion: civic standing, not departmental, no grade, no probation, not subject to review, **released by no person.** Oren Pell says so in the minute, and Bea Osunde says the word on it will be true on 18 April and wrong by the 22nd.
5. **A craft guardrail was also exceeded and is logged here rather than fixed: the civic-record density in Chapters 46 and 47.** The standing ceiling is *at most one notice in an ordinary chapter* (`bible/power-system.md`). **Ch 46 carries ten distinct civic records** — three plate prints, the registrar's condition line, the unclaimed-burden determination, and **five** board lines, two of which restate positions already settled in Ch 28–36; **Ch 47 carries the four-clause resolution read in full plus the register entry.** **None was cut, because every one of them is load-bearing downstream** — Councillor Sore quotes the 6-L/14 and 6-L/19 board lines word for word in the same chapter, Jonas's only question in the committee is about the register entry, and the resolution is the volume's institutional result. **This is a real overrun and Volume 02 must hold the ceiling of one notice in an ordinary chapter.** If a future chapter needs four board lines, two of them must be compressed into one line of narration.

## Chapters 49–59 established canon — VOLUME 02 OPEN

- Ch 49 = **Monday 21 April.** Ch 50 = **Monday 21 April 22:00 into Tuesday 22 April morning.** Ch 51 = **Tuesday 22 April into Wednesday 23 April, 07:20.** Ch 52 = **Wednesday 23 April, 09:30 to 13:10.** Ch 53 = **Wednesday 23 April 23:40 into Thursday 24 April, 07:20.** Ch 54 = **Thursday 24 April, 20:00 to 23:00.** Ch 55 = **Friday 25 April, 09:40.** Ch 56 = **Sunday 27 April.** Ch 57 = **Monday 28 April, 10:00 to 11:30.** Ch 58 = **Tuesday 29 April 23:40 into Wednesday 30 April, 07:40.** Ch 59 = **Wednesday 30 April, 10:40 to 20:40, closing on a coda dated Tuesday 20 May.**
- **20 April Sunday · 21 Monday · 22 Tuesday · 23 Wednesday · 24 Thursday · 25 Friday · 26 Saturday · 27 Sunday · 28 Monday · 29 Tuesday · 30 Wednesday · 1 May Thursday · 2 May Friday · 3 May Saturday · 19 May Monday · 20 May Tuesday · 26 May Monday · 29 May Thursday · 18 July Friday.** Counted from Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March. Never reconcile against the real calendar.

### CivicCore Schedule 4 to the Saint Orra service agreement, 2019 — continuity pricing

- **Valid since 1 March 2014 and read by nobody in this city until 22 April.** It is four pages in the assessment and 460 pages in total. It is not a plan and it is not a lie; every sentence in it is true and it accepts Oren Pell's finding of 18 April that a contractor's contractual definition of failure is not a civic determination of a node's state.
- **The rules, verbatim in effect:** a node in failure is an **unfunded node**; an unfunded node **is not disconnected, it is deferred**; a deferred node **serves its last verified essential use, and if none is recorded, the use is the node's maintenance**; a node in failure may be placed on a **continuity schedule** by the district in which it stands, on payment of a **continuity fee**, at a **tier**; the party holding the standing continuity authorisation may place nodes on a schedule **at any time**; **the order of tiers is set by the fee, a higher tier outranks a lower tier in any precedence question, and an unfunded node is outranked by every node that is not**; **a tier may be challenged by a district or by a node in the same tier and may not be challenged by a person.**
- **Fees for 2014–15, per node: Tier 1 £1,880 · Tier 2 £4,200 · Tier 3 £11,400.**
- **The three Lattice notices Schedule 4 produces, all now canon:** `THIS NODE IS NOT ON A CONTINUITY SCHEDULE. A NODE NOT ON A SCHEDULE IS NOT DISCONNECTED. IT IS DEFERRED.` · `A DEFERRED NODE SERVES ITS LAST VERIFIED ESSENTIAL USE. IF NONE IS RECORDED, THE USE IS THE NODE'S MAINTENANCE.` · `THE ORDER OF TIERS IS SET BY WHAT IS PAID. IT IS NOT SET BY WHAT IS NEEDED.`
- Two further plates carry the corollary sentences: **the holder of a point pays for it and the point is not the payer's** (Wren Court meter cupboard, Ch 54) and **a tier may be challenged by a district or by a node in the same tier, it may not be challenged by a person** (Carrow Lane meter room, Ch 57).
- **No faction authored Schedule 4 and no character in this volume invented the injustice.** The document is the answer to a question the city refused to answer. Do not write it as somebody's plot.

### The last verified essential use — the volume's central instrument

- A recordable fact about a node that determines who it serves when the money stops. **It can be written in nine seconds by an uninformed person. It then stands, because a record can only be replaced by another record and only by a person, and a registrar is a registrar and not a clerk.**
- **Ivo Bamba's record, 6-44 mortuary van gate, Saint Orra west, about twenty past eleven on Wednesday 23 April, in about nine seconds, in biro, on the back of a laundry delivery note, no date, no initials:** `ANYBODY WITH A DEAD BODY OR A PERSON WHO CANNOT GET IN ON THEIR OWN`. He is twenty-nine, the night orderly, on since ten, **not on the rota, not a holder**, and he did it because a van had come through that gate and because on 15 April he was the least-bad option in a corridor holding a door he had no business holding and the public box said forty minutes.
- **This is a hollow term** — a permission that functions through a witness who did not know what the field was — **and it is the first one in this city in nineteen years.** It is also the correct answer to the question and it keeps a mortuary gate working.
- **Oren Pell refused to strike it** and gave the reason: a hollow term is a permission that functions; striking it empties the field; an empty field means the node's maintenance; the maintenance on a mortuary gate is a man with a key. He said he has struck four in nineteen years and that there are about nine hundred and **nobody counts them**, and that this is a defect of the design and not of maintenance.
- **Ivo Bamba's name was removed from the first Return of a Deferred Node by Nina Calder**, because the form is not the place to make a man findable at a gate. The record stands on the plate and is findable in the city.

### The participation agreement and rule 4(c)

- **Clause 4(c), verbatim:** a signature on this agreement is a consent mark for the purposes of the Civic Lattice and **may not be withdrawn except on the same date next year**, on which date the signature may be renewed or allowed to lapse.
- **Consent is graded by date.** The people who signed were not deceived, not coerced and not in a hurry in any sense the Lattice can read. Nell Ardent's finding, on the page, and it is not softened anywhere: *those people were not deceived, and there is no version of this in which a person can un-sign a door.*
- **Nell Ardent found it in a document she had carried since 2 April.** The version in her folder is dated 12 April; the version actually signed in the community room is dated **12 March 2014**, which is the date the sign date and the lapse date are counted from.
- **A person can stop paying a bill. A person cannot un-sign a door.**

### The prices

- **Saint Orra west site chain, nodes in failure:** **6-44 mortuary van gate; staff stair 2; car park barrier 3; service riser 1.** The care entrance **6-41 is not in failure.**
- **Halvorsen has £4,200 for the year.** Tier 2 on one node is £4,200. Tier 2 on all four is £16,800. Tier 1 on all four is £7,520. **He can pay for one and has to choose which, and he asked Jonas Mercer, and was refused, and Jonas told him to write to the mortuary porter.** The decision is unmade and the three unnamed nodes are not decided.
- **Carrow Lane Housing Association: four blocks, 128 rooms, A (1961), B (1974), C (1938, shared stopcock in a cupboard the association has no key to), D (lift, nineteen years old).** Tier 2 bought **11 March 2014**, **£16,800 a year**, passed on at **£2.60 a room a week**, a rise from **£11 to £13.60**. **Four of 128 had missed a month by late April; the association expects to lose Block C.**
- **Wren Court, Salter's Yard: 31 rented rooms over a furniture workshop in Tideglass, no civic address, named steward.** Fee **£11 → £13.60 a week**. **Nineteen of 31 signed on 12 March 2014; four had missed a month by late April; eleven of the nineteen were in the building on the night of 17 April and all eleven were on a lapsed term, and the water was at half for eleven days.** Yolanda Fofana's own signature lapsed **1 April** and she told the block meeting on the 8th that there was *a mains matter*. Clause nine stands: disconnection for non-payment, sixty days' notice, one visit, a photograph of the meter.
- **THE VALE CLINIC, KESTREL HILL — DEVELOPED IN CHAPTERS 81–90 AND NOW CANON.** A private clinic on a private road, eleven patients and a day room, in a building freeheld by **the Aldbrook Trust**. Node **KH-4/19** was placed at **Tier 3, £11,400**, on 4 March 2014 by CivicCore under a standing continuity authorisation, signed by a power of attorney and not with a name. **A second node, KH-4/20, the residential floor, is at Tier 2, £4,200, placed by the Trust on 29 May 2014, term to 06:00 Monday 4 August 2014, and nobody has re-priced it.** The whole of KH-4/19 was assigned away and revoked on 4 July 2014, and the parent revives on 1 October 2014.
- **The lowland: six water points. Tier 1 is £1,880 each. Six points is £11,280 a year.** Marguerite Oyelaran worked this out on 12 March and wrote it on her own corridor board in her own hand on **Monday 28 April, 10 past eleven**, in front of Jonas Mercer, having told him she would be able to look at him afterwards and find out whether he was going to do anything with it. **He did nothing with it. Nobody has asked.**

### The Return of a Deferred Node

- **Nina Calder's form, NDC crest, form number in the corner, printed at 2 p.m. on 30 April 2014 by a records clerk called Doina Serkis who had never printed one.** Not adopted, not minuted, not enforceable, establishes nothing, names nobody. Issued to four parties. **It is issued because the Lattice cannot publish a narrowing** — the drawing layer draws a shape, a term, a date and a claim and **has never once drawn what a thing stopped doing** (the man at the basement printer, who volunteered this and has never been asked about anything in his life) — **and the city can, because the city has paper and a printer and a records supervisor who is extremely annoyed.**
- **The ten fields:** NODE · LOCATION · DISTRICT · HOLDER · TIER · DATE OF DEFERRAL · WHAT IT SERVES NOW · WHAT IT SERVED BEFORE · WHAT WOULD BE NEEDED TO RESTORE · WHO ANSWERS FOR IT.
- **The tenth line is a blank rule and it is not a design feature.** She put it there so that the shape of the hole is already in the document when somebody prints it on a district that has a name. **A form with a hole in it is the only honest thing available to her this week.**
- **The first return, the same evening, read aloud in the porch under the glass canopy in the rain:** nine lines; four parties returned; one returned the form entirely blank with the word *unknown* and a signature. **Five: Saint Orra west care entrance, holder on duty, not on a schedule, serving a verified clinic care claim, a verified transfer with a bed and a named receiving shift, and a return-of-care handoff named by a local steward. Restored needs: a rota and a payroll line.** Six: Marsh Lane substation. Seven: Bellow Market loading hatch. Eight: the Saint Orra bridge west-lane hinge. Nine: a school gate at Tarn Street, **held on a key, no holder field.** Two: car park barrier 3 and the mortuary van gate 6-44, with the note about the laundry delivery note and without Ivo Bamba's name. Three: the Carrow Lane Housing Association meter room. **Four: a lowland water point, with no number, no location of record, no district, no holder, no tier and no date of deferral, serving *a pump*.**
- **Oren Pell's filing ruling, in the porch, in about forty seconds: the node may not be described further, because describing it would require a location of record, and a location of record would be a survey, and a survey is a count, and a count is the price.**
- **The answer to *what would be needed to restore* is the same on every line where it appears: a person, a price, or a schedule.**
- **By Tuesday 20 May the fourth return carried a Cooper's Reach Thursday clinic — forty-one patients, nine over seventy-five, three housebound — and the answer to *who answers for it* was *nobody*, and it was the first time since 1 April that anybody in the city had written that word in a public document and meant it.**
- **The claims box at the Old Ward board closes Friday 18 July** (ninety days from Saturday 19 April) and holds one card, about a stair, which Jonas Mercer did not finish reading and put back.

### The two refusals that cost Jonas the volume's method

1. **The mortuary porter, 61, thirty-four years in the building, Wednesday 22 April, four seconds:** *I have not got the people who do not come in a van. If I write *a van and somebody who is upset* on that plate then in four years the gate opens for a van and somebody who is upset and it shuts for everybody else and there will be a piece of paper in a binder saying that is what it is for.*
2. **Marguerite Oyelaran, Monday 28 April:** she named herself into six lowland holder fields in four seconds — *I will do it on Monday. It will take four minutes and I have done it before* — and then declined, because **a plate will not take a bare role**, and a housing association officer who can be found at six water points on a street with no address is a document with a name on it. **She said yes in four seconds to a thing she knew was wrong, said so out loud, and asked him to write it down.** Jonas Mercer did not argue it, did not ask her again that day, and wrote that the draw ends and the job does not.

### What Jonas Mercer established on the page in Chapter 58

That the thing standing between a street and its water may be a thing nobody has ever said no to, **because nobody has ever needed to, because it has never been in a room.** A stage-2 mending needs a person with local standing to instruct it and a named holder to take it; he has done that three times in five weeks; every one of those was human. **A restoration route for a burst pipe is a price, the price is a term, the holder field on that term is the name of a company, and a company cannot be argued with, does not get tired, and is not standing in a chamber at half past twelve in the morning.**

### New named figures, Volume 02

- **Wendell Pryce**, 38, CivicCore continuity field manager, lanyard and a CONTINUITY FIELD badge. **At a Cooper's Reach panel on 23 April and in a car on a lowland embankment at one o'clock in the morning on 29 April for fifty-five minutes.** He says three things and none of them is a trick: the power of attorney works in eleven minutes, it is not a favour because it is a purchase, and he cannot make it a favour without creating a rota of nights. **He is right, and the third reason is the one he was told to say, and he added a fourth of his own: about two years after the rota of nights there is a tender for *emergency discretionary continuity, section four*, and it is awarded on a track record of discretionary actions.** He has never met Marguerite Oyelaran. He offered the clinic without the fee and was told there is no clinic without a fee.
- **Marguerite Oyelaran**, 47, chief officer of the Carrow Lane Housing Association, two rooms above a betting shop on Carrow Lane, eleven years in post, no assistant, sign says ASK FOR MARGUERITE. Bought Tier 2 on 11 March in about eleven minutes and had already read to page two. Put 128 participation agreements through 128 letterboxes herself on Saturdays in January and February. Says in a yard of her own corridor that **she is going to lose Block C.**
- **Prue Calloway**, 58, borough safety panel chair, eleven years, used section nine of the panel rules for the first time in her career and asked for it to be minuted that she wanted it minuted.
- **Mira** — the utilities district duty engineer, about forty, nineteen minutes away at one o'clock in the morning, who said no on the telephone in the plainest professional English Jonas Mercer has heard in five weeks and promised a written declaration at eight.
- **Doina Serkis**, records clerk, printed the first Return of a Deferred Node and put Ivo Bamba's name in before Nina Calder stopped her.
- **The man in the basement printer**, 31, print cardigan, who has printed a hundred and forty service diagrams and has never been asked about one: *it will draw you a shape and a term and a date and it will tell you the claim under it, and it has never once in the history of that machine drawn what a thing stopped doing.*
- **F. Oyelowo & Partners (CivicCore Emergency Works)** is **not** used; the collar's works order is stamped **CIVICCORE EMERGENCY WORKS, CONTRACTOR REF H. SOWERBY, ORDER 4471, 11 APRIL 2014, REMOVE ON RETURN 18 APRIL 2014.** Do not connect the Oyelowos to Marguerite Oyelaran.

### Instrument and fault classes named in this batch

- **Unfunded node**, **deferred node**, **continuity schedule**, **tier**, **last verified essential use**, **consented participation agreement**, **hollow term** (Volume 02's first instance is also its most sympathetic), **unclaimed burden** (unchanged, still Achebe's).
- **The lowland spur main went down at 23:40 on Tuesday 29 April** and was fixed at about ten past three on Wednesday 30 April by Emeka Rusk and Jonas Mercer in a chamber off the lowland road, four hundred metres short of the dip, by taking off a **commissioning clamp, two hundred and thirty millimetres of galvanised strap and a bolted collar**, fitted 11 April with a removal date of 18 April and left on for eleven days through a surge and a closed works order. The main itself was sound; the collar had stopped it expanding twice a day. **Emeka Rusk worked four unpaid hours on a gantry afterwards and the fault went in at ten past four with the operator box blank.**
- **The Weir trap can no longer be cleared by anybody, ever.** The six lowland points have no term in force, therefore no recorded last verified essential use, therefore the use is the node's maintenance, and the maintenance is the pump that is in the silt trap, and the water that would clear the trap is only authorised for the pump that would clear it. **Margo Doré's answer, at half past one on Wednesday 30 April, unasked: she wants the trap left packed and the bank left gone and the street left with nothing on it, because a pump is a thing and a thing can be bought, and the day this street has a pump somebody is going to ask what it is attached to.**

### Standing threads carried forward, unspent

- **The Weir's three positions**, the ring of pencil round a blank space, and the bank line. **The trap.** **The bank.** **The letter burnt in the cook-house stove in March.** **Perdita Sow's dislocated finger and eleven weeks.** **The boy of nineteen on the bicycle, whose name nobody has asked for and still must not be asked for.**
- **The name in biro on the map in the coat.** Folded with the margin inward. Not asked.
- **The map's two INSPECTED marks** in four millimetre corners, with a date, no inspector and no location of record. Untouched in this batch and still the live question of who may be looked at.
- **The panel chair's finding, 23 April:** the only route that would have won the Brine Wharf appeal is a location of record, and *a building has an address, it is the people who have no location of record, and a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate.* **This is the sentence that hands Volume 03 its question and it must not be resolved in Volume 02.**
- **Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause:** still in the file, nine days old as a file, no paid hours on it, eighteen months of fight, and Jonas has promised to keep it on a card in his coat and not to tell anybody he was told.
- **Royden Achebe's 6-L/14 report, due 29 May.** The bank is gone. The trap is lost. The burden is his.

## Chapters 60–70 established canon — VOLUME 02 MIDPOINT

### Calendar (authoritative; the Volume 01 anchor is unchanged and was not recomputed)

- Ch 60 = **Thursday 1 May into Friday 2 May.** Ch 61 = **Friday 2 May into Saturday 3 May.** Ch 62 = **Saturday 3 May into Sunday 4 May.** Ch 63 = **Saturday 10 May.** Ch 64 = **Friday 2 May, then Tuesday 6 May.** Ch 65 = **Wednesday 7 May into Thursday 8 May.** Ch 66 = **Thursday 8 May into Friday 9 May.** Ch 67 = **Thursday 15 May.** Ch 68 = **Saturday 17 May from 07:00 into Sunday 18 May, 20:00.** Ch 69 = **Sunday 18 May from about 20:00, then Monday 19 May from 07:10.** Ch 70 = **Monday 19 May, 10:00 to 16:10.**
- **1 May Thursday · 2 May Friday · 3 May Saturday · 4 May Sunday · 5 May Monday · 6 May Tuesday · 7 May Wednesday · 8 May Thursday · 9 May Friday · 10 May Saturday · 11 May Sunday · 12 May Monday · 13 May Tuesday · 14 May Wednesday · 15 May Thursday · 16 May Friday · 17 May Saturday · 18 May Sunday · 19 May Monday · 20 May Tuesday · 21 May Wednesday · 22 May Thursday · 23 May Friday · 26 May Monday · 27 May Tuesday · 28 May Wednesday · 29 May Thursday · 9 June Monday · 12 October 2014 Sunday · 18 July Friday.** Counted from Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March. Never reconcile against the real calendar.

### THE 19 MAY HEARING — canon, exact wording

- **Committee room two, Old Ward, Monday 19 May 2014, 10:00–16:10.** Chair **Fenella Marsh**. Members **Iven Sore, Douglas Imre** (41, four years, chaired nothing), **Hargreaves, Petrie Okonjo, Denman**. **Oren Pell in attendance**, coat over the back of the seventh chair. **Alden Rook** and **Ceri Vaughan** for CivicCore, plus an unintroduced man with a lanyard. **Gallery full at 10:55: forty-one folding chairs and about thirty standing at the rail.** Clerk **Frances Tolley**, twenty-two years.
- **Schedule 4 STANDS and is REFERRED ON.** Not defeated, not adopted.
- **The discrepancy, recorded and not resolved:** tabling list item 5 said *referred on*; **covering submission, 24 April, paragraph 2: the Committee is invited to APPROVE Schedule 4 and to ADOPT it as a schedule of the Committee for the remainder of the financial year.** The chair refused to rule. Eleven minutes. The Committee proceeded on the document in front of it.
- **Item one: the 17 April determination noted and suspended. Item two: the clerk's cover note, four sentences, read in full.** The fourth: *the Return of a Deferred Node is not before the Committee, is not adopted, is not minuted, is not enforceable; it establishes no service, no holder, no district, and no number of nodes in this city, and it is printed solely because the covering submission at item three refers to the number of deferred nodes and no other instrument in this city is capable of supplying that number.* **Frances Tolley wrote the fourth sentence on Friday afternoon on purpose, after reading the form, and rang Nina Calder to ask whether she minded.**
- **The union's finding:** Councillor Petrie asked that the minute record that the author of the document the contractor relies on is **not present**. He is not. Marsh: *a finding is a finding whether its author is in the room or not,* and the clerk had minuted it before the chair asked.
- **Nobody succeeded at beating a price with a sentence. Four attempts, four failures, every failure correct:**
  1. **Rook**, at 12:10, did not define *holder* and never will, and argued the volume's best case: *the schedule does not price access to urgent care, it prices the continuity of a node; a care claim is not a node; the west clinic care entrance is not in failure and is not on a schedule and Schedule 4 did not touch it, and that door has been open every night since 2 April on a term read aloud at ten o'clock by two people who can say when it ends, and that is the only thing anybody in this city has done right since 3 April.* He added: *I have never argued that a schedule is a kindness. I have argued that it is cheaper than a queue.*
  2. A member answered that this is therefore a finding — a volunteer arrangement with an unknown expiry while a contractor prices the alternative. Rook: *when it expires the door is a deferred node, and Schedule 4 is the document that tells you what a deferred node does before it happens rather than afterwards.*
  3. **Sore's four sentences about a lift**, which changed nothing, because a lift is not a node and a chair cannot rule that a piece of plant is a service.
  4. **Ceri Vaughan offered the contractor's own definition — Schedule 4, page 460, sub-paragraph 3, present since 1 March** — and a member asked for it. **Pell leaned forward an inch and advised the chair in eleven seconds: a definition given in the course of considering a document is a determination of that document; the Committee has not determined to determine Schedule 4; if a definition is minuted today the Committee will have adopted a four-hundred-and-sixty-page schedule without a division and nobody will have voted on it.** Marsh said it was correct, said stopping it is not the chair's job and that she was doing it anyway, and told Imre what would happen.
- **Councillor Imre WITHDREW his motion to define *holder*, and asked that the withdrawal be minuted with the reason: the registrar's advice is good and he will not have it become a rumour in six weeks that the Committee declined out of cowardice.** **At 13:20, on a motion of the chair agreed without a division, the Committee RECORDED THAT IT IS UNABLE TO DEFINE THE TERM *HOLDER*, in Imre's words, and will report on a definition at its next sitting if one is before it.**
- **The chair's two determinations, 15:10. (1) The Return of a Deferred Node is RECEIVED into the records of the Committee — not adopted. (2) EVERY PARTY HOLDING CONTINUITY IN THIS CITY IS REQUIRED TO ANSWER A RETURN OF A DEFERRED NODE, IN PUBLIC, ON THE FORM CIRCULATED WITH THE PAPERS, BY THE NEXT SITTING, AND THE ANSWERS ARE TO BE READ INTO THE RECORD AS THEY ARE RECEIVED; AND WHERE THE ANSWER TO *WHO ANSWERS FOR IT* IS NO PARTY, THAT IS TO BE RECORDED AS NO PARTY, IN THOSE WORDS, AND READ INTO THE RECORD AS NO PARTY.** Her reasons, in order: a node with no answering party is a fact about a node and not a fact about a person; this city has no instrument that can record a fact about a node; it has four hundred and sixty pages of price list; and at some point somebody is going to have to be the person who starts the record, and it is her, and in four years the chair will be somebody else who will not know why.
- **THE CHAIR'S OPERATIVE SENTENCE, AND THE MECHANISM OF THE WHOLE HEARING: A PARTY THAT ANSWERS MAY BE ASKED WHAT IT WILL DO, AND NOT WHAT IT WILL COST. THE CHAIR STATED THAT THIS IS NOT IN THE DOCUMENT, THAT SHE DID NOT WRITE IT, THAT SHE HAS HAD IT IN HER HEAD SINCE THE SECOND WEEK OF APRIL, AND THAT SHE CANNOT SAY WHERE IT CAME FROM — and that two members of the Committee had assumed it was on page nine and it is not.** Underneath it, and in the document, at page nine, is the opposite: **A TIER MAY NOT BE CHALLENGED BY A PERSON.** The chair's own working of the pair, which is the only thing she added to the determination that was not on the sheet in front of her: *a tier may not be challenged by a person, so a person is never in the way, and a claim nobody can answer is a claim nobody is ever in trouble about. A party that answers may be asked what it will do, so a party that answers is in the way, permanently, and the moment that sentence exists in the record it exists in the mouth of whoever reads it out, which in this borough is apparently a woman with a form and a telephone number, and I cannot stop that and I have stopped trying.* **Jonas Mercer, in the fourth seat back on the left, is the only person in that room who has read the sentence on a plate, and he did not say. No state file and no later chapter may say the chair quoted the plate or read it off a plate.**
- **Sore's motion, 14:25, four sentences, unexplained:** the Committee is asked to consider a document whose entire operative content is a price list; **a Committee that is unable to define the word *holder* is entirely capable of counting what has been paid for a schedule**; she is not asking the Committee to like the number, only to put it in the minute; a Committee that will not do the arithmetic of a document it has been asked to uphold is not a Committee. **It went into the minute as an outstanding request for a return of schedule commitments. No figure exists.** CivicCore stated four, including **the Tier 1 fee of £1,880 for 6-L/16 placed on a schedule on 12 May**. The borough holds none and cannot say what it does not hold. **Marguerite Oyelaran stated £16,800 for four nodes, twice, at the chair's request, from the public seats.**
- **Nell Ardent, 14:40, one sentence, allowed without motion or notice as a point of order about the papers, and it is in the minute:** *the Committee is asked to note that the only instrument in this city capable of producing the number it has been asked about was printed on the thirtieth of April and has no adoption behind it.*
- **THE THREE THINGS THE CITY PUTS INTO THE RECORD: a registrar's record of 18 April; a form with a hole in it, received and not adopted; and a return whose ninth line has no answer in it.** **At 14:19 the chair said the word *nobody* in the room and deliberately did not minute it** — *a chair should not put a word into a minute that a records supervisor has not put into a form* — **and Nina Calder, from the public seats, said the word aloud and said she would write it into the form on 20 May in her own words. That is the origin of the 20 May coda and it must not be contradicted.**
- **Jonas Mercer asked ONE question, at 15:50, from the fourth seat back on the left: is the Committee going to require somebody to answer for the six?** The answer was **no**, on page nine, and the chair asked for it to be minuted that she was asked to require a person at six lowland water points and declined in the only words available. **The reason nobody has asked Marguerite Oyelaran is the reason the Committee cannot answer him:** the six nodes have no party that answers for them, and that is not a mystery and not a scandal and not anybody's failure; it is the position of a thing nobody maintains, nobody has priced and nobody can be found at, **in a city where being found at is the price.** In September there is to be a public, minuted record of the nodes in this city with no party answering for them, carrying no name, no address, no survey and no count of anything that lives anywhere.
- **Tomas Renn was not in the room.** He asked Jonas on 27 April not to come and Jonas did not come.
- **Marguerite Oyelaran was in the back row on the left, a party to nothing.**
- **The cooperative steward board was not formed and was not moved.**

### Saint Orra west after Chapter 63 — canon

- **The Saturday decision, 10 May 2014, 10:00, unvoted.** **Tier 2, £4,200, on 6-44, the mortuary van gate, effective 11 May 2014, term to 06:00 on Sunday 12 October 2014.** Halvorsen signed it at about twenty to one under the clause that lets the party holding the standing continuity authorisation place nodes on a schedule at any time.
- **Three of the four nodes in failure were not services.** **Staff stair 2** — Dermot Fahey, facilities supervisor, six years, wrote *this is a stair.* **Car park barrier 3** — Ines Tarrant, car park supervisor, eleven years, wrote *car park barrier three is a car park barrier, it is not a service, there is nobody in this car park who is a customer, they are all staff, and I have been doing this job for eleven years and nobody has ever asked me what it is for and I have assumed it is a barrier.* **Service riser 1** — a wet riser serving one room on level zero, drawn by **Ronan Vasquez**, estates office, eleven years, in ninety seconds, with a cross on the two taps it serves, and the sentence **THIS IS A PIPE IN A BUILDING. IT IS NOT A SERVICE. I HOLD ITS MAINTENANCE. I AM TAKING IT OFF THE LIST.** He removed it himself under his own holding in nine minutes, because the schedule lets the party holding the maintenance take a node off as well as put one on. *Nobody has ever asked whether a thing on a list of things to pay for is a thing.*
- **The mortuary porter, 61, thirty-four years, came in at half past ten, did not know he had written the four words, and said: *that is mine, is it. — Right. Then I would like it left where it is and I would like it not to be on a form.*** Minuted as **attended, declined to sign.** His letter of 30 April, in writing, dated and signed: *I still have not got the people who do not come in a van.*
- **The care entrance plate 6-41 printed on 10 May at about 12:20: THIS NODE IS ON A CONTINUITY SCHEDULE. TIER 2. THE SCHEDULE EXPIRES 06:00, SUNDAY 12 OCTOBER 2014.** **There is no field on that plate for a schedule and no field for a tier and no field for money.** The plate has five fields — holder, witness, fallback, term, claim — and a cursor. The trust's finance side says the cost *will fall out of the year end.* Nobody has been billed. **Mina Okafor put it in the public box in her own hand with the date, under the forty minutes and not under the recent notices, because it is not a notice and it is an event: *a woman asked on the tenth of May and nobody knew.***
- **The mechanism Jonas worked out on Saturday (Ch 63), which is the batch's institutional fact:** the clause says *nodes*, and the care entrance is on a **chain** plate with two other things on it that are not nodes. Reading a schedule as a man with four nodes and £4,200 is not reading it.
- **Ronan Vasquez's sentence, unprompted, at about ten to one on 10 May: *it expires on a Sunday. Every site in this borough expires on a Sunday. I have been putting these on for eleven years and I have never once had to think about what a Sunday is, because the last three times it has expired nobody in the building was standing there on the Sunday.***
- **Halvorsen's rule about asking, stated once and binding on the next batch: every time he asks one of those four people about a date, one of them ends up on a rota because of the asking. He is not going to ask. He says Jonas may tell them.**

### The 12 October problem (Ch 67) — live, dated, unresolved

- **THE SIX-DAY WINDOW IS 07:00 TO 06:00 SIX DAYS LATER. This is what makes the whole problem.**
- **The handover on Saturday 4 October 2014 at 22:00 read a term running 07:00 Sunday 5 October to 06:00 Sunday 12 October — which ends at the exact minute the schedule expires, to the minute, and nobody put them there on purpose.** **That is the last term in that building that exists entirely inside a funded schedule.**
- **The renewal is due at the 22:00 handover on Saturday 11 October 2014, and a term read then runs 07:00 Sunday 12 October to 06:00 Sunday 19 October — one hour after the schedule has gone.** **So the 11 October handover is the crossing.** It is a Saturday night, in a corridor, read by two tired porters, and no field on the plate says *this is the last one that counts*.
- **There is no scale.** It is a cliff, and there is a cliff inside it, and nobody writes a scale between two cliffs. The plate's standing rule settles the rest: **the node serves the term it was last read into, and not the term somebody expected.**
- **IF THE 11 OCTOBER RENEWAL DOES NOT HAPPEN, on 06:00 Sunday 12 October the care entrance is not on a reduced term or a deferred term — it is on NOTHING.** No term. An empty field means the node's maintenance; the maintenance is a rota; there is no rota; and the only thing left in that building at 07:00 on a Sunday is a door and two names, and the fallback asks a named on-call clinician, and there are two, and one of them is part-time.
- **The word is a declining reduction, and it is Priya Ranganathan's discovery, not Jonas's.** He had it and would say only the words.
- **The four who could read the 11 October 22:00 handover: Ruth Nwosu, Ezra Mbeki, Bram Osei, Priya Ranganathan. All four have said, correctly, that they will not.** Bram's is a refusal of a whole Thursday that he has asked to be left in the same box with the same words, and **Priya has said she is not going to be the person who asks him for a second refusal in October so that there are two on a board.**
- **THE TWO-REFUSAL RULE, LIVE AND DATED, AND THE DATE IS THE ELEVENTH OF OCTOBER, NOT THE TWELFTH.** Tomas Renn has it, and this is the first time in four months anybody has given him a date for his own question. He is going to ask it in the shop on **Monday 19 May** in front of about nine people, and then go and stand in a corridor in Saint Orra west on **Thursday 22 May** and say the date out loud to the four of them. **He has told Jonas not to come into the west site on the 22nd or the 29th or any day between, and that if he needs him he will ask in a way that can be refused.**

### The lowland spur, the outfall duty, and the first transfer (Ch 68) — canon

- **6-L/16 — LOWLAND SPUR AND OUTFALL. SALT ROW BOUNDARY TO THE DIP. 480 m, NO INTERRUPTION.** New canon node number.
- **CivicCore placed 6-L/16 on Tier 1 at £1,880 on Tuesday 12 May 2014**, signed with a power of attorney and not with a name, under *the party holding the standing continuity authorisation may place nodes on a schedule at any time.* **The party holding that authorisation is CivicCore, because they hold the maintenance of the gauge that dropped at 23:11 on 29 April.** The holder field had held the duty engineer for the district, named, and the name has gone.
- **The outfall inspection duty** — go to the outfall when the supply is restricted or the gauge drops, look at the screens and the chamber, enter a fault, stand there while the restriction is on — **was held on this node and fell out of it at about 20:00 on 29 April when the term broke. It had no holder from that moment. Emeka Rusk has been doing it unpaid since, seventeen days by 17 May, and put the last fault in with the operator box blank.**
- **THE INSTRUMENT: A DUTY IS NOT A PLACE.** Pell's 30 April ruling was that describing the *water point* would require a location of record, and a location of record would be a survey, and a survey is a count, and a count is the price. **An outfall is not a water point. A duty is an act. An act can be held by a findable person in a findable office, and the holding does not put the lowland in a book, and nothing on the record can be read and turned into a street.** This is not a recognition of a place or its people and must never be slid into one.
- **THE TRANSFER, 17 May 2014 — the first in this series that has succeeded.** Entered by Nina Calder as an **entry on the series 6 lowland-chain file and expressly NOT a register** (a register is a list, and she has spent a week in May learning what a list does to a hundred and twenty-eight people in four blocks; *there will be forty of them on it in nine years and by then it will be a list, and by then I will not be here*). Six items, read back, initialled by Jonas before it was typed:
