  - **Claim:** LOWLAND OUTFALL INSPECTION DUTY, 6-L/16 (outfall and screens, Carrow Lane). **Basis:** held claim lapsed 29 April 2014, 23:40, on the term of the parent node. **Duty described as an act and not a place; no location of record created; no address, household, or person recorded.**
  - **Moved by: MERCER, J.** — civic standing, register of attachment, released by no person.
  - **Held by: ACHEBE, R.** — interim lowland maintenance post, in his own name.
  - **Term:** from the reading, to 06:00 on the last day of the fee year of the parent node, without carry-forward. **Renewal by the holder only.**
  - **Withdrawn by: —**
  - **Claim:** the holder inspects the outfall and screens after any restriction on the supply, and stands the restriction down, and enters the fault. **THE CLAIM IS UNPAID AND THE PARTY THAT PAYS FOR THE SPUR DOES NOT ANSWER FOR THIS.**
  - **Witness: E. RUSK, pump operator, docks, twenty-six years. Second witness: NIL.**
- **Read in daylight at 20:00 on Sunday 18 May in a garden in Tideglass**, per the registrar's rule, in the place where the receiving person keeps what they are holding. Emeka Rusk read the claim in his own words. Achebe read the six items back slowly, got one wrong, was corrected, and took it.
- **THE RULE HE CANNOT GET OUT OF: a holder may offer a held claim back once, in writing, within seven days of the reading; the offer is recorded; THE OFFER DOES NOT TRANSFER THE CLAIM; the claim is released only when a successor consents to hold it; AND A PERSON WHO HAS NOT BEEN ASKED MAY NOT BE NAMED.** He offered it back inside the first hour and is not going to use a minute of the rest. **The only door out is to make somebody findable, and he will not do that to a man he met once at a bus stop, or to Emeka Rusk, or to the duty engineer who said no in forty seconds in the dark on 30 April and was right.**
- **WHAT IT COST HIM, in his own words, and it is the Stage 3 cost on the page: *I would rather have had it on the twenty-ninth of April. In April I was refusing a thing. Refusing is what a man does. This week I am asking to give one back, and asking is what a man does, and asking puts a date and a name and a shape on me, and there is nobody in this city who is on more shapes than I am, and every one of the shapes so far has had somebody else's name in it, and this is the first one where the shape is me and the name is also me.*** He also **required the cost to be written into the terms in Jonas's own words**, so that in August he can point at a piece of paper and not at a memory.
- **6-44's unclaimed burden, Royden Achebe's report due 29 May, is unchanged.**

### The Return of a Deferred Node after Chapter 65 — canon

- **It is a PUBLIC DOCUMENT as of 07 May 2014.** Form number, crest, obtainable at the records office. **Not adopted, not minuted, not enforceable, establishes nothing, names nobody.** Every party holding maintenance is on notice that a copy of what they wrote is a copy of a public document.
- **Nina Calder's reason, decided on the Sunday and told on the Wednesday:** a trade association rang on Tuesday for a copy of line three and said *I have it off a photocopy of the board, the whole page, including your crest.* **A form with a blank line at the bottom is honest. A form no party is required to answer is also a form four parties can decline to answer for as long as they like, and one a trade body can hold a copy of, and one that gets a photocopy passed round a market at six in the morning.** *I have never in nineteen years made a thing public that a private person had written in their own hand and given to me for a purpose.*
- **THE EIGHTH LINE OF THE SECOND RETURN, 1 May: the Tarn Street school gate reads HOLDER — A KEY**, put there by a records clerk because a man had been in the field on the first pass and stopped, and the field would not take blank, and there was nowhere else to put it. **The caretaker's answer, by telephone on 1 May: *the key is the honest answer and the key is wrong and the line is mine, because I am the one who has to be wrong about it for the rest of the year.*** **A holder field on a form is not a holder. It is a box.**
- **The second notice of the batch printed on the Carrow Lane meter-room plate on 8 May, and the plate now carries three lines, the other two being *a tier may be challenged by a district or by a node in the same tier, it may not be challenged by a person* and, since 3 May, the term-it-was-last-read-into line.**
- **The 19 May determination requires every party holding continuity to answer it in public by the next sitting, and requires that where the answer to *who answers for it* is no party, that shall be recorded as no party, in those words.** The word *nobody* is written into a form by Nina Calder on **20 May**, from the chair's mouth on the 19th. **NO DATE FOR THE NEXT SITTING IS ESTABLISHED ANYWHERE IN CHAPTERS 49–70.** Chapter 70 says only *referred on to the next sitting* and *would report on the definition at its next sitting if and when a definition was before it*. **There is no 1 June report date, no 2 June sitting, and no deadline that has been running since April. A determination with a deadline and no date on it is not a deadline, and the answers are discharged by the act of answering and not by anybody receiving them — this is available to Chapter 0079 and is a live pressure, not a settled fact.**
- **CivicCore's own returns arrived on 14 May, on the city's form, as Rook said in a stairwell it would, and CivicCore's return is pinned over line three and answers for the same meter room the borough's return answers for, with no precedence between them.**

### The Carrow Lane arithmetic — canon, and it is exact

- **£2.60 a room a week = £135.20 a room a year. 122 paying rooms = £16,494.40. The schedule cost £16,800. The shortfall is £305.60.** 128 paying would have been £17,305.60, a surplus of £505.60. **The cost of the six people who would not sign is eight hundred and eleven pounds and twenty pence, to the penny — £505.60 of margin the association never had plus the £305.60 hole, all of it landing in the same place because there is no margin — and nobody in that office had ever worked it out.** 128 − 122 = the six. At 28 April, on the page Marguerite Oyelaran handed over, the count was **120 of 128 on the first sheet with a second sheet behind it carrying two more**; by 2 May it was 122 of 128 and six had not signed. Her false start in the office is 30 rooms at £2.60 for fifty weeks, which is the audit's method, not hers.
- **Block C is a void: thirty rooms, 1938, shared stopcock in a cupboard nobody has a key to, eight households carrying the water in buckets since 1998, twenty-two households moved since Sunday 26 April out of a void allowance, and a letter of 29 April isolating the supply on Tuesday 6 May. Block C going empty is £4,056 a year of income that has stopped; the shortfall goes from £305.60 to £4,361.60; the void allowance is nil in October.**
- **Block consumption is what the term is sized on. It rose thirty-one per cent in April, because all four blocks were full for eight days after the surge and none of them was empty for the eleven days in March the price was calculated on. The fee is priced on a year in which these blocks are half empty and is being paid for in a year in which they are full, and the difference is in April's metered recharge on four invoices to a hundred and twenty-eight people and is not in a letter.**
- **TWELVE of the hundred and twenty-eight had asked to leave the agreement by Saturday 3 May, and ELEVEN of the twelve did it in one morning, in their own handwriting, in their own doorways, in the same hour, on a Tuesday.** The twelfth letter was hand-delivered face down on Thursday 8 May at 11:10 by the woman from Block B room eleven, who counted on Saturday morning at breakfast and has told her daughter.
- **On Thursday 8 May a man of about forty from Block B room six, one of the three who did not understand the agreement and the one Marguerite would not ring a fourth time, came up and asked to sign**, because his block is on a piece of paper in a public building with a fee on it and being outside the agreement is a worse place to be than being inside it. **She signed his name onto the page in a room at the end of the corridor.**
- **On 7 May a member from Salt Row put in a council chamber minute, unanswered by seven members: should the Carrow Lane Housing Association be a party to the 19 May hearing at all — because if it is not, the only line in that room that says what a tier costs a person who lives in the block will be read out by a chair from a piece of paper a man off a register brought in at ten to eight in the morning, and that man is not a party either.**
- **A four-page circular from the borough association of housing officers, paragraph four, with a one-page letter signed by two people Marguerite has worked with for eleven years: officers in that position should take independent advice before 19 May, and *we do not consider that a fee recoverable from rents can be defended in a public document without an answer to a question we have not been able to ask.* Marguerite has not telephoned them back.**
- **She did not go into the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane on 8 May**, where eleven households have been living at her expense since 17 April, and a woman who rang on Monday was not called back until Monday 22 May. **The fee does not stop is still her condition and it is going to be said in a room on 22 May and it will not be true that day.**
- **£16,800 was stated publicly for the first time on 19 May, by her, twice, at the chair's request.**

### Six lowland water points — the figure, and where it now is

- **Six lowland water points. Tier 1 £1,880 each. £11,280 a year. Nobody has asked.** Worked out by Marguerite Oyelaran on 12 March, written on her own corridor board in her own hand on Monday 28 April at about 10:11 a.m. in the presence of Jonas Mercer: **SIX LOWLAND WATER POINTS. TIER 1. £1,880 PER NODE PER YEAR. £11,280 A YEAR. NOBODY HAS ASKED ME.**
- **IT IS NOW IN A COMMITTEE MINUTE.** Jonas Mercer wrote it on one side of a departmental form on the evening of Sunday 18 May at 9.40 p.m., with four statements: he does not ask any party to place the six nodes on a schedule; the six nodes have no number, no location of record, no district, no holder and no recorded date of deferral, and nothing in the paper supplies any of them; no person, household, occupancy figure or list is asserted and none may be derived; he is not a party, a member or an officer and is in the gallery. **He gave it to the chair in a corridor at 07:50 on Monday 19 May, and the chair read it in full at 11:11 a.m., commented on nothing, and it went into the minute as a document received and not as evidence.** He did not put it in the claims box and did not put it in a speech. It is on the record before it was said.
- **He did not go up the Weir path on Sunday night and did not telephone Margo Doré, with the sheet already written.** One of the six lowland points is the Weir stand. **This is unpaid, unargued, and must not be resolved in the next batch without her.**

### The council flood forum — canon, and it is a list

- **The Saint Orra and District Flood Forum.** A council committee constituted in March of this year after a review. **Eleven places on the membership process and about £90,000. TEN ARE FILLED; THE ELEVENTH HAS BEEN EMPTY SINCE THE SECOND WEEK OF MARCH AND THE CHAIR ADMITTED ON 6 MAY THAT HE HAD NOT PUT THAT IN A MINUTE.** The ten cards: a housing trust, a school, a market, CivicCore as **continuity contractor (non-voting)**, and **six** ordinary service districts — shapes the drawing layer makes out of terms in force, with names on them and no people in them at all. **No street on it, because a council body cannot seat a person** — and the lowland is not drawn on the layer at all, so it cannot be on the body.
- **Gareth Raynor-Hale, about fifty, resilience officer, lanyard with a plastic sleeve replaced twice.** He has asked eight people a **second** question, which is not about a seat: *what happened on the night of the seventeenth on the lowland embankment, and what does the crossing do in ordinary weather*, from an operations review of 28 April in which somebody wrote *lowland evacuation capacity: unresolved.* He says where every answer came from, in the room, for the minutes.
- **Kofi Sarr, 2 May, gave an answer instead of a refusal for the first time:** bay four closed at 11 minutes past two on a stated reason, **sixty-one on the fare machine, four and a half miles, about forty over seventy, nothing else.** *You do not refuse a man for four months and then find out that the reason you refused him was not principle. It is that you did not have the number yet.*
- **Nora Bell, 6 May, was asked the second question — the fourth asking — and answered with a number nobody asked her for: nine hundred and forty.** Nineteen years of pencil marks in a book; the number of *times* somebody has stood at that barrier end and been unable to cross. *It is not nine hundred and forty people. It is a few hundred people, more than once each, and the number of people is not on the page and I have never tried to work out the rest of it, because if I work it out then it is a number of people.* Raynor-Hale said where it came from, in the room: **that number is one woman's own private record and it is not in any inspection, and it exists because nobody ever asked her for it and she kept it anyway.**
- **She took the eleventh place at 16:10 and handed it back at 16:25 and asked for her name to be taken off the roll.** **Councillor Martyn Craddock, 44, chair of the forum, refused, and was unforgivable and correct:** the roll is a list, it is the only tool a body with £90,000 and eleven places and a quarterly report has, and if names come off the first time a person is uncomfortable then this becomes a room where people only put their names down when they are certain. He did not fill the place and did not write *vacant*, because the minute says eleven and the minute is public. **Her words went into the minute unedited: *I took a place at about ten past four and I gave it back at a quarter past four, and I am not sorry about either, and I would like it recorded that a crossing is not a body and that I was not asked whether I wanted to be a body.*** Her name is in the attendance column and will be in the September quarterly report.
- **Raynor-Hale has been in the Saint Orra bridge file and is going to be talking about the empty-crossing clause in September whether anybody tells him or not.** Jonas answered him truthfully in a stairwell and said, out loud and against a promise, that the man whose it is is going to fight it for eighteen months on his own. **He did not telephone Dev Singh, because Dev Singh asked him not to be told and said he would deny it and would not be lying.** The clause is already public. The only private thing in it is a shop steward's dignity and a dispatcher's pride.

### The decontamination room (Ch 66) — canon

- **The bench steriliser in the decontamination room on level zero at Saint Orra west, four years old.** Its supplier's service contract **expired at midnight on 30 April**. Annual service **£1,100**; an activity, not capital; due in the west site's activity budget by **17:00 on Friday 9 May** or the room goes on a public compliance report in June, with a paragraph number, and there are four hundred and twenty women in the borough whose instruments come out of that room every weekday.
- **The activity budget had £913 in a dead 2011 coffee-and-cakes line item until 22 April, when Halvorsen committed all of it to a part-time on-call clinician line.** So **£1,100 out of that budget is the contract or the second clinician line, and it is both or neither.**
- **The £4,200 cannot help. A continuity fee buys a node. A supplier's maintenance contract is not a node and cannot go on a continuity schedule.** *I have told it to two people and they both said yes and neither repeated it, and that is how I knew it was true.*
- **Dr. Sanaa Iqbal found £410**: a quarterly service plus a logged weekly chamber temperature verification, twenty minutes a week, the log to the governance file. **She refuses to be thanked, in advance, in a corridor, in daylight**, and stopped Jonas saying *noted*: *you are not allowed to write that down and put it in a coat.*
- **THE COST, decided by Gunnar Halvorsen on Friday 9 May with three hours and ten minutes to go, and announced to Jonas in a doorway:** the £410 is signed; **the part-time on-call clinician's line is suspended from 1 June**; the letter to the education office went on his desk at 09:00 on Monday 19 May and **he told Sanaa in person at nine and did not telephone her**; the twenty minutes a week goes into a corridor rota with nobody against it, written by him, and he has not asked and is not going to. *If you ever see a line on a board in that building that says verification, decontamination, twenty minutes with nothing against it, then that is me, and you will not tell her that either.*
- **THE JUNE RECKONING'S DATE: MONDAY 9 JUNE 2014, TEN IN THE MORNING.** Not the committee, not CivicCore, not a hearing. **A review of the west clinic's own witness structure, chaired by Dr. Sanaa Iqbal because she signed the renewal clause that needs two people in a corridor at ten at night, and she is the only person who can chair it and she has not asked anybody.** Nine people in the room and four of them are on that roster. **Jonas Mercer is not on it and there is nothing on it for him, and she told him the date on 8 May, four weeks early, precisely so that he would not ask her for it in a corridor in June.**

### Priya Ranganathan on 15 May (Ch 67) — canon

- **Forty-one, part-time, on the west roster as a named on-call clinician since 2 May, two days a week, and she has a daughter of eleven.** **She came to the 22:00 handover on Thursday 15 May, a night she is not rostered for, because a handover needs two named people and there was nobody else** — she had asked Mina Okafor on the Monday whether she could sit in on one.
- **For the first time in five weeks the care entrance was opened by a person who is neither Ruth Nwosu nor Bram Osei nor Ezra Mbeki nor a declaration.** At 22:10 on 15 May she declared the ninth emergency opening of the month, in about seventy seconds, in writing, for a woman of eighty-one with a bed booked and a named receiving shift. Door open 22:20, closed 22:23, settled on level two. It went in the box.
- **She read the five lines back unasked, got one wrong, was corrected by Ezra Mbeki, and took the correction.** Ruth Nwosu: *that is the fifth one this month and the first one from a clinician.*
- **She refuses to be thanked for any of it: *being thanked for turning up to the witness requirement of your own job is a kind of thanks that stops a person coming back.*** Jonas says he was not going to and it is the first time in five weeks he has managed not to.
- **She asked the question nobody has asked: WHAT IS A DECLINING REDUCTION.** She worked the mechanism out standing at the plate, and Jonas said the two words and nothing else, and she said *I did not think you were going to.*
- **She named, in order, the three reasons a mending is impossible to her: the being-on-a-rota problem, a plate will not take a bare role and she has a daughter of eleven, and she will not be thanked.** And then she gave the answer nobody wanted: **nobody is going to offer**, and she gave each of the four a separate correct reason, including **that she is not going to be the person who asks Bram Osei for a second refusal on a Sunday in October so that there are two of them on a board.**
- **Her sentence to Jonas, and it is the same sentence she gave Sanaa Iqbal in April: write it down and do not do anything with it.**

### The four Lattice notices of Chapters 60–70

- **Ch 61, Carrow Lane meter-room plate:** `A NODE WITH A HOLDER THAT HAS NOT BEEN RENEWED FOR ITS TERM SERVES THE TERM IT WAS LAST READ INTO. AND NOT THE TERM SOMEBODY EXPECTED.`
- **Ch 65, same plate, second line:** `A RETURN MADE BY A PARTY HOLDING MAINTENANCE IS EVIDENCE OF A STATE. IT IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A PERSON, A HOUSEHOLD, OR A NUMBER.`
- **Ch 68, outfall plate 6-L/16:** `A NODE THAT IS ON A SCHEDULE AT A TIER IS NOT HELD BY THE PARTY THAT PAYS. IT IS HELD BY THE PARTY THAT ANSWERS. A PARTY THAT ANSWERS MAY BE ASKED WHAT IT WILL DO AND NOT WHAT IT WILL COST.`
- **Ch 70, Old Ward board:** `WHERE TWO TERMS APPLY TO ONE USE AND NEITHER HAS PRECEDENCE, THE USE IS PERFORMED AND ENTERED, AND NOBODY IS BLAMED, AND THE ENTRY IS MADE TWICE.`
- **Four notices in eleven chapters, at most one each, and none of them defines *holder*.** The Ch 46 ceiling was not repeated.

### New named figures, Chapters 60–70

**FRANCES TOLLEY**, committee clerk of the Standing Committee on Civic Continuity, twenty-two years. Wrote the cover note's fourth sentence on purpose on Friday 2 May, and rang Nina Calder to ask whether she minded. Told Jonas not to put a paper under the door, and told him the gallery fills at about eleven and the front two rows go to an unchecked press list, and that she would tell him now that the chair would not read his paper to the Committee. **The word *nobody* on the 20 May return is Nina Calder's, and her one sentence from the public seats on 19 May is its origin. It was not written by a clerk and not by a chair.** **DOINA SERKIS** (already), records clerk, who put *A KEY* in a holder field and apologised to a caretaker for it. **IBRAHIM SESAY** (already), 51, Tarn Street caretaker. **DERMOT FAHEY**, facilities supervisor, Saint Orra west, six years, author of *this is a stair.* **INES TARRANT**, car park supervisor, eleven years, author of *nobody has ever asked me what it is for and I have assumed it is a barrier.* **RONAN VASQUEZ**, estates office, eleven years, author of *this is a pipe in a building*, who removed the riser from the list himself in nine minutes and who said the sentence about Sundays. **FENELLA MARSH** (already). **Iven Sore** (already). **DOUGLAS IMRE**, 41, four years on the committee, chaired nothing; withdrew the definition motion and asked for the reason to be minuted. **HARGREAVES, PETRIE OKONJO, DENMAN** — the other three members; Petrie asked that the minute record the union's author is not in the room. **MARTYN CRADDOCK**, 44, chair of the Saint Orra and District Flood Forum, longest-serving member since March. **GARETH RAYNOR-HALE**, about 50, resilience officer, lanyard, and the man with a card that gets him into about nine buildings and one of them he should not have been in. **A member from Salt Row**, about fifty, four years in, with a bad head cold, whose question about whether the housing association should be a party is on the council chamber minute of 7 May unanswered.

## Standing threads carried forward from Chapters 60–70, unspent and unresolved

- **The Weir's three positions**, the ring of pencil round a blank space, the bank line, **the trap**, the letter burnt in the cook-house stove in March. **Nobody was added to the rota and Bo Trimble had nothing added to him. No pump was offered in this batch, and if one is offered Margo Doré refuses it in her own words and it is not argued out of her.**
- **The name in biro on the map in the coat.** Folded with the margin inward. Not asked. Untouched in this batch.
- **The boy of nineteen on the bicycle.** Untouched. Not asked.
- **Prue Calloway's finding of 23 April** — *a building has an address, it is the people who have no location of record, and a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate* — **is not resolved, is not opened, and is the Volume 03 door.**
- ~~**The nineteen of thirty-one at Wren Court, Salter's Yard** ... Untouched in this batch. Do not resolve.~~ **SUPERSEDED BY CHAPTERS 83–86. Wren Court is the payment for this volume and it is resolved, in a room, without Jonas Mercer in it, on 2–4 July 2014. See the canon block for Chapters 81–90. It is not resolved any further: nineteen of thirty-one signed, the twelve are not asked to sign, the four who missed a month are not listed, clause nine is read out four times and not tidied, and the arrangement that survives is one findable person holding a thing nobody can ask her about.**
- **The rota at Saint Orra west has still not been built again.** Every term is six days. The reason is in Ruth Nwosu's handwriting in the handover book: *no six-day term until the rota is built again.* **The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled and Bram Osei's refused Thursday is still on the board and he asked that it stay.**
- **Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause** is now in the hands of a resilience officer with a laminated card and will be in a report in September. **Jonas said, in a stairwell, the thing he promised not to say, and did not telephone him.**
- **The claims box closes Friday 18 July** and holds one card about a stair. Nothing may be written into it that establishes a service or a person.

## Sections B and C of the outline, reconciled after Batch 0002

- `outline/volume-02.md` **Section B (Chapters 60–70)** was delivered as **the participation agreement made public and priced**: rule 4(c)'s calendar made visible in four blocks and a doorway, the Return turned from a form into a public document and thereby into an instrument of exposure, and the hearing on 19 May that could not define *holder* and did not defeat anything. Its central beat — *Jonas watches a woman he respects choose a price and call it the only door in the room* — is delivered as **Marguerite Oyelaran in the public seats of the 19 May hearing, asked about a lift, giving the four best sentences anybody said all day, and a member replying that it is a matter for the association and not for this committee, and her agreeing.** The Committee's own determination is the only door in the room and it is a form.
- `outline/volume-02.md` **Section C (Chapters 71–80)** opens with **the heat**, on a Borough Heat Threshold Protocol already pinned to the Old Ward board, and **the first transfer that has to be repeated** is set up: Jonas's name is now on a record, Achebe cannot hand the claim back, and in August somebody will ring him about a Tuesday.
- `outline/volume-02.md` **Section C's central beat** names **Royden Achebe as the receiving holder who refuses to hand it back** — delivered early and exactly, in Chapter 68, and the refusal is not argued out of.
- **The cooperative steward board is named in the 19 May transcript, argued about, and refused in one sentence, and is not formed.**


## Reserved reveals — as of Chapter 70

- **The Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the community-watch term, the three-line brass tokens, Evan Mercer's counter-amendment and his second signature, and Iona Vey's preserved identity are all still unspent and were not touched in Chapters 49–59.** The Volume 02 outline forbids any root reading, any root clause and any succession in this volume.
- **The central root is still blank and was not asked after.** The notice on the enamel strip at the Old Ward board is unchanged and nothing in Volume 02 reissues it.
- **A priced-access case is not a recognition case.** A node with no term is not a person with no address, and Prue Calloway's 23 April finding — *a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate* — is the Volume 03 door and **must not be opened in Volume 02.**

## Chapters 71–80 established canon — VOLUME 02, THE HEAT (BATCH 0003)

### Calendar (authoritative; the anchor is unchanged and was not recomputed)

- Ch 71 = Tue 20 May into Wed 21 May. Ch 72 = Wed 21 May into Fri 23 May. Ch 73 = Thu 22 May and Fri 23 May. Ch 74 = Sat 24 May into Sun 25 May. Ch 75 = Tue 26 May into Wed 27 May. Ch 76 = Thu 28 May into Fri 29 May. Ch 77 = Thu 29 May into Fri 30 May. Ch 78 = Fri 30 May into Sat 31 May. Ch 79 = Sun 1 June into Mon 2 June. Ch 80 = Sun 8 June into Mon 9 June, ten in the morning.
- **20 May Tuesday · 21 May Wednesday · 22 May Thursday · 23 May Friday · 24 May Saturday · 25 May Sunday · 26 May Monday · 27 May Tuesday · 28 May Wednesday · 29 May Thursday · 30 May Friday · 31 May Saturday · 1 June Sunday · 2 June Monday · 3 June Tuesday · 9 June Monday · 12 June Thursday · 19 June Thursday (NEW, set in Ch 79) · 18 July Friday · 11 October Saturday · 12 October Sunday.** Counted from Ch 21 = Thursday 13 March. Never reconcile against the real calendar.

### The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol — canon

- **A4, council crest, enquiries number, pinned to the Old Ward board frame on the morning of Monday 19 May 2014 while the Committee sat. Activation on three consecutive days at 27°C or above. From the date of activation, residential care premises are required to confirm occupancy and a named responsible person for every address in their custody.** Smaller line, added after: *The threshold is measured at the borough's designated station. The designated station is the Saltmarsh Station, Fourteen Dock Road.*
- **ACTIVATED Monday 26 May 2014 at about nine in the morning.** Return date on the form: **4 August 2014** (printed on a laminated card in the civic offices as `HEAT PROTOCOL — ACTIVATED 26/05 — RETURN BY 04/08`, which Nina Calder put in a drawer on 2 June, correctly).
- **The threshold is measured at ONE station, the Saltmarsh Station, four miles out.** It is the only station in the borough with a continuous daily record since 1974, and Raynor-Hale has asked twice whether a second station could be named and was told that a threshold that moves is not a threshold. **There is no instrument in this city that records the temperature on a street.** Ellis Fenn, the basement printer, thirty-one, print cardigan, said it again on 20 May: the layer will draw you a shape and a term and a date and the claim under it, and it has never once drawn what a thing stopped doing, and **to know the temperature on the lowland you have to stand on it.** Raynor-Hale has taken about forty readings since Saturday 24 May with his own mother's thermometer at four o'clock in a car park and has nowhere to put them.
- **The definition of *residential care premise* is the 2009 heatwave wording: premises providing residential accommodation to persons who may require assistance with, or in respect of, their daily living.** Ravenscourt, thirty-one beds, Gantry Ward, inquest nine days, 2009; the recommendation was about temperature at night and not about water and not about who you telephone. **The Carrow Lane Housing Association is inside the definition because of a gas inspection in 2011 and not because of care.** Every line of the email that put it there is correct and Raynor-Hale has shown it to nobody because there is nobody to show it to.
- **The heat protocol and the Return of a Deferred Node are NOT the same instrument.** They are two forms with a crest on them, from two bodies that cannot make anybody answer, in the same building, asking one question in different words. The protocol's first box is a **number box** with a ruler-width line and no ceiling. Its second box wants a named responsible person for every address. The Return wants a state, a date, a party, and a blank rule at the bottom. **Asking for occupancy is asking for a state; a number is a list; a party that answers with a list has answered a different question.**

### Block C — state after 6 May, and the numbers that now exist (canon)

- **Block C: thirty rooms, 1938, thirty households. The internal riser died in February 1998 and was never replaced**; the fix was quoted and is in a file in a carrier bag; water for thirty rooms has come ninety yards from a standpipe at the end of the lane, in buckets, by the households, ever since. **It has never been in a letter.** The tag in the meter cupboard reads `STOPCOCK / 1998`. **The cupboard has no lock and never has, and there is no key, and the stopcock is reachable under a cover plate with four slotted screws and a plastic lifting handle, and it turns.**
- **The freeholder's agent isolated the supply on Tuesday 6 May 2014** under a letter of 29 April. **Twenty-two households moved out between 26 April and 23 May** into a hostel and two flats at Oyelaran's own expense (since 17 April for eleven households; she visited all of them on 22 May and one telephone does not work). **Eight households are still in Block C and they are the eight who have been carrying the water since 1998**, and since 6 May they have been carrying it for all thirty rooms, twice on Tuesday for the top landing. Ivy Marsden, seventy-two, asked for **one letter saying the buckets are ours** and was refused twice in sixteen years and is owed it. **Nadine Prentice, 29, the agent's clerk, fourteen months in post, wrote `stopcock accessible, no key, tag reads 1998` in her own file, unbidden, and said she will not be thanked and will not be blamed because the second is more likely. The agent's standing instruction of 14 April is that four meters come out of that cupboard.**
- **The Block C node is STILL on Tier 2 and has been since 11 March.** £4,200 a year is being paid for the continuity of a node with no water behind it. **The block is on its seventeenth day without water at 27 May and was still without it on 9 June.**
- **Oyelaran has not stopped the £2.60 on the eight households and will not, and she said so in a yard: refunding them would mean admitting in writing that a service in one of her four blocks has stopped, and a service that stops is a node that goes into failure, and a node in failure is a thing this city has a document about.** She said she would rather be hated for it than find out in August what she has done by stopping it, and Ivy Marsden said it was the first straight answer she has had about that money.
- **The plate in the Block C meter room has four lines: the tier-challenge line, the term-it-was-last-read-into line, the return-is-a-state line, and (new, Thursday/Friday 22–23 May) the seasonal-narrowing line.** The association's own log's last entry is `Stopcock found closed. No action. Unable to locate stopcock key. Housing Association informed 29/4.` The block's line on the return reads: state NO SUPPLY, since 06/05, who answers M. OYELARAN, CHIEF OFFICER.
- **THE SEASONAL NARROWING AT THE LOWLAND STANDBIPE.** For the season the borough narrowed the supply at the end of Carrow Lane. **The log says the restriction is lifted on 2 June at 06:00. The line on the form says 14 May at 06:00.** Neither man is lying; the two are not required to agree. Four people queueed at about half past ten on Monday 26 May for a tap with a forty-second cycle. **A narrowing is performed and entered and the entry is made in the form and in the log and the two are not required to agree.**

### The numbers that now exist in public, and the harm they did (canon, exact)

- **THE ASSOCIATION HAS ANSWERED THE HEAT PROTOCOL. On Thursday 28 May at about twenty past four in the afternoon, without Marguerite Oyelaran's knowledge and without her authority, the form was completed and posted by DENISE HOYLE, 61, who has done the letters and the arrears on a Tuesday and a Friday for six years in two rooms above a betting shop and is not an officer and has never been in a room.** She filled in two boxes, in biro, in the correct order: **`106`** in *number of occupied addresses*, and **`M. OYELARAN — CHIEF OFFICER — CARROW LANE HA — BY POST AND TELEPHONE`** plus the ring number in the second box.
- **106 = 128 rooms less the 22 households that have moved out. It is the first time a legible number about the association's stock has been in a public document.** The borough's own housing data for the same four blocks is **122 on the rent roll** (128 less the six who never signed). **The only difference the two public numbers show anybody is SIXTEEN: sixteen rooms on a rent roll with nobody living in them. That is a void, it is a landlord's exposure figure, it reads as bad management, and Oyelaran says so first: the legible difference is sixteen and sixteen is void and void is my fault.** The number the association is actually paid for is **92, not 122** (122 less Block C's thirty rooms on a void allowance since 6 May), and the shortfall is £4,361.60, and that figure is on no form and is nobody else's business. **106 minus 92 is fourteen: eight of those fourteen are the eight households still in Block C carrying water for thirty rooms since 1998, and six are the six who never signed, two of whom have told her to her face that they will not sign a thing that makes her name a door. Those are the only two numbers in the business anybody would recognise as hardship and they are the only two not on anything, because every document that could hold them is a list of who does not pay.** She will not publish them and has refused three times this week to give the breakdown of the sixteen. **The form is a public document on completion and was recorded at 16:20 on 28 May and the query and her answer both went on the file.**
- **Oyelaran's own figure line, unchanged: 128 × £2.60 a week = £135.20 a room a year = £17,305.60. 122 paying = £16,494.40 against a cost of £16,800. Shortfall £305.60. Block C's thirty rooms gone = £4,056. Shortfall £4,361.60.** Never to be run together with Wren Court's £11 → £13.60 and nineteen of thirty-one.
- **In the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane on Saturday 31 May, at about half past ten, with about forty people, Marguerite Oyelaran said THE FEE DOES NOT STOP, once, in about four minutes, having lost Block C and a quarter of a return and a whole afternoon.** She told the room there are **twelve** households who have asked to leave the agreement and that **twelve is a number and not a group, and she would not say which ones**, and she did not tell the room that clause 4(c) exists, and she said so in advance on the Friday. **The queue from 8 May and the queue from January are not the same room and not the same people, and both are this road.**
- **She raised £11,280 in that hall herself, on Saturday 31 May, in front of about forty people, at about twenty past eleven, and said the sixth sentence she could not finish: the minute says six lowland water points and nobody in this city can say which six, and if nobody can say which six the water points are, then nobody can say whose six they are either, and the chair who read the number out on 19 May did it without asking anybody, and she has not decided in nine days whether she can live with that. She was in the gallery and heard the chair read it; Jonas corrected the attribution in the hall — he wrote down a number she wrote first, a chair read it out, he did not choose the six, and he has never been able to find out who the six are.** She said it having been in the gallery on 19 May and heard the chair read it. **Jonas Mercer was at the back, said nine words in an hour and a quarter, said he did not choose the six and has never been able to find out who the six are, and was thanked by a man at the back for it, and the meeting continued.**

### The transfer that had to be repeated (Ch 74) — canon

- **Saturday 24 May, the lowland chamber.** Gauge on 6-L/16 drops four-tenths in twenty minutes at 08:11. The spur is on Tier 1 at £1,880, paid on 12 May by CivicCore with a power of attorney, so a restriction now has a stated time and a party to telephone. **ROYDEN ACHEBE IS THE HOLDER OF THE OUTFALL INSPECTION DUTY AND WAS NOT AT THE PLACE. He was forty minutes away in Gantry Ward with the 6-L/14 report in front of him. He said: *I cannot send it, the offer does not transfer, and the only door out is a successor who consents, so the duty waits, and I would be grateful if you did not do anything about it.* His seven-day window to offer the claim back expires 25 May and he used no minute of it.**
- **Jonas Mercer went down, looked, and DID NOT ENTER THE FAULT**, because entering a fault is an act by a person with standing and the person with standing was not there. **Emeka Rusk, 51, twenty-six years at the docks, entered the fault in his own hand with the operator box left blank, for the twenty-sixth time since 2 April.**
- **THE ATTEMPTED REPEAT TRANSFER WAS REFUSED BY THE MAN IT WAS FOR.** Emeka Rusk, in three reasons: if he holds it the £1,880 node is his and he cannot ring the company; he will not have Achebe handed a thing that has already been handed about once, and neither man asked him; and **a duty cannot be half-shared — if he holds it Achebe is out, and helping is what he has been doing and helping does not go on a form.** What he wanted instead: that the duty be recorded, and **he does not want it on a form.** He has had a month of thinking about it alone with nobody asking him questions.
- **THE REGISTRAR'S RULING, 24 May, Pell, by telephone at 20:20, eleven seconds, written down as he heard it and read back before it was typed:** *the record has no field for a duty that is held by one person and performed by another, and where a record has no field, the entry is made twice, and nobody is blamed.*
- **THE SECOND ENTRY, 24 May, series 6 lowland chain, Nina Calder's typing: duty HELD BY ACHEBE, R., holder not at the place, DUTY NOT MOVED; duty PERFORMED BY RUSK, E., in his own hand, operator box left blank, NOT A HOLDER, NOT A WITNESS TO THIS ENTRY; THE ENTRY IS MADE TWICE AND NOBODY IS BLAMED; moved by MERCER, J.; second witness NIL.** **Jonas Mercer's name is on that file a second time, at his own insistence, because Nina Calder told him she was going to put it there whatever he said and read it back to him before typing it. It is the second time in a month his name is on a record for two incompatible reasons and the file will not know the difference.**
- **ACHEBE'S OWN NOTE, heard on the telephone late on the night of Saturday 24 May and written down by Jonas Mercer on a page at about half past eleven that night, in his own words and not the man's, not in the shop book and not shown to anybody. Jonas wrote *corridor* in the margin first and struck it out, because it was a garden:** *in August somebody will ring him about a Tuesday, and it is Jonas Mercer, and the man who will ring him is me. And he has got until then to work out whether he is going to be the one who answers, and I have not got the right to be angry with him whatever he decides, because I asked him in a garden on the eighteenth for eleven hours of his life and he gave it, correctly. The only thing left in this account that is mine to do about it is to be the one who rings, and I am not going to pretend to anybody that I am calling about a screen.*

### The heat, the price chosen in daylight, and the batch's central beat (Ch 75) — canon

- **Saint Orra west's estate water came up out of a chamber on the north side at 03:11 on Monday 26 May and did not come back.** Node **6-51**, tank feed, chamber, meter, two tanks on the roof, in the borough asset register since 1974, holder field has held the estates office for eleven years and the estates office is three people and one is on leave until June. No fee, no tier, no restoration route. The borough's emergency crew got there at 11:20 — **eight hours** — and the fault was a gland nut done up in a hurry in 2011 and a rag in a strainer, and the fault box on Monday read DEBRIS and Jonas Mercer's instruction to the crew afterwards was: **write RAG, not DEBRIS, because debris means a design and there was no design.** Between about 04:30 and about 19:10 the domestic water in the hospital ran at about a fifth, and the reduction was performed correctly and entered correctly in two books, **and the two books agreed, for the first time in six weeks.**
- **TUESDAY 26 MAY, ABOUT TEN IN THE MORNING, IN DAYLIGHT, AT A TABLE, WITH A FORM: GUNNAR HALVORSEN SPENT £1,880 OF AN ACTIVITY BUDGET OF £2,140 TO PUT 6-51 ON A CONTINUITY SCHEDULE AT TIER 1 FROM 1 JUNE.** Three working days to activate; a restoration route, a stated time, a party to telephone. He gave the three alternatives and none of them is a softening: a tanker is about £600 a load with no line for it; requisitioning takes four days and a board that meets on Thursdays; do nothing is Monday. **He said, twice, that it is the only door in the room.** He was right and everybody in the building agreed he was right. **£260 is left. The 11:00–13:30 gap is still a gap. Priya is still two days a week until Saturday the thirty-first. A continuity fee buys a node and is not an hour of anybody's time, and he said so out loud, unasked, in advance, and said that if anybody in that building ever tells a member of staff that this fee has paid for a gap in a rota then they are lying and he will deny having heard it.**
- **HE DID NOT ASK JONAS FOR ANYTHING AND SAID SO TWICE, AND JONAS SIGNED NOTHING, INCLUDING NOT THE WITNESS LINE, BECAUSE HE WAS NOT A WITNESS AND WAS NOT ASKED TO BE ONE.** Halvorsen's standing rule, restated: *do not sit in that chair and decide you have found the pattern in it; I am not asking you for anything and I have never once in six weeks; sign the witness line if you are a witness, otherwise go and read your plate.*
- **THE BATCH'S CENTRAL BEAT IS PAID, DRAMATISED, NOT NARRATED, AND NOT TWICE.** It is paid by Jonas's doing nothing, correctly, and then sitting with why the doing-nothing was the whole of what he had. The recognition is Bea Osunde's, in a yard, and Jonas is given only one of her four sentences: *I have never once in four months been in a position where doing nothing was the correct thing and I have not got a single useful thing to say about it.* **He may not say the word *schedule* in his own head as a lesson and he does not.** The card's alternative home for the Section B recognition debt was Card 0078, and Card 0078 was used for the batch's second refusal instead. **It may not be paid again.**
- **EVERY REPAIR IN THE WEEK IS CORRECT AND THE DISTRICT STILL RUNS OUT OF WATER:** 6-51 restored and a stated time on a plate · Block C without a drop for a seventeenth day while £4,200 a year is paid for its node's continuity · six lowland points in their fifth week on a trickle, one of them the Weir stand, whose trap cannot be cleared by anybody, ever, and which in thirty-one degrees is the only thing anybody on that street wants. **A man said exactly that to Jonas Mercer on the lowland on Tuesday afternoon and Jonas did not offer anybody a pump and did not go up the path and did not telephone anybody.**
- **THE DECISION IN THREE DAYS: Thursday 29 May. Halvorsen has to put his name against the 11:00–13:30 gap out of £260.** He told Jonas the date on Wednesday afternoon and said he would not tell him which place he would not be in, and would let him find it from the transcript in about nine days. **That is Achebe's report day.**

### Tomas Renn's corridor question, 22 May (Ch 73) — canon, and it was not staged

- **Tomas Renn asked it in the shop on Monday 19 May, off-page, as he said he would, and Jonas looked for him in the first recess of the hearing and did not find him and did not telephone. He asked it again in a corridor at Saint Orra west on Thursday 22 May at about eleven in the morning, to Ruth Nwosu, Ezra Mbeki, Bram Osei and Priya Ranganathan, with the date on it: the eleventh of October, ten o'clock at night, the handover, and if two of the four say no.**
- **Jonas Mercer was at the River Stacks shop on 22 May and did not go into the west site. He learned the shape afterwards from MINA OKAFOR, who was at the card table and was not in the corridor, on the telephone at 16:30, in ninety seconds and one fact: at 12:10 Ruth Nwosu put an A5 on the card table with the word `unscheduled` in capitals and nothing else on it, and said *if you see a man with a date on the back of his hand, put it in the box, and do not put a name on it*, and Mina put it up at 12:10 with who put it up and at what time and wrote that she did not know where it came from.** It is the only paper in that box in five weeks with no name on it. Mina's rule: she will not put anything in the box she could not hear.
- **Every one of the four gave a correct answer and none of them answered the question.** Ruth: she does not know and has never known and will not put it in writing. Ezra: nothing has ever happened, which is the problem. Priya: nobody has ever written it down, so it cannot be asked. Bram: *there is no rule and I am not going to be one — and stop asking us, because every time you ask one of us about a date one of us ends up on a rota because of the asking.* **THEY CANNOT ANSWER IT WITHOUT BEING ON IT.**
- **TOMAS COUNTED ONE, ON PURPOSE, OUT LOUD, IN A CORROR, IN FRONT OF FOUR PEOPLE: ELEVEN Thursday handovers from 6 March to 15 May performed by a day porter who is not on the roster, unpaid, uncounted, out of a shop diary and a porter's wage book, in nine minutes. The twelfth is 22 May. The registrar said in April there are about nine hundred hollow terms and nobody counts them.** He wrote it down. **Ezra Mbeki asked him in front of the other three not to write it down, and he wrote it down anyway, in a shop book, and has sent it to nobody and is not going to, and the cost is that a twenty-six-year-old man now knows a shop steward has him in a diary.** Tomas's own reckoning: the count is in one hand together with what it costs, and that is the first honest thing anybody has handed him since 18 April.
- **PRIYA ASKED FOR A SUNDAY IN OCTOBER TO BE PUT IN FRONT OF THE BOARD IN THE SAME BOX AS BRAM'S REFUSED THIRSAY, in the same words, with her name and a date — Saturday 11 October, the handover. BRAM OSEI SAID SHE MAY NOT.** *You may not put your name next to mine, because the second one on that board is not a second refusal, it is a reason for the first one to be looked at, and in about four months somebody is going to use her to get me, and she has got a daughter of eleven.* Tomas refused to put her name to it without her saying it twice, and told the board he would put it in front of them. **Nothing has been put in front of the board.**

### The 6-L/14 report, 29 May (Ch 77) — canon, and what it did and did not do

- **Filed 11:00 on Thursday 29 May 2014 in a room in Gantry Ward with two doors, with nobody from this account in it, which Royden Achebe and Jonas Mercer arranged on Wednesday in about nine words on a telephone.** Jonas Mercer is not a party and could not have the papers. Pell's standing rule from 18 April holds: a gallery is for people who want to listen, and if anybody puts a microphone on him the registrar will stand up and say he is not a party.
- **Eleven pages, nine hundred words, correct.** The chamber is not maintainable. The bank is not maintainable. **The works owed are four tonnes of stone and one sill beam: about £1,180, in one line, without argument.** And: **the burden of 6-L/14 should be released.** And the last page, in four lines: a burden is not a claim; a burden cannot be refused by the person who holds it; a burden may be released only as a claim, and a claim only to a successor who consents. The successors are the persons who would use the structure. **There is no party that answers for 6-L/14, no district, no number, no location of record, no holder, and there has not been one since 6 April. He is not permitted to name a successor. He has looked for a way to name a street and there is not one, and he has concluded the reason there is not one is the correct reason.**
- **THE REPORT CONTAINS NO RESIDENT OF THE WEIR AND NO PERSON OF ANY KIND.** And getting it to that state took three decisions in three rooms in eight weeks: **Pell's registrar's record of 18 April**, which read a condition line into a public record and put under it, for the file and not for any person, that the condition line on 6-L/14 was available to be recorded on 1 April and was not; **Achebe taking the interim post **in the Assembly on 1 April at two o'clock, standing up, after Jonas declined it in the room** — a department cannot hold a plate and a man can — and then, on 18 April, a registrar attaching 6-L/14 to that post, which he accepted by telephone at about 19:06 from a yard in Gantry Ward in nine words, having said he would rather have had it before he had the post, which he has now said twice**; and **six words written in eleven minutes in a records room on 14 March by a probationary grade three technician — `NON-CIVIC PLANT` — which the Reclamation Season review accepted, so that no civic claim attaches to the chamber or the bank.** Nobody has asked him to unwrite them and nobody in this city knows they are his. **No civic claim means no invoicee, so the £1,180 of real work in the report cannot be put on anybody's schedule. Nobody has asked him to unwrite the six words, and nobody in this city knows they are his except the two of them: Achebe lodged them on 22 March under Jonas's reporting officer number and has known since that day and has not asked him about it.**
- **THE REPORT NAMES A DUTY HELD IN A DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT BY THE SAME MAN.** Page nine, paragraph three: the outfall inspection duty, described at 6-L/16, transferred to the holder of this post on 17 May 2014, is not discharged by this report. **That duty is in Nina Calder's typing on the series 6 lowland chain file in a different building; this report is on the field services file in a drawer in a cabinet in a corridor in Gantry Ward. Neither file knows the other exists and there is no rule anywhere that anybody has to read both.**
- **THE ACCEPTANCE: the department will accept it at about level three — a tray, about eleven weeks, opened by somebody who has never heard of any of it, and the acceptance will be a signature. A burden is not a claim and a vacancy is a thing that has to be made. What the acceptance makes is a LINE: a line on a file with his name in it and `reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released` in the next column. Achebe's sentence: a line like that does not sit on a file for a year. Lines like that get found.** It was not, in the event, the 6-L/14 line that produced Nina Calder's sentence; it was the 2011 gap.
- **NINA CALDER'S SENTENCE, said on Friday 30 May at 13:10, unasked, in advance, once: the last time there was a gap in that file it was 2011; it got filled inside about four months by a man sent out from estates, and the thing that got him the job was being told the line was plant; he has, from about six things said near her in nine years without anybody meaning to say them, a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket under a tap. That is all of it. He is not a bad man. He is a good man and she has worked with him for nine years and has never been able to ask him.** She asked for nothing in return, said the arrangement with Jonas has been in place since April, and would not see the book.

### The second clinician, and the letter of 19 May (Ch 78) — canon

- **THE LETTER AND THE CONVERSATION WERE NOT IN CHAPTERS 60–70 AND ARE NOW ON THE PAGE. Friday 30 May at 08:10, in the corridor at Saint Orra west, on foot, in daylight, in front of about thirty people, because that is what Gunnar Halvorsen said he would do on 9 May.** He said the truth first: the letter went on his desk at 09:00 on Monday 19 May and he did not tell her on the 19th and has had eleven days to work out why, and the reason is that on the 19th he was about to be in a Committee room. He gave her the A4. **He said he was not asking her for anything, and that he had not asked her for anything since 9 May and was not starting at ten past eight on a Friday, and that he knew two things about her and was not pretending to know only one: that she would have said no and been right, and that she would have said yes and been right and would not have enjoyed it and it would have gone into a governance file with a paragraph number in eleven weeks.**
- **She said thank you for telling me, and then: it has been eleven days; and you did it on foot, in daylight, with the paper, which you said you would do; and I have been waiting eleven days to find out which of us was wrong about the other. And then: I am not thanking you. Those are the words. And the rule that stopped anybody from telling anybody anything is a good rule and a correct rule and it means nobody ever tells anybody anything, and the people it protects are the four people and not the twenty thousand.** And: I am not going to use you, or a letter, or a budget line, or a man with a register, in the room on 9 June, and you will not be in that room either, and I have thought about that a great deal since 9 May and have not told you.
- **He replied: I would not have come. She said: that is why I have been able to sleep.** He has not been thanked. Nobody telephoned her about it.
- **Priya Ranganathan worked her last shift on the funded line on Saturday 31 May and opened the door herself.** From 1 June the line is nothing. **On 9 June she asked for her name to stay on the record of who has held it: *in about two years somebody is going to ask whether the second clinician ever existed, and she did, and she was on it for eight weeks, and she was paid out of a coffee-and-cakes appeal from 2011, and I would like that in a document while there is still one person left who remembers the appeal.*** It goes in, unedited.

### The June reckoning, Monday 9 June (Ch 80) — canon

- **Review of the west clinic's own witness structure, level two, off the corridor, 10:00, Monday 9 June 2014. Chair: Dr Sanaa Iqbal, because she signed the renewal clause in the first week of April and is the only person who can chair it and she has not asked anybody. Nine people: herself; Ruth Nwosu, Ezra Mbeki, Bram Osei, Priya Ranganathan (four on that roster); Gunnar Halvorsen; Mina Okafor; Yvonne Amoah, ambulance duty officer, eleven years; Fiona Grieve, 52, trust governance and quality, who said she had no standing and spoke three sentences in the whole morning. Jonas Mercer sat at the back on a chair from the corridor with nothing in his hands, was given one question in advance by the chair, and used it on the decontamination log.**
- **The finding, in her words, to be typed by somebody else: THE RENEWAL CLAUSE REQUIRES TWO WITNESSES IN A CORRIDOR AT 22:00 AND CANNOT REQUIRE ANY PERSON TO BE EITHER. IT HAS NOT FAILED THROUGH DRAFTING. IT HAS FAILED THROUGH THE ABSENCE OF A ROTA. A PRACTICE OF UNPAID, UNSCHEDULED, VOLUNTARY WITNESSING HAS HELD A CARE ENTRANCE OPEN AND IS NOT A SERVICE AND CANNOT BE MADE INTO ONE BY ANY INSTRUMENT HELD BY THE BOROUGH, THE CONTRACTOR, OR ANY PERSON. THE THIRD TIME A SURGE BREAKS THIS, A SCHEDULE WILL BE WRITTEN, IT WILL BE A GOOD SCHEDULE, AND IT WILL BE ADOPTED. The 11:00–13:30 gap is published and unfilled. The refused Thursday is on the board in the words of the person who refused it. Neither is to be altered by this review.**
- **She refused to defend it and said so in the first thirty seconds, and gave the other half first, before the finding: **The forty hours are the failure, and she says so first, in those words.** 06:00 Tuesday the fifteenth of April to 22:00 Wednesday the sixteenth, 2014: a woman of eighty-four in bay six for the whole of it, a man of eighty-one who wanted his own sheets, about forty other people, and she signed the clause. **And eight days later, on the night of Wednesday the twenty-third of April, the same structure did the other thing.** Not despite. Because of., and not despite but because of it, and the forty hours were not for anybody in that room. A witness arrangement is a promise made by people who are tired at ten at night, it is the only kind of promise anybody in this city has been able to make, and anybody proposing to replace it with something not made by tired people at ten at night has not read April.** She has read all four hundred and sixty pages and says page nine is a good sentence and that it is the sentence that has made a person never in the way, and that she would rather have the practice than the service, because the practice can be refused and the service cannot.
- **THE DEEPEST REFUSAL IN THE BATCH IS HERS AND IT IS THE FINDING.** There is a steriliser on level zero running on £410 and a logged weekly verification of twenty minutes a week. The supplier's regime is signed by her. The supplier will take a signature from any named member of staff. **Since 9 May the log has carried no name and no signature and the verification has not happened once.** If it does not happen before the quarterly visit in July there is a file with no signature in it and a compliance paragraph in the autumn, with a paragraph number, in front of four hundred and twenty women. **She could do it and did not, and said so, and gave the reason: if her name is against twenty minutes a week, then twenty minutes a week has got a person on it, that person is a doctor, and she is the one already in the building at eleven at night and seven in the morning, and then it is a rota with one name on it, and a rota with one name on it is what everybody has been refusing for four months, and Bram's Thursday is a rota with one name on it and he asked for it to be left in exactly those words. And both of those refusals are the failure and neither is cowardice.**
- **Yvonne Amoah, minuted: that is the first time in eleven years I have heard a doctor say *I could do it* and then not do it.** Mina Okafor wrote it down.
- **Bram Osei, for the minute, having said nothing for eighty minutes: he has refused that Thursday since the first week of March because he could not do it and could not say why; it is a bad refusal; he would rather be the bad one on the wall than part of a good one on a form; and the second refusal anybody puts on that board will be asked about it.**
- **The one question Jonas Mercer asked, and the answer he got: the decontamination log. Was there a name on it. No. Was there a name on any of them. No — not one signature since 9 May, and there is no facility on the log for a name, and the machine logs its own chamber temperature on a strip that goes in a drawer, and the weekly verification is a person looking at the strip and writing their name under it. At the quarterly visit in July the supplier will ask for the log, and if there is no log there is a non-conformance, and a non-conformance has a paragraph number, and a paragraph number is on a public compliance report in the autumn.**
- **What she asked for is a record, not a rota: a document with a date saying what the five lines are and when they are read and by whom and what a person may refuse and what happens when a person refuses, and saying in its own fourth line that it is not a service, that it is unpaid, and that any proposal to make it a service must come back to that table. Fiona Grieve's three sentences: it goes on the governance file today, it is not a compliance document and must not be produced as one, and the fourth line is to be copied word for word into anything that describes this clause in any other document in this city, without amendment and without a summary.**
- **The terms are still six days. The gate still runs on its local term. The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled. Bram's Thursday is still on the board. Jonas Mercer did not renew anything and brought nothing.**

### The cooperative steward board — named a second time and refused, and NOT FORMED (Ch 79)

- **Section 11 of the 2008 continuity regulations, found by FRANCES TOLLEY, committee clerk of twenty-two years, on Friday 30 May in about an hour and a half, copied out by hand because she did not trust herself not to improve it. It is PERMISSIVE — *may be constituted* — and it is not a duty on anybody. A body of that kind is constituted with NINE SEATS: one for each service district drawn by the layer, and a NINTH, for a person who answers for a service and is not a body, who is not a veto, is not a chair, cannot be removed by the other eight, and is written as *a person who answers for a service and is not a body*. It is called A COOPERATIVE STEWARD BOARD. She did not put it to the chair. She put it on a table on Monday 2 June with fourteen returned forms and nowhere to put them, and she said that is the whole of the reason: a return with nowhere to go is a return that has not been made, and there is no provision in the Civic Assembly standing orders for a required answer that arrives with nowhere to go.**
- **OREN PELL REFUSED IT IN ONE WORD AT A VOLUME THAT MADE A WOMAN TWO FLOORS DOWN LOOK OUT OF A DOOR, AND SAID HE WAS NOT THE MAN WHO KILLED IT, AND THIS IS THE BEST THING HE SAYS IN THE VOLUME.** *It is page nine of Schedule 4. A tier may not be challenged by a person. A body of nine seats has one power: it may require a party holding continuity to answer, receive the answer, and be asked to consider a matter. It cannot require a person to do anything, hold a duty, or answer a question, and there is no appeal from it to anybody, and the reason there is no appeal is page nine. So the ninth seat is a chair. Not a chair with a person in it, which is what a person always is. A chair with a person in it that nobody can ask a question. A steward board whose ninth seat cannot be challenged is not a steward board. It is a register with chairs. And every one of the four hundred and sixty pages of that schedule is a register with chairs, and it has been in force since 1 March, and a chair on 7 May said the opposite of this in public and said she could not say where she got it from.*
- **He asked Frances Tolley to put his job title next to it. He was right — she wrote it down — and the refusal is in the record, and it is not the registrar's ruling and it is not minuted and no sitting has taken place.** The chair's position, given by a member's aide on the telephone from the Old Ward and read out: **the chair will not constitute a body at all on the strength of a permissive power found in a corridor on a Friday afternoon, and will say so in writing if anybody puts it in writing.** Pell: then write it down, that is the second useful thing anybody has done in this room in nine days and it has been done by a woman reading down a telephone.
- **THE THIRD LIST, NINA CALDER, Friday 1 June, in a pad, stopped before anything was written on it: a register of attachment with one name on it, a register of narrowings on a public board with fourteen on it, and a proposed board with nine seats and a person in the ninth. Three lists in one city. A list is not a record. A record is about one thing at one time. A list is a set of things somebody intends to go on adding to, and the intention is the danger.** She would not be the fourth.
- **What would have to be true, said once, at 12:10 on Monday 2 June, to the table, with two people in the room, and it is not a question and it was not answered: for nine seats to be anything other than nine chairs, one of the nine has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there. That is the whole of it. There is no second half.** She asked anybody who thinks she has got it wrong to say so in writing and it will go in the folder with their name on it, and she will not answer it. She will be there on Thursday 19 June at nine o'clock with a folder.
- **THE BOARD WAS NOT FORMED. NOTHING WAS CONSTITUTED. NOBODY HAS BEEN CHARGED WITH MAKING IT EXIST.**

### The determination with a deadline and no date — fact, not speech

- **For eight days after 19 May the city had a public obligation with no clock on it.** The chair required every party holding continuity to answer a Return, in public, on the form, by the next sitting. **There is no next sitting. Nobody in the building could date it; two offices said *after the recess* and one said nothing at all, which is the same answer with more conviction. The obligation was discharged by the act of answering and not by anybody receiving it. Nobody was in breach of anything and nobody will ever be told that they are not. There are fourteen returned forms: eight on Nina Calder's form, four on the borough's, two on both — and the two on both answer for the same meter room and neither knows the other existed, and that is the position since 18 April and it did not resolve itself.**
- **AT 11:20 ON MONDAY 2 JUNE A MEMBER'S AIDE RANG FRANCES TOLLEY BACK AND GAVE HER A DATE: THE NEXT SITTING IS THURSDAY 19 JUNE 2014, TEN IN THE MORNING, COMMITTEE ROOM TWO. The returns are to be read into the record as they are received. The question of the term *holder* is on the papers if and when a definition is before the Committee, and the chair has said she will not arrange one.** Frances Tolley's note, unedited: *nine days ago that determination had no date on it and it was not a deadline and the fourteen of us had complied and nobody was in breach and nobody would ever be told. And this morning it has a date, and the only thing that has happened is that I telephoned an office at eleven o'clock and asked a question I have been allowed to ask since 1992. That is not a finding. I am going to write the two of them on the same piece of paper, one above the other, with the date on both, and put it in the front of the folder.*
- **Ronan Vasquez, 48, estates office, Saint Orra west, eleven years, brought his own answered form to the table to have it recorded that he answered it in nine minutes on 8 May, and said a form which cannot be answered in nine minutes is asking about something else.**

### The Weir, 1 June — canon, and what did not happen

- **Jonas Mercer went up the path at about eleven on Sunday 1 June on foot, unasked, not a party, with nothing in his hands and nothing to do, having spent thirteen days not going because not going was the correct thing. He did not put the sheet on the table and did not read it out. He told Margo Doré the whole of the 19 May minute in four minutes, in the order it happened, with the source and the hand and the date.** She asked him to say the number again and made him say it again.
- **She said two things in a fixed order. One: you have not done it again and you have done it worse — you promised me a sentence and a fortnight, you kept the sentence, and you used it on my water in a room without me, and I have decided I do not want to know whether anybody in this city would ask me a question before asking a question about me, because the answer is going to be no and I have had it four times and this morning it came with a price on it in your mouth. Two, and she wanted it said out loud in a kitchen: I am not angry with you, because you did the thing you said you would do, I would have said no, my no would have been eleven words and would not have been in a minute, and in September the sentence on the paper will be mine and it will have been in a room.** She poured two cups, which she has not done since 30 April, and did not offer him any information about the six, and he did not ask.
- **SHE REFUSED A PUMP IN ADVANCE, IN HER OWN WORDS, IN THE KITCHEN, UNARGUED, AND NOBODY ARGUED.** A man will come up that path in about two months with a van and a clipboard and a scheme and a photograph of a stand, and he will be correct, and the water will come; and she is going to say no to him, and the reason is that a pump is a thing and a thing can be bought, and the day this street has a pump somebody is going to ask what it is attached to, and the answer will be a paper, and the paper will have one of her neighbours' names in a field on it, because that is how any of them has kept a door open since 3 April. **NO PUMP WAS OFFERED IN THIS BATCH AND NONE EXISTS.**
- The three positions stand on the wall with a name against each and a fourth line with a pencil ring round a space with nothing in it, not gone over since 14 March. **Nobody was added to the rota; there is no rota. Bo Trimble, seventeen, came in, said there is no water, was told he is not going to fix it, said right, and went home. Nothing was added to him. He is findable at four hundred yards in a lane and was already findable.**
- **THE THREAD IS NOT RESOLVED AND WAS NOT RESOLVED BY AN EXPLANATION AND A FORGIVENESS. What was resolved is that she has the fact and has had it since 20 May, and he has been told what it cost her, and nobody has asked her for anything.**

### The four Lattice notices of Chapters 71–80

- Ch 72, Carrow Lane meter-room plate: `WHERE A SERVICE IS NARROWED FOR A REASON OF SEASON, THE NARROWING IS PERFORMED AND ENTERED, AND THE ENTRY IS MADE IN THE FORM AND IN THE LOG, AND THE TWO ARE NOT REQUIRED TO AGREE.`
- Ch 74, outfall plate 6-L/16: `WHERE A DUTY IS HELD BY A PERSON AND THE PERSON IS NOT AT THE PLACE, THE DUTY DOES NOT MOVE. IT WAITS. AND A DUTY THAT WAITS IS STILL A DUTY AND STILL UNPAID.`
- Ch 76, Old Ward board, enamel strip: `A PARTY THAT HAS BEEN ASKED TO CONFIRM OCCUPANCY HAS BEEN ASKED FOR A STATE AND NOT FOR A LIST, AND A PARTY THAT ANSWERS WITH A LIST HAS ANSWERED A DIFFERENT QUESTION.`
- Ch 79, 6-L/14 plate at the top of the Weir slip: `WHERE TWO INSTRUMENTS DESCRIBE ONE PIECE OF GROUND AND NEITHER KNOWS THE OTHER EXISTS, BOTH OF THEM ARE TRUE, AND THERE IS NO RULE ANYWHERE THAT ANYBODY HAS TO READ BOTH.`
- **Four notices in ten chapters, at most one each, and none of them defines *holder*. The condition about a heat protocol's requirement of a named person for an address was NOT printed and no plate was manufactured carrying it. The Ch 46 ceiling was not repeated.**

### New named figures, Chapters 71–80

**Gareth Raynor-Hale** (already), resilience officer, who is the person the enquiries number belongs to in practice, who has taken forty readings with his mother's thermometer and has nowhere to put them, and who will not show the email to anybody because there is nobody to show it to. **NADINE PRENTICE**, 29, the freeholder's agent's clerk, fourteen months in post, who wrote `stopcock accessible, no key, tag reads 1998` in her own file unbidden. **IVY MARSDEN**, 72, Block C room 14, who has carried the water for thirty rooms since 1998 and wants one letter. **DELE FASHANU**, Block B, nineteen years, who told Jonas in a doorway that the last three men who came into that hall worked for somebody and he has not got a list, a hat or a photograph. **DENISE HOYLE**, 61, two days a week, six years, not an officer, who completed the heat protocol return without authority and without knowledge. **RONAN VASQUEZ** (already), 48, who answered a form in nine minutes and brought it to the table. **FRANCES TOLLEY** (already), who found section 11 in an hour and a half. **ELLIS FENN**, 31, the basement printer, print cardigan, extractor broken since February, who said the true thing about the drawing layer for the fourth time in six weeks. **FIONA GRIEVE**, 52, trust governance and quality, who said three sentences in the whole of 9 June. **YVONNE AMOAH** (already), 41, ambulance duty officer, who minuted *I could do it* and then not doing it. **MILO** was not used and is not a character in this novel. **There is only one Marguerite, one chair called Fenella Marsh, one clerk called Frances Tolley, one printer called Ellis Fenn, one records supervisor called Nina Calder, one deputy registrar called Oren Pell, one service manager called Gunnar Halvorsen and one Dr Sanaa Iqbal in this account. Do not create a second of any of them.**

## Status

**Chapters 1–48 are complete prose and Volume 01 is closed. Chapters 49–80 are complete prose and Volume 02 is open at Chapter 80. Section C of `outline/volume-02.md` is delivered.**

Jonas Mercer is Stage 2, thirty-two, probationary grade three with no appointment and no cabinet, the issuer line on a live plate at a hospital door, three bounded civic edits done at hinges he does not own, a seam mark past the elbow, a numb right hand, **his name on a second file in Nina Calder's typing for a duty he refused to move, a register entry in his own name that no person can release, and a woman on the Weir who has told him in her own kitchen that she is not angry with him and that is the whole of what he has got.**

The heat has been and gone and it did what weather does. It activated a public-health notice that asks a landlord for a state and hands her a number box, and the number that went in is one hundred and six, and it cost a chief officer an afternoon and put six households into a public document as a difference between two legible figures. It cost a hospital eight hours of water and bought a plant a stated time and left a gap in a rota exactly where it was. It cost thirty rooms a seventeenth day without water while £4,200 a year was paid for their node's continuity. It cost a trap its last chance, and a street its last chance, and a man in a kitchen the fact that a price on six water points is in a minute and she was not asked.

And the thing the volume turns on has been said out loud in a room by the only person in this city who is paid to say things: **the third time a surge breaks a clause that depends on a rota, a schedule will be written, and it will be a good schedule, and it will be adopted.** Nobody has a rota. A service manager with £260 has a decision on Thursday the twenty-ninth of May. A committee's determination had no date on it for eight days and then had one, because a clerk of twenty-two years telephoned an office. And a board with nine seats and a person in the ninth one was named, and refused, and the reason it was refused is a sentence that has been in the document since the first of March and has been read at four people in four rooms this month and is the best sentence in it.

**A deadline with no date on it is not a deadline. A determination that requires a city to answer a form is discharged by the act of answering and not by anybody receiving the answers. Nine seats with a person in the ninth is a register with chairs. The next sitting is Thursday 19 June 2014, and a Sunday in October is the eleventh of it, and it is a Saturday night, and no field on any plate says so.**
