## CANON ADDED IN CHAPTERS 81–90 (Section D — Revocation) — binding on every later volume

### The chain, every link, and who signed what

**THE VALE CLIMATE CLAIM, reference `T3/11400/0304`. Every link is valid, nothing was forged, nobody lied, and every assignment was made by a person with authority on a day when they had authority.**

1. **4 March 2014 — placed.** CivicCore Limited, acting under a standing continuity authorisation, placed a claim at **Tier 3, £11,400 a year**, on node **KH-4/19**, the Vale Clinic, a private clinic with eleven patients and a day room on a private road in Kestrel Hill. Signed by a **power of attorney and not with a name**. The reference is the tier, the annual fee and the date of placement, and it never changes.
2. **21 March 2014 — assigned whole.** CivicCore assigned the entire claim to **the ALDBROOK TRUST**, freeholder of the building, for value. The Aldbrook Trust is a Kestrel Hill freehold trust; about nine exist in the borough.
3. **2 May 2014 — assigned one-half.** The Aldbrook Trust assigned one-half of the claim to **the HALLIWELL AMENITIES FUND**, a Tideglass fund, **trustee DOROTHY ALDERMAN, 71**, sole trustee, eleven years in post, retired school caretaker, who paid for it and thought it good value. **The assignment was made by CERI VAUGHAN, 45, continuity counsel, for the fund.** Consideration: proper and not recorded.
4. **27 May 2014 — assigned one-half onward.** The Halliwell Fund assigned its half to **the Salter's Yard managing agent**, **BEVERLEY ANNIS, 54, principal, twenty-six years in post, four hundred and ten flats**. Consideration: **£600.** A half of £11,400 is £5,700 a year; the price is about a tenth, and the reason is that **a share of a claim is a share of a place in a queue**, and a place in a queue you will never need is worth a tenth of nothing.
5. **3 June 2014 — entered, not assigned, and signed by nobody.** Beverley Annis went into a meter cupboard under the stairs at Wren Court with a torch and read the plate — `THE HOLDER OF THIS POINT PAYS FOR IT. THE POINT IS NOT THE PAYER'S.` — and entered **the named steward of the building** into the holder field in her own name, because the point is not the payer's. **She was correct, she had authority over her own firm's node, and she had never spoken to the woman, and she has not slept about it.**

**The last link in the chain is not a company. It is a sentence on a plate read by a woman with a torch.**

### What a valid assignment transfers, and what it does not

**Schedule 4, page 219, eleven lines, and it is the only instrument in this city that caused what happened next.** A claim placed under the Schedule may be assigned, in whole or in shares, to a party having authority in relation to the node. An assignment takes effect on execution, does not create a new claim and does not create a new reference, and the assigning party remains the party that placed the claim. **The assignee takes the claim, and takes what is owed on the claim, and takes the duty of the claim.** A share of a claim carries the tier in proportion. **A share of a claim is enforceable by the assignee against the assigning party in respect of the tier only** — everything else travels down the chain and none of it comes back up, and there is no clause about the bottom of a chain, and the Schedule does not require anybody to look down one.

**Derived and now canon, said by Ceri Vaughan in a room in Kestrel Hill on 23 June: a share of a claim is a share of a precedence, not a share of the work. A duty moved four times got lighter four times, and no person in the chain was ever given the job of maintaining the point. And nobody in the chain has ever asked what happens when the person at the end says no, because a tier may not be challenged by a person, and a person cannot be required even to be told.**

### The node with a person in its holder field

**METER ROOM 3, SALTER'S YARD, TIDEGLASS — a building with no civic address.** Party holding continuity: **the named steward of the building, a person**, entered 3 June 2014. Tier 3. Claim reference `T3/11400/0304`.

### THE 19 JUNE SITTING — Thursday 19 June 2014, 10:00, committee room two — exact wording

Chair Fenella Marsh. Members Sore, Imre, Hargreaves, Petrie Okonjo, Denman. Oren Pell in attendance. Frances Tolley at the clerk's table. Alden Rook and Ceri Vaughan for CivicCore. Gallery: forty-one folding chairs and about fifty standing from 11:15. **Jonas Mercer was in the gallery, on the left at the back, with nothing in his hands; a member's aide said into a microphone at the chair's request at 09:45 that he was not on the agenda, not a party, not a member and not a departmental officer, and the minute records it. He asked ONE question, in nine words.**

It was a **determinations meeting on fourteen returns and not a hearing about a price**, and every single thing said in it was correct.

**THE TWO ENTRIES, as read into the record, verbatim:**

> **METER ROOM 3, SALTER'S YARD, TIDEGLASS. Party holding continuity: THE ALDBROOK TRUST, Kestrel Hill, as freeholder of the fee payer, one-half. Tier 3. Claim reference T3/11400/0304. Answer received 6 June 2014.**

> **METER ROOM 3, SALTER'S YARD, TIDEGLASS. Party holding continuity: THE NAMED STEWARD OF THE BUILDING, named in the form as received. Tier 3. Claim reference T3/11400/0304. Answer received 16 June 2014.**

**The minute now contains two entries for one meter room that cannot both be the answer. There is no procedure in the standing orders for correcting a minute in a sitting. The one procedure that exists requires the Committee to be satisfied that it has been misled, and the Committee has not been misled — it was read to, which is the correct finding. Both entries stand permanently and neither can be amended.**

**THE CHAIR'S ANSWER TO THE QUESTION, in the minute, in her words:** *No. And I will not require a person at that meter room, any more than I would require one at six water points on a lowland, and I have asked for that to be minuted twice in this city already and I am asking for it three times. A tier may not be challenged by a person. A person is not a party. A person may not be required to do anything, and this is the part that took me four months — a person may not be required even to be told. There is no instrument in this Committee, in the department, or in Schedule 4 that will put a telephone call in my hand, and the honest position is that the holder of that field is a woman who has not been told that she is holding it, and this Committee cannot tell her, and that is not a failure of this Committee, it is the design of a document that has been in force since the first of March.* And then: **If that person has not been told, then somebody in this city is going to tell them, and it is not going to be this Committee.** Pell, without opening his folder: **the records office is required to.**

**THE 19 MAY DETERMINATIONS — what was complied with and what failed.** (1) The Return of a Deferred Node was RECEIVED, not adopted: complied with, unchanged. (2) Every party holding continuity required to answer in public by this sitting, no-party answers to be recorded as no party: **complied with — fourteen returns in by 18 June. The six lowland water points are absent from the fourteen, and their absence is compliance, not failure, because a node with no party is not a party and was never required to answer.** The failure is structural: **the two answers for one meter room, and the fact that the person at the end of the chain is the only party in this city who did not answer and was never told she was required to.** Sore's outstanding request for a return of schedule commitments was re-entered and the chair put no date on it; the minute records that, and Nina Calder said from the public seats that a request with no date is a request with no clock, and the chair said she knew.

**THE ANSWER TO PAGE NINE, 19 June:** there was none, and there could not be. **The Committee recorded that page nine was not before it, that no challenge was made by any party, and that a challenge cannot be made by a person.** Nothing was resolved about the word *holder* and no definition was before the Committee.

**THE METHOD, stated aloud in public for the first time, by Oren Pell, registrar:** *A price is a statement about what a thing costs. A chain is a statement about who is standing behind the price. The Committee cannot unmake a price and it has no power to and it should not try. What the Committee can do is name the chain in public and put the last person in it into a position where somebody has to ask her a question. Every one of the last four months has been spent on the price. Nobody has read the chain. That is not a scandal. It is an ordering, and it was the correct ordering until today, because there was nothing in the chain to read.*

### THE REVOCATION — what was done, on what ground, and what it cost

**Ground: the consent of the last holder, under section nine of the 2008 regulations, which permits a person to withdraw a claim held in their own name at a counter without notice to any other party and without any formality. The chain was BROKEN IN THE MIDDLE, not at the end, and it was broken against the last holder's consent and against nobody else.**

**Pell's ruling of Monday 30 June 2014 at about ten past nine, binding on every transfer in this series:** *A mending may be performed at a hinge the issuer does not own, provided a person or group with local standing instructs it. Where the man who found the chain is also the mender, the standing must be given again — aloud, in the room, after the chain is known. Twice. The second time is the one that is entered.* It is the **third** ruling of the year that is a door and not a wall, and the registrar says so and says he is tired since 11 June. **The fourth is the ruling of Wednesday 9 July 2014 and Pell telephoned the shop on Monday 7 July to say that it was the one he had been putting off.**

**Sequence, exact.** First standing: **Monday 30 June 2014, 10:00**, in the workshop under Wren Court, six people present, **four lines** on a sheet of A4 in her own hand, and the four lines read *I have read **three** assignments and I understand what the claim is and I understand that a claim is a duty and I am the duty* — including *I do not want it replaced with anything, and I am not authorising a registrar to substitute a party.* — **Tuesday 1 July, 11:00**, Jonas Mercer refuses to mend at the hinge, in a cupboard, out loud, to nobody, and does not open the tool roll. — **Wednesday 2 July, 09:04**, Pell reads the four lines and **enters nothing**, and records the date of the refusal on the record. — **Wednesday 2 July, 23:00**, the **second** standing, aloud, in the workshop, **twenty-nine people present**; two were at a hospital with a relative and one of them was on a drip. — **Thursday 3 July, 09:00**, the entry is made, and the second statement goes into a registrar's record **in her own words, unedited.** — **The term ran to 06:00 Friday 4 July 2014.** The node is not on anything and is not replaced with anything.

**COST, in the attachment to the Return of a Deferred Node, public on the board from Monday 7 July, nine items, and the one name on it is MERCER, J.:**
1. It is correct. The chain is broken, the Tier 3 is off the node, the block's fee did not stop, and six lowland water points are no longer outranked by a block of thirty-one rented rooms.
2. **One day and seven hours** — **11:00 Wednesday 2 July**, the second standing, **to 06:00 Friday 4 July** — of somebody at a stand at the bottom of Quarry Street carrying water, and it is the tail of a longer window: **the whole delay is three days and twenty hours from 10:00 Monday 30 June to 06:00 Friday 4 July, and one day and twenty-three hours of that is Jonas Mercer's, and the last one day and seven hours of it is a term that no power in this city can shorten.** The form records the tail and says which end of it is which, **unnamed, and the man who cost it has never had her name and did not ask for it.** He says so in the form and says that a woman in Salter's Yard has been asked for a list of four people twice and said no twice.
3. £2,431.16 out of a workshop, which is a saving and is not a win.
4. **A riser.** The block's node, off any schedule, serves its maintenance, and its maintenance is a riser last looked at in 2011, and the water is what it was in April, and eleven households were in that building on a lapsed term on 17 April and have now been told.
5. **KH-4/20, left at Tier 2.** The residential floor of the Vale Clinic, placed by the Aldbrook Trust on **29 May 2014 at £4,200 a year**, term to **06:00 Monday 4 August 2014**, eleven patients and a day room above it. Nobody has re-priced it, nobody is required to, and when it lapses it serves its maintenance. The trust's finance director has said in writing he will not seek a replacement from anybody at the Salter's Yard address and **has not been asked about his own building.**
6. **A minute that cannot be corrected** — the two entries for Meter Room 3.
7. **The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol return is due again on 4 August 2014** from a body whose first answer cost a clerk of sixty-one an afternoon and put a chief officer's name in a public document. Jonas has not telephoned that chief officer since 31 May.
8. **NINETY DAYS' NOTICE. Served at 11:00 on THURSDAY 3 JULY 2014, on the day the entry was made, on the Aldbrook Trust, the Halliwell Amenities Fund, CivicCore Limited and two unnamed parties. Expires WEDNESDAY 1 OCTOBER 2014, and ninety days from the third of July is the first of October and no other date is the expiry.** The clause (Schedule 4, annex six): *A withdrawal takes effect against the party that placed the claim on the date of the entry. Against every other party in the chain it takes effect ninety days after service of notice. On and after that date the placing party may re-assert the claim, or any part of it, at the tier, in respect of the node, and may do so without the consent of any person whose name appears in the holder field of that node at any time before that date.* **The placing party has not noticed yet. The last answer in the chain is a trust in Kestrel Hill: a machine answered the letter on Friday 4 July and a person answered it on Monday 7 July, and the person is sixty-three and she signed the assignment of the second of May.**
9. **One name: MERCER, J., LAST WORKING SYSTEM ADMINISTRATOR, CITY OF NACRE.** Civic standing, no grade, no probation, not subject to review, released by no person. He filled the form in, on a public counter, with his name in the box that asks who is putting it in, and it is the first time his name has gone on a public civic form, and it is deliberate, and Nina Calder says so out loud.

### THE REGISTRAR'S RULING OF WEDNESDAY 9 JULY 2014 — the fourth ruling of the year, a door and not a wall

> **A claim discharged for the holder is discharged for the holder. It is not discharged for the party that placed it, and it is not discharged for any party that assigned it, and it is not discharged for the party that received the assignment.**
>
> **The person who caused the arrangement may be findable. Findability is not answerability. There is no power in this city by which the fact that a person can be located can be converted into a liability for what they arranged, and no registrar may convert it, and no Committee may convert it, and it is not converted by the fact that a person has said a thing out loud in a room and been right.**
>
> **Nothing in this determination exonerates any party. It records that the discharge of a claim runs one way: from the person who holds it outward to nothing at all, and not from the person who caused it back to anybody.**

**With it, in the same room, and said and not minuted because they are not rulings: there is no mechanism in this city to make the top of a chain pay, and there is no mechanism in this city to make a person take a duty, and those are the same sentence, and it is not a hole in the law, it is what a law is for.** Who pays for the refusal: **nobody.** There is no mechanism, and there has not been one since 2008, and this is the transfer-stewardship cost named in law rather than in feeling, and it is a cost and not an absolution.

**And, in a corridor, in nine words, and mentioned once and not again in this batch: there is a regulation about this, it is 2008, it is section eleven, and Frances Tolley has a copy of it in her own hand, and it has been read in the registrar's office twice and neither time by anybody who could act on it.** Pell: *I have no power to constitute anything. The chair has no power to constitute anything. You have no power to constitute anything and you are not a body and there is no instrument in this city that would let you be.*

### WREN COURT, SALTER'S YARD — the batch's payment, and it is recorded

**WREN COURT IS TOUCHED, late in the batch, in a room Fofana runs and Jonas Mercer is not in, across three evenings, Wednesday 2 to Friday 4 July 2014, in about six pages. It is not a win and it is not a loss and it is not a list.**

- **Wren Court, Salter's Yard, Tideglass.** Thirty-one rented rooms over a furniture workshop, **no civic address**, named steward **YOLANDA FOFANA, 54**, nineteen years in the block and fourteen as steward, a former ward sister at a Tideglass hospice. Fee **£11 → £13.60 a week**. **Nineteen of 31 signed on 12 March 2014. Four have missed a month. Eleven of the nineteen were in the building on the night of 17 April and all eleven were on a lapsed term, and the water was at half, and the steward had not told them and had told the block meeting on 8 April that there was a mains matter. Clause nine: disconnection for non-payment, sixty days' notice, one visit, a photograph of the meter.** **Fofana's own signature lapsed on 1 April 2014 and she has now told the block, in a room, over three evenings, having said in a workshop on 26 June that she was going to say it in three weeks' time.**
- **Her arithmetic, which she can defend line by line.** The tin is **19 × £13.60 = £258.40 a week = £13,436.80 a year**, and was **19 × £11 = £209 a week = £10,868** before 1 June. The block's share of the Tier 3 claim was **£2,850 a year (one quarter of £11,400) plus a £30 registration charge = £2,880 a year, charged at £72 a month**, and **the £72 a month was not being paid by anybody in the building — it was being charged to the water bill**, which had been about six hundred pounds a quarter short since 11 March. **Four charges. £2,431.16 short. Paid by the steward out of a joinery workshop. Never told to anybody until 30 June 2014.** The £72 stops on 4 July and goes back to a trust in Kestrel Hill and **none of it comes to the block, and the block's water is exactly what it was in April.**
- **The terms the thirty-one arrived at, on Wednesday 2, Thursday 3 and Friday 4 July. Nothing was signed. There is no minute and there is going to be none. (1) The fee does not stop — thirteen pounds sixty, into the same tin, on the same Wednesday, and when somebody asked for a receipt she said that a receipt is a document and a document is what twelve of them refused in March. (2) Clause nine is not tidied: read out in full four times and not repealed, not suspended, not rewritten; the four who missed a month are told in front of everybody that it is there and that the room cannot vote to remove it. (3) The twelve are not asked to sign and the four are not written down, and the room's answer to any request for names remains the address of the building. (4) The riser is about £900 and the tin cannot do it and the money that stopped does not come to the block, so the riser is two years away, and nobody is to be asked, because asking produces a list of who has got money. (5) Thirty-one people in a building with no address are not a body and cannot be seated, cannot vote, cannot require anybody to do anything, and cannot be challenged, because a tier may not be challenged by a person — and therefore they cannot be a steward board, and they did not try. (6) The third condition, restated by the people it protects: the fee does not stop, the water is not the point, and the arrangement is one person, findable, holding a thing she cannot be asked about by any of the thirty-one people whose water it is.**
- **Worse for some, better for others.** Better: eleven households are told, in a room, that they were on a lapsed term in April, and the four who missed a month are named by nobody and made a category by nobody, and a woman of thirty-one said she had known hers had gone off since April and had never minded because nobody had asked her. Worse: the steward has told twenty-nine people something she held for three months, and a woman in flat twenty-two has not been telephoned back since April and has stopped asking, and the riser is two years away, and the water is at half when the building is busy.
- **The only date anybody in that room can be given is the fourth of August 2014, and it is somebody else's return deadline** — the Borough Heat Threshold Protocol, seventy days after activation on 26 May, a laminated sheet on a wall in the Old Ward that nobody has read out loud, asking a residential place to confirm occupancy and a named responsible person for every address in its custody. Nobody in that room is a residential care premise. It is the only date the building has been given in four months and it is about a boiler.
- **NINETEEN AND TWELVE ARE BOTH GOING TO BE LEGIBLE BY THE END OF JULY** — a number box on a public form, the only box on it anybody will ever fill in, and a data set is when somebody else makes the list.

### Nina Calder's fourth page — the instrument

**She started it on Thursday 5 June 2014 and wrote nothing on it. On Friday 4 July at 20:20 she put it in the bin tray under her desk. On Monday 7 July at about 08:30 she took it out and wrote on it. It is the only piece of paper in that office that has been in a bin and been rescued.**

> **WEEK OF 30 JUNE TO 4 JULY 2014. FURNITURE WORKSHOP AND STAIRWELL, WREN COURT, SALTER'S YARD — a building with no civic address.**
> **Counted: three evenings of a meeting, and the numbers of people in the room, which were twenty-nine and thirty-one and thirty-one, and a second line under that which is that twenty-nine of thirty-one were also the twenty-nine of a second number, and the two numbers are not the same people, and this slip does not say which nineteen, and will not.** One person holding. Zero signatures. Zero minutes. One clause read out in full four times and not altered. Two people who were not in the room on the first evening because they were at a hospital, and both of them were in the room on the third, and one of them was on a drip.
> **This is not a register. There is no index, there is no name box, and nothing may be added to it. It is a record of one thing at one time. It expires when the thing changes and it is not carried forward. It is not a rota and it is not a service and it may not be required of anybody. A person may refuse to be counted and the count then says so and says how many refused.**

**She has a fifth one in her head and is not writing it down, and if she writes it down it becomes a scheme.**

### State of the standing items as of Friday 18 July 2014

- **THE SAINT ORRA WEST ROTA, on 14 July 2014:** the 11:00–13:30 gap is published and unfilled and has been since 2 April; Bram Osei's refused Thursday is on the board in his own words with *no reason I can give* written under a *no reason given* in somebody else's hand; Priya Ranganathan's card is on the board at her own insistence and stays; **terms are still six days; the gate still runs on its local term and cannot be renewed by Jonas, the department or a manager. The rota has still not been rebuilt.**
- **THE DECONTAMINATION LOG, on 17 July 2014: the supplier's quarterly visit HAPPENED, at 09:20, and a NON-CONFORMANCE WAS RAISED.** The log is a grid of fifty-two Sundays with a tick in the temperature box for four books and **a signature box empty for all fifty-two.** The engineer said: *you do need a name on it, anybody in the building can sign it, once a week, twenty minutes, that is the whole of it, it does not have to be clinical, it is a person reading a strip and writing their name, it is nobody's job.* Nobody signed it. **The acceptance is about eleven weeks away, the compliance report is annual and comes in the autumn, and there are four hundred and twenty women in this borough whose instruments come out of that room every weekday. Dr Sanaa Iqbal's refusal stands, has not been argued out of her, and the reason is the 9 June finding.**
- **THE CLAIMS BOX CLOSED ON FRIDAY 18 JULY 2014.** The one card, about a stair, was taken out, sealed into an envelope, dated and numbered in biro, and put in a drawer in a records store on a lower floor. The box is empty. **Nothing was written into it. Jonas Mercer stood eight feet away with a folded piece of paper in his hand and did not put it in, and it was not a claim, and it would not have established a service or a person if he had.**
- **THE 6-L/14 LEVEL-THREE ACCEPTANCE: not yet.** A tray, about eleven weeks from 29 May, so a signature about 13 August. `reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released` stands. Four tonnes of stone and a sill beam, about £1,180, and no civic claim, so nobody to invoice. A line like that does not sit on a file for a year, and it now has an attention date on it.
- **THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS, on the Old Ward board, fourteen entries on 1 June and fifteen by 4 July — the fifteenth is a node in Salter's Yard that came off a schedule and went to its maintenance, and a register that only grows when somebody is hurt is a register with a motive in it**, including **a node in a block of 1938 in Carrow Lane with no water since 6 May whose node is still at Tier 2 at £4,200 a year**, and a node in Tideglass **withdrawn 4 July 2014, term ended 06:00, no replacement sought**, and a row of six lowland water points with a number in the households column. The blank line at the bottom of the form is still there and is still not a design feature.
- **THE COUNCIL FLOOD FORUM'S ELEVENTH PLACE: empty, and the line says *seat*.** *Vacant* was taken out in June and put back after the chair of the forum and one other person told Raynor-Hale he was wrong, and he has stopped trying to improve a line on a piece of paper. **Nora Bell's name is in the tenth place and in the attendance column in a different ink, and she has come to four meetings of eleven and spoken twice, and both times she has said a number nobody asked her for and said where it came from, in the room, for the minutes.**
- **THE SEPTEMBER QUARTERLY REPORT is three weeks from the printer and has a section headed `lowland evacuation capacity: unresolved` and nothing under it.** The only number anybody has is **nine hundred and forty**, which is nine hundred and forty *times* that somebody stood at the barrier end of the lowland embankment and could not get across, out of a private pencil book kept for nineteen years, and **it is not a number of people and has never been worked out into one, and the reason is that then it would be a number of people.**
- **JONAS REFUSED A NUMBER IN JULY.** He could have manufactured **six** in about four minutes out of a public record and refused, in front of a man with a deadline, and gave him a sentence instead. It is the first act in five months he has done in this city for no reason at all.
- **THE 9 JUNE FINDING is a governance document and not a compliance document and has not been produced as one, and the only people in the world who have read it are the nine who were in the room** — and none of them has told the people who do the practice what is in it, and that is deliberate, and it is the worst correct thing anybody in this city has done this year, and the fourth line of the record that exists says any proposal to make it a service must come back to that table.
- **A LETTER IS OWED AND WAS TELEPHONED ABOUT.** On Friday 11 July at 16:25 a woman telephoned the shop, gave no name, said *I am one of the eight*, and said the letter asked for twice — the second time about six years ago, and it went to a company and not to a person — has not been written, and that **there is no date on it and that is why she is telephoning a shop.** He has the number and has not rung it back.

### New named figures, Chapters 81–90

**YOLANDA FOFANA, 54** (already, now the second most important person in the volume), **BEVERLEY ANNIS, 54**, principal, twenty-six years, four hundred and ten flats, who read a plate with a torch and entered a woman's name and has not slept about it. **DOROTHY CRESSWELL, 63**, clerk to the Aldbrook Trust, nineteen years, a lever-arch file and a lanyard from 2009 with nothing on it. **DOROTHY ALDERMAN, 71**, sole trustee of the Halliwell Amenities Fund, retired school caretaker, who paid for a share of a Tier 3 claim and thought it good value. **CERI VAUGHAN, 45**, continuity counsel, who made the 2 May assignment, does not want to be asked about it, answers correctly, and asked for her job title to be written next to what she said. **CYNTHIA AKINTOLA, 61**, nineteen years, has paid every week since 1995. **MALCOLM REID, 44**, nights, one of the twelve who did not sign, who read clause nine out of his coat. **ESIO KOTTO, 29**, call centre, three years, who worked out in nine seconds that nineteen and twelve are both going to be legible. **DEBBIE OKPARA, 31**, who knew her term had gone off in April and never minded because nobody asked her. **AMOS BOATENG, 73**. **GIDEON HARRIS, 61**, who does the block's books on a Thursday for a pint and a set of keys. **A woman of forty-one in a yard** who said *that is the last one* and went to work. **A woman of thirty-four with a folding trolley at the bottom of Quarry Street at twenty to seven**, unnamed anywhere on the record, and the man who cost her one day and seven hours — the tail of a delay of three days and twenty hours, of which one day and twenty-three hours is his — has never had her name and did not ask. **There is only one Marguerite, one Fenella Marsh, one Frances Tolley, one Ellis Fenn, one Nina Calder, one Oren Pell, one Gunnar Halvorsen, one Sanaa Iqbal, one Gareth Raynor-Hale, one Ceri Vaughan, one Ivo Bamba, one Mina Okafor and one Jonas Mercer in this account. Do not create a second of any of them.**
