# CANON ADDED IN CHAPTERS 91–100 (Section E and the close of Volume 02) — binding on every later volume

### DIVERGENCES FROM PLAN, DECLARED

- **The series outline ends Volume 02 at Chapter 96. Chapters 97–100 are the volume's close and were added by the batch prompt**, which states on its own face that 91–96 are Section E exactly as `outline/volume-02.md` fixes them and that 97–100 are an added closing movement and are not a new section. The volume is closed at 100.
- **`outline/volume-02.md` Section E says one of the nine seats is "held by a man who classifies streets as plant." DELIBERATELY NOT DELIVERED.** The batch prompt's operative description of the ninth seat is a woman who is findable at an address, and the hard constraint that Jonas Mercer is not a member of anything and may not become one is repeated and explicit. `NON-CIVIC PLANT` is Jonas Mercer's six words of 14 March and they are the reason a lowland piece of ground carries no civic claim and no invoicee; putting him in a seat is the first step toward putting a street in a register, which the batch prompt forbids in terms. **The plant man is in the room on 4 September and is not seated, and nobody has been told why, and the file in Gantry Ward carries his own date on somebody else's classification.** Later volumes may pay this. It is unpaid.
- **The outline's Section E promise that "Sanaa trusts him with a patient-level claim and not with a city-level one" is DELIVERED IN PART, on 15 August 2014**, ninety seconds in a corridor at Saint Orra west: she gives him a patient-level fact and asks for nothing. There is no city-level claim and there is not going to be one in Volume 02.

### CORRECTIONS APPLIED AFTER THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW — canon edits, not new events

**No chapter was rewritten and no plot was moved. These are corrections of fact inside Chapters 91–100, and every state file now agrees with the prose.**

1. **The one refusal to sit is NAMED, and it is Ronan Vasquez, and it is dated Friday 22 August 2014, ten days before the eight seats were filled, and he has never read section 11.** Ch 95's board sheet said "the man who refused it read four pages of a regulation before he did it, and the reason he gave is on the second page." That was false on the page: the only four-page readers in this account are Frances Tolley, Oren Pell and Nina Calder, and Pell says outright in Ch 93 that "the man with the register has not read it and will not be told." **He also never read the eight-page-old sheet's own account of the seats, so the sheet may not say he did.** The sheet now names him, dates the refusal, and gives his reason as the sentence he actually wrote on 15 August. **The eighth seat is Gloria Nye's and Nye is seated; a man may be offered a seat and refuse it and a different person then sit in it.** Nothing else about the eight changes.
2. **The second tray is not in Jonas Mercer's coat pocket in Ch 100.** It is on a shelf in Gantry Ward under a rubber band with a date on it, it was out on a departmental van from 4 August, he was refused any copy of anything, and he explicitly declined to write *no* on the query. He goes down to the lower floor with his hands in his pockets, which is the point of the scene.
3. **The bench is planed once, not twice.** Ch 100 keeps the full bench-and-ghost scene; Ch 98's coda is reduced to one clause.
4. **The two dates on the bench are about an inch and a half high, in biro.** **Nine millimetres belongs to the TIDEGLASS 3 chalk ghost in Gantry Ward and to nothing else**, and no measurement in this account may do two jobs.
5. **THE ENAMEL STRIP IS UNSIGNED ON ITS FACE AND UNDATED. The two initials and the word *not mine* are on the BACK, in pencil, and they are a note by the hand that put it up, not a signature to the condition.** Calder's card on the same board is on the city's paper, dated 14/8 and initialled N.C., and Ch 93 gives her reason. **The two conditions on that board have opposite provenance and this is deliberate: one is claimed, dated and explained, and one is unclaimed. Do not reconcile them.**
6. **THE SEPTEMBER RECORD NO LONGER SAYS THE LOWLAND NODES HAVE NO RECORDED DATE OF DEFERRAL**, because 6-L/16 — the spur and outfall — is entry one of the register of narrowings, dated 12 May, hanging in the ground-floor vestibule of the same building. The record now says they have no number, no location of record and no district and no holder, and then concedes the spur and outfall in one sentence, **on the grounds that a register is not a district, an entry is not a holder, and the date on a register is the date it was written down and not a date of deferral.** The public record is therefore still a record of shapes and is now defensible to anybody who has read the board beside it.
7. **THE LETTER'S WATER TIMELINE IS NOW EXPLICIT.** No mains water in the block since February 1998, incoming supply isolated by the freeholder's agent on 6 May 2014, no water in the block at all since that day, and the households have been carrying what there is, in whole or in part, since 1998. **The letter can no longer be read as false on its face, and its central argument — that a meter would give the water a landlord — stands.**
8. **The letter's "twelve people in another block refused in March" is gone.** It is **six agreements in four blocks that never came back since January, two of whom have told Oyelaran to her face that they will not sign a thing that makes her name a door, and she has not chased them** — which is what she said into a transcript on 28 August, five days earlier, and Jonas Mercer was in the gallery for it.
9. **Nina Calder's "three of them" is the same three in all three places.** A heat protocol with a number box and a chief officer's name under it that she did not put there; a housing association's night porter on a chalk board in Gantry Ward for six weeks; and a third the volume has not named. **It is not a man with a register, and no later batch may make him the third.**
10. **A corrupted clause is deleted:** "He has still never ridden the thirteenth of October yet." It is not English, has no antecedent and no counterpart in the prose.
11. **The refusal-to-be-thanked beat is now spent three times in Volume 02, not four.** The Ch 91 instance is cut. **It may not fire again anywhere.**
12. **The consent-twice condition may not be restated in narration again.** In Ch 96 it is printed, in the same chapter it is quoted in speech, in Ch 99 it is quoted in full in speech, and in Ch 100 it is a pointer.


### THE PUBLIC CASE — Thursday 28 August 2014, ten in the morning, committee room two

**A registrar's public determination, with a transcript, a press list unchecked at the door, nineteen in the gallery and about thirty standing.** Applicant: **CARROW LANE HOUSING ASSOCIATION, chief officer M. OYELARAN**, in person, with a ring-number file and no assistant. Respondent: **CIVICCORE LIMITED, operations director A. ROOK**, with C. Vaughan. Registrar: **O. PELL**. Nineteen in the gallery; a woman from a trade paper photographed the empty chair at the applicant's table four minutes to ten and nobody stopped her. **Jonas Mercer was in the gallery, not on the agenda, not a party, not a member and not a departmental officer, and he asked one question in eleven words at four minutes to twelve.**

**Marguerite Oyelaran brought the case against her own interest, told nobody, and put it on a board in a shop on a Monday. Jonas Mercer has still not telephoned her. That is now the fifth time and it is a fixed position in the human geography of this city, not an omission: he has said so, she has not rung him, and neither is going to.**

**ROOK DID NOT CONTEST IT, said so before she began, and asked for it to be minuted that he offered to say it before she began.** He did not contest a fact. His whole case, in four minutes on page nine: *"We found out. That is the whole of my case and it is the whole of my weakness. We audit the nodes we hold. Nobody else in this borough has audited anything since about 1994... A paid claim is a more trustworthy claim only because the payer found out, and that is a true sentence, and it is a terrible reason to price a door."* And the second half, in a doorway afterwards: **"A thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for."** He is unsoftened. He is right. He said he had never in twenty-two years had a better accounting and he was not going to be sour about it.

**Oyelaran's nineteen minutes, eleven pages of transcript.** She asked for one sentence to be taken out of the way of the order and nothing else, and said in terms that she could not write Schedule 4 and would not stand in a room and call a document a scam. She said she did not know what the £4,200 bought, that a riser costs about £900, that she could have had water in that block by 9 May and did not, and that **£4,200 would not have fixed it.** She said there are six words in a records room in this city that were written in eleven minutes in March and accepted by a panel on 1 April, that they are *non-civic plant*, that they are on a series six sheet, that she has known since 2 April, that she has never been able to find out who wrote them and is not going to, and that they are the reason the stone on that bank is going to be paid for by nobody, ever. She said in the room that Mr Mercer was in the gallery and was not a party and that she was not going to look at him.

**JONAS MERCER'S ONE QUESTION, in eleven words: *Does this determination do anything to the notice that was served on the third of July.*** Answer, from the registrar before the respondent could get to it: **it does not, and the notice is untouched, and nothing in Schedule 4 or in this city will move it, shorten it, or require anybody who has been served to be aware of having been served.** Thirty-four days to the first of October at the time of the ruling. Rook said "Correct," and put his pen down.

**THE RULING — verbatim, and it is the whole of what Volume 02 won:**

> **1. Where the order of precedence between two nodes is set by the amount of a continuity fee paid, and by no other criterion, the tier so acquired is not a determination of priority.**
> **2. This determination is made on a sub-substitution of the Schedule and is limited to precedence. It does not affect the validity of the Schedule, the validity of any placement, the maintenance of any node, the physical works of any party, the amount of any fee paid before this date, or the notice served on the third of July 2014.**
> **3. Nothing in this determination requires any person to do anything. No party is required to refund any sum. There is no mechanism in this city by which a fee already paid may be recovered, and none is created by this determination, and I have said so in public twice this year and I am saying it a third time because a member wrote it into a note and I would like the note to be right.**
> **4. The class of node to which paragraph 1 applies is: a node whose precedence was acquired by the payment of a fee and which is not otherwise distinguished from any other node by anything that is not itself a fee.**

**WHAT IT DISSOLVES: nothing.** Schedule 4 stands. CivicCore is not dissolved, is not re-run, and is not an offer to anybody. **CivicCore keeps the physical network, the hinges, the floor at Gantry Ward, the board with forty-one entries on it and every maintenance contract.** It loses one thing: it can no longer sell a place in a queue. **It keeps everything it built and loses the thing it was selling, and the cost of the narrowness is not a cost to CivicCore: a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for, in public, repeatedly, by people who are not paid to argue, and a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to.**

**Paragraph 4 is a class and a class is a door nobody has a key to yet.** Pell read out the example: a node in a building of thirty-one rented rooms in Tideglass, withdrawn at 06:00 on 4 July at the consent of a person who is not a party and could not be required to be told and was not, and who was told by a party for no reason anybody has established. **Its holder field is empty. Its last verified essential use was never recorded. There is no party, no address, no district and no invoicee. An empty field is not a distinction; an empty field is the absence of every possible distinction. So it is in the class, and there is no consequence provided for.** A woman in the gallery said the question that had been in the room the whole time — *the managing agent's meter-round at that building is on a Saturday morning at ten o'clock; what is she going to find* — and nobody answered her, and the transcript records her as *a member of the public* who did not wish to be recorded. **Saturday 30 August 2014, ten in the morning, is a date held by other people.**

### THE COOPERATIVE STEWARD BOARD — it FORMS

**Section 11 of the 2008 continuity regulations, four pages, permissive, and Frances Tolley has it in her own hand because she did not trust herself not to improve it.**

- **Page 1–2:** *may be constituted*; **nine seats**; eight for the service districts the drawing layer draws, and **a ninth, for a person who answers for a service and is not a body, who is not a veto, is not a chair, and cannot be removed by the other eight.**
- **Page 3:** the board's powers and their limits — it **may require a party holding continuity to answer, receive the answer, and be asked to consider a matter**, and it **may not require a person to do anything, hold a duty, answer a question, or be told anything**; it may not be given money; it has no quorum that can require a person; **it has no appeal from it to anybody, and the reason is Schedule 4 page nine, and Pell stated in the determination that that will not be corrected by any instrument in this city within the next four years.**
- **PAGE 4, WHICH THREE PEOPLE HAVE READ: a board of that kind IS DISSOLVED WHEN SIX OF THE NINE SEATS FALL VACANT.** Read by Frances Tolley (twice, since 30 May), by Oren Pell (twice in August), and by Nina Calder (on Tuesday 19 August). **Nina Calder told nobody and gave a reason in a records office, in front of Jonas Mercer and a committee clerk, and was not argued out of it: a person may not be required even to be told; a person who knows she is the only seat that can empty a board is findable in a way she is not now, and a lever in the hand of a body that cannot use it is still a lever, and in four years it will be in the hand of a body that can.** Pell said the same thing in a corridor in nine words on 21 August and would not read the page out. **Yolanda Fofana does not know. That is the state at the close of the volume and it is live.**

**THE EIGHT SEATS, filled Monday 1 September 2014.** A seat is not an appointment. A seat is a person already doing a thing. Each was filled by the person named in the answer to a question asked **for a state and not for a list**, where that person lives under the node they answer for. **Nobody was appointed and nobody was asked in advance whether they would sit; a person named without being asked may decline afterwards in his own words and nothing follows from it.** This method is the volume's own method (Ch 89) turned into a body.

1. **YVONNE AMOAH**, 41, ambulance duty officer, eleven years. State: *what happens at this door at ten at night.*
2. **IBRAHIM SESAY**, 51, caretaker, Tarn Street school, thirty-one years. State: *a gate with a key in it and a line in a form that is his fault.*
3. **DERMOT FAHEY**, facilities supervisor, Saint Orra west, six years. State: *this is a stair.*
4. **INES TARRANT**, car park supervisor, eleven years. State: *nobody has ever asked me what it is for and I have assumed it is a barrier.*
5. **STANLEY UMEH**, 58, market porter and lock-up keeper, thirty years, Bellow Market. State: *the shutter and the padlock and what the padlock is for.*
6. **KATHLEEN FERRIS**, 33, night bus controller, nine years. State: *the last bus and who is standing at the stop when it does not come.*
7. **WINIFRED CRABBE**, 62, linen supervisor, nineteen years, a care home in Gantry Ward. State: *the laundry runs on one machine, and when that machine is off the linen is in a corridor at the end of somebody else's shift, and nobody has ever been told whose shift it is.*
8. **GLORIA NYE**, 44, self-employed heating engineer, twenty-one years, three shifts a week. State: *the plant room in the basement and the man who is nearest when it goes.*

**THE ONE REFUSAL, on a piece of paper in a file: RONAN VASQUEZ, 48, estates office, Saint Orra west, eleven years, was offered the eighth seat and declined it, on Friday 22 August 2014, in a piece of paper in his own words. He did not read section 11 and nobody has told him what is in it. The reason he gave is the same sentence he put on a supplier's acceptance seven days earlier, and it is not about money and is not about standing: *it is not a service, it is a person reading a strip, and if anybody makes this a rota I will stop and they can have the paragraph number instead.* He signed the decontamination verification on 15 August and said the same sentence twice in the same fortnight. **He has told the corridor he will do the twenty minutes weekly until the end of October, and that after October it will lapse, because a job in November has a department in it and a department is a rota.** He is the only person in this city who has refused a seat and a log in the same fortnight for the same reason and has been right both times. **The refusal is named on a public sheet in the vestibule of the Old Ward, dated to 22 August, and is the reason the eighth seat is Gloria Nye's and Nye is seated.**

**WREN COURT, SALTER'S YARD, THIRTY-ONE ROOMS, NO CIVIC ADDRESS, WAS NOT SEATED. THE ROOM TRIED.** Thirty-one people in a building with no civic address are not a body and cannot be seated, cannot vote, cannot require anybody to do anything and cannot be challenged, because a tier may not be challenged by a person. It is right, all of it, and it has not helped them since 4 July, and being right about it is now printed on a board in a corridor as the reason thirty-one people are not in a body that is supposed to represent what happens underneath the thing.

**THE NINTH SEAT — YOLANDA FOFANA, 54, named steward of Wren Court, nineteen years in the block, fourteen as steward.** Filled **Thursday 4 September 2014 at ten past seven, in eleven minutes, in the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane, about thirty people, in an aisle, not on a stage and not into a microphone.** Nobody asked her and she said so, and said she had been in nineteen years of rooms where somebody was asked. She stated the whole price out loud and did not hide any of it: **there is one of her, she has a lease and a shopfront, she has been findable since 1995 and has never in nineteen years been found by anybody holding a document; she is not being paid and the board has no money; she has been doing it for nothing since 4 July and will be doing it for nothing until she is sixty-one; she is a person and not a body, she is not a veto and not a chair, and the other eight cannot remove her; and the thirty-one people whose water it is cannot be seated and are four hundred yards away and she is not going to pretend that she is the same thing as them.** She is the one woman out of about forty people in that hall who anybody can write to. She said the building has no civic address and the board has no money and the ninth seat is a person, and that is the transaction.

**CAN THE NINTH SEAT BE REMOVED IN FRONT OF THE OTHER EIGHT? NO. AND NOBODY CAN, AND ONLY SHE CAN, AND NOBODY SAID THE LAST PART OUT LOUD.** Pell's answer in a corridor on 26 September: the other eight cannot, because the board's one power is to require a party to answer and a person is not a party; **a registrar can require the board to tell her in writing what she is answerable for, and has done it, and cannot require her to be removed, attend, or answer.** The eight are removable only by declining, because they were named without being asked. **And the ninth seat is the only seat in the body that cannot be emptied by anybody else, and is therefore the only seat that can empty it, and that is the reason Nina Calder's condition of 2 June — that one of the nine must be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there — is met in the only way it can be met, which is by the person with the least protection being the removable one. Pell did not say that sentence. Frances Tolley wrote *the next sentence was not said* in the minute, in those words, and put Calder's reason under it, and nobody in the corridor added anything.**

**The board has no chair. Jonas Mercer was asked to chair the second sitting on Wednesday 1 October and refused in a corridor with a reason, and the reason is that a chair is a person who decides which of two things gets heard first, and section 11 does not require a chair, and the ninth seat is not a chair, and so the nine take it in turns and a person may decline the floor. He also said the other half: in about two years somebody is going to use the word *administrator* about him in a room, and Alden Rook said so in June, and he would be the wrong man for it in a way four other people in this borough are currently the wrong man for it.**

**The board's first act, at ten past nine on 4 September, was to have the registrar's statement read out, and the first thing anybody said was a question from the thirty-three-year-old night bus controller: *then what are we for.*** They spent an hour and ten minutes on it. They are not allowed to answer it. A board with one power and no way to lose it is nine chairs, and the ninth of them is a person.

### NINA CALDER'S FOURTH PAGE — no fifth page was written, and she refused on the page

**On Monday 1 September 2014 a request arrived for a one-page standing procedure for the board. She refused, in the right order, in a public room, at a counter.** The instrument, the whole of it: **the fifth page is the form; the form is a scheme; a scheme is a list with a future in it. You write one page for a body of nine seats and in four years that page is in a drawer with nine names on it and somebody wants to tenth it, and then it is a register, and the register is on a wall, and the wall is in a room that people come into. A form is the last thing a person writes and the first thing an institution keeps.** She named the 2011 precedent, the heat protocol with a number box in it, and a chief officer's name under it that she did not put there. She said she has had a fifth one in her head since 7 July and is not going to write it down, and that if anybody argues with her about it she will put the sheet in the drawer with the laminated heat card in it and it can stay there until the borough is dissolved. **Nobody argued. She asked for no reason to be written down and no job title to be put next to it, because the last time a job title went next to a refusal in this city it became a finding about the standing orders, and a refusal with a job title on it is a policy.** This is the standing fifth refusal of the volume and it may not be argued out of her.

**The fourth page stands: one week, one place, one number, what was counted, one sentence saying what it is not, no index, no name box, nothing may be added, and a person may refuse to be counted and the count then says so and says how many refused.** It is on a public board. Nobody has asked her what it is for and she has not said.

### THE DECONTAMINATION LOG — who signed it, and what the non-conformance became

**RONAN VASQUEZ, 48, estates office, Saint Orra west, eleven years, not a doctor, not on a rota, not clinical.** He signed the supplier's acceptance at **16:20 on Thursday 15 August 2014**, in a flat sheet in a plastic wallet, having thought about it since the previous afternoon. He had asked at the card table on Thursday what a supplier needed and Mina Okafor said a name, and he said *a name is a person*, and he went down, and he came back.

**Nobody arranged it. Jonas Mercer was asked nothing by anybody and arranged nothing and did not sign and did not do the twenty minutes.** His refusal, said out loud to a bench in an empty decontamination room on 5 August, and the reason is the load-bearing one: **filling it in is a service; once there is a name against twenty minutes a week, twenty minutes a week has got a person on it, and that person is a person somebody can ask, and a rota with one name on it is exactly what this building has been refusing since the first week of March, and he has spent five months saying out loud that the practice is not a service and cannot be made into one and he is not going to be the man who signs the first line of one.** He also did not do the twenty minutes, because doing them once is the first twenty minutes of the next fifty-two.

**What he wrote on the acceptance, in his own words, unamended:** that the twenty minutes is not a service and is a person reading a strip; that he has held the maintenance of that room's pipework since 2011; that if anybody makes it a rota he will stop and they can have the paragraph number instead; that his name goes on a log and **does not go on the corridor board, and both of those are true and neither cancels the other**; and that he will do it until the end of October and that after October it will lapse.

**NINA CALDER'S RIDER, stapled to the second page of the acceptance, no crest, no form number, four lines: this record is one thing at one time and is not a service and is unpaid; a person may refuse to be counted and the count then says so and says how many refused; any proposal to make it a service must come back to that table; **and the fourth line is copied here word for word, without amendment and without a summary, because this is the first document in this city that describes the practice and it is a compliance document.** The fourth line of the 9 June finding is now in a supplier's acceptance, on a supplier's reference, against a paragraph number. **The practice is not thereby a service, the finding is not thereby a compliance document, and the first person who tries to make twenty minutes a week a rota will be arguing with a piece of paper that is already in the file and will not be holding it.** Fiona Grieve's third sentence from 9 June, obeyed four months late by somebody else.

**THE NON-CONFORMANCE DOES NOT GO AWAY.** The supplier, on Monday 18 August, in four sentences: the going-forward verification is granted and the forward half closes; **the historical half cannot be granted, because every Sunday on that grid with a tick in the temperature box has an empty signature box, and a compliance record that cannot be reconstructed is not reconstructed.** It carries forward and is on the annual compliance report in the autumn with its paragraph number, and it will read that the verification was not evidenced for a period of three months, and that it is now evidenced weekly by a named person, and that **the named person is not on a rota.** Yvonne Amoah, minuted out loud in a corridor: *it was not a rota on the Wednesday, it is a paragraph in the autumn, and that is worse, and it is worse because you can appeal a rota and you cannot appeal a paragraph.* **The autumn report has not arrived. The volume ends before it. It is live and owed.**

**Sanaa Iqbal's refusal stands and has not been argued out of her and the reason is still the 9 June finding.** She is not on the log, she was not asked, and she was not thanked. On 15 August she gave Jonas Mercer a **patient-level fact** — bay nine, level one, the bed by the window, a node going since 4 July with no last verified essential use recorded — and asked for nothing, and gave the reason out loud: he is the only person in this city who cannot be required to do anything and is therefore the only person a fact can safely be handed to. **There is no city-level claim and there is not going to be one in this volume.**

### THE FOURTH OF AUGUST — two dates in one morning, and what the return cost

**06:00 Monday 4 August 2014, in the vestibule, two things happened and neither made a noise.**

**(a) THE BOROUGH HEAT THRESHOLD PROTOCOL RETURN fell due and was complete, and the number in the ring-number box is NINETY-TWO.** Deliberately, by somebody who had read about the first one. Box two carries M. OYELARAN, CHIEF OFFICER, CARROW LANE HA, BY POST AND TELEPHONE, and a ring number in the records office's own hand. It was handed in at a counter at about ten to five on Friday 1 August. It is a public document. Calder telephoned Oyelaran at twenty to nine on the Monday and got one word back. Calder: **there are now two public numbers from that body — 106 in May, 92 in August — and neither of them is about a household; the difference between them is a number and the difference is not a household; there is no instrument in this city that can turn a difference into a household; and that is the only good thing about it and also the reason it is going to hurt somebody.**

**WHAT IT COST, in her arithmetic and his: 122 on the rent roll, so the Block C void of thirty rooms is now publicly legible as a difference between two public numbers; the shortfall is £4,361.60 and is on no form; and 106 minus 92 is fourteen, and the only figure in this business anybody in this borough would recognise as hardship, and a difference is not a figure and is not on anything and there is no instrument in this city that could make it one. She chose it. She would rather be a bad landlord in public than be the woman who put a hundred and twenty-eight people in a ring-number box, and she said so in a room with nineteen people in it in August. He was not told and not asked and has still not telephoned her.**

**(b) KH-4/20 LAPSED AT 06:00** — the residential floor of the Vale Clinic, a private road in Kestrel Hill, eleven patients and a day room, placed at Tier 2 at £4,200 on 29 May by the Aldbrook Trust, term to 06:00 Monday 4 August 2014. Nobody re-priced it; nobody was required to. **Nobody had ever recorded a last verified essential use, so the use is the maintenance, and the maintenance of that node is the stair, the door and the lift.** The plate on the frame at ankle height, since 4 August, reads *LAST VERIFIED ESSENTIAL USE: NONE RECORDED. SERVES: THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE* and, under it, *THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE IS THE STAIR, THE DOOR AND THE LIFT.* The lift has said OUT OF SERVICE since 2011.

**AND IT KEEPS WORKING, by accident, and the accident has a name on the floor and not on any document: a red plastic fire door wedge, put there in May by HENRY ODUYA, 61, day-room assistant, nine years, who opens that door at a quarter past seven every morning, who has a key that no longer turns the plate, who is still on the paper rota, and who said to a man who was not there for anything: *don't tell me it's nothing's changed, because something has changed, and the day somebody reads a notice and takes me off the paper rota that door shuts at seven in the morning and there's no wedge in the world that survives me.*** He asked for nothing. Jonas Mercer told nobody, is not a party, has no authority over a freeholder's building, and did not ring the Aldbrook Trust, whose notice has been in the post since 3 July and whose clerk of sixty-three is going to read it in October and do her job.

**THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS has SIXTEEN entries and has had sixteen since the morning of 4 August 2014, and that is deliberate and is now stated in a corridor: it is a record of a period and it is not a register and it is not going to be a register, and if it starts again in October then somebody has broken something.** The blank line at the bottom of the form is still blank. It has been asked about four times since May and answered four times in the same nine words, and a fifth time in a Committee room on 21 August from the public seats, in front of a chair who had written *no name* on a page and refused to have a box for it.

### THE 6-L/14 LEVEL-THREE ACCEPTANCE — date, signature, name, line

**Went out Monday 11 August on a departmental van to field services, Gantry Ward, about two weeks late because field services moved offices in July. CAME BACK SIGNED ON WEDNESDAY 13 AUGUST 2014 AT ABOUT FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON, in about ninety seconds, by whoever was on the level-three rota that week.**

**Signed: PETER NAYLOR, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward, eleven years in post, out of estates since 2011.**

**The line, and it is unchanged and it does not go away: `REPORTED NOT MAINTAINABLE. RELEASE RECOMMENDED. NOT RELEASED.`** He did not read the eleven pages, he could not have, nobody was asked to tell him, and nobody thought it was worth explaining. Jonas Mercer is not entitled to a copy, neither is the records office, and Calder did not photocopy it in a drawer.

**PETER NAYLOR IS THE MAN NINA CALDER SAID OUT LOUD IN A RECORDS ROOM ON 30 MAY AND HAS NEVER BEEN ABLE TO ASK.** On 15 September she gave the name to Jonas Mercer and nothing else, and said: *that is him, he has a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket under a tap, it is not a joke, I have never said it to a second person and I have now said it to you, he is the reason four tonnes of stone are not going to be bought by anybody ever, he does not know that, and I have worked with him for nine years and I have never once been able to ask him whether the line he signed is the line he believed in — and the reason I have never been able to ask him is that six words went out of this office in March under a reporting officer number that is not mine, and the words made the thing he was signing about a pipe instead of a service, and I typed the form, and I did not know what they were.* **Nobody has rung him. Two people told Jonas Mercer not to and he did not.**

**A SECOND TRAY, raised Tuesday 12 August 2014, is OPEN ON A SHELF IN THAT BUILDING with a rubber band round it and a date on the band in biro, and one question on it is outstanding: IS THERE ANY PARTY WHO MAY CONSENT TO THE WORKS. The answer is on page four of the report and it is no, and it has been no since 29 May, and it was no on 13 August when he signed, and it will be no in four years.** Jonas Mercer did not answer it. He has no standing. He will go back in December and be told to go away.

**The 6-L/14 index, nine lines, on the inside of the front cover, has never been read in order by anybody, and the officer who read it out says so: people read the last line and go away.** Line 3 is the Reclamation Season review of 1 April 2014 — *6-L/14 NON-CIVIC PLANT. ACCEPTED. NODE LEAVES THE REVIEW.* Line 4 is *no civic claim, no invoicee.* Line 5 is the duty at 6-L/16 transferred to the holder of this post on 17 May. **Three facts, one drawer, one building: the duty is held by the man who lodged the classification and the file says so and it does not say whose name it was, and there is no rule anywhere that anybody has to read both.**

**THE VOLUME'S ANSWER TO 6-L/14, and it is a sentence and not a fix.** On Tuesday 16 September, at a lowland chamber at 6-L/16, with a screen being rebuilt on a node CivicCore holds, **WENDELL PRYCE, 38, CivicCore continuity field manager, came over the lip in an apron and asked Jonas Mercer for a piece of paper that says anybody may put four tonnes of stone on that bank tonight. There is no such paper. The only line that exists says not released.** Jonas Mercer wrote three lines into a margin and initialled the margin and gave him a copy, and the third line is the sentence: *there is a man in this city, forty-four, who came out of estates in 2011, and he was told a line was plant and he got a job out of it, and he has a fortnight somewhere with a bucket under a tap, and nobody in this borough has ever been able to ask him one question about any of it.* And then: *that is the answer to your question and it is not a fix, and I have written it down because you asked me to write it down and because a mason on a day rate is going to ask it again on Friday.* **Pryce: that is a worse answer than the one I came for. I have never not done it before and I have no idea what I am going to do instead, and a price was never what I was doing it for — the thing that has kept this borough running is that somebody eventually puts the stone on, it is never the same somebody, it is never on a form, and if I stop then about nine years later a woman of sixty is going to be standing at a gate with a pencil. So you have not solved anything. In about four years one of us is going to have to say the sentence out loud, and it will not be a registrar, and there is no schedule for it and no form and no party to serve it on.** He is not a villain, he is not softened, and he is right.

**THE TUESDAY.** On Wednesday 14 August Royden Achebe telephoned the shop, as he had known since 18 May that he would, and said so. A booked restriction on 6-L/16 on **Tuesday 16 September 2014**, notice served, four hours to rebuild the screen, a node with a fee on it and therefore a stated time and a party to telephone, and he is going to be in Gantry Ward with a man from Manchester. **He was not asking Jonas Mercer to do it. He was asking him to turn up, keep his hands off the plate, write down the time he arrived and the time he left, and have it entered twice.** Jonas Mercer was the one who answered. On the Tuesday, Jonas Mercer stood on the lip for three hours and eleven minutes and did not go down the ladder and did not touch the plate; **Emeka Rusk, 51, pump operator, docks, twenty-six years, did the work, in his own hand, with the operator box left blank, for the twenty-seventh time since 2 April; the entry was made twice because the record has no field for a duty held by one person and performed by another; nobody was blamed; and Jonas Mercer's name is on that sheet in a margin he initialled, and there is no field on the form for the man who watched it, so he wrote one in.**

### THE LETTER — written, by the wrong person, with his name at the bottom

**Written in ink on one sheet on Friday 19 September 2014 at a bench in River Stacks, in about an hour and a half, and posted by hand on Monday 22 September. NOT TYPED. NO CREST. NO FORM NUMBER. NO NUMBER EIGHT. NO NAME BUT ONE.**

To the managing agent, freehold, four blocks, Carrow Lane. It says the block of 1938 at the rear has thirty rooms; the internal riser has not existed since February 1998 and has not been replaced; **water for those thirty rooms has been carried, by hand, in containers, by the households of that block, since 1998; no household is named in this letter, no household is asked to confirm anything in it, no list accompanies it, and a request for one will not produce one; the stopcock is in the meter cupboard, the cupboard has never had a lock and there is no key, the stopcock is reachable under a cover plate with four slotted screws and a plastic lifting handle, and it turns, and the tag in the cupboard reads STOPCOCK / 1998 and that tag has been there since the riser failed and is the only document that has ever existed about this arrangement.** It asks for one thing: that the four meters proposed for that cupboard are not installed, because the year on the tag is the whole argument, and because a meter means a landlord, and in about four years somebody will ask the landlord who draws from it, and a list of who draws water in a block with no water in it is a list of the households that are still there.

**It is signed at the bottom: MERCER, J. — and under his name, in the same ink: *This letter is not sent by the housing association and the housing association is not a party to it and has not asked for it. It is written by me. I am a probationary grade three technician. The civic standing I hold in this city has no grade, no department and no probation, and it gives me no authority whatever over the supply, the freehold, the block or the households, and I have never in my working life had authority over any of them. I am not authorised to write this letter and I have not asked anybody's permission to write it. I am writing it because it has been asked for twice since about 2008 and because a person telephoned this shop on the eleventh of July and said that there is no date on it, and there is one now.***

**THE WRONGNESS, named in the margin of his own draft: the wrongness is not the four meters and it is not the year. The wrongness is that there is his name at the bottom of it and there is nobody else's, and the woman it is for has not asked him to write it, and nobody has asked her anything.**

**THE TELEPHONE CALL, Friday 19 September at ten past four, four rings.** She did not give her name and he did not ask. He asked one question: *what does it have to say.* She said: **the year.** *Put nineteen ninety-eight on it and do not put who on it. That is the whole of what I have wanted for sixteen years and I have never said it to anybody and I have said it to a shop in about nine seconds.* She is about seventy-one. She said: **do not send me a copy and do not tell me that you have sent it — a person who knows that a letter has been sent is a person somebody can ask whether the letter has been answered**, and that is the reason she is still on the telephone. She refused a name and refused to be one of a list and said she has said no to two things in sixteen years, one of them them and one of them a company, and there is a third thing she says no to every week and it is her own name. **He told her the argument and did not ask her permission and said so: I would have written it if you had said no.**

**NINA CALDER REFUSED TO TYPE IT AND GAVE THE REASON: a letter typed on this office's machine with this office's crest at the top is a departmental document, and a departmental document is a record, and a record gets kept and indexed and asked for, and the first person who asks for it is not the woman it is about, it is the next managing agent in four years, and that agent will be looking for a list of who carries water in a block with no water in it.** Write it out. Do not type it, do not print it, do not put a crest on it, do not put a date in a box — put the date in a sentence like a person.

**BEA OSUNDE FOUND THE ONLY SENTENCE THAT COULD HURT SOMEBODY: *the households of the block*, and it had been written three times, and once as *the eight households*, and a count in a letter to a managing agent in a year when that block has thirty empty rooms in it and a chief officer has put ninety-two in a public box on purpose, is a number of who.**

**The block's node is still at Tier 2 at £4,200 a year. There is no water in that block. The fee did not stop and the letter does not stop it and the letter is not going to fix a riser, and he wrote that on the outside of the envelope in pencil and rubbed it out, because a letter that says what it is not is a second letter.**

**IVY MARSDEN, 72, BLOCK C ROOM 14, HAS NOT BEEN TOLD AND WAS NOT ASKED.** She asked for one letter in a Methodist hall in May, in front of about sixty people, and Jonas Mercer was in that hall and did not ask her one single question. He saw her come into a hall on Carrow Lane on 4 September with a walking frame and go out again and did not go to her. He has still never spoken to her. He has written that down twice and both times it was true, and the reason is that she asked in a room full of people and the person who did not ask her anything was him, and if he goes to her now and asks what she wants in it then he is a man who finally got round to it, and he will have got round to it because somebody telephoned a shop.

### THE SEPTEMBER RECORDS — two documents, both published, both public, one with a name in it and one without

**THE SEPTEMBER QUARTERLY REPORT of the Saint Orra and District Flood Forum was begun in July, five weeks early, and WENT TO PRINT ON THURSDAY 31 JULY 2014 with a hole in it, and was tabled on Monday 8 September at ten o'clock and published in September.** Council flood forum, Councillor Martyn Craddock in the chair, ring binder, eleven plastic sleeves, a laminated card whose sleeve has been replaced twice, about fourteen people. **The eleventh place is empty and the line says *seat* and is not written *vacant*, and Raynor-Hale has stopped trying to improve a line on a piece of paper.**

Section 4, verbatim as printed:

> **lowland evacuation capacity: unresolved**
> **State: unresolved. As at 4 September 2014 there is no party in this borough who answers for the lowland crossing and there has not been one since 6 April 2014. No party has been asked for a figure and no figure has been supplied by anybody, including this officer.**
> **Source of the only figure that exists about the lowland, stated here in full because it is a public document and a public document cannot hold a thing without saying where it came from: a private record kept for nineteen years by a person who lives at the barrier end, in pencil, in a book. Nine hundred and forty marks. It is a count of occasions and it is not a count of persons. The number of persons is not on the page and has never been worked out and will not be.**
> **Next state: not before 31 December 2014.**

And at 4.3, in Raynor-Hale's own words and not Jonas Mercer's, as he insisted in July: *The empty-crossing clause at the lowland crossing is contested by the operator and no other party. The resilience officer has sought a second question and been refused by the operator on grounds he has given and would give again. No party other than the operator is affected by the contest and no party other than the operator is engaged in it.* **DEV SINGH WAS IN THAT ROOM AND JONAS MERCER DID NOT GO TO HIM AND STILL HAS NOT TELEPHONED HIM. He would deny that he minded and he would not be lying.**

**THE PRICE, and it was paid on purpose:** Raynor-Hale said he had known since Wednesday, and did it anyway, and put his own name on it in the minutes, and told Jonas Mercer not to argue. **NORA BELL, about sixty, is now findable in a public document that goes to the Assembly, in a paragraph that says the source of the only figure about the lowland is a woman who lives at the barrier end, and in about two years somebody is going to go and look at the ledge and the book.** The first two lines of that paragraph are Jonas Mercer's, dictated in a room on 5 September, and he had known what the other four lines said since the Wednesday afternoon before and said nothing, and it took him eleven seconds to say nothing. **He has never had her name and did not ask and did not write a face in a document. On 8 September at twenty to seven in the morning she stopped with the trolley and said *you are the one who cost me the day and the seven hours*, and he said yes, and she said *I know it was you, because a man came and stood over that plate in the rain and did not say a word to anybody*, and then: *there is no date on me, there is no date on this stand, and I have been keeping to that for eleven years with nobody helping me, and if anybody ever writes a piece of paper about this stand there is going to be a date on it and no person on it, and that is the only deal there is.*** She gave no name. He asked for nothing. He wrote nothing down for two days.

**A member asked her for a cost per head. She said no and gave the reason and said it twice because the first time nobody wrote it down: *because if I work it out then it is a number of people.* It is in the minutes in her words. She read the wording before it went and said it was accurate.**

**THE CHAIR'S PROMISED RECORD — THE NODES IN THIS CITY WITH NO PARTY ANSWERING FOR THEM. One page, the city's own paper, no crest, no form number, no index, no name box, delivered to the chair on Thursday 21 August 2014 and published September 2014. Verbatim:**

> **The layer draws nine service districts. Eight are named. The ninth is not drawn.**
> **Nodes on a continuity schedule on the day this record was made: a number, held in a register with chairs, and a node in a schedule is a node with a party, whatever else is true of it.**
> **Nodes in a state of deferral on the city's own public register: sixteen, listed by reference, all of which are checkable by any person at a counter.**
> **Nodes on the lowland: six water points, of which one is a stand, and a chamber, and a spur, and an outfall. NONE OF THEM IS ON THE LAYER. NONE OF THEM HAS A NUMBER, A LOCATION OF RECORD, OR A DISTRICT, AND NONE OF THEM HAS A HOLDER. The spur and the outfall are one entry on a public register in the ground-floor vestibule of the building this Committee is sitting in, and a register is not a district, an entry is not a holder, and the date on a register is the date it was written down and not a date of deferral. Their presence in this record is a figure of six and a figure of six is not a list.**
> **THIS IS A RECORD OF SHAPES. IT MAY NOT BE MATCHED AGAINST ANY LIST OF ADDRESSES, AND ANY DOCUMENT THAT ATTEMPTS IT IS RECORDED AS ATTEMPTING IT.**
> **It names no person. It is a document and it is not a service and it establishes nothing.**
> **There is no field on this page for the person who put it together, and none has been designed, and that is a decision and not an oversight.**

**Douglas Imre: *there is no name on it.* Calder, from the public seats: *you want me to put a name on it because you think a record with no name on it cannot be argued with. It can. It is being argued with right now, by you, in a room, in public, and I am glad of it. It is easier to argue with than a form. That is the whole point of it.*

**NINE HUNDRED AND FORTY IS A NUMBER OF TIMES AND WAS NEVER A NUMBER OF PERSONS AND MAY NOT BE WORKED OUT INTO ONE, AND IT WAS NOT PUT IN THE SAME SENTENCE AS FOURTEEN IN ANY CHAPTER, AND NEITHER FIGURE IS A FIGURE ANYBODY CAN BE HELPED BY.** Jonas Mercer refused to manufacture a count of the nodes with nobody answering for them, a second time, in August, having already refused it in July; the record that exists was made by asking for states, and is a record of shapes.

### WHAT HAPPENED ON 1 OCTOBER, AND TOMAS RENN'S QUESTION

**NOTHING. IT HAS NOT HAPPENED. IT IS TWO DAYS AWAY AT THE CLOSE OF THE VOLUME AND THE VOLUME ENDS ON MONDAY 29 SEPTEMBER 2014 AT ABOUT TEN PAST SIX IN THE EVENING, IN A SHOP, WITH TWO DATES WRITTEN IN BIRO ON A BENCH.**

**A ninety-day notice was served at 11:00 on Thursday 3 July 2014, on the day the entry was made, on the Aldbrook Trust, the Halliwell Amenities Fund, CivicCore Limited and two unnamed parties. It expires Wednesday 1 October 2014 and no other date is the expiry. The Aldbrook Trust has answered a machine once and a person once, and the person is DOROTHY CRESSWELL, 63, clerk, nineteen years, a lever-arch file and a lanyard from 2009 with nothing on it, and she will do her job with the notice in October and nobody asked her to and nobody is going to. The 28 August determination did not touch it, said so in terms, and could not.**

**TOMAS RENN'S QUESTION, asked in the shop on 8 July 2014 and repeated in a corridor on 8 September 2014, is UNANSWERED and is now the volume's last live question: what happens on the day after, when she is in the field again because she has to be, and a consent can be given twice — which is not defeating the clause, it is complying with it, twice.** On 24 September **Yolanda Fofana DECLINED TO ANSWER IT**, in a kitchen, and gave the reason, and asked for it recorded in those words: *because the first person in this city who says that answer out loud is going to be quoted in October by a man with a lever-arch file who is going to do her job, and the answer is going to be ask her again tomorrow, she is in the field anyway, and that is true, and it is the clause working, and it is not defeating anything, it is complying with it, twice — and the only thing that stops it is a person who has decided in advance, in her own words, on a date, what she will say when she is asked the second time. And I am not going to say it out loud to you, because you are a man who writes things down, and a person who has decided in advance what she will say is not a person anybody can find, and if you write it down then in four years there is a piece of paper in a file that says what Yolanda Fofana will say on the second of October, and I am not going to be that in a drawer. Findability is not answerability.* **She told him not to write it down. He did not write it down. He wrote down that he was told not to write it down, and that is the first page anybody in this city has produced since March that cannot be given to anybody.** Nobody argued with her. This is a standing refusal and may not be argued out of her.

### THE SIX STANDING REFUSALS AFTER SECTION E — none revisited, none overturned, none argued out

1. **THE MORTUARY PORTER, 61, thirty-four years** — four words in nine seconds in biro on a laundry delivery note, 23 April, no name wanted, not on a form, minuted *attended, declined to sign*. The registrar did not strike them because striking them would have emptied the field and the maintenance on a mortuary gate is a man with a key. **Unchanged and untouched in Chapters 91–100.**
2. **MARGUERITE OYELARAN'S NINE SECONDS** — she put one hundred and twenty-two of a hundred and twenty-eight consent marks through letterboxes herself in January and February and **in May she put 106 in a ring-number box for a body she was inside the definition of, without authority, and in August she put 92 in on purpose.** The refusal was the person-box, not the number. **Untouched. And in August she brought a public case against her own interest and did not contest a fact and did not ask for her money back and gave the reason into a transcript.**
3. **EMEKA RUSK'S**, 24 May — a duty cannot be half-shared, he will not be handed Achebe a thing that has been handed about once, and helping does not go on a form. **Untouched. He did the work at 6-L/16 on Tuesday 16 September for the twenty-seventh time since 2 April, in his own hand, with the operator box left blank, and nobody asked him and nothing was written on a form except the two entries there have to be.**
4. **MARGO DORÉ'S ADVANCE REFUSAL OF A PUMP**, 1 June, in a kitchen, unargued, and no pump was offered and none exists. **Untouched. Jonas Mercer did not go up the path in this volume. She knows about the £11,280 in the minute, is not angry with him, has not forgiven him, and was not asked. The three positions stand and the ring of pencil round a blank space is untouched and the trap is lost for good.**
5. **SANAA IQBAL'S TWENTY MINUTES A WEEK** — she could do it, said so, and did not, and the reason is the 9 June finding. **Untouched. She was not asked again, she signed nothing, she is not on the log, and on 15 August she gave a patient-level fact to the one person in the city who cannot be required to do anything and asked for nothing in return.**
6. **IVO BAMBA'S REFUSAL TO BE THANKED**, 16 July, spent in Chapter 90, and **not reused. No character in Chapters 91–100 refuses gratitude in the Chapter 90 sense — a person's thanks being a record that makes them findable — and nobody in this batch thanks Ivo Bamba or Yvonne Amoah.**
7. **NEW IN THIS BATCH, AND IT STANDS: NINA CALDER'S FIFTH PAGE**, refused 1 September, in a public room, at a counter, unargued, and nobody may come back to it.
8. **NEW IN THIS BATCH, AND IT STANDS: YOLANDA FOFANA'S REFUSAL TO ANSWER THE DAY AFTER**, 24 September, in a kitchen, with her reason, and nobody argued.

### THE 19 JUNE DETERMINATION AND THE 9 JULY RULING — what has since been complied with, and what has failed

**THE 19 JUNE 2014 DETERMINATIONS, item 3, second determination on Thursday 21 August 2014, ten in the morning, committee room two.**
- *The two entries for Meter Room 3 stand and cannot be amended.* **Complied with and unchanged.** The Committee again found it had been read to and again recorded that finding, and again recorded that the person who read them in is not in the room.
- *No procedure for amending a minute.* **Complied with, and the Committee used the only thing it had: a READING RULE, its first act in four months that was not a form — where a party relies on either of the two entries, the other entry is to be read with it.** It is not an amendment and it cures nothing and it changes no word.
- *The chair will not require a person, and will not require one even to be told.* **Complied with, and said a fourth time minuted.** Her answer to Jonas Mercer's one question, in her words and asked to be minuted: **a counter is required to answer a telephone. A counter is not required to make one. There is no duty on any officer of this city to begin an enquiry, to write a letter, to hand anything to anybody, or to tell a person that a thing has happened to them.** And she asked the minute to record how the woman in the field came to know: **she telephoned a counter, a man with a register put a number in a box on a public form in a corridor and did not have her name, a records supervisor wrote *merely* in a tray, and that is the entire chain of causation and none of it was designed.**
- *Every party holding continuity to answer a Return by the sitting, no-party answers recorded as no party.* **Complied with in June and unchanged. Fourteen returns, two drawers, two on both for the same meter room, and the two entries are permanent.**
- *The chair's promise of a September public and minuted record of the nodes with no party answering for them.* **DISCHARGED ON 21 AUGUST AND PUBLISHED IN SEPTEMBER. One page. A record of shapes. No name box, by decision and not by oversight. The count Jonas Mercer refused to manufacture was never made and never had to be.**

**THE REGISTRAR'S RULING OF WEDNESDAY 9 JULY 2014 — the fourth ruling of the year.** *A claim discharged for the holder is discharged for the holder, and not for the party that placed it, nor for any party that assigned it, nor for the party that received the assignment. The person who caused the arrangement may be findable; findability is not answerability; no registrar and no Committee may convert it. Nothing in this determination exonerates any party. And: there is no mechanism in this city to make the top of a chain pay and none to make a person take a duty, and those are the same sentence, and who pays for the refusal is nobody.*
- **Complied with in substance and unremedied in law, and both of those are the answer.** The chain stays broken; the Aldbrook Trust holds its letter; the £2,880 a year went back to Kestrel Hill in July; the water in Salter's Yard is what it was in April. Nobody pays. There is no mechanism and there has not been one since 2008.
- **On Monday 22 September 2014 Pell came to the shop to say he was not going to say it again.** He has now said it in an office, a corridor, a Committee room, a determination and a shop, and it is in paragraph 3 of a published determination, and he is finished with it. **He handed Jonas Mercer a job instead of a ruling: the day you ask somebody, it will not be me and it will not be a committee, it will be a person in a room who can say no, and when they say no that ends the ask. He asked for it to go in the pad in those words so that in about two years a minute cannot say he was told by a registrar in a corridor in July.** He also said, standing in a doorway: *do not telephone me about the first of October,* and then, after two minutes: *forty-one minutes, Mr Mercer. I have walked it.*
- **Pell's mention of section eleven in a corridor on 9 July, in nine words, has now been paid three times over:** the fourth page is a dissolution clause; the ninth seat is the only seat that can empty a board; and nobody in a corridor in September said the sentence that follows from that.

### THE BATCH'S THREE LATTICE NOTICES — three printed, in three chapters, at most one each. Two are conditions on a board and one is a plate. The cap is four.

- **Ch 91, the KH-4/20 plate at ankle height in a corridor in Kestrel Hill, new:** `LAST VERIFIED ESSENTIAL USE: NONE RECORDED. SERVES: THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE. / THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE IS THE STAIR, THE DOOR AND THE LIFT.`
- **Ch 93, a printed card on the Old Ward board beside the register of narrowings, new:** `A BODY THAT CANNOT BE REQUIRED TO DO ANYTHING CANNOT BE ASKED WHAT IT WILL DO. A BODY THAT MAY NOT BE CHALLENGED CANNOT BE TOLD TO STOP. THE TWO SENTENCES TOGETHER ARE THE REASON A REGISTER IS NOT A ROOM.` Printed 14/8, initialled N.C., and **it is not a plate and it is not on the Lattice and it is not a form; it is a piece of paper on a board, and a records supervisor initialled it deliberately, because a condition nobody has put their name to is a condition that can be put in an edition, and a condition in an edition is a rule.**
- **Ch 96, the enamel strip under the register of narrowings, new, UNSIGNED, UNDATED AND UNATTRIBUTED ON ITS FACE, and nobody in the building has admitted to putting it there:** `WHERE A CONSENT MAY BE GIVEN, AND MAY BE GIVEN AGAIN, THE RECORD OF THE SECOND CONSENT IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT THE FIRST WAS WITHDRAWN, AND THE PARTY THAT TOOK THE FIRST CONSENT MAY RELY ON IT UNLESS THE RECORD SAYS OTHERWISE.` **On the BACK of the strip somebody has written two initials and one word in pencil, and the word is *not mine*, and the initials are a note by the hand that put it up and not a signature to the condition. The two objects on this board have opposite provenance by design: Calder's card is on the city's paper, dated and initialled, and she has said out loud why she initialled hers; this one is unclaimed. Do not reconcile them and do not ever decide in prose whose the initials are.**
- **Three new notices printed in ten chapters, in three chapters, at most one each, within the cap of four.** The Ch 76 occupancy condition was not reprinted. Nothing defined *holder*. The line on the enamel strip from 1 March was not reissued, not restated and not quoted. **Within the window the consent-twice condition is printed once (Ch 96), quoted in speech in the same chapter, quoted in full again in Ch 99, and referred to by pointer only in Ch 100; it may not be restated in narration again.**

### THE CLAIMS BOX, THE ENVELOPE, THE REGISTER, AND THE BENCH

**The ninety-day claims box closed Friday 18 July 2014 and is empty and stays empty. The one card about a stair is sealed in an envelope, dated and numbered in biro, in a drawer in a records store on a lower floor. NOTHING WAS EVER WRITTEN INTO THE BOX AND NOTHING MAY BE WRITTEN INTO THE ENVELOPE AND NOBODY HAS OPENED IT.** On Monday 21 August Jonas Mercer went down to that floor with a second tray from Gantry Ward in his coat and stood in front of the drawer for about six minutes and did not open it and did not put his hand on it. The only reason in the world: **a stair is a thing a person climbs, a stair cannot be held in a field, and if there were a form that held one then the drawer would be a register and the box would have been the fourth list.** That is not a good enough reason and it is the reason he has got.

**THE BENCH.** On Monday 29 September 2014 at about ten past five in the evening, a man wrote two dates on the bench in the shop in ink, an inch and a half high, along the grain, on the flat, in the place where Saturday's plane had brought up a pale ghost of a four-month-old date. **1 OCT and 11 OCT.** Bea Osunde shut the roller, saw it, and said: *you have written two dates on my bench. / In biro. / I have been oiling that bench since about 1998 and I have never once been able to get a mark out of it, and that is not because of anything to do with oil. **That is because there is nothing in this borough that can be argued with a mark on a piece of wood that nobody is required to read.*** She put the light out over it and left it.

### STATUS

**VOLUME 02 IS CLOSED AT CHAPTER 100. Chapters 49–100 are complete prose.**

Jonas Mercer is Stage 2, rule mender, unchanged in capability, **and a transfer steward in practice and not in title.** In this volume he performed no civic edit, moved one duty on 17 May and attended a second he could not hold, refused a log twice in his own words out loud, refused to chair nine people's meeting in a corridor with a reason, refused to be the man who fills in a form, refused twice to manufacture a number, and **put his own name at the bottom of a letter he is not authorised to write and on a public form in July and in a margin at a lowland chamber in September, and every one of those names is a person somebody can ask, and he knows it, and he has been told four times this year by four different people that he is the only one anybody can be given a fact to and cannot be asked for anything in return.** The body cost is unchanged and is not a cost he has mentioned to anybody: the seam mark runs past the elbow, the right hand is numb to the elbow and will not close, and on 25 September it went at the fourth line of a page and stayed gone, and he wrote the rest with his left hand, and he has been meaning to mention it to somebody for a month.

**Nothing is dissolved. CivicCore keeps the network. The board is nine chairs and one of them is a person. The old continuity schedule is still in force and still has nobody auditing it. A district that cannot pay has a seat and a person in it. A paragraph number is on a supplier's system and is owed in an autumn report in front of four hundred and twenty women. Nine hundred and forty is a number of times in a pencil book at the end of a wall. A woman on a landing cannot be asked anything by anybody, ever. A man with a register cannot be released by no person, and has a woman of fifty-four in the ninth seat who is the only one who can end what he has started, and he does not know she knows, and neither does she.**

**And a Saturday night in October is in six weeks, on a stair, at ten o'clock, and no field on any plate in this city says so.**
