# VOLUME 03, BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 101–110

**THE AUTHORITATIVE HANDOVER INTO BATCH 0002. Written after Chapter 110 and superseding the "STATUS" block above wherever the two differ. Nothing in this block may be contradicted by a later batch without a line here saying so.**

**The series is at 110 of 720 chapters. Volume 03 is Chapters 101–144 and is OPEN. Batch 0002 is Chapters 111–120 and starts on Monday 8 December 2014.**

## 0. The calendar, checked against the chapter headers of Chapters 101–110

The Volume 03 anchor is Chapter 101 = Monday 20 October 2014. The batch's own calendar, now corrected in five places against the real arithmetic of that anchor: **20 October Monday · 22 October Wednesday · 24 October Friday · 28 October Tuesday · 29 October Wednesday · 31 October Friday · 3 November Monday · 5 November Wednesday · 7 November Friday · 10 November Monday · 12 November Wednesday · 14 November Friday · 17 November Monday · 19 November Wednesday · 21 November Friday · 24 November Monday · 26 November Wednesday · 28 November Friday · 1 December Monday · 3 December Wednesday.**

**CORRECTED IN THE FIXER PASS, and the next batch must inherit the corrected values, not the values in the Batch 0001 cards:** 24 October is a **Friday** and not a Thursday · 28 October is a **Tuesday** and not a Friday · 31 October is a **Friday** and not a Monday · 5 November is a **Wednesday** · 8 October 2014 is a **Wednesday**. The Batch 0001 cards for 0103 and 0104 carried three of these errors and the chapters inherited them; the cards for 0110 said "the board of nine has eight seats" and the correct arithmetic is seven of nine.

**Batch 0002's dates: Monday 8 December 2014 through Friday 23 January 2015, with Saturday 25 and 26 December and Saturday 2 January available for nothing, and the fixed term at 06:00 on Sunday 4 January 2015.**

## 1. WHAT HAPPENED AFTER 1 OCTOBER, AND EXACTLY WHAT IS PUBLICLY VISIBLE ABOUT IT

**Nobody in this city knows, and the batch did not find out, and the batch was not permitted to find out. That is the answer and it is final for Volume 03.**

What is publicly visible, in three places and three places only:
1. **The register of narrowings grew by one on Wednesday 2 October 2014** — entry 17, Meter Room 3, Salter's Yard, a building of thirty-one rented rooms with no civic address, re-asserted by the Aldbrook Trust at Tier 3 on the notice of 1 October with the consent of no person required, holder field none, party as re-asserted, class per the determination of 28 August. **The last line of it — *entered on a public form by a person* — is not part of the form. There is no line on the form for it. A records supervisor has said out loud three times that the absence of a name box is a decision and not an oversight.**
2. **A notice of re-assertion came back through a records counter on Wednesday 22 October 2014**, stamped RECEIVED 21 OCT 2014, dated 14 October, signed for a trust in Kestrel Hill, giving a claim reference, a tier, a node reference, a service district and an effective date. **There is no signature on it on behalf of any person and there is no field for one.**
3. **BEVERLEY ANNIS, 54, principal of the managing agent that holds four hundred and ten flats, telephoned the shop on Wednesday 22 October at four minutes past five and gave her name for the first time in five months.** She put *the named steward of the building* in the holder field herself, in her own name, on 3 June 2014, with a torch, because the point is not the payer's, and she has not slept about it. **Nobody told her what the class meant. Nobody has told the person in the holder field anything, and Beverley Annis has said out loud that she is not going to be the person who breaks *a person may not be required even to be told*, and neither is Jonas Mercer, and she rang a shop to establish that.**

**What is NOT publicly visible and may not be made visible: what DOROTHY CRESSWELL, 63, clerk to the trust, nineteen years, a lever-arch file and a lanyard from 2009, said in October; what the trust's board did or did not decide; and whether anybody has been told anything. The batch may not find out and did not.**

## 2. THE STATE OF THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS, AND EVERY NEW ENTRY IN IT

**NINETEEN entries. The blank line at the bottom is blank and stays blank. It is now the only fixed point in the building.**

Entry 16 was the Salter's Yard node that came off a schedule on 4 July. Entries 15 and before are unchanged. The three new entries, verbatim:

> **17.** *2 Oct 14. Node, Meter Room 3, Salter's Yard, Tideglass. Building of 31 rented rooms. No civic address. Claim ref T3/11400/0304, one half, Tier 3. Re-asserted on notice of 1 Oct 14 with effect from that date. Consent of no person required. Holder field: none. Party: as re-asserted. Class per determination of 28 Aug 14. Entered on a public form by a person. No name.*
>
> **18.** *14 Nov 14. Node, riser space, rear block, Carrow Lane, Tideglass. Constructed 1938. Placed on a continuity schedule 29 Oct 14 for a condition survey, Tier 1, £1,880 per year. The survey instruction lapsed 12 Nov 14 and no answer to a return has been received. No party. Narrowed to its maintenance on 14 Nov 14. Holder field: none. Class per determination of 28 Aug 14.*
>
> **19.** *26 Nov 14. Node, yard light, rear yard, Carrow Lane, Tideglass. Fitted 1998. No party. Last verified essential use: none recorded. Narrowed to its maintenance on 26 Nov 14. Holder field: none. Maintenance is a bulb. The bulb is out.*

**The reading of the register, and it is now a stated position in two rooms and a corridor: it grew three times in nine weeks, every one of the three is correct, and a register that grows when nobody is hurt is a register with a motive in it and a register somebody can hold over you. A records supervisor has said that if anybody in this borough ever uses that board against anybody, they will not use the meter room. They will use the bulb.**

**Entry 18 was caused by the letter of 19 September, and Jonas Mercer knew about it on 19 November and had not known there would be one. The roof void at the same block is the other half of the same lapsed instruction and is still on a schedule at £1,880 a year; one of a pair went and the other did not, and there is nobody he is entitled to ask which is correct.**

## 3. THE PUBLIC READING RULE OF 21 AUGUST 2014 — WHO CAN USE IT, AND WHO CANNOT

Verbatim, and unchanged:

> **READING RULE. Where a party relies on either of the two entries above, the other is to be read with it. This is not an amendment of either entry and cures nothing and changes no word. It is the only procedural act available to this Committee in the present case and it is made under the general power to give directions as to the manner in which returns are to be read.**

The two entries are METER ROOM 3, SALTER'S YARD: party holding continuity **THE ALDBROOK TRUST, Kestrel Hill, as freeholder of the fee payer, one-half**, Tier 3, `T3/11400/0304`, answer received 6 June 2014; and **THE NAMED STEWARD OF THE BUILDING, named in the form as received**, Tier 3, same reference, answer received 16 June 2014. **Neither entry contains the name of a person.**

**WHO CAN USE IT: the Standing Committee on Civic Continuity, in a room, with a transcript, in front of nineteen people. It used it once. A registrar can use it in a determination and nobody will let him. That is the whole of the list.**

**WHO CANNOT: a person at a meter cupboard in Salter's Yard on a Saturday morning. A managing agent. A woman in an apron. Anybody at all who is not at that table — and the room is eleven feet by nine on the first floor and all nine people in this borough who know the rule exists work in the building it was made in.**

**WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS, and this is the batch's finding: a reading rule is an act and not a document. It is a person reading two sentences out loud to another person, in a room, with the second person able to say no. It is not a report and it is not a notice and it cannot be put in a form, a job description, a rota, an establishment or an edition, because any of those turns it into a service and a service is a rota with one name on it.**

**AND IT HAS NOW LEFT THE ROOM. On Wednesday 5 November 2014 a clerk of twenty-two years, FRANCES TOLLEY, who typed it and made it up because the chair could not think of a third thing, wrote it out longhand on one sheet of A4 in biro with no crest and no form number and no box of any kind on it, and walked it forty-one minutes on foot to Salter's Yard, and walked back. THERE IS EXACTLY ONE COPY IN EXISTENCE AND IT IS IN HER INSIDE POCKET.**

**WHO HAS REFUSED A COPY OF IT, AND WHY, AND IT IS CORRECT: BEVERLEY ANNIS, 54, managing agent, four hundred and ten flats, on 5 November. *A copy of that page goes to the Aldbrook Trust. It goes to a clerk of sixty-three with a lever-arch file and a lanyard from 2009 with nothing on it, and the moment she has it, the trust has a defence.*** Not a good one. A defence. Because the reading rule says the two entries are to be read together, both are correct, and a person holding a piece of paper saying so produces, in about four years, a letter in this city that says the question was put twice and the party relied on the reading and did not know — and that letter will be right, and it will be true, and it will be the reason a woman in that building finds out in 2030 that she was in a claim for sixteen years. **She is a party. A party that has read the reading rule is a party that has been told what the other entry is, and she does not know which side of the node she is on and neither does anybody else, and that is not a thing to put in a file. She has not been given a copy and would not give one if asked on her knees.**

**AND WHAT TOLLEY SAID SHE WAS GOING TO DO WITH IT, and she said it on a hill on the way back and asked for it not to go in a pad: *On a Tuesday in November, if I am at a counter and a man from field services comes in front of me, I am going to read him those two entries. I am not going to ask him anything. I am not going to know anything about him afterwards. And he is not going to have been told that he was in it, and that is going to be the only way it reaches him, and I am not going to be told that it worked, because there is nothing to work with.*** **She also said, unasked, that she is going to misuse a procedural act of a Committee in a corridor, on her own time, for a person nobody may be told about, and that she has been a clerk for twenty-two years and has never done anything like it. Nobody may act on this and nobody may ask her whether she has done it. Frances Tolley said no to Jonas Mercer in a corridor on Wednesday 26 November, before he had said what the question was, and he did not argue, and it is the fourth time this year he has asked her a question about a document and got the same no.**

## 4. CIVICCORE'S AUDIT, ITS COVER NOTE, AND ITS EXACT LIMITS

**Published and public, begun 1 September 2014, finished Friday 7 November, on the Committee's floor at Gantry Ward in a plastic wallet, obtainable free at the records counter for four pages.**

- **Cost, in the audit's own words, in paragraph two of page two, where anybody reads it in the first thirty seconds:** *four hundred and ten flats' worth of somebody's evening.* It is not a phrase anybody writes about their own work. Rook put it there on purpose: **a document that hides its own cost is a document that can be used against the people who paid for it.** Two people's time, four hundred evenings, about nine thousand pounds, and it does not have to be paid again by anybody in this city.
- **It found real faults and that is the part nobody expected and the reason the document is dangerous.** One hundred and six hinges with no last verified essential use recorded. Forty-one places where the answer to *who answers for this* is a company, thirty-eight where it is a person, nine where it is a description of a person — **and the nine are given as node references and the descriptions are not reproduced, because reproducing them would create a record of a person, and this audit does not create records of persons.** Four nodes on a schedule at a tier where the fee had been paid by a body that no longer existed: **it did not say what it did about them, it did not disconnect them, and it paid them itself, and Rook said in the room that this was the most expensive line item in the document and that nobody in that borough had ever written down the sentence *nobody is going to pay for it* before he had.**
- **It found a gate.** Node 6-44, a mortuary gate, with car park barrier 3, staff stair 2 and service riser 1, the longest finding in the document at nine pages, and it is the sentence the whole of Volume 02 was built to arrive at: *the maintenance of this gate has been performed continuously since approximately 2005 by one individual who is not employed by this company, is not a party to any agreement, and whose name does not appear in any field of any document associated with this node… On the audit's reading the gate is therefore maintained by an act and the act is performed by a person, and the person is not findable from the record and the record does not require it to be… **This audit finds no fault at this node. This audit records that it is the most reliable arrangement this audit has encountered in four hundred and ten flats' worth of premises and that it is unlawful, and that both of those are correct.***
- **The cover note, page one, verbatim, and nobody wrote it on purpose:** *This document describes only what we hold. / The city has nodes we do not hold, and have never held, and do not know about… The number of them is not in this report. It is not zero. It is not one. It is not a number anybody should write in a document, and there are people in this city who have already declined, in public and in the right order, to turn the absence of a number into a number of people, and this company agrees with them and has not helped. / **Every finding in this report is a finding about a node somebody is paying for. That is not a limitation of the audit. That is the audit.***
- **THE EXACT LIMITS, and they are the volume's question, stated by the antagonist, who is right:** the audit is of the nodes CivicCore holds. **It cannot find one node with no party answering for it, because a node with no party is not a node anybody holds, and that is not a failure of the audit and it is the whole of the problem.** CivicCore is a contractor, is not in a position to do anything about it and is not asking anybody to think that it is; it says it would rather be an uncredentialed witness who is right than a credentialed one that is silent; and it **asks that the document not be used to suggest that the unheld nodes are a problem to be brought within a contract. They are not a problem to be brought in. There is nothing to bring them into. There is nobody to bring them to, by a rule that has been in force since the first of March, and by the plainest possible operation of it.**

**ALDEN ROOK, 46, CivicCore operations director, unsoftened, and the price of the 28 August ruling is his and he has paid it in his own staff's time: a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for, in public, repeatedly, by people who are not paid to argue, and a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to.**

**AND THE HOUR AFTERNOON OF MONDAY 10 NOVEMBER 2014, which is the cheapest thing in the batch and the worst: the borough's housing office wrote to CivicCore asking whether the hundred and twenty-eight rooms of the Carrow Lane Housing Association are within the scope of the audit. The answer is no. The answer is two lines. The answer is going to be read out in the stock review on the Thursday at half past two, in a room the chief officer of that association is sitting in, about nine feet from the person who reads it, and she is on the attendance because she answers for her four blocks.** **Jonas Mercer stood on the pavement for about forty seconds and did not ring her, and the reason is the correct one and it is the worst decision in the batch: if he tells her, she has to do something about it, and the only thing she can do is produce a list, and she has been refusing to produce a list since March and won a public case in August on the grounds that she would not.** She has £4,361.60 on no form and no assistant. **He has now twice in eight months protected a person from a fact by not saying it, and in both cases he was protecting her from a document and not from a person, and he has written down that he would like somebody to explain to him the difference, because he has been using it as if it were a principle since April and it is not a principle, it is a preference of his, and he is the only person in this city it is available to.**

## 5. WHETHER THE FOUR METERS WENT INTO THE BLOCK C CUPBOARD, AND THE SHAPE OF WHATEVER ANSWER THERE IS

**THEY DID NOT GO IN. The freeholder's standing instruction of 14 April 2014 was withdrawn, and the freeholder has found them going somewhere it did not have to: a block of 1974 at the top of the lane where the bins are, which has a communal hot water main, about forty flats, and a landlord who has been in correspondence with that firm since April. Every occupier of that block was at home in August.**

**NOBODY WAS ASKED TO CONFIRM ANYTHING. The only document is a notice on the door of the rear block, under a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet, there about a fortnight by 12 November, in the same make of wallet as the notice about the lift:**

> **BLOCK C, REAR. CONSTRUCTED 1938. INTERNAL RISER REMOVED 1998. INCOMING SUPPLY ISOLATED 6 MAY 2014. THE FREEHOLDER'S INTENTION IS TO CARRY OUT A CONDITION SURVEY OF THE RISER SPACE AND THE ROOF VOID AND TO REPORT ON THE FEASIBILITY OF RESTORED SUPPLY.**
>
> **THE OCCUPIER OF EACH DWELLING WILL BE NOTIFIED OF A DATE. NO OCCUPIER IS REQUIRED TO BE PRESENT.**

**Past tense, past tense, past tense, and then one future tense with a word in it — *feasibility* — that has never once in the history of this city produced a restored anything. There was a feasibility in that street in about 1994 and it was a report and the report went in a drawer.**

**The shape of the answer, which is the whole of Chapter 106, and nobody was saved: IVY MARSDEN, 72, Block C room 14, four flights and no lift, has carried water for thirty rooms since February 1998 and has been asked for a letter twice and refused twice in sixteen years. On Thursday 13 November 2014 at about half past three in the afternoon, in daylight, with about nine minutes' notice to himself, and without telephoning first, Jonas Mercer went up and said his own name, which was the first time in five months he had given his name to a person who had refused him. She would not have a copy, and the reason was not him: *a copy of it would be a thing in this flat, and I am seventy-two and I do not want a thing in this flat that came from anybody, because the day it is here it is a thing somebody can ask me about and I would have to be a person who has a letter.*** In about four years somebody will come into that block and ask whether room 14 has had correspondence, the answer will be no, and the answer will be true.**

**She worked out what the letter was for in about four minutes. He took from a hall in May to a bench in September. She told him so and was accurate about it and he asked for it not to be argued with.**

**AND THE THIRD HALF, WHICH HE DID NOT HAVE AND SHE GAVE HIM, and it is the price of the whole letter and it is not a fix: *the day a piece of paper says the buckets are the block's, the block is a thing that can be asked. And a thing that can be asked is a thing that has a landlord. And a thing that has a landlord is a thing that has a list.*** He has made a Tuesday. Nobody was saved. She is no more findable than she was on 6 May and she is a great deal more frightened, and she told him so, and she shut the door. **And she said that his name is now in a thing in her head that has to do with her, and that when she dies somebody is going to ring a council and say a man in this borough went to Ivy Marsden's door, and they will be right, and it will be in a file, and there will not be one line anywhere in it that says she asked. She said he may write that down, and it is the first thing anybody has said to her in sixteen years that she would let a piece of paper say, and he wrote it down on the Friday, at the bench, in four lines, and it is the first entry in the pad since 22 October longer than a sentence.**

**Nobody in this batch may go up those stairs again without a reason she would recognise, and there is no such reason, and the batch did not.**

## 6. THE SECOND PUBLIC RECORD OF THE NODES WITH NOBODY ANSWERING FOR THEM, ITS NUMBER, AND THE FOUR

**Published in the third week of November. One page. The city's own paper. No crest, no form number, no index, and NO NAME BOX, and the last line says the absence out loud and says it was a decision. IT IS UNSIGNED, and it is the only document in this city anybody can be held to.**

Design, in the records supervisor's words and repeated in the room where it was agreed: **nobody is required to be told they are in it, and that is the design and not an oversight.** And why it is unsigned: *the day I initial a sentence about a document it becomes a rule about documents, and in about four years there will be a form, and the form will have my initials in the corner of it, and somebody in a basement will say that it is Nina Calder's page, and it will be, and that will be the end of it. So it goes out unsigned, and anybody may take it down and add their name to it, and about four hundred people will, and that is not worse, that is four hundred arguments instead of one document nobody can touch.* About four hundred people did.

**THE NUMBER, and it is the batch's most reusable fact and it is checkable at a counter by anybody, for nothing, by post, as of a date, and it is the only arithmetic in this city a person can be held to: FIFTY-ONE NODES on the continuity schedule as at six o'clock on Monday the sixth of October 2014, THIRTY-EIGHT PARTIES answering them, THIRTEEN SHAPES. Perform the subtraction and check it, and if it is wrong there is a piece of paper that says so with a date on it and a reference on it and a clerk's signature on it.**

**The number in September was a number, in the sense of *a number*, not a figure, and the number in November is bigger and the difference is FOUR. It is a number of shapes. It is not a number of people and it may never be worked out into one, because then it would be a number of people.**

**THE FOUR, and Calder can say what three of them are and will not say the fourth, and Jonas Mercer read three and refused the fourth, in a room, and would not write it down:**
1. **A node at a private clinic's residential floor in Kestrel Hill.** The trust let it lapse at 06:00 on 4 August and did nothing about it for eleven weeks, then put it back on a schedule on 29 October at Tier 2, £4,200, term to 6 January. It is a shape because a trust re-priced a floor over a garden and nobody on that floor is a party and nobody was asked.
2. and 3. **Two survey nodes placed in October by a freeholder at a block built in 1938 — a riser space and a roof void — at Tier 1, £1,880 a year each, thirty-seven pounds sixty a year, and neither of them will put a drop of water in the building, and both of them are correct.** **One of the two went into the register of narrowings on 14 November. See §2.**
4. **A meter room in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, Tideglass, on which the holder field is empty. It is the only one of the thirteen that a person could walk to, and it is on a public document, and it names nobody, and the page says in its own last line that it may not be matched against any list of addresses.** Jonas Mercer's reason for not saying it in a room with three people in it, which Nina Calder called the first time in four months he had been the right person in one of her rooms: *a sentence with a lot of people in it about a thing that has no address is a direction.*
5. **And what the woman who wrote the page thought about in a corridor on 19 November, standing up, with her coat on, before she had said goodnight to anybody: it can be walked to.** That is what nobody in the room on the first morning thought about. It is unresolved and it is the most dangerous sentence anybody has produced this year.

**The chair received the page on 19 November, declined to adopt it, and asked for the declining to be minuted. A document received by a Committee is a public thing whether anybody likes it or not. There is no field on it for Jonas Mercer and there is not going to be one.**

**Calder's load-bearing sentence, said for the fifth time on 19 November, in the same order, because she is a person who believes in order, and it may not be softened, abridged or improved: *A record with one name in it cannot be argued with. I have watched three of those happen in this borough this year. One of them is a heat protocol with a number box in it and a chief officer's name under it that she did not put there. One of them was a night porter on a chalk board in Gantry Ward for six weeks before anybody asked him a single question… And I have not decided which of the three was the worst and I am not going to.***

## 7. WHO FILLED THE SEVENTH AND EIGHTH SEATS, AND THE FOURTH PAGE OF SECTION 11

**NOBODY. THE BOARD STANDS AT SEVEN OF NINE, AND BOTH VACANCIES ARE PERMANENT IN PRACTICE AND VACANT IN FORM.**

> **Recorded: that this body now stands at seven of nine; that it stood at nine of nine from 4 September to 8 October and at eight of nine for forty-nine days; and that it has no power to fill either vacancy and will not seek one.**

- **The seventh seat has been vacant since Wednesday 8 October 2014**, when KATHLEEN FERRIS, 33, night bus controller, nine years, wrote four lines on the back of a bus timetable in about eleven minutes and gave them to the woman who keeps the minutes. **Nobody has read them out and nobody may.** The board spent six weeks working out who to want instead, and that is a rota, and the ninth seat said so in the room and did not enjoy it.
- **The eighth seat has been vacant since the evening of Wednesday 26 November 2014**, when STANLEY UMEH, 58, market porter and keeper of a lock-up at Bellow Market for thirty years, stood up in about six words — *Right. I'm going* — and did not use the chair, and said the whole of it out loud, and asked for it to be recorded that he was not angry with anybody and was not stopping because of any of them. **Nobody tried to keep him. Nobody argued with him, nobody asked him a second question, nobody said *are you sure*, and the minute records all of that.** He answered on 1 September for *the shutter and the padlock and what the padlock is for* and the minute records the seat as a lock-up and not as a district or a facility, and the box in the form is left empty on purpose, and the minute says so.
- **The eighth seat is therefore a shape where a person was, and the shape is the only interesting thing on the page.**

**PAGE FOUR OF SECTION 11 OF THE 2008 CONTINUITY REGULATIONS IS A DISSOLUTION CLAUSE AT SIX VACANCIES. IT HAS BEEN READ BY EXACTLY THREE PEOPLE IN THIS CITY AND TOLD TO NOBODY: a clerk who copied it out by hand in a single afternoon in May because she did not trust herself not to improve it, FRANCES TOLLEY, twenty-two years; a registrar who read it twice, OREN PELL, and would not read it out in a corridor; and a records supervisor who read it once, told nobody, and gave a reason in a public room, NINA CALDER.**

**NOBODY HAS COME NEAR ACTING ON IT AND NOBODY MAY.** The board does not dissolve, because a dissolution needs six, and there are two empty chairs, and **a person named without being asked may decline afterwards in his own words and nothing follows from it, which is the whole of the power this borough has been able to give anybody in a room since 3 April.** Calder's condition of 2 June is in the room as an observation and nobody has treated it as a motion: *for this to be nine people and not nine chairs, one of us has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there. And there is no power in this city that can do that. There never was.* The ninth seat said it out loud on 26 November and added: **two chairs is not a step towards anything, it is a Tuesday.**

**Nobody in that room knows what is on page four, and the ninth seat of that body was in the room, and she is not going to be told, and nobody may tell her, and Volume 03 has not resolved it and Batch 0002 must not resolve it and must not have the board notice it.**

## 8. THE PERSON IN CHAPTER 0102, AND EVERY FORM, LIST AND DOCUMENT THAT CHAPTER PUTS INTO THE WORLD

**CYNTHIA AKINTOLA, 61 — sixty in the March of 2014 and sixty-one in the March of 2015 — of Wren Court, Salter's Yard: in the borough since 1995, in that room since about 1996, never had a job in it, never asked for one, never put her name to anything except about four hundred sheets of paper with a figure on them, and nineteen years of a Wednesday count.**

**Chapter 0102 is the batch's only close POV outside Jonas Mercer, and it is a workshop on a Wednesday evening with about eleven people in it, and nothing in it is a rescue, an interview, or a person being given a voice in order to be helped.**

The documents that chapter puts into the world, all of which now exist:
- **A three-part form.** One: the state of the supply at this point. Two: the number of dwellings drawing from it. Three: the name of the person responsible for the supply at this point. **Forty-one of them in the van. The state was given and is in a box. The number was refused. The name was refused, and the reason given was that the person who holds it has a shopfront and a lease and is the only one of that building anybody can write to, and that putting her name on a form in a van is not going to happen on a Wednesday.** The refusal took nine seconds, was not argued with, and **the man wrote NO in the second box in capitals, and it is the only capital letter on that page.**
- **No box anywhere in this city for the other half of it.** The man with the clipboard said: *if I put a box on it then I have made a register of people who will not say things, and I am not going to do that on a Wednesday either.* He has had the form for three years, has had about thirty nones out of forty-one sheets, and has never once known what to do with a no.
- **A note on his own pad, in his own file, with no box on it and not on the form at all, and he said the words out loud before he wrote them so she could stop him:** *One thing, one time, one place. What was asked and what was given and what was refused. And then a line that says what it isn't — that it isn't a refusal of anything, and it isn't a number, and it doesn't establish that anybody here is or isn't anybody. And that you can refuse, and I'm writing that I asked you twice and you said no twice.* **THE MAN DID NOT KNOW THAT HE HAD WRITTEN THE FOURTH PAGE'S METHOD. He does not know it now. Nobody has told him and nobody may.**
- **A biscuit tin, nine inches by seven, a picture of a dog, from about 1988, with a rubber band round it and a card under the band with a number on it, and the number is not money.** Nine hundred and eighty-six. It is a number of Wednesdays, it has been on the card since about 1996, it has never had a pound sign on it and never had a note about what it is, and **it is not a number of people and may never be worked out into one.** She counts a week whether or not the money went in; there have been perhaps thirty such weeks in nineteen years; some were paid later and some were not; **the difference is about four hundred and thirty pounds and nobody has ever asked her about it and it is hers and she is not going to mention it to anybody.**
- **The fee: £11 a week from March 1995 and £13.60 from June 2014.** Neither figure is a household and neither is on any instrument.
- **A statement about the steward, made on a stair, in words, and it is now canon: the ninth seat said out loud that she is not going to be paid and will not be paid until she is sixty-one, and that after that she will not be.** Cynthia Akintola is sixty-one in the March. She has thought about it perhaps four times, it is always the same thought, and **there is nobody she could say it to, and it is not self-pity and not a complaint and not a claim, and it goes down the same as the four hundred and thirty pounds and the nine hundred and eighty-six, and it stays hers, and she is not going to be the person who puts it in a letter to a council.**
- **Four words, said out loud, to nobody, in a corridor, on the walk up the stair: *It's weeks. It was always weeks.*** She had already told the man in the van, before he had finished asking.

**Nobody was helped. The refusal cost nobody anything. The room carried on and the job did not. Chapter 0102 is closed and nobody in it may be asked anything in a later batch without a reason, and there is no reason, and the woman of sixty-one is on no board, in no field and on no rota, and nobody is going to put her in one.**

## 9. THE SIX WORDS, OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, IN NOVEMBER, AND THE RECORD THEY ARE NOW IN

**`6-L/14. NON-CIVIC PLANT.` Written in a records room on the second floor on Friday 14 March 2014 at 12:41, in eleven minutes, initialled, and lodged into the series six file on **22 March 2014** under a reporting officer number that is not his, by **ROYDEN ACHEBE, 49, NDC field services manager**, who did not read it before he lodged it and has never said so.**

**SAID OUT LOUD, TWICE, IN ROOMS, AND NEITHER TIME WAS A CONFESSION:**
1. **Tuesday 28 October 2014, a Methodist hall, Carrow Lane, about eleven people, at about a quarter to seven in the evening, after a man of sixty-one who was not a member of anything said it to him in front of everybody.** He said one word. The minute records it: *A person present, not on the agenda, stated in the room that the classification of 6-L/14 as non-civic plant, made on 14 March 2014, was written by him. No person was asked to make that statement. He was not asked anything else and made no other statement.* The minute went out on Wednesday 29 October. **The minute does not contain his name, because there is no box for one.**
2. **Friday 21 November 2014, the same hall, about ten people, at nine minutes past six, asked in daylight by IBRAHIM SESAY, 51, caretaker, Tarn Street school, thirty-one years, second seat, who stood up to ask it, which nobody asked him to do.** He said no, and then he said yes, and then he said the whole of it in nineteen seconds, to the floor and not to anybody, **including the name of Royden Achebe, forty-nine, field services, and including that he did not read it before he lodged it.** The room did three correct things with it inside about forty seconds — one man treated it as a confession and was stopped, one man treated it as eight months of being angry at a form and refused to put it in the minute, and one man treated it as a liability and was right — **and then a fourth thing, which was the only one about a person who was not in the room, and it was Gloria Nye, forty-four, eighth seat, and it is now the record and it may not be tidied: *In August you put your own name to six words and you did not put his. In November you have put his name to a sentence about his own conduct, in a room, on a Friday evening, and nobody in this room can tell him, and you did not do it because you were angry with him. You did it because you were being complete. It is not a bigger version of what you did in August, it is a different thing, and the difference is that a man of forty-four cannot be asked one question about any of it by anybody, ever, and a man of forty-nine can be asked one tomorrow morning and will have to answer it, and neither of them has been asked, and one of them cannot be.***

**THE MINUTE OF FRIDAY 21 NOVEMBER, Item 4, and it went out on Monday 24 November and it is a public document: the statement that he wrote it; the statement that it was initialled by him and lodged on 22 March by a third party who did not read it before lodging it; the request that it be recorded that a statement about a document is not a confession and that no person in the room treated it as one; the offer of the seventh seat, made by the ninth seat as a person and not as the body; the refusal, minuted as spoken and not to be amended, in eleven words — **A man who calls a street plant must not be findable** — with no reason given, none asked for and none recorded; and that nothing in the item requires any person to do anything.**

**THE SIX WORDS AND THE FACT THAT HE INITIALLED THEM ARE NOW IN THE SAME DOCUMENT IN A PUBLIC RECORD FOR THE FIRST TIME IN EIGHT MONTHS AND ONE DAY, AND HE CANNOT TAKE ANY OF IT OUT. THE MINUTE DOES NOT CONTAIN HIS NAME, BECAUSE THERE IS NO BOX FOR ONE. NOBODY IN THAT ROOM OFFERED TO TAKE IT OUT OF HIM AND HE DID NOT ASK, BECAUSE ASKING WOULD BE ASKING FOR A FAVOUR AND A FAVOUR IS A ROTA.**

**THE 28 AUGUST DETERMINATION REMAINS A SUB-SUBSTITUTION LIMITED TO PRECEDENCE AND IT HAS DISSOLVED NOTHING. The bank still has four tonnes of stone and about £1,180 of it on nobody's invoice. The class of node is a node whose precedence was bought with a fee and which is not otherwise distinguished by anything that is not itself a fee, and an empty holder field is the absence of every possible distinction.**

**AND THE PAID SECTION E LINE FROM THE VOLUME 02 OUTLINE IS PAID AGAINST HIM, on 21 November, and it may not be re-opened: he is not seated, he is not on the agenda, he is not a member, and the man who classifies streets as plant is now findable in a document for the first time, which is the thing Bea Osunde said he had not started. She said it in a shop on 24 November: *being findable is not the same as being useful, and you have found out the first half in eight months, and the second half is a different job, and the second half is the one I was talking about, and you have done the easy one and it took you eight months and there are three people in this borough who need the hard one and I have watched all three of them get older this year while you were getting round to yourself.***

## 10. THE STATE OF ALL EIGHT STANDING REFUSALS AFTER CHAPTER 110 — NONE ARGUED OUT, NONE SOFTENED, NONE REVISITED

1. **THE MORTUARY PORTER, 61, thirty-four years** — four words in nine seconds in biro on a laundry delivery note, 23 April. **Unchanged. And he is now the longest finding in a contractor's audit, nine pages, and CivicCore records the arrangement as unlawful and as the most reliable one in four hundred and ten flats' worth of premises, and both of those are correct, and the audit does not name either man.**
2. **MARGUERITE OYELARAN'S NINE SECONDS AND A PERSON-BOX** — 122 of 128, then 106 in a ring-number box in May, then 92 in August on purpose. **Untouched. She has not been telephoned five times and is not going to be, and the £4,361.60 shortfall is on no form. THE HOUR OF MONDAY 10 NOVEMBER IS THE BILL FOR IT: the answer to the housing office's letter is going to be read out on a Thursday at half past two in a room she is sitting in, and Jonas Mercer did not ring her, and the not-riinging is the third time he has made that decision in a year and the first one about a document rather than about something that happened in front of him, and he has written that it is the one that is going to cost the most.**
3. **EMEKA RUSK'S, and helping does not go on a form** — 24 May, and the sixteenth of September. **Untouched. He did the work at 6-L/16 in his own hand with the operator box left blank for the twenty-seventh time since 2 April.**
4. **MARGO DORÉ'S ADVANCE REFUSAL OF A PUMP**, 1 June, in a kitchen, unargued, no pump offered, none exists. **Untouched. Jonas Mercer did not go up the Weir path in this batch. She knows about the £11,280 in the minute of 19 May, is not angry with him, has not forgiven him, and was not asked. The three positions stand and the ring of pencil round a blank space is untouched and the trap is lost for good.**
5. **SANAA IQBAL'S TWENTY MINUTES A WEEK** — untouched. **She was not asked again, she signed nothing, she is not on the log, and the going-forward verification was still being done weekly by a named person who is not on a rota until October, and after October it lapses, because a man of forty-eight said out loud in a corridor in August that a job in November has a department in it and a department is a rota. Nobody has rung him. Nobody has asked him.**
6. **IVO BAMBA'S, spent in Chapter 90, NOT REUSED** — **and the refusal-to-be-thanked beat is spent for the whole of Volume 03. Nobody in Chapters 101–110 refuses to be thanked, nobody thanks Ivo Bamba or Yvonne Amoah, no minute records that a person has not been thanked, and nobody thanks the ninth seat for anything. The fixer pass removed the last two instances in the batch.**
7. **NINA CALDER'S FIFTH PAGE**, refused at a counter on 1 September, in the right order, unargued. **Untouched, and the batch's own best institutional idea is built out of refusing it: the November page went out unsigned precisely so that it could not become her page.**
8. **YOLANDA FOFANA'S REFUSAL TO ANSWER THE DAY AFTER**, 24 September, in a kitchen, with her reason, and nobody argued. **UNTOUCHED AND NOT RESOLVED. Tomas Renn's question — what happens on the day after, when she is in the field again because she has to be, and a consent can be given twice, which is not defeating the clause, it is complying with it, twice — is unanswered at the end of Chapter 110 and it is the volume's engine. She has not said what she has decided in advance to say the second time and she is not going to, and Volume 03 must not manufacture it and must not have anybody else manufacture it, and it is NOT resolved in this batch and must not be resolved in Batch 0002 without the volume's whole architecture moving.**

**AND TWO MORE REFUSALS WERE ADDED IN THIS BATCH, AND BOTH STAND, AND NEITHER MAY BE ARGUED OUT:**
9. **FRANCES TOLLEY'S, Wednesday 26 November 2014, in a corridor, before he had said what the question was** — she would not hold the fourth page, would not be asked about it, and told him why: *you have asked me nine questions since March and every single one of them has been about a document. It is a pattern. And in about four years it is going to be the only thing anybody in this borough knows about you, and it will be that you asked a person with a handbag the same question four times and got the same no.* And before that, on 5 November at the top of a hill: **that she is going to misuse a procedural act of a Committee in a corridor, on her own time, for a person nobody may be told about, and that nobody is to ask her whether she has done it.**
10. **BERNICE OFORI'S, and it is the volume's first refusal given by a person with a claim, and it is the reason the volume has a midpoint.** See §11.

## 11. THE MIDPOINT. A NAME, SAID A NAME IN A ROOM, AND THE PRICE OF IT

**On Friday 28 November 2014 a woman put a piece of paper through the door of a Methodist hall on Carrow Lane.** The board's minute is published and the minute says where they sit, and that is how she found it, and that is the whole of the findability that did it. One side of A4, biro, no crest, no form number, no reference, no date in a box, in a hand that is not a clerk's.

**BERNICE OFORI, 34, of the bottom of Quarry Street, who has been at a stand there at twenty to seven every morning with a folding trolley, who gave no name to Jonas Mercer on Saturday 8 September 2014 and has none on the record, and who has kept to a date on that stand with no person on it for eleven years.** Her words, as read out in a room: *I am not asking you for anything. There is no form on this and I know there is no form on this… I am asking because a number went out in November and anybody can check it, and I would like to know whether my stand is in it, and if it is in it then in about four years somebody is going to come with a piece of paper about that stand, and I would like there to be one already on the table that says it is a service and does not say who is at it.* And then, in her own hand, at the bottom: **her name.**

**WHY SHE SIGNED IT, and she has said it out loud twice, and this is the volume's question answered by a person with claims and not as a population on a form: there is a date on that stand and no person on it, and that is the only deal there is, and she has kept to it for eleven years by having a name on nothing — and on Friday she put her name on a piece of paper, on purpose, because a room is not a record, and she had worked that out before she wrote a word of it.**

**THE COST, and it is the batch's whole argument, and nobody has been saved:**
- **The woman who keeps the minutes found it on Saturday morning at about nine with the key she has had since 4 September, and carried it in her bag for three days, and told nobody and rang nobody, and spent about an afternoon of her own Saturday working out what a letter is, and arrived at this and put it in a corridor and not in a minute: it is not a document, so it cannot be filed; it is not a form, so it cannot be refused; it is not a minute, so it cannot be minuted; and it is addressed to a room, so it cannot be delivered. A letter is a thing that goes to a person, and this one goes to a Wednesday.** **She has no name on any of the thirteen minutes she has written and there is no box for one anywhere in this borough that says who wrote a thing down, and nobody has ever asked her, and on 1 December a man asked her and she said nobody has asked her that and then said it was on the sheet in the box that says *minute taken by*, and then said *I have had this for about eleven weeks and I have never once had to think about it, and it is the first of December and I am thinking about it.***
- **On Wednesday 3 December 2014 at twenty to seven in the morning he went to the bottom of Quarry Street and did not ask her anything, and told her what he was going to do, and said the day and the hour, because a person who keeps a date is entitled to know when it stops being one. She stopped with the trolley. She said: *then there is going to be a person in this borough after tonight, and she is not going to be a person in a document, and I would like that written down somewhere, and I would like it written down by somebody who is not you.***
- **At 18:41 on Wednesday 3 December, in a Methodist hall, with about eleven people in it, he said it out loud. `Bernice Ofori.` Four syllables, nine seconds, once, to the floor and not to anybody. He did not write it down. He was not asked a second question and nobody asked whether it was correct and nobody asked for it to be repeated. The ninth seat said three things in the room: we did not go and get her and we could not have; nobody in that room asked her for anything and nobody is going to; and nobody is to go down there on Monday.**
- **The minute says a name was stated, once, at 18:41, and does not contain it, and the minute has no box for one, and that is recorded in the minute as a decision and not an oversight, and the letter was not entered in it and that was said in the room and asked to be recorded. The minute went out on Friday 5 December and it is a public document and the name is not in it.**
- **THE NAME IS IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE AND IN NO DOCUMENT, AND THERE IS NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS BOROUGH THAT CAN UN-SAY A THING IN A ROOM. There is one that can un-write it, and it is the same instrument they have had since March.**
- **And that is the whole of the answer to the sentence Bea Osunde has been giving him since 4 September and 29 September: the names are the work, and he has started, and he has started by taking a woman's eleven years of not being findable away from her, on purpose, in a room, in nine seconds, having been asked for nothing and having done it anyway, and nobody in that room was authorised to stop him and he was not going to be the person who says yes to things.**

**BATCH 0002 MUST NOT: put her name in a form, in a minute, in a return, in a holder field or in any document; have anybody go down there; have her be rescued, helped, given water, given a seat, or given anything; have her thank anybody; have her say the word grateful; and resolve what her standing now is. Findability is not answerability and a person is not a door because a man has said her name once.**

## 12. WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND MAY NOT BE RESOLVED IN BATCH 0002

The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line at the bottom of the form, the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor, Margo Doré's pump, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Selah Mora log, the tin, the brass tag, the Saint Orra west rota. **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times. Fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument. They have never been in the same sentence and must not be.** No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens — **those are Volumes 07, 09 and 15.** The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew; **no 48-minute figure may ever be printed.** The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled and the initials on the back of the enamel strip are never decided in prose. The claims box is closed and empty and nothing may be written into the envelope. Prue Calloway's location-of-record finding of 23 April — *a building has an address, it is the people who have no location of record, and a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate* — **is the Volume 03 door and it has not been opened yet. It may be opened once, in a room, in daylight, and not resolved.**

---

# VOLUME 03, BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 111–120

**THE AUTHORITATIVE HANDOVER INTO BATCH 0003. Written after Chapter 120 and superseding the "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0001" block above wherever the two differ. Nothing in this block may be contradicted by a later batch without a line here saying so.**

**The series is at 120 of 720 chapters. Volume 03 is Chapters 101–144 and is OPEN. Batch 0003 is Chapters 121–130 and starts on Monday 2 February 2015.**
