**Batch 0003's calendar, to be counted from the same anchor and checked against its own chapter headers when it is written: Monday 2 February 2015 · Wednesday 4 February · Friday 6 February · Monday 9 February · Wednesday 11 February · Friday 13 February · Monday 16 February · Wednesday 18 February · Friday 20 February · Monday 23 February · Wednesday 25 February · Friday 27 February · Monday 2 March · Wednesday 4 March · Friday 6 March · Monday 9 March · Wednesday 11 March · Friday 13 March. Saturdays 7, 14, 21 and 28 February and 7 March are available and are not the day anybody is going to write down.**

## 1. THE ANSWER TO THE HOUSING OFFICE'S LETTER, AS IT WAS READ OUT ON A THURSDAY, AND WHAT THE CHIEF OFFICER DID ABOUT IT

**Thursday 18 December 2014, half past two, the stock review, a room with a book in it, about nineteen people. Item seven. DOREEN WHITLOCK, about sixty, eleven years, reads every answer in that book aloud, and the book has a column in it for the person who reads. The answer, CivicCore Limited, received 27 November 2014, reference HC/CR/1147, verbatim:**

> **The association is not a party to CivicCore's service agreements and its premises are not within the scope of the audit.**

**Two lines. It is true. CivicCore paid about nine thousand pounds and four hundred evenings of two people's time to be able to write it, and said so on page one.**

**WHAT MARGUERITE OYELARAN DID, and it was the right thing and it is the worst thing that could have happened to her, and both of those are true at once:** she asked, from her seat, without waiting to be asked, for the answer to be minuted in the borough record **as a thing that has been said in a room**, so that the word *scope* would stand in two files in two buildings; and she said the reason out loud, in about eleven seconds, and the reason is the sentence of the batch: *because a thing that is wrong in one place and right in another is a thing that gets corrected in four years by whoever is left.* The chair could not give her a form of words for it and she asked for that to be recorded too.

**AND THE PRICE OF IT, and it is the thing to carry into Batch 0003: she has now made the word *scope* public twice, and in exchange she has a date against her name in a borough record and the word *disputes*, and a list is the only instrument this borough has ever offered her, and she has just made herself the person most likely to be asked for one. She has not produced a list. She is not going to. Her nine seconds and her person-box from March 2014 and her 92 in August all stand.**

**THE SECOND PUBLIC NUMBER ABOUT HER STOCK, AND THE FOURTEEN. A hundred and six was in a ring-number box in May, given to somebody who had not asked her for a list, because a ring-number box is a thing a person can hold up and a list is a thing a person has to make. Ninety-two was August, and she gave that one on purpose, having worked out that a number she has chosen is a number she can be held to. A member of that room worked the subtraction out loud on the Thursday. THERE ARE NOW TWO HUNDRED AND TWELVE, A HUNDRED AND SIX AND NINETY-TWO, AND A DIFFERENCE OF FOURTEEN BETWEEN THE LAST TWO, AND A HUNDRED AND SIX IN A HOUSING FILE AND A HUNDRED AND SIX IN A BOROUGH RECORD, and fourteen in a housing file reads as fourteen households and is not fourteen anything. It is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument, and there is no form anywhere on which anybody could write *this is not fourteen households*, and it must never be worked into one. Fourteen and one hundred and six were eleven weeks apart in the same file.**

**Jonas Mercer asked one question in the corridor outside that room before it started — *Who reads the answers out* — and none in the room, and he did not warn her, and standing at the back on the left he is the only person in this borough who understands what she did and has nobody he is allowed to tell.**

## 2. THE SAINT ORRA WEST TERM AFTER THE FOURTH OF JANUARY, WHO READ IT ALOUD, AND WHAT WAS AND WAS NOT WRITTEN DOWN

**The term expired at 06:00 on Sunday 4 January 2015 and the gate opened at 07:00, and the gate opened because of the handover on the Saturday night before and for no other reason.**

**WHO READ IT ALOUD, on Saturday 3 January 2015 at ten o'clock at night, in a waiting area off a corridor on level two with four chairs in it and a machine for hot water that has not worked since about August: EZRA MBEKI, who has been in and out of that building since 1992, and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, who is not on a roster because the roster does not exist. Neither is on any instrument in this city. Neither knew until about nine o'clock that they were going to be there. It took about nine minutes and then they sat down and went home at about half past six.**

**WHAT WAS WRITTEN DOWN: nothing. There is no document, no minute, no log entry and no date. There is no piece of paper in this city anywhere that says the handover for the term beginning 07:00 on Sunday 4 January 2015 was read at 22:00 on Saturday 3 January 2015, and there is not going to be one, and the reason it is not an oversight is that it is nobody's job. THE CLIPBOARD ON THE FOURTH CHAIR — eleven rows, the first four written on at some point in October by a man who then stopped — WAS NOT TOUCHED BY JONAS MERCER, and the reason, given out loud in a corridor twice this year to people who did not want an explanation, is that a log with one name in it is a rota with one name in it, and the day he signs it the man who stopped in October is a man who let somebody down.**

**HE WAS NOT AT THE HANDOVER AND THAT IS THE POINT. He stood four hundred yards away in the rain at about ten o'clock and did not come, and he did not telephone to ask whether it went, and ringing is the same as going: if he is in that corridor on the last night before a term expires then two tired people can say *he was there* in about four years, and that is a rota with one name on it. He did not use the building's keypad, did not go in, and stood on the first landing outside a fire door for about ten minutes on Monday 22 December for a different reason entirely (see §3).**

**THE ARITHMETIC, and it is not a fault anybody has been told about: a term is six days, the gate opens at seven, and once a week, every Sunday morning, for the hour between six and seven, there is a door that exists because a person decided to be there. In about four years somebody is going to write a schedule for that hour and the schedule will be good.**

**THE STATE OF THE SAINT ORRA WEST BOARD, unchanged and unchanged and unchanged: the 11:00–13:30 gap is published and unfilled and has been since 2 April. Bram Osei's Thursday is refused on the board in his own words — `B. OSEI. NO.` / *no reason I can give* / *(no reason given)* — the second line in somebody else's hand, and he has asked twice that it not be looked at. Priya Ranganathan's card is in a plastic sleeve on the Friday and Saturday rows because she put it there. The rota has still not been rebuilt. THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT WHO DID THE WEEKLY VERIFICATION FOR ABOUT ELEVEN WEEKS IN THE AUTUMN STOPPED IN OCTOBER, SAID IT OUT LOUD IN A CORRIDOR IN AUGUST — a job in November has a department in it and a department is a rota — HAS NOT BEEN ASKED ABOUT IT SINCE, NOBODY HAS RUNG HIM, AND THE AUTUMN COMPLIANCE REPORT IS OUT WITH A PARAGRAPH NUMBER ON IT IN FRONT OF FOUR HUNDRED AND TWENTY WOMEN.**

**AND THE NEXT ONE. A term read at 22:00 on Saturday 24 January 2015 began at 07:00 on Sunday 25 January and expires at 06:00 on Sunday 1 February 2015. A TERM IS SIX DAYS AND IT IS READ ON THE SATURDAY NIGHT BEFORE THE SUNDAY IT STARTS ON, SO ONE SATURDAY NIGHT IS A WEEK AFTER THE SATURDAY NIGHT BEFORE IT: 31 January, 7 February, 14 February, 21 February, 28 February, and 7 March 2015, which is the seventh of them in six weeks. Never say that a term is seven days: the term is six days (vol 1 ch 12/13, vol 2 ch 63/67, and chapter-0115 states the rule in full), and it is the SATURDAY NIGHTS that are a week apart, which is why there is an hour on every Sunday morning between six and seven when no term is in force. There is no date on any piece of paper in this city for any of them, and there is not going to be one for the seventh of them, which falls on Saturday 7 March 2015, because a person is not required to be told and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it.**

## 3. WHETHER ROYDEN ACHEBE WAS EVER TOLD, BY WHOM, AND WHAT IT COST

**HE WAS NOT TOLD. Nobody told him. Jonas Mercer did not go to him, did not telephone him, did not use the switchboard, did not go to his team, did not go to the building, and did not ask anybody about him. That is the whole of the answer and it stands.**

**Monday 22 December, a counter, one question in eleven words — *what would it take to reach Royden Achebe without a record* — and the answer was nothing, and the answer was exact: he has a post, a telephone on a switchboard with a queue and a person and a list, a team, a building, a reception with a book, a reporting officer number that is Jonas Mercer's and is on a piece of paper a man of forty-four signed in ninety seconds in August, and a duty at 6-L/16 described in two files that do not know each other exists. EVERY ROUTE TO HIM IS A DOCUMENT AND A PERSON WHO WILL REMEMBER IT.**

**A man of forty-nine is a door and a man of forty-four is a wall, and the difference between them has nothing to do with either of them. A man who can be asked anything, for ever, by anybody, is not a favour. He stood on a first-floor landing outside a fire door in Gantry Ward on Monday afternoon for about ten minutes and did not go in.**

**THE REFUSAL, given out loud in a shop on Wednesday 24 December, and it is the hardest refusal anybody in this borough has watched him make, and the reason it is the hardest is that nobody was arguing with him and nobody was asking him for anything: an apology from the man who named you in a room you were not in has to be said in a room; it cannot be said in a corridor, on a telephone, in the Methodist hall, in a records office, at a counter, in a meeting room, or on a bench outside a shop; **the only kind of room left in this borough where it could be said is somebody's kitchen, and he has not got one and is not entitled to one**, and turning up at a man's door at half past six is what he did to a woman of seventy-two in November and it cost her the last thing she had left. **He would rather be the man who did it in a room than the man who did it in a file. In a room it happened to him and he could answer back and it would be over. In a file it is a handle.**

**He wrote the reason down, and not the apology, on Wednesday 24 December, on the bench in the shop, on the back page of a pad where he keeps the things that are his, and Beatrice Osunde told him that is what a pad is for: *you have been writing things down since April for the people in the room. That last one is yours. Do not lose it and do not show it to anybody, and there is no box on it anywhere and that is also the point.***

**THREE MEN, none of them told anything, none of them resolved, none of them a rescue: the man of forty-four, who signed in ninety seconds and cannot be asked one question by anybody, ever; the man of forty-nine, who can be asked one question tomorrow morning and has not been; and a man of forty-eight who did a verification weekly for about eleven weeks in the autumn with no name against it and stopped in October and has not been asked about it since. FOUR THINGS IN THIS ACCOUNT THAT A PERSON HAS BEEN PUT INTO THAT THEY CANNOT GET OUT OF, THREE OF THEM JONAS MERCER'S, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO DO A FIFTH.**

## 4. WHETHER THE READING RULE WAS EVER USED IN A CORRIDOR, AND WHAT IS NOW KNOWN

**YES. ONCE. On a Tuesday in January 2015, in a corridor at the records counter on the second floor, at about eleven in the morning, in about eleven seconds, by FRANCES TOLLEY, clerk of twenty-two years, sixty-one, to PETER NAYLOR, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward. There is no date on it anywhere and nobody is going to be told which day it was. Nobody asked her to and nobody has asked her since and nobody may.**

- **HE HAS NOT BEEN TOLD HE WAS IN IT. He does not know what any of the words in the two entries mean and has never known, and no instrument in this borough can tell him, and Jonas Mercer did not tell him and is not going to.**
- **Jonas Mercer found out because the man said four words at the counter to somebody else, in about a quarter past eleven on Thursday 22 January, in the way a man says a thing that has come back to him about nine times and has nowhere else to go: `I was read to.`** The counter clerk told Jonas Mercer nothing and told him that she was not going to and that if he asked she would answer, because a counter is required to answer. **He did not ask. It is the fifth thing put in front of him about a person in eight months and he has stopped keeping a list.**
- **She is not going to say whether it worked and nobody is going to ask her. That was the arrangement on the hill in November and it has not been broken.**
- **The rule is still in her inside pocket. It has been there since 5 November. It goes to Salter's Yard on a Tuesday and comes back on a Tuesday, on her own time, forty-one minutes each way, in the wind, at sixty-one, and nobody knows and nobody is to know.**
- **THE PRICE OF THE DESIGN, said out loud in a corridor on 22 January, in about nine seconds, to the one person in the city who cannot use it: there is no way at all to say that she has not done it on a given day, and she would not want them to be able to, and on any given Tuesday nobody in the world can tell whether the rule was read in a corridor in Kestrel Hill or in Tideglass, including her. She can put it in a rota, a form, an establishment, a job description, an edition, a standing order or a handbook, and every one of those is the obvious solution and every one of them makes it a service.**
- **AND WHAT A CORRIDOR IS FOR, in her words, and this is the answer to the question he has been asking her since June: a corridor is the only place in this borough that has no minute in it, no agenda, no box, no name, no gallery and no publication, and a rule is a door, and a door in a corridor is a door that can be opened by a person on her own time for a person who turns up, and there is no other room in this city where that sentence is true.**
- **WHAT IT ESTABLISHED: nothing. And it can be argued with, and being arguable is the point, and the only sentence anybody in this borough is permitted to say about it afterwards is that a person was read two sentences and was able to say no and did not.**

**Beverley Annis still has no copy and has not asked for one. Oren Pell can still use it in a determination and nobody will let him. The Committee used it once. About four hundred people have added their names to a page that says a thing is arguable.**

## 5. THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS, THE SEVENTH REFUSAL, AND THE REASON COLUMN

**NINETEEN ENTRIES ALL BATCH. THE BLANK LINE AT THE BOTTOM IS BLANK AND WAS CHECKED AT TEN PAST TWO IN THE MORNING OF SUNDAY 4 JANUARY 2015 AND WAS BLANK, AND IT IS THE ONLY FIXED POINT IN THE BUILDING. IT DID NOT MOVE ONCE BETWEEN 26 NOVEMBER 2014 AND 28 JANUARY 2015, THIRTY-SEVEN DAYS BY 2 JANUARY, THE LONGEST IT HAD BEEN STILL SINCE 4 AUGUST.**

**THE SEVENTH REFUSAL. On Friday 2 January 2015 a letter arrived at the records counter addressed to NINA CALDER by name, which is not a thing anybody in this borough does, with a wrong reference in the corner in ballpoint. It is from **M. FURNEAUX, 61, resident, the rear block, Carrow Lane**, and it is the politest and the most sensible letter anybody has put in front of her in a year, and it is the most dangerous document anybody in this borough has handed to that counter since March, for one reason: HE IS NOT ASKING FOR A NAME. He is asking for a REASON — one line, free text, against each of the nineteen entries, not a drop-down, not a category, in the hand of the person who decided it, dated — because in about four years he would like to be able to sit in a kitchen and explain that board to somebody who is frightened of it, and he cannot, and the reason he cannot is that nobody has ever written down why.**

- **WHY IT IS THE MOST DANGEROUS THING: a reason column is a sentence column, and a sentence column is a name box with the serial numbers left off. A serial number cannot be read out; a sentence can, and there are nineteen of them, and decisions are made by people, and a sentence has a shape and a shape can be asked after. The serial numbers stay because the serial numbers are a machine. The sentences are about us.**
- **SHE REFUSED IN THE RIGHT ORDER AT A COUNTER IN A PUBLIC ROOM IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, in four numbered parts, and it is the seventh, and it stands, and nobody has come back to it and nobody may. The four parts are: the name box stays away by decision; a reason is a sentence and a sentence is a thing a person is found in; the only instrument this borough has ever offered anybody who needs it is a list and she has watched that go wrong twice this year, both times by people who meant no harm, and she was in the room the first time; and the reason she said it in eleven seconds and not one is that *one word is a door closing and eleven words is a door with a reason in it.***
- **THE ENVELOPE WENT BACK with nothing in it — no covering note, no refusal letter, no form, no box, no name, no reason, no date of her own — on Monday 5 January, with a stamp of the same denomination, and nobody will know that it went and nobody will know that it came back. He will write again in about four years and she will say no a second time in the same words and it will not be the eighth of anything, because it is not a list.**
- **HE HAS NOW GIVEN HER, ON THE RECORD, the reason she refuses and the reason the book exists: *there are two of us in this borough who could put that in a file before lunch and neither of us is going to, and that is the whole of what this borough has instead of a rule.* Neither of them has written any of it down.**
- **NINA CALDER HAS SIX REFUSALS IN HER IN NINETEEN YEARS AND DOES NOT CALL THEM THAT AND HAS NEVER CALLED THEM THAT, and there is now a seventh, and she wrote the six out once on a pad on the Friday night, in order, and crossed the list out and wrote under it: *seven. Stop counting.***

**The two conditions on the Old Ward board were re-read in the vestibule on 4 January at ten past two in the morning and on 26 and 28 January and are still not the same document and are still unreconciled: the city's own paper printed 14/8 initialled N.C., and sixteen inches below it an enamel strip with nothing on its face, undated, unattributed, with two initials and one word in pencil on the back of it, and the word is *not mine*. The initials are never decided in prose. The 1 March line is not reissued, restated or quoted.**

## 6. THE STANDING OF THE SIX LOWLAND WATER POINTS IN JANUARY, AND WHETHER A SEVENTH SHAPE WAS ADDED

**UNCHANGED, AND THE PRICE IS NOW A WINTER PRICE. Six water points at Tier 1, £1,880 each, £11,280 a year, paragraph 6.1 of the minute of 19 May, recorded as received and not as evidence, no party has been asked for any part of it, and the chair's marginal note from June stands: *I will not require a person at six water points. Noted. The reason nobody has asked is the reason this Committee cannot answer.*** On Tuesday 13 January 2015, in a Methodist hall, in daylight, in front of about nine people, GLORIA NYE did the conversion out loud: **that is one boiler in a small flat, the cheapest anybody quoted her in two years was £1,790 and she would not recommend it, and the reason the six of them cannot be bought at all is not that the people who need them are poor — the reason is that we are not on the map.** And the registrar: **a district that cannot pay has a seat and a person in it and has not thereby lost its vote. The reason there is no seat for the lowland is that a node with no party is not a party, and a place is not a person, and there is no instrument in this city by which a ninth district could be constituted in a Methodist hall by anybody in a bad mood. That reason is correct, has been correct since 1 March, and IS NOT GOING TO BE FIXED.**

**ABU BAKAYOKO, 58, who has had a stand at the top of the lowland market for twenty-six years and gets there at five in the morning with a bucket, two urns and no drain, and who pays a man to empty them twice a day and has done since about 1989 and does not know his name and has never once asked — HE ASKED TO BE COUNTED, in a room, in daylight, and what he wanted was not a seat and not money and not even a number: he wanted a line in a minute saying that a thing exists at a place where a thing is needed, in his words, because in about four years somebody is going to come with a piece of paper about that market and there is going to be nothing on the table that says anything was there.**

- **HE GAVE THE STATE OF FIVE OF THE SIX IN ABOUT TWO MINUTES WITHOUT STOPPING AND IT WAS THE BEST TESTIMONY THAT HALL HAS HAD IN FOUR MONTHS AND NOTHING IN IT WAS WRONG.** A standpipe on a post that runs when somebody has paid the water and has not run since Friday 28 November, and he has been carrying water in a bowser from the one at the church since that date at four pounds a bucket. A trough by the lock-ups, frozen since about 22 December. A stand with a bucket on a chain and a board of forty slots that works because a man puts money in the slot and takes the bucket and washes it and puts it back, and Jonas Mercer could not tell you his name either. A stand at the back of a school wall that runs on a timer for forty minutes at a quarter to eight and then is a pipe. And a tap by the yard, dry since the summer.
- **AND HE REFUSED THE SIXTH, out loud, before anybody asked, and asked for the refusal to be written down, and the reason is the volume's method in one sentence: there is somebody on that street who has told the county that nothing is there and she is right to and he is not going to be the person who takes it off her. NOBODY ASKED HIM. THE NINTH SEAT SAID: write down that a refusal was given at the end of the sixth and do not write down anything else.**
- **NO SEVENTH SHAPE WAS MADE AND THE REASON WAS GIVEN OUT LOUD IN SEVENTEEN WORDS BY JONAS MERCER, who asked no question in that room at all: A SHAPE HAS TO HAVE A PLACE YOU CAN POINT AT. THAT HAS A PERSON IN IT. It is in the minute. It is in nobody's document about the lowland. The sixth shape is where it has been since 28 October and it will not be named in this volume in any document that leaves the street. THE SEVENTH IS NOT RESOLVED.**
- **THE MINUTE WENT OUT ON THURSDAY 15 JANUARY 2015 AND IS PUBLIC AND MAY BE HAD AT A COUNTER FOR NOTHING. It has no number in it and no name in it and it records the state as a statement and not as a return, and it records the refusal, and it records that this body has no party to serve, no seat to refer the matter to, no money and no power to require any person to do anything, and it records THAT THIS BODY HAS DONE NOTHING, IN THE CORRECT WORDS, AND THAT THE DOING OF NOTHING IS NOT A DEFECT IN THE MINUTE. Nothing in the item requires any person to do anything.**
- **AND NOBODY WAS ASKED TO PAY ANYTHING, AND THE MAN AT THE BOWSER STILL DOES NOT GET HIS TUESDAY BACK, AND THE ONE BY THE YARD IS STILL DRY, AND THE MAN AT THE FORTY-SLOT BOARD IS STILL NOT FINDABLE FROM ANY RECORD IN THIS CITY. A person who is not counted is not a person who is helped, and no chapter in this batch has argued with that sentence.**

**THE WIER IS UNCHANGED AND UNRESOLVED: trap lost for good, bank gone, the three positions on a kitchen wall with a name against each, the fourth line with a ring of pencil round a blank space and nothing in the ring, Margo Doré not angry and not having forgiven him, nobody gone up that path since June, no pump offered and none to be offered, and there are pumps for sale in four shops in this borough at about £240 and not one of them has ever been in a van on that street. No Weir resident is described in any document that leaves the street, and nobody on the Weir has told the county, the department or a contractor that anything exists — and the one thing that has been said to the county from that street is a DENIAL that something is there, which discloses nothing, and it is the one thing on that street that is hers to give and nobody has taken it off her.**

## 7. THE SECOND PIECE OF PAPER, AND WHAT A NAME IN A ROOM IS NOT

**BERNICE OFORI'S STANDING AT THE END OF CHAPTER 120, and it is UNRESOLVED AND MAY NOT BE RESOLVED IN BATCH 0003: she is a name in about eleven people in a Methodist hall in Tideglass and in no document, and there is no list of who has it and there is not going to be one, and nobody is to go down there, and nobody has gone down there.**

**A NAME SAID IN A ROOM CANNOT BE:** un-said · produced · checked · argued with · minuted · published · attached to a claim · found by anybody who is not one of the eleven people who were in that room. There is no ninth item and there is not going to be one. In about four years somebody is going to come with a piece of paper about that stand, and there is not going to be one thing anybody in this borough can put beside it, and whether that is the best outcome available or the worst one is not going to be said this year.

**WHAT DID HAPPEN, and none of it was a rescue and nobody was helped and she was given nothing and asked for nothing:**

- **A printed slip came to the Methodist hall on Monday 19 January 2015 from COMMITTEE SERVICES, on borough stock, with a crest, a reference, an issue date and a form number, addressed to *the person who submitted correspondence to the Cooperative Steward Board*, care of the hall, because the return address on a letter that went through a door is the door. It says correspondence is not admitted to a sitting unless the writer is present, and that if you are not present your correspondence will be treated as withdrawn, and it carries THREE BOXES — *I will attend / I withdraw it / I have not been asked to do either* — and a line for a name and a line for a date, and the third of those three boxes is the only correct answer on the sheet and there is nowhere on the sheet to put it. NOBODY IN THAT HALL SENT IT. The body cannot require a person to attend a sitting it is not required to hold, and the date says *to be confirmed* and has been to be confirmed since 28 November.**
- **The woman who keeps the minutes put it in a tray under the trestle table, wrote a second, unsigned, handwritten note beside it in about four minutes, and left the two of them on the table at about half past three on Wednesday 21 January, and shut the hall and went out. She told nobody.**
- **At about twenty past four on Wednesday 21 January, in daylight, in a hall with the heating off and nobody in it, a woman of thirty-four came in through the door she had put a letter through on 28 November. Nobody went and got her. Nobody telephoned her. There is no number in this borough for anybody to telephone. Jonas Mercer was four hundred yards away at a counter on the second floor and did not know and was not told.**
- **She read both pieces of paper standing up in about four minutes, put no mark in any of the three boxes, and DID NOT WRITE ON THE PRINTED ONE. A sheet with a crest and a form number and a reference is a document, and a name at the top of a document is the first thing anybody would want in about four years, and nobody asked her for it. She wrote on the second one instead, which is a sheet of A4 with four lines of biro on it, unsigned, no heading, no box, no line for anything, and which cannot be used to show anybody anything at all. Her name, the street, and the date, in her own hand, in biro, in a line across the top like somebody signing a form — the first time in eleven years those four things have been on one piece of paper together, and she did it on purpose and in daylight — and then nine seconds, which cost her nothing:**

> **I have not withdrawn anything. I asked a question. There is no sitting to attend and nobody has asked me to attend one.**

- **She left both pieces of paper on the table and took nothing with her, because there was nothing of hers on that table to take: her own letter of 28 November has been in a handbag in a kitchen four hundred yards away since 3 December and it was still in it. She went out through the door at about ten to five, on a bus she had planned herself.**
- **The woman who keeps the minutes came back at eleven minutes past five, read the top of it twice, put the printed slip back in the tray where a printed thing goes, and put that whole sheet in a plastic wallet with a rubber band in the drawer of the trestle table, and shut the drawer. THERE IS NO KEY. She told nobody and is not going to tell anybody, there is no list of what is in any drawer in that building, and the only record in the world of any of this is a sheet of A4 with four lines of the minute-taker's own biro on it and four more of somebody else's above them, and anybody could have written it and nobody did.**

**THE TWO PIECES OF PAPER WITH HER NAME ON THEM ARE: her letter of 28 November, in a handbag in a kitchen off a landing, and her own four lines, written at the top of a sheet of A4 that already had four lines of the minute-taker's biro on it, in a plastic wallet in a drawer with no key in a Methodist hall. Neither is a document. Nobody may open either. The ninth seat has not been told which drawer, and neither has Jonas Mercer, and neither is going to be.** The ninth seat still has the November letter in her bag and has not decided anything about it and is not going to be told.

**AND THE ANSWER GIVEN IN A SHOP ON FRIDAY 23 JANUARY, by the ninth seat, to one question, and it is not an answer: *A person may put whatever she likes in her own bag. That has never needed an instrument and it is not going to get one.***

**NEITHER OF THE TWO PIECES OF PAPER IS A DOCUMENT. NEITHER HAS A CREST, A FORM NUMBER, A REFERENCE OR A BOX. NEITHER CAN BE USED FOR ANYTHING. HER NAME IS NOT ON THE PRINTED SLIP AND NEVER WAS, AND MAY NOT BE PUT ON IT. AND NEITHER SAYS ONE WORD ABOUT A STAND AT THE BOTTOM OF A STREET, WHICH IS WHY A PERSON WITH A FOLDER WILL BE ABLE TO PUT NOTHING ON THE TABLE AT ALL. A document is a lever; she has put her own name on two pieces of paper that are not documents; she is thirty-four; nobody was in that hall; she was given nothing; she came on a bus she had planned herself.**

## 8. THE SENTENCE, AND WHAT IS ON THE FLOOR, AND WHO SAID IT

**Asked in a room, in daylight, on Wednesday 28 January 2015, by a man of about fifty-six who is not a member, did not come as a member, and is not on any list, who said he had followed all of it from a car park and could not tell what any of it was for. He is the man who asked Jonas Mercer to chair on 3 December. He asked it because he is not on the agenda either, and he said so, and he was right.**

**Jonas Mercer said first that he was not going to answer it, and then answered in five items and about four seconds, and the answer is a sentence and not a scheme, and it is not the sentence anybody expected, and it is the sentence he has been carrying since 3 April without knowing it:**

> **A counter. A person who is at it. A state asked for instead of a list. A date. And a blank line at the bottom of a board that nobody is required to write on.**

**And where it came from, said out loud: the third of April was a Thursday and it was the night two people said no — to a rota, to a board, to a form, to a clause, separately, in their own words, and both of them were right — and at six nineteen in the morning a gate went up anyway, and the reason it went up was that two people happened to be in a corridor and neither of them was required to be and neither of them was on anything. I have spent eight months trying to say that in a sentence that would survive being written down, and it will not, and I have found that the only sentences that survive are the small ones with a date on them. He added, unasked: a description is not an answer; he has said what it is made of and not what it is for; three people in this borough know why and he is one of them and he is not going to say.**

**AND WHAT IS ON THE FLOOR, which is the volume's question asked as a physical question, and the answer is a room and not an institution: a counter along the left, eleven feet of it, with a slot in it for post that has been EMPTY since 18 July, not full, not emptied; a board at the end of it with nineteen entries and a blank line; two conditions sixteen inches apart above it with opposite provenance; a clerk from the second floor who comes down at about ten and about three; AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE A DAY, four on a wet one, between four and eleven since 4 September, counted on the back of a hand and not going in a book. The room is eleven feet by nine and nine people have to stand sideways in it.**

**AND THE SERVICE, at about ten past ten on Monday 26 January 2015, to a man with a bowser and a man on the bowser who wanted a state and not a list, in about eleven seconds, and it is now the sentence of the volume: a counter is required to answer and a counter is not required to make one; **it cannot be argued with because there is nothing in it to argue with, and that is the point, and it will be argued with, and any person may come back to that counter on any morning and say the state of the market standpipe is not that, and be right, and be argued with, and get a second date.** It is not an institution, it does not scale, it has no form, and it is the cheapest thing anybody in this borough has produced this year and it is worth what it costs, which is eleven seconds. The registrar, in the corridor outside, gave the two sentences the volume runs on and did not come in, because a registrar who comes into a room of eleven feet by nine turns it into a room he was in: *a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for in public, repeatedly, by people who are not paid to argue* (Rook's sentence about Pell, August), and *a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to* (Pell's, 2 June), and *a list of who needs it is a list of who exists, and in four years the list is in a drawer with a date on it, and the date is the day somebody asked, and the asking is the harm.***

## 9. THE DOOR, OPENED ONCE

**PRUE CALLOWAY, 58, borough safety panel chair, eleven years. HER FINDING OF 23 APRIL 2014 WAS STATED OUT LOUD, ONCE, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, ON WEDNESDAY 28 JANUARY 2015, by Jonas Mercer, to the floor and not to anybody, in about eleven seconds, and it has now been opened and it did not open all way:**

> **A building has an address. It is the people who have no location of record. And a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate.**

**AND WHAT WAS SAID AFTER IT, and it is the position going into Batch 0003: it is in a minute, nothing in it requires any person to do anything, no person in the room was asked to do anything, and it is not resolved. He did not say what he thinks it means. He said that he has wanted to say what he thinks it means in a room since August, and that there is a fourth page of a regulation from 2008 that three people in this borough have read and told nobody, **that he is not one of the three and is not going to say who the three are** (they are Frances Tolley, Oren Pell and Nina Calder, per the Batch 0001 block, and he has never read it), and that he is not going to say what is on it in a room with nine people in it, and he asked for that to be minuted as a decision and not as a virtue. NOBODY WAS TOLD WHAT THE FINDING MEANS AND THE NINTH SEAT WAS IN THE ROOM AND WAS NOT TOLD ANYTHING.**

## 10. THE STATE OF ALL TEN STANDING REFUSALS AFTER CHAPTER 120 — NONE ARGUED OUT, NONE SOFTENED, NONE REVISITED

1. **THE MORTUARY PORTER, 61, thirty-four years** — four words in nine seconds in biro on a laundry delivery note, 23 April. **Unchanged.** CivicCore's audit records the arrangement as unlawful and as the most reliable one in four hundred and ten flats' worth of premises and does not name either man.
2. **MARGUERITE OYELARAN'S NINE SECONDS AND A PERSON-BOX** — 122, then 106 in a ring-number box in May, then 92 in August on purpose. **Untouched, and it cost her on 18 December** (§1). She has not been telephoned five times and is not going to be, and the £4,361.60 is on no form, and she has not produced a list.
3. **EMEKA RUSK'S, and helping does not go on a form** — 24 May and 16 September. **Untouched.**
4. **MARGO DORÉ'S ADVANCE REFUSAL OF A PUMP**, 1 June, in a kitchen, unargued, no pump offered, none exists. **Untouched.** He did not go up the Weir path in this batch. She knows about the £11,280, is not angry, has not forgiven him, and was not asked. The three positions stand and the ring of pencil round a blank space is untouched.
5. **SANAA IQBAL'S TWENTY MINUTES A WEEK** — untouched. The log has carried no name since 9 May, the weekly verification has not happened, and the man of forty-eight who did it for about eleven weeks stopped in October and **has still not been asked and has still not been rung**, and the autumn compliance report is out with a paragraph number on it in front of four hundred and twenty women.
6. **IVO BAMBA'S**, spent in Chapter 90, **NOT REUSED**, and the refusal-to-be-thanked beat remains spent for the whole of Volume 03. **Nobody in Chapters 111–120 refuses to be thanked, no minute records that a person has not been thanked, and the ninth seat is not thanked for anything.**
7. **NINA CALDER'S FIFTH PAGE**, refused at a counter on 1 September, in the right order, unargued. **Untouched, and the seventh refusal of 2 January is built out of it** (§5).
8. **YOLANDA FOFANA'S REFUSAL TO ANSWER THE DAY AFTER**, 24 September. **UNTOUCHED AND NOT RESOLVED. Tomas Renn's question is unanswered and is still the volume's engine. She has decided nothing in advance, has said nothing, has said she is not going to, and on 11 December in a kitchen she said she has not got an answer ready and is not going to get one ready in a room, and that it is the same refusal as the day after and the same reason and it stands. That is not an answer to the question and it is not a manufacture of one, and BATCH 0003 MUST NOT RESOLVE IT.**
9. **FRANCES TOLLEY'S**, Wednesday 26 November 2014, in a corridor, before he had said what the question was — **and a FIFTH on Thursday 22 January 2015 in a corridor, about a document, which she gave the price of out loud in the same nine seconds** (§4). **The reading rule has been used once, in a corridor, on her own time, at her own risk, and she is not going to be asked whether it worked.**
10. **BERNICE OFORI'S**, and it is the volume's first refusal given by a person with a claim. **Unchanged, unresolved, and a SECOND refusal was written on 21 January 2015 in nine seconds with her name at the top of it, on her own initiative, in daylight, on a second unsigned handwritten note the woman who keeps the minutes had left on a trestle table — and NOT on the printed slip, which nobody sent, which she was never asked for, and which has no name on it and may never have one** (§7).

**AND ONE MORE, WHICH IS NEW IN THIS BATCH AND STANDS, AND IT IS A THIRD MAN:** a man of forty-eight, in a corridor, in August 2014, who said that a job in November has a department in it and a department is a rota, who did a weekly verification for about eleven weeks without a name on it, who stopped in October, and **whom nobody has asked about it since and whom nobody has rung.** (§3)

## 11. WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND MAY NOT BE RESOLVED IN BATCH 0003

The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line at the bottom of the form, the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor, Margo Doré's pump, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Selah Mora log, the tin, the brass tag, the Saint Orra west rota, the 11:00–13:30 gap, Bram Osei's Thursday, the unverified compliance week, the man of forty-eight, the roof void at Carrow Lane, the second of the pair that did not go into the register, the third of the four differences between the two public records, the fourth of the four, the two conditions on the Old Ward board and the initials on the back of the enamel strip, and the seventh shape. **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times; nine hundred and eighty-six is a number of Wednesdays; fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument. None of the three has ever been a number of people and none of them may be, and they have never been in the same sentence and must not be.** The claims box is closed and empty and the sealed envelope may not be opened or written into. **The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew; no 48-minute figure may ever be printed; the forty *hours* of 3 April is the gate being open and is a different number about a different thing.** No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens — **those are Volumes 07, 09 and 15.** **A person who is not counted is not a person who is helped, and Volume 03 may not refute that sentence in a chapter.** The ninth seat is not a chair and may not be made into one, and **there is no chair of that body at all** — corrected in this batch, because the Batch 0001 material said it had none and Volume 03 must say so. **Prue Calloway's finding was opened on 28 January 2015, once, in a room, in daylight, and it is not resolved, and it may not be opened a second time.**
