# VOLUME 03, BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 121–130

**THE AUTHORITATIVE HANDOVER INTO BATCH 0004. Written after Chapter 130 and superseding the "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0002" block above wherever the two differ. Nothing in this block may be contradicted by a later batch without a line here saying so.**

**The series is at 130 of 720 chapters. Volume 03 is Chapters 101–144 and is OPEN. This batch closed the volume's second half; it did not close the volume. Batch 0004 is Chapters 131–140 and runs from SATURDAY 14 MARCH 2015 TO WEDNESDAY 29 APRIL 2015, ten cards inside that window, and the 14 March handover is staged once in Chapter 0131.**

## 0a. REPAIRS APPLIED TO THIS BLOCK AFTER REVIEW — READ THIS BEFORE THE PROSE

**A reviewer found that nine slips in a drawer and about eleven people a day could not both be true, and that a character broke the batch's hardest rule by saying the number out loud. BOTH WERE REPAIRED IN THE CHAPTERS AND NOT BY CHANGING THE PLOT, and the following is now canon and supersedes any earlier wording in this block or in any other state file:**

1. **NINE SLIPS IS CORON AND THE THROUGHPUT IS CORON AND THEY ARE TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT THINGS.** A slip is only written when a person asks what was true on a day. Out of nine people a day, and then eleven, the ones who ask that are three men who have all come more than once, and one other person on a Friday afternoon in the last week of February about whom nobody has said a word, and that is the whole of them. **The two may never be added together, and a form that added them would say *this counter sees eleven people and produces one document*, which is a level of service, and there is no level of service in that room.** Chapter 0121 now carries the reason in her own mouth on 2 February, Chapter 0123 states the separation in the corridor, and Chapter 0129 states it again in the inventory and accounts for all nine.
2. **THE NINE, EXACTLY: 26 Jan (Abu, five nodes on one slip) · 2 Feb (Abu, the trough) · 2 Feb (Otieno) · 4 Feb (Otieno) · 5 Feb (Bramble) · 5 Feb (Otieno) · 6 Feb (Otieno) · 13 Feb (Abu, five things, sixth declined) · and one written on a Friday afternoon in the last week of February to somebody who was not in a room with anybody, about whom nobody has said a word and nobody is going to.**
3. **NINA CALDER GIVES NO COUNT TO ANYBODY.** In Chapter 0129 she no longer says *there are nine pieces of paper in the drawer*, and she no longer says *there are about eleven people a day who have not worked that out*. She describes the drawer and gives no figure, which is correct, and the answer is better for it. **The count of nine is the count of this account and not the count of a person, and no character in the batch knows it.**
4. **THE TOTAL OF STANDING REFUSALS IS ELEVEN, NOT TWELVE, AND THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT IS THE ELEVENTH.** The reason column refused on 2 January and again on 16 February is ONE request refused twice and is not two refusals, which is what Nina Calder says out loud in Chapter 0125.
5. **THE FIRST REFUSAL TO FIND SOMEBODY IS THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT, IN CHAPTER 0122, AND IT IS THE FIRST IN NINE MONTHS.** The second is Royden Achebe on 25 February, twenty-one days later, which is what Chapter 0127 already says. The old phrase *the second time in three days* was an arithmetic impossibility and is gone.
6. **THE BATCH 0004 CALENDAR WAS EXTENDED, NOT THE CARDS MOVED.** All ten cards now sit inside 14 March – 29 April 2015, and the 14 March handover is staged once, in Chapter 0131. The Saturday-night numbering is fixed at 24 Jan = 1 through 2 May = 15.
7. **EMPHASIS.** The batch was set 48–83 per cent in bold against a prior-batch baseline of about 45. It is now 42–52 per cent and the batch mean is 44, the prose is unchanged word for word, and the bold markers are the only thing that was touched. Nothing else about the chapters was reformatted, nothing was cut, and no sentence was reordered.

## 0. The calendar, checked against the chapter headers of Chapters 121–130

**Volume 03's anchor remains Chapter 101 = Monday 20 October 2014, counted from Chapter 21 = Thursday 13 March, and never to be reconciled against the real calendar.**

**Batch 0003 as written, line by line: Monday 2 February 2015 · Wednesday 4 February into Friday 6 February · Friday 6 February into Monday 9 February · Wednesday 11 February into Friday 13 February · Monday 16 February into Wednesday 18 February · Friday 20 February into Monday 23 February · Wednesday 25 February into Friday 27 February · Monday 2 March into Wednesday 4 March · Friday 6 March into Monday 9 March · Wednesday 11 March into Friday 13 March.**

**Batch 0004's calendar, to be counted from the same anchor: Monday 16 March 2015 · Wednesday 18 March · Friday 20 March · Monday 23 March · Wednesday 25 March · Friday 27 March · Monday 30 March · Wednesday 1 April · Friday 3 April · Monday 6 April, and the batch may reach Wednesday 8 April. Saturdays 14, 21 and 28 March are available and are not the day anybody is going to write down.**

## 1. THE SAINT ORRA WEST TERM AFTER 1 FEBRUARY AND AFTER 1 MARCH, WHO READ IT ALOUD, WHAT WAS AND WAS NOT WRITTEN DOWN, AND THE FIFTH REFUSAL

**THE RULE, unchanged and restated because it is the single most mis-stated fact in this account: A TERM IS SIX DAYS. IT IS READ AT 22:00 ON THE SATURDAY NIGHT BEFORE THE SUNDAY IT STARTS ON. THE GATE OPENS AT 07:00 ON THAT SUNDAY. THE TERM EXPIRES AT 06:00 ON THE FOLLOWING SUNDAY. THE SATURDAY NIGHTS ARE WHAT ARE A WEEK APART, WHICH IS WHY THERE IS AN HOUR ON EVERY SUNDAY MORNING BETWEEN SIX AND SEVEN WHEN NO TERM IS IN FORCE. NEVER CALL THE TERM SEVEN DAYS. NEVER CALL IT A WEEK.**

**THE CHAIN, now written out in full, and every one of these has been reached and none of them has a document: read 22:00 Sat 24 January 2015 → began 07:00 Sun 25 January → expired 06:00 Sun 1 February. Read Sat 31 January → began Sun 1 February → expired 06:00 Sun 8 February. Read Sat 7 February → began Sun 8 February → expired 06:00 Sun 15 February. Read Sat 14 February → began Sun 15 February → expired 06:00 Sun 22 February. Read Sat 21 February → began 07:00 Sun 22 February → expired 06:00 Sun 1 March. Read Sat 28 February → began 07:00 Sun 1 March → expired 06:00 Sun 8 March. Read Sat 7 March → began 07:00 Sun 8 March → expired 06:00 Sun 15 March. Read Sat 14 March → began 07:00 Sun 15 March → expired 06:00 Sun 22 March.**

**WHO READ THEM ALOUD, on every one of them since Saturday the third of January 2015: EZRA MBEKI, who has been in and out of that building since 1992, and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, who is not on a roster because the roster does not exist. Neither is on any instrument in this city. Neither knew until about nine o'clock that they were going to be there. It took about nine minutes and then they sat down and went home at about half past six. NOTHING WAS WRITTEN DOWN, nothing is going to be, and the reason it is not an oversight is that it is nobody's job. There is no form in this borough on which anybody could record that a handover happened. JONAS MERCER HAS NEVER BEEN TOLD THAT ANY OF THEM OCCURRED, HAS NEVER ASKED, AND IS NOT GOING TO ASK, and asking would be the whole of the problem.**

**WHAT WAS AND WAS NOT WRITTEN DOWN, on the term in force at the end of this batch: nothing. There is no document, no minute, no log entry and no date. The eleven-row clipboard with the first four rows written on in October by the man who then stopped is on the fourth chair of the waiting area and Jonas Mercer has not touched it and was not asked to and would not.**

**AND THE FIFTH REFUSAL, which is the one this batch spent, and it is a refusal and not an absence, and it was said out loud:**

- **He had been in that corridor ONCE, at half past four in the morning on Sunday 4 January 2015, for the last hour and a half of a term. He had not gone on the Saturday night before it and had stood four hundred yards away in the rain and had not telephoned, because ringing is the same as going. And on Friday 27 December, in a different building and for an entirely different reason, he had stood on a first landing outside a fire door for about ten minutes and had not used the keypad. Three decisions, all different.**
- **AND THEN THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY CAME AND WENT AND HE DID NOT GO AND HE DID NOT DECIDE NOT TO GO, AND THERE WAS NOTHING ON ANY DAY OF THAT WEEK THAT SAID A MAN HAD CHOSEN ANYTHING. That was the fourth one. That is what a fourth one is. A fourth is a habit, and a habit is a rota with one name in it, and in about four years that is not a memory, that is a duty.**
- **ON MONDAY THE SECOND OF MARCH AT ABOUT ELEVEN IN THE MORNING, IN A GROUND-FLOOR CORRIDOR AT SAINT ORRA WEST, IN DAYLIGHT, ON FOOT, OFF THE STREET, THE WAY ANYBODY WALKS INTO A HOSPITAL, HE TOLD BRAM OSEI OUT LOUD, ON PURPOSE, THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO BE IN THAT ROOM ON SATURDAY THE SEVENTH OF MARCH.** He is not on the rota, not on a board, not a member of anything, and there is no box anywhere in that building that says he may not be in the corridor. He told the porter he was not going to ask him a question and he did not ask him one.
- **BRAM OSEI'S ANSWER, and he is a porter and the day bearer and he has a refused Thursday on a board in his own handwriting with *no reason I can give* under a *no reason given* in somebody else's hand: `Then I shall not say that you were there, and I shall not say that you were not there, and I have not said it to anybody, and a thing said by two people in a corridor is a thing that two people know, and I do not want to be two.`** He has told nobody. Nobody has asked him.
- **THE COST OF IT: the cost of it was that he made a porter a person who knows something, on purpose, having told that same porter six weeks earlier that the only instrument in this borough that keeps a man from being askable is a man who does not know. He said so in the corridor and did not apologise for it and Bram Osei agreed that it was worse. It is the only thing Jonas Mercer has said in this account that is actually about himself.**
- **AND THE FINDING OF THE CHAPTER, which is the finding of the whole batch: THE MAN WAS NEVER IN IT. THE GATE HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN OPENED BY ANYTHING HE DID IN A CORRIDOR AT HALF PAST FOUR IN THE MORNING, AND THE ONLY REASON HE WAS EVER THERE IS THAT HE WAS NOT REQUIRED TO BE ANYWHERE.**

**THE BOARD AT SAINT ORRA WEST, unchanged and unchanged and unchanged: the rota has still not been rebuilt and cannot be; the 11:00–13:30 row is still empty and still not shaded and still does not say *vacant*; Bram Osei's Thursday is still refused in his own words and he has asked twice that the second line not be looked at and nobody has looked at it; Priya Ranganathan's card is still in a plastic sleeve on the Friday and Saturday rows because she put it there. A counter in this borough was asked nothing about the gate by anybody else, and looked for nothing about the gate, and the reason, given out loud, is that a counter with an opinion about a gate is a service — and the only person who asked anything about that gate asked in order to be told that looking would be the wrong thing to do, and she told him, and the sentence is below: `if I went looking and found that there was a connection, then I would have created it by looking, and you would have created it by asking, and neither of those would be a fact about a gate. They would be a fact about two people in a corridor on a Wednesday.`**

## 2. THE SHELF: HOW MANY, WHO WROTE THEM, WHAT THEY ARE ABOUT, AND WHY THEY ARE NOT A SERIES

**NINE PIECES OF PAPER IN THE DRAWER UNDER THE GROUND-FLOOR COUNTER, AS AT MONDAY 9 MARCH 2015. The drawer is not locked, there is no key on it, there is no list of what is in it, and a tray is sitting on top of it. Jonas Mercer has seen the edges of them from about four feet away and HAS NOT COUNTED THEM, HAS NOT LOOKED, AND HAS NOT ASKED. The number nine is in this account because this account has been counting things in this borough that nobody is supposed to be counting since the fourth of September, and that is the only sin it has.**

**AND NINE SLIPS AND ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY ARE TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT THINGS, THEY ARE NOT A CONTRADICTION, AND THEY MUST NEVER BE ADDED TOGETHER. THE REASON IS THE BATCH'S OWN AND IT WAS GIVEN OUT LOUD ON 2 FEBRUARY AND WAS NOT UNDERSTOOD AT THE TIME: a piece of paper only gets written when a person stands in front of her and asks what was true on a day. Out of nine people a day, and then eleven, the ones who ask that are three men who have all come more than once, and one other person on a Friday afternoon in the last week of February about whom nobody has said a word, and that is the whole of them. A form that added the two numbers together would be a form that said *this counter sees eleven people and produces one document*, and that is a level of service, and there is no level of service in that room and there is not going to be one. THIS IS CANON AND IT IS BINDING ON EVERY LATER BATCH.**

- **WHO WRITES THEM: NINA CALDER, about fifty, nineteen years in that office, designing lists since 1999, who comes down from the second floor at about ten and at about three because nobody has ever told her to and nobody could. ONE HAND. A date on each and a hand on each and NO reference, NO number, NO crest, NO form, NO box, and NO name on any of them, including hers.**
- **WHAT THEY ARE ABOUT: a market and a trough; the six lowland water points; a standpipe on a post at the bottom of a car park; and a great many other things that about eleven people a day bring in off the street with them. That last clause is the reason there are nine of them and not three hundred: eleven people a day come through that door and almost none of them ask what was true on a day.**
- **THE NINE, EXACTLY, AS AT 9 MARCH 2015: 26 January, Abu Bakayoko, five nodes on one slip. 2 February, Abu Bakayoko, the trough. 2 February, Emmanuel Otieno, the tap on the yard wall. 4 February, Otieno. 5 February, Howard Bramble, the standpipe at the bottom of his car park. 5 February, Otieno. 6 February, Otieno — which is the seventh she had written since the twenty-sixth of January and the fourth for the same man. 13 February, Abu Bakayoko, five things with the sixth declined. And a ninth, written on a Friday afternoon in the last week of February, to somebody who was not in a room with anybody, which nobody has said a word about and which nobody is going to.**
- **SHE KEEPS A COPY OF EACH. The person who is handed a slip carries it away in a pocket and that is the end of it. This was established out loud on 2 February and is canon.**
- **THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS BOROUGH, ON ANY COUNTER, ON ANY TRAY, IN ANY OFFICE, ON WHICH ANYBODY COULD WRITE *THESE ARE NOT A SERIES*. A BOX THAT SAYS *NOT A SERIES* IS A SERIES. Nobody put them there on an instruction. There is no rota, board, establishment, job description, edition, standing order or handbook, and she is not on one and cannot be made to be on one.**
- **NOBODY HAS ASKED THE COUNTER HOW MANY OF THEM THERE ARE AND SHE IS NOT GOING TO TELL, and she has said out loud that if he asks she will answer, because a counter is required to answer, and that she will answer in a way that is a counter's answer, and that she would rather he did not spend his question on it. THE REASON IS NINE WORDS AND IT IS IN A PAD AND NOWHERE ELSE: A NUMBER OF WHAT A COUNTER DOES IS A LEVEL OF SERVICE, AND THE DAY A FLOOR HAS A LEVEL OF SERVICE SOMEBODY CAN CUT IT.**
- **AND THE ARITHMETIC OF A DRIFT, which is this batch's engine and Jonas Mercer's own finding, worked out in the corridor outside on Monday 2 February in about eleven minutes: A STATE GIVEN ON A TUESDAY CAN BE WRONG ON THE WEDNESDAY. That is not a fault, that is what a state is. And a counter that is required to answer and is not required to make one will go on answering the same state for as long as nobody comes back to argue it. THERE IS NO OBLIGATION IN THIS CITY TO COME BACK. THERE IS NO FORM ON WHICH A PERSON MAY WRITE *I DISAGREE WITH A STATE GIVEN ON A DATE*. A COUNTER DOES NOT DRIFT BECAUSE ANYBODY DID ANYTHING WRONG; A COUNTER DRIFTS BECAUSE THE ONLY THING THAT STOPS IT MOVING IS A PERSON TURNING UP, AND A PERSON TURNING UP IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT, IT IS A COINCIDENCE, AND A COINCIDENCE EVERY WEEK IS A SERVICE.**
- **AND THE THIRD THING, which nobody is going to be in the room for: a shelf grows the way the register of narrowings grew, out of correct things done by careful people; nobody is hurt by it; and in about four years somebody is going to use one of them against somebody, and it will not be the bulb. NINA CALDER SAID ON 1 DECEMBER THAT A REGISTER THAT GROWS WHEN NOBODY IS HURT IS A REGISTER SOMEBODY CAN HOLD OVER YOU, AND THAT IF ANYBODY USES THAT BOARD AGAINST ANYBODY THEY WILL NOT USE THE METER ROOM, THEY WILL USE THE BULB. She is about to be proved right about this class of thing for a second time, and it will not be about the bulb, and Jonas Mercer has worked out that he is not going to be in the room and has not told her.**
- **THE INTERVAL, worked out in the corridor on Friday 13 February: three pieces of paper in this borough mention the trough by the lock-ups. The gap that matters is the second of February to the thirteenth. THAT IS ELEVEN DAYS. A fortnight is the shortest word anybody in this borough has for an interval like that one; a week is a word because a Saint Orra west term is six days and is read on a Saturday night; eleven days has no word; and the whole of the difference between a piece of paper and a series is that a series has a word for the gap. Somebody in this borough is going to work out what eleven days is.**

## 3. THE SECOND AND THIRD ASKER, WHAT WAS OFFERED, AND WHAT IT WAS WORTH

**THE THREE, and they are the only people in this borough who have come to that counter about a thing, and none of them is on a list.**

1. **ABU BAKAYOKO, 58**, twenty-six years at a stand at the top of the lowland market, the man with the bowser and a man on the bowser paid on Fridays out of his own pocket. He was the first asker on Monday 26 January. **HE CAME BACK TWICE IN THIS BATCH.**
2. **EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38.** Agency, ten years, on the books of four different agencies in nine years and the same two in the last four. Nights at a depot, four to ten, and a second job on Fridays he does not talk about. Rents the top floor of a building on a road he has never seen the name of on any paper. **A daughter of nine who walks to a bus stop at the end of that road.** He is not on any list, is not a member of anything, has no claim, no reference, no party and nothing in any file in this city. He has been to a counter five times in his life: four of them in that vestibule between the second and the sixth of February, and one in another town in January that took eleven days and had a reference on it.
3. **HOWARD BRAMBLE, 61.** Fourth floor of a block of forty flats in Tideglass, on the estate that has a parade of shops on the top of it. Nineteen months. A wife who cannot carry anything heavier than a kettle. He went and asked for the unsigned extract himself in the third week of January and read it. He is not on any list.

**WHAT EACH OF THEM ASKED FOR, AND WHAT EACH OF THEM WAS GIVEN, AND WHAT IT WAS WORTH. THE PRICE OF ALL THREE IS THE SAME AND IT IS ELEVEN SECONDS, AND NOT ONE OF THE THREE IS BETTER OFF THAN HE WAS.**

- **ABU BAKAYOKO, 2 FEBRUARY: the trough by the lock-ups came back on the Friday night. It is not frozen and there is about two inches of something green in the bottom of it that is not water, and he has to decide by lunchtime whether to fill from it. He was given a second date on a second slip saying what was true at about ten past ten that morning and nothing else. He asked `Is it drinkable` — three words, and it was not Jonas Mercer's question and it never was, and he asked no question in that room at all — and was told no, in one go: she cannot tell him what is in it, nobody in that building knows what is in it, there is no record of what is in any trough in this borough, and if she looked it up she would be a records counter that has told a man whether to put water in a bowser, and that is a service, and she would not know on Tuesday what she had done.**
- **ABU BAKAYOKO, 13 FEBRUARY: the second asker, and the second asker is not the first asker, because A MAN WHO ASKED ONCE COULD STILL BELIEVE IT WAS AN ACCIDENT, AND FOUR WEEKS IS LONG ENOUGH TO STOP BELIEVING THAT. He brought the copy of the minute of 15 January in a plastic wallet and read the words `as a statement and not as a return` out loud twice, slowly, the way a man reads a thing he has read before to somebody he is trying to convince. HER NINE SECONDS: `It is a statement, and it stands, and a statement is a thing that is true, and it is on the public record, and nobody may take it off. It is not a return. A return is a thing that goes to somebody who has to do something about it, and there is no somebody and there has not been one since the first of March and there is not going to be one this year.`**
- **AND THEN SHE OFFERED SOMETHING, and it is the first thing anybody in this borough has offered anybody in about four months, and it is worth nothing and it works, and it is not a service and it is not a scheme: A SECOND DATE, ON A SECOND SLIP, SAYING WHAT WAS TRUE ON THAT DATE, SIGNED BY NOBODY, KEPT BY A PERSON IN A VESTIBULE ELEVEN FEET BY NINE. He would have it back on any morning: she would tell him what is true that morning and put a date on it, and if it matched the one in his pocket then the two pieces of paper were the same piece of paper twice, and if it did not then the one in his pocket was a thing that was true and has stopped being true, and both of those are free.**
- **AND IT WORKS, and the sentence is the batch's: A MAN WHO CAN BE WRONG IN PUBLIC IS NOT A MAN WHO HAS TO BE RIGHT IN A VAN. Nothing about the six water points has changed. Four pounds a bucket is four pounds a bucket. The tap by the yard is dry and will be dry in June. Nobody in this borough is going to get their Tuesday back. What he has got is the ability to be wrong in public on a date, and a date is the cheapest thing in this city, and it is worth what it costs, which is eleven seconds.**
- **EMMANUEL OTIENO, 6 FEBRUARY: he asked for two things in one go. He was given a state and a date about the tap on the wall of the yard where he parks the van, in about eleven seconds, the SEVENTH TIME SHE HAD WRITTEN ONE FOR ANYBODY SINCE THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JANUARY AND THE FOURTH TIME SHE HAD WRITTEN ONE FOR THE SAME MAN. He was refused, in about eleven seconds, on the bus stop at the bottom of his road, and he was not angry, and the reason is that she can be held to what is true on a date and that is a thing about a time, and he asked for a thing about a place, and a place does not hold still, and nobody has ever written down anything about a bus stop in this borough by anybody on any date, and the day she goes and finds out is the day that counter starts having a department. A COUNTER IS REQUIRED TO ANSWER. A COUNTER IS NOT REQUIRED TO GO AND LOOK. THOSE ARE TWO DIFFERENT JOBS AND THIS BOROUGH HAS MONEY FOR NEITHER OF THEM AND A PERSON FOR ONE OF THEM. He said eleven words on the way out the door, and they are the finding of the batch: `A person can ask a question here and get a date.`**
- **AND HE WORKED OUT THE REST OF IT HIMSELF, in a kitchen, on the Sunday, alone, and nobody taught him and nobody in this borough is able to tell him what to do with it: THE DIFFERENCE IS NOT THE ANSWER. An answer is a thing about a state of the world. A date is a thing about a piece of paper. THE DIFFERENCE IS THAT A DATE CAN BE WRONG, AND IF IT IS WRONG YOU CAN COME BACK. And then the second thing, which is the danger: HE CAN NOW DO THIS TO ANYTHING. Not to water. Not to a bus stop. To anything with a date on it. He had a plastic wallet in the glovebox of a van he does not own, and nobody in this city can say how many pieces of paper are in it, and he had told nobody, and he stood at a sink and felt something he did not have a word for, and the word he did not have a word for was powerful, and it is not power, and he does not know that, and it is going to be called power later by somebody else.**
- **HOWARD BRAMBLE, 16 FEBRUARY: he asked for a reason — one line, free text, against each of the nineteen entries, not a drop-down, not a category, in the hand of the person who decided it, dated — and he said first, and it was true, that he is not asking for a name and has been careful about that for two months, and that the bulb is out, which he can see from his car park, so that one is honest, and he wanted that noticed. HE WAS REFUSED IN THE RIGHT ORDER IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS AND IT STANDS AND IT IS NOT THE EIGHTH OF ANYTHING, because a list is nineteen entries with something against each of them and you can count it, and this is a thing somebody has asked for twice and been told no twice by a person standing in front of you, and if she writes a number against it then she has made a list of people who think the board is dangerous, and in about four years that list is a drawer.**
- **AND HE SAID THE WORDS OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, IN FEBRUARY: IT IS NOT A LIST. Five words, in biro, on the back of the left hand of the man of about fifty-six, who is not a member and did not come as a member and is not on any list, in about four seconds, at the wall. Nobody saw him do it. Nobody has ever asked him what is on his hand and nobody is going to. It is not a document, it is not a minute, it is in biro on the back of the left hand of a man who has been sitting in a car park since December, and it is the truest sentence anybody has said in this borough in nine months and there is nowhere in this city to put it.**
- **AND HER ANSWER, which is the whole of the counter-offer and it is the price, said out loud: if the reason is going to exist anywhere, it is going to exist on a slip from a counter, with a date on it, and nobody's name on it, in a hand, on a day, and it is going to be about a thing and not about a decision. AND THAT IS A VERY POOR DOCUMENT AND IT WILL SETTLE NOTHING. AND SHE WOULD RATHER IT DID NOT EXIST AT ALL.**
- **AND HIS ANSWER BACK, which is correct and is the standing cost of every refusal in this account: if the reasons are never going to be written down, then in about four years somebody is going to come to a frightened person in a kitchen and there is going to be nothing at all to put on the table. Not the meter room. Not the bulb. Nothing. And that person is going to be him, and he is sixty-one.**
- **AND THE FINDING: A REQUEST CAN BE REFUSED TWICE AND THE SECOND REFUSAL IS CHEAPER. Not easier. Cheaper. By the second time both of them know what it is for. The reason the second argument could be better is that the first refusal was public enough to be found, in the sense that a person who has been told no at a counter talks about it, and that is the only mechanism this borough has for improving a refusal, and nobody designed it and nobody can close it. It cost her about nine minutes instead of eleven seconds. She is going to be judged on the seconds and the nine minutes are the price of doing it properly.**

**AND THE THIRD ASKER, WHO IS NOT ONE OF THE THREE, AND WHO WAS ASKED FOR A STATE ABOUT A THING THAT IS NOT IN THIS BOROUGH, AND THE COUNTER COULD NOT ANSWER IT. On Friday 6 March a man of about fifty came in at about ten to eleven, in the rain, and asked whether that was where you go if you want to know about a person. He was told no, and then he was told the whole of it: there is nothing in this borough that can tell him where his sister is, there is no form on any counter in this city that would take his question, and if there were one then in about four years there would be a list of sisters, and she could not give it to him and neither could anybody else. HE WAS NOT ANGRY. HE ASKED, AND IT WAS A REQUEST AND NOT A QUESTION: `why do you do this at all.` THE ASKING WAS RECORDED BY NOBODY. THE NOT-ANSWERING WAS CORRECT. He went out into the rain with nothing at all, and nobody in this borough can be blamed for that, and nobody was helped, and nothing was rescued.**

**AND THE FOURTH ASK, WHICH IS THE ONLY THING ANYBODY IN THIS BOROUGH HAS ASKED THAT COUNTER ABOUT ITSELF. She answered it in about eleven seconds, and nobody asked her to, and it was not a service, and it is the only thing anybody in this borough has ever said that was both free and a use: **SHE DID NOT GIVE A NUMBER AND THAT IS CORRECT AND IT IS THE BATCH'S HARDEST RULE HOLDING. `Because there is a drawer under this counter and everything in it has a date on it and a hand on it and no name on it, and the reason it is in there is that a person stood here on a day and said what was true on that day, and I wrote it down, and I am not required to and there is nobody who can make me.`** She also did not give him the number of people who come through the door, in the same answer, on the same morning, and the only reason is the one above: THE BACK OF A HAND IS NOT A BOOK. **The count of nine is the count of this account and not the count of a person, and it has never left the narrator, and no character in this batch knows it.** And: THE COUNTER WORKS BECAUSE A PERSON IS ON IT, AND SHE IS NOT FINDABLE, AND SHE IS NOT ON ANYTHING, AND SHE HAS A JOB ON THE SECOND FLOOR AND SHE COMES DOWN TWICE A DAY BECAUSE NOBODY HAS EVER TOLD HER TO AND NOBODY COULD, AND IF SHE STOPPED COMING DOWN THERE WOULD BE A COUNTER AND THERE WOULD NOT BE A COUNTER. He said: `That is the answer to my question and I did not ask it.` She said: `No. You asked me why I do this and I have told you about a drawer.`**

**AND SHE REFUSED JONAS MERCER THE SAME MORNING, five words, `What is this counter for`, in three parts, in the right order, and the third part is the whole of it: A COUNTER THAT CAN BE ASKED WHAT IT IS FOR IS A COUNTER THAT HAS BEEN ASKED TO BE SOMETHING, AND THE DAY IT HAS BEEN ASKED TO BE SOMETHING IT IS A SERVICE. And the difference between the two: she would not tell a man with civic standing and no grade and a pad what a counter is for, and she told a man with a wet coat in about eleven seconds, and both of those are correct, and the difference is not trust.**

**THE ELEVENTH THING, WHICH IS THE FIRST ONE ANYBODY HAS ADDED, IS THIS, AND IT IS NOT AN ITEM AND IT IS NOT GOING ON A WALL AND IT IS IN NO MINUTE:**

> **THE FLOOR CAN BE ASKED ABOUT ITSELF, AND THE ONLY REASON IT CAN BE ASKED ABOUT ITSELF IS THAT THERE IS A PERSON ON IT AND THE PERSON IS NOT FINDABLE.**

**Nobody may restate the five items as a list, in any form, in any room, in any document. The sentence of the twenty-eighth of January may not go on a wall.**

**AND THE THROUGHPUT, which is on the back of a hand and in no book and is going to stay there: about nine people a day on the twenty-sixth of January, four on a wet one, and about eleven on the sixth of March, and it has not been written down and is not going to be. A number of nine in a record is a capacity; a capacity is a promise; a level of service is a thing a person may also be given LESS of; nobody has to cut it — the number does the cutting, by existing. THIS IS A COUNT OF PEOPLE AND NOT A COUNT OF DOCUMENTS, and it is not the same number as the nine in the drawer, and the two may never be added together, and no character has ever been told either of them.**

**AND A LETTER CAME INTO THE RECORDS OFFICE ON THE SECOND FLOOR ON WEDNESDAY THE EIGHTEENTH OF FEBRUARY AT ABOUT HALF PAST NINE, in an envelope that was not on borough stock, with a departmental reference on it in the correct typeface, about four lines long. NINA CALDER HAS NOT OPENED IT IN FRONT OF A SECOND PERSON AND IS NOT GOING TO, HAS TOLD NOBODY IT IS THERE, HAS NOT PUT IT IN THE TRAY ON THE GROUND FLOOR, AND HAS NOT ANSWERED IT AS OF THE END OF THIS BATCH, TWENTY-THREE DAYS LATER (it was nineteen days on Monday 9 March, and the count is the only thing in this account that is exact about it). It is not a reason column. It is the smallest request anybody in this borough has made since the first of March. WHAT IS IN IT IS NOT KNOWN TO ANYBODY IN THIS ACCOUNT AND MAY NOT BE STATED IN A ROOM AND MAY NOT BE OPENED IN A DOCUMENT, and it is the batch's live hook. Nobody is going to be helped by it and nobody has been harmed by it and it is the only thing in ten chapters that is going to cost anybody something.**

## 4. WHETHER THE COMPLIANCE PARAGRAPH WAS EVER ANSWERED, AND BY WHOM, AND WHAT IT COST

**IT WAS NOT ANSWERED. IT WAS NOT ANSWERED BY ANYBODY, EVER, AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE ANSWERED, AND NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ABLE TO ANSWER IT, AND THE BATCH SAYS THAT OUT LOUD AND DOES NOT APOLOGISE FOR IT.**

- **THE AUTUMN COMPLIANCE REPORT IS PUBLIC. PARAGRAPH 4.11. DECONTAMINATION, WEST SITE, LEVEL ZERO. THE HISTORICAL HALF OF THE WEEKLY VERIFICATION RECORD CANNOT BE RECONSTRUCTED AND THE NON-CONFORMANCE IS CARRIED FORWARD.** There is no name in it because there has never been a name field on the sheet the paragraphs are counted from.
- **THE LOG IS A SUPPLIER'S GRID OF FIFTY-TWO SUNDAYS WITH A TICK IN A TEMPERATURE BOX FOR FOUR BOOKS AND A SIGNATURE BOX THAT IS EMPTY ON EVERY LINE. Nobody signed it. It has carried no name since 9 May. The weekly verification has not happened once.**
- **THE MACHINE IS THE BENCH STERILISER ON LEVEL ZERO, FOUR YEARS OLD, running on four hundred and ten pounds of service a doctor bought herself after the supplier's contract expired at midnight on 30 April. It is still running. The room is still in use. About four instruments an hour come out of it. FOUR HUNDRED AND TWENTY WOMEN IN THIS BOROUGH HAVE INSTRUMENTS OUT OF THAT ROOM EVERY WEEKDAY AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS ON THE FAR SIDE OF A PARAGRAPH NUMBER, AND NO PERSON IS ON THE NEAR SIDE OF IT, AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT EITHER.**
- **THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT WAS NOT TOLD ANYTHING IN FEBRUARY AND WAS NOT ASKED AND WAS NOT RUNG. Jonas Mercer worked out on the morning of Wednesday 4 February that he could go and find him, and did not, and said the reason out loud, once, in a ground-floor corridor at Saint Orra west, to Bram Osei, a porter, and it is the batch's most exact refusal: `BECAUSE IF I TELL HIM, THEN HE IS THE PERSON WHO KNOWS. That is what it is. It is one of the things a person can be put into that they cannot get out of, and three of them are mine, and I made this one in August by not asking him a question, and I am not going to unmake it by asking him two.`**
- **AND THE SENTENCE, which is Bram Osei's and which is the first time in this account that the correct action has been to leave a person in ignorance on purpose: `THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS BOROUGH THAT KEEPS A MAN FROM BEING ASKABLE IS A MAN WHO DOES NOT KNOW, AND THERE IS NO OTHER ONE. THAT IS ALSO TRUE ABOUT ME.`**
- **WHAT IT COST: nothing measurable, and that is the finding. Nobody has been harmed and nobody has been helped. The report stands, the paragraph number stands, four hundred and twenty women stand behind a paragraph number, and the whole of the price is one man of forty-eight sitting somewhere in this borough not knowing that a paragraph with his shape in it is out.**
- **AND THE HOOK, which is Jonas Mercer's own inference and is not a fact he verified: the machine keeps its own cycle record on a strip, and the strip goes in a drawer in the room. A STRIP IS A PIECE OF PAPER. A DRAWER IS A PLACE. AND A PIECE OF PAPER DOES NOT PUT ITSELF IN A DRAWER. So somebody has been putting strips in that drawer about forty times a day, in a room on level zero, and that person is not on a form, is not on a rota, is not in a paragraph, has not been asked, HAS NOT BEEN FOUND EITHER, and Jonas Mercer is not going to go and find him, and that is the FIRST TIME IN NINE MONTHS THAT HE HAS DECIDED ON PURPOSE NOT TO FIND SOMEBODY, and the reason is the one he gave out loud in that corridor on the Friday morning, and it is going to be the sentence he is judged on. (It is the first; the second is Royden Achebe on 25 February, and the two are twenty-one days apart, and he has been told by a better person than him that a pattern of two is a policy.)**
- **THE ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THIS BATCH, on the wall by the lift at Saint Orra west, and the only one anybody in this borough has put up since the middle of January:**

> **LEVEL ZERO DECONTAMINATION. BENCH STERILISER 2.**
> **THE MACHINE KEEPS ITS OWN CYCLE RECORD ON A STRIP. THE STRIP GOES IN THE DRAWER IN THE ROOM.**
> **THE QUARTERLY RETURN IS MADE BY THE SUPPLIER. NO PERSON SIGNS THIS SHEET.**

  **It does not say who empties the drawer. It does not say what a strip is held to prove. NOTHING ON IT DEFINES ANYTHING, INCLUDING *HOLDER*, AND NOTHING IN VOLUME 03 MAY DEFINE *HOLDER*.**

## 5. WHETHER ROYDEN ACHEBE WAS EVER TOLD, BY WHOM, AND WHAT IT COST

**HE WAS NOT TOLD. Nobody told him. Jonas Mercer did not go to Gantry Ward, did not telephone, did not use the switchboard, did not go to the team, did not go to the building, did not ask anybody whether he was in, and did not stand on a landing. That is the whole of the answer and it stands. The five routes are unchanged from the twenty-second of December and every one of them is a document and every one of them is a person who will remember it.**

**WHAT CHANGED IN FEBRUARY IS NOT ACHEBE. IT IS THE PRICE.**

- **A COUNTER IN THIS BOROUGH NOW MINTS FINDABILITY, AND FINDABILITY USED TO COST SOMETHING. For nine months the only thing this borough had to make a person findable was a person going to a place and standing in a corridor, and a corridor costs a day and a raincoat and a decision, and a decision is expensive, and a man who has spent eight months refusing to be a person anybody can ring has understood that expense in his body and has been living on it. In the third week of January a woman stood up in a vestibule eleven feet by nine and said that a person could ask a question and get a date, AND A DATE IS THE CHEAPEST HANDLE IN THIS CITY.**
- **AND THE ARITHMETIC, on the back page of a pad in a shop, and it took him four days to be able to write it down: A DATE IS A STATE WITH A DAY ON IT. A date about a trough costs eleven seconds and a slip and is worth exactly that. A date about him saying a man's name in a room on the twenty-first of November would cost him about the same — one slip, one hand, one date, twenty-first of November, a man said a man's name in a room and the man was not in it and the sentence is in two public minutes and neither minute contains a name. THAT IS A STATE. SHE COULD HOLD HIM TO IT. AND IT WOULD BE THE CHEAPEST THING ANYBODY HAS OFFERED THAT MAN IN EIGHT MONTHS, AND THAT IS THE PROBLEM, AND THAT IS WHAT HE HAD TO WRITE DOWN.**
- **AND HIS ONE QUESTION, five words, on Wednesday 25 February, at the counter on the second floor, to the woman who has been at that counter for eleven years and is not Nina Calder: `DOES A CORRECTION GO ANYWHERE.` The answer, in full, and both halves are correct: A CORRECTION IS NOT A DOCUMENT, IT IS NOT A RETURN, IT IS NOT A MINUTE AND IT IS NOT A FILE, and there is no box on anything in that building that would take it, and if you put it in the post it becomes a letter, and a letter is a thing that exists in a building that keeps post, and in about four years somebody can say that a man with civic standing and no grade wrote to a manager in the borough on a date, and they cannot say what was in it, and that is exactly why they will say it. `If I could make it go nowhere, it would be worthless. If I could make it go somewhere, it would be a record. Those are the same door and it only opens one way, and the way it opens is into a file.`**
- **AND BEATRICE OSUNDE, in the shop, on Friday the twenty-seventh of February at about two in the afternoon, did not argue and did not improve it and did not tell him he was brave, and she said once: A MAN WHO CAN BE ASKED ONE QUESTION ABOUT ANYTHING, FOR EVER, BY ANYBODY, IS NOT FINDABLE. HE IS AVAILABLE. AND A HANDLE IS CHEAP. `A RECEIPT IS NOT AN APOLOGY. A RECEIPT IS A THING YOU HAND TO A PERSON SO THAT FROM THAT DAY THEY CANNOT SAY THE BILL WAS NEVER PRESENTED. AND THE WHOLE OF WHAT YOU HAVE BEEN PROTECTING THAT MAN FROM FOR EIGHT MONTHS IS A BILL.`**
- **AND THE SENTENCE THE BATCH ENDS ON, said once, to the bench oiling, in about four seconds, and it is the reason he wrote it down at all: **YOU HAVE BEEN FINDING PEOPLE FOR EIGHT MONTHS AND YOU HAVE NOT YET FOUND OUT THAT THE EXPENSIVE HALF IS NOT BEING FOUND. IT IS BEING ASKED.**
- **AND THE FINDING: FINDABILITY AND ASKABILITY ARE TWO DIFFERENT CURRENCIES, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MINTING THE FIRST ONE VERY SUCCESSFULLY SINCE THE THIRD OF APRIL AND HAS NOT SPENT ONE UNIT OF THE SECOND ONE AT ALL. A wall is a person who cannot be asked. A door is a person who can be asked. Both of them are findable. THE PRICE OF A DOOR IS THAT IT OPENS. THE PRICE OF BEING FOUND IS NOTHING. A BOROUGH THAT HAS LEARNED HOW TO MAKE PEOPLE FINDABLE HAS NOT THEREBY LEARNED ANYTHING AT ALL ABOUT WHETHER ANYBODY CAN BE ASKED ANYTHING.**
- **WHAT IT COST: nothing, again, and again that is the finding. A man of forty-nine can be asked one question tomorrow morning about anything, for ever, by anybody, and he is not untouchable, and he is not to be protected, and there is no argument in this borough in which protecting him is the right thing to do, and he is not going to be asked. He is forty-nine. He does not know.**

**AND THE OTHER MAN, WHO CANNOT BE ASKED AT ALL, AND WHOSE FORTNIGHT CAME ROUND AGAIN IN FEBRUARY: PETER NAYLOR, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward, out of estates since 2011. He has a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket and a tap; he had one before Christmas and one in the middle of January and one again at the end of February, and he goes on his own and he is not on a rota and no instrument in this city has ever recorded that he goes. IT HAS BEEN FOUR DEGREES ALL WEEK. THERE IS A FROST WARNING OUT FOR THE TWENTY-EIGHTH THAT NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ANYBODY'S BUSINESS TO ACT ON. AND THERE IS NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH WHO IS ABLE TO ASK HIM WHETHER HE HAS GOT A COAT. Nobody has been helped. Nobody was asked. That is where the batch ends on that man and it is unresolved.**

## 6. THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS, THE BLANK LINE, AND THE SLOT FOR POST

**NINETEEN ENTRIES, UNMOVED. IT DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN THIS BATCH. IT HAS NOT MOVED SINCE WEDNESDAY 26 NOVEMBER 2014. On Friday 6 March 2015 the blank line under the nineteenth had been blank for a hundred days. On Friday 13 March 2015 it was a hundred and seven days, and it is the longest it has ever been still since the fourth of August. Entry 19 is a yard light, the maintenance is a bulb, and the bulb is out. NINA CALDER WAS RIGHT ON 1 DECEMBER AND SHE IS STILL RIGHT AND BEING RIGHT ABOUT IT IS THE REASON THE LINE HAS STAYED EMPTY.**

**THE SLOT FOR POST, in the counter along the left, about four inches by two, set into the wood at about the height of a person's hand: EMPTY FROM THE EIGHTEENTH OF JULY UNTIL THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY 2015. On the twentieth of February somebody put a card in it — four inches by two, folded once, not a form, no crest, no reference, no box, and no envelope. NINETY PEOPLE SAW IT AND NOBODY TOOK IT OUT AND NOBODY PUT IT UP. NINA CALDER HAS NOT READ IT AND IS NOT GOING TO, AND THE REASON, GIVEN OUT LOUD ON MONDAY 23 FEBRUARY, IS THIS: A SLOT IN A COUNTER IS NOT MINE. THERE IS NO BOX ANYWHERE IN THIS BUILDING THAT SAYS WHO EMPTIES THAT SLOT, AND IT WAS LAST EMPTIED ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF JULY AND I DO NOT KNOW WHO EMPTIED IT THEN AND NEITHER DOES ANYBODY ELSE ON EITHER FLOOR. A SLOT THAT GETS EMPTIED IS A RULE. AND A RULE IS A SERVICE, AND A SERVICE IS A ROTA WITH ONE NAME ON IT, AND MINE IS THE NAME IT WOULD HAVE, BECAUSE I AM THE PERSON AT THE COUNTER. `I am not leaving it in it. I am not taking it out of it. Those are different, and the difference is the whole of what this room is, and if you can hold it in your head for four years without saying it in a room, then you are the only person in this borough who can.`**

**WHAT IS ON THE CARD IS NOT KNOWN TO ANYBODY IN THIS ACCOUNT AND MAY NOT BE STATED.** The card being in a service and nobody removing it from it is, at present, the correct arrangement, and the day somebody empties that slot is the day this floor has a rule.

**THE TWO CONDITIONS ARE SIXTEEN INCHES APART AND HAVE NEVER BEEN RECONCILED AND WERE NOT IN THIS BATCH. The city's own paper, printed the fourteenth of August, with two letters in the corner in a hand that is not a capital hand. And an enamel strip with nothing on its face, undated, unattributed, two letters and one word in pencil on the back of it, and the word is *not mine*. THE TWO LETTERS ON THE BACK OF THE STRIP ARE NEVER DECIDED IN PROSE AND WERE NOT LOOKED AT AND WRITTEN DOWN BY ANYBODY IN THIS BATCH. The 1 March line is not reissued, restated or quoted. NOTHING DEFINES *HOLDER*.**

**AND THE FINDING OF THE BATCH ON THAT WALL, and it is physical and not moral, and it is Jonas Mercer's, and it took a fortnight to work out and about four minutes to accept: A PERSON WALKING UNDER TWO CONDITIONS THAT DO NOT AGREE GOES FASTER. If there were one condition, a person would have to obey it, refuse it, or stop, and all three of those are slow and all three are visible to the person behind them, and a second condition that disagrees takes all three off the table. What is left is walking. A CONTRADICTION IS NOT A PAUSE. A CONTRADICTION IS A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL, AND EVERYBODY CROSSES A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL FASTER THAN A FLOOR THAT IS. Timed at about three miles an hour down that corridor and about four through the sixteen inches; the difference is about a second and a half; it is nothing; it happens about four times a day on the two days he watched, which is not a rate and he knows it is not a rate; and in about four years somebody is going to write a report about a corridor. And the registrar's sentence of 2 June is the sentence it is built on: A CLAUSE THAT CANNOT BE CHALLENGED BY A PERSON CANNOT REQUIRE THEM TO. Put beside a woman with a hand up in front of her face: A PERSON WHO GOES FASTER UNDER A WALL OF TWO CONTRADICTORY CONDITIONS HAS BEEN REQUIRED TO DO SOMETHING BY NEITHER OF THEM. And the corollary, which is the batch's whole claim about a service floor: THE FLOOR OF A SERVICE IS A PLACE WHERE A PERSON CAN STAND UNDER A CONTRADICTION AND BE ANSWERED. That is all it is. It is eleven feet by nine. It is not an institution and it is not a scheme and it has no rule on the wall that applies to it, and it is not going on a wall.**
**AND THE MOST DANGEROUS SENTENCE ANYBODY IN THIS BOROUGH COULD HAVE SAID IN FEBRUARY WAS *THAT IS THE RULES*, AND NOBODY SAID IT, AND ONE OF THE TWO CONDITIONS WOULD HAVE BEEN RIGHT IF THEY HAD, AND NINA CALDER REFUSED OUT LOUD, UNASKED, TO SAY WHICH ONE, AND GAVE THE REASON, AND THE REASON IS THE SAME REASON JONAS MERCER GAVE IN A ROOM ON 28 JANUARY ABOUT A PAGE OF A REGULATION FROM 2008: IF YOU ASK ME ON THE SECOND OF JUNE I SHALL TELL YOU THE SAME THING IN THE SAME WORDS. She would rather stand under a contradiction for another four years than be the woman who settled it.**

## 7. WHAT IT COSTS, IN ONE SENTENCE, IN THE VOLUME'S OWN TERMS, AND WHO SAID IT

**Said out loud, in a room, in daylight, on Friday the thirteenth of March 2015, by JONAS MERCER, in a vestibule eleven feet by nine with about eleven people in it standing sideways, in answer to a question asked by a man of about fifty-six who is not a member and did not come as a member and is not on any list. It is a sentence and not a scheme, it is not the sentence anybody expected, and it is not the five items again. It is recorded in a public minute that went out on the Friday and contains no names.**

> **A SERVICE FLOOR COSTS THE PEOPLE WHO ARE ON IT AND NOT THE PEOPLE WHO USE IT.**
>
> **THE PRICE IS FINDABILITY.**
>
> **EVERY PERSON IN THIS BOROUGH WHO HAS EVER STOOD AT THAT COUNTER IS NOW SLIGHTLY EASIER TO FIND THAN THEY WERE, AND NONE OF THEM WAS ASKED, AND EVERY SINGLE ONE OF THE REASONS IT IS BETTER IS A REAL REASON AND NONE OF THE REASONS IT IS WORSE IS A COMPLAINT.**

**IT IS BETTER BECAUSE A PERSON CAN BE WRONG IN PUBLIC ON A DATE, AND BEING WRONG IN PUBLIC ON A DATE IS WORTH MORE THAN BEING RIGHT IN A VAN — WHICH IS THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL THING HE HAS SEEN ANYBODY DO IN THIS BOROUGH IN NINE MONTHS. IT IS WORSE BECAUSE A PERSON WHO IS FINDABLE IS A PERSON WHO CAN BE ASKED.**

**THE MINUTE, in full, and it is the whole of what is in it:**

> **Item 4. A person present, not on the agenda, not a party, not a member and not a departmental officer, was asked a question in the room, in daylight, by a person who is also not on the agenda. He answered. His answer is recorded above.**
>
> **Recorded: that a service floor costs the people who are on it and not the people who use it.**
>
> **Recorded: that the price of it is findability.**
>
> **Recorded: that no person referred to in the answer has been asked whether they can afford it, and that this body has no instrument for asking, and that the reason is that this body cannot require any person to answer anything, including that.**
>
> **Recorded: that this minute has no box for the name of a person, and that the sheet is the minute until about four o'clock and after that it is only a sheet, and that nobody has asked her about it since December.**
>
> **Nothing in this item requires any person to do anything.**

**THE SPECIFIC PEOPLE HE NAMED IN THE ROOM AND WHAT EACH ONE'S PRICE IS: a woman of fifty-four at a shopfront, unpaid since 4 July, the only door in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, now very slightly easier to find than she was in June, not told, not going to be told, and cannot un-find herself; a records supervisor of about fifty who is not on a rota and cannot be made to be on one, whose method is now knowable because a man of thirty-eight worked it out in a kitchen on a Sunday, and in about four years there will be a person who can describe exactly what that counter does, and that person will not be her, and that is the correct outcome and it is also the thing that is going to happen to her; a man of forty-eight who said a correct thing in a corridor in August and is now a man somebody could ask about, except that nobody is able to, because the only instrument that keeps a person from being askable is a person who does not know; a man of forty-four and a man of forty-nine, one of them made in August and one of them made in November, neither in a room, neither told anything, neither unmakeable. And four hundred and twenty women behind a paragraph number, and a woman of thirty-four in a drawer with no key, and a woman of seventy-two behind a door, and a woman of sixty-one with nine hundred and eighty-six Wednesdays on a card under a rubber band and nobody has ever asked her about it.**

**AND THE SENTENCE THAT CLOSES IT: NOBODY HAS BEEN ASKED WHETHER THEY CAN AFFORD THAT, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IT HAS EVER HAD IS A FORM, AND A FORM HAS A BOX ON IT, AND THE BOX IS WHERE THE ANSWER WOULD GO, AND A PERSON'S ANSWER TO *CAN YOU AFFORD TO BE FOUND* DOES NOT FIT IN A BOX AND IF IT DID IT WOULD NOT BE TRUE.**

BATCH 0004 MUST NOT RESOLVE IT, MUST NOT MANUFACTURE IT, AND MUST NOT HAVE ANYBODY ELSE MANUFACTURE IT.**

**AND TWO NEW ONES, WHICH ARE NOT ELEVEN AND ARE NOT TWELVE, AND WHICH ARE NOT ADDED TO ANYTHING: A CARD IN A SLOT IN A COUNTER THAT NOBODY EMPTIES, AND A LETTER IN A TRAY THAT NOBODY HAS OPENED IN FRONT OF A SECOND PERSON. Neither of them is a refusal. Neither of them is anybody's duty. Neither of them has a name on it.**

## 9. THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 130 — Friday 13 March 2015

- **THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS IS AT NINETEEN ENTRIES. THE BLANK LINE UNDER THE NINETEENTH IS BLANK AND HAS BEEN BLANK FOR A HUNDRED AND SEVEN DAYS.** It was last checked in daylight in that room on 9 March and it was blank. The slot in the counter has had a card in it since 20 February that nobody has removed and nobody has put up.
- **THE SEPTEMBER RECORD IS PUBLIC AND THE DETERMINATION OF 28 AUGUST IS PUBLIC AND THE QUARTERLY IS PUBLIC AND HAS A NUMBER OF TIMES IN IT AND THE SOURCE OF THAT NUMBER IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH.** The second public record went out in the third week of November: 51 nodes, 38 parties, 13 shapes, checkable at a counter by anybody, for nothing, by post, as of a date. **UNSIGNED. NO NAME BOX. About four hundred people have added their names. About eleven have taken the extract away and two have written back and been wrong and the clerk wrote *enclosure received, no comment*, and that is in the file.** The difference from September is four and Calder can say what three are and will not say the fourth.
- **THE READING RULE OF 21 AUGUST IS IN THE INSIDE POCKET OF A CLERK OF SIXTY-ONE AND HAS BEEN USED ONCE, IN A CORRIDOR, IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, ON A MAN OF FORTY-FOUR WHO WAS NOT TOLD HE WAS IN IT AND DOES NOT KNOW WHAT ANY OF THE WORDS MEAN.** Nobody may ask Frances Tolley whether she has done it and nobody may ask Peter Naylor one question about any of it, ever.
- **THE COOPERATIVE STEWARD BOARD STANDS AT SEVEN OF NINE. TWO CHAIRS. THE SEVENTH EMPTY SINCE WEDNESDAY 8 OCTOBER 2014 AND THE EIGHTH SINCE THE EVENING OF WEDNESDAY 26 NOVEMBER 2014. NOBODY CAN FILL EITHER AND NOBODY CAN UNFILL EITHER AND THERE IS NO POWER IN THIS CITY THAT COULD. THERE IS NO CHAIR OF THAT BODY AND THERE NEVER WAS, and a chair is not one of the three parts of its one power, which are: require a party holding continuity to answer, receive the answer, be asked to consider a matter. The ninth seat is not a chair, a veto, a job, a paid office, a rota, or anything anybody can be required to do, and nobody in this batch noticed what she can do, and the eight did not learn it.** Four of the nine have said they will stop if it turns into a job and one of them has given a reason and asked for it not to be written down and it is not in the minute. **The ninth seat is fifty-four, unpaid, findable at a shopfront since 4 July, the only door in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, and nobody in this borough has told her and nobody is going to.**
- **THE LOWLAND CANNOT BE SEATED AND THE REASON WAS NOT FIXED AND MAY NOT BE FIXED. A node with no party is not a party. A place is not a person. Six water points at Tier 1, £11,280 a year, paragraph 6.1 of the minute of 19 May, recorded as received and not as evidence, a winter price that is one boiler each, and nobody has been asked to pay any part of it. NO SEVENTH SHAPE WAS ADDED TO ANY RECORD IN THIS BATCH AND THE SEVENTH IS NOT RESOLVED.** Abu Bakayoko's refusal of the sixth stands, is minuted, and nobody may ask him where it is, and the sixth shape will not be named in this volume in any document that leaves the street.
- **A NAME IS IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE AND IN NO DOCUMENT, in two pieces of paper that are not documents, in a drawer with no key and in a handbag.** Untouched in this batch.
- **THE AUTUMN COMPLIANCE REPORT STANDS WITH PARAGRAPH 4.11 ON IT IN FRONT OF FOUR HUNDRED AND TWENTY WOMEN AND NOBODY HAS BEEN ANSWERED AND NOBODY HAS BEEN HARMED AND NOBODY HAS BEEN HELPED.** A strip goes in a drawer on level zero about forty times a day and the hand that puts it there has not been found either.
- **THE COUNTER IN THE VESTIBULE ANSWERS IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS AND GIVES A STATE INSTEAD OF A LIST AND A DATE, AND A BLANK LINE SITS UNDER THE NINETEENTH ENTRY AND NOBODY IS REQUIRED TO WRITE ON IT. There are nine slips in a drawer with no key and no list. About eleven people a day. A card in a slot that nobody empties. Two conditions sixteen inches apart.**
- **WHAT IT COSTS, said out loud on 13 March: a service floor costs the people who are on it and not the people who use it, and the price is findability, and nobody has been asked whether they can afford that, and this borough has no instrument for asking.**
- **THE WINTER HAD ONE SUNDAY IN IT, ONE IN EVERY WEEK, BETWEEN SIX O'CLOCK AND SEVEN, WHEN NO TERM IS IN FORCE AT ALL.** There was one on 4 January, one on 25 January, one on 1 February, one on 8, one on 15, one on 22, one on 1 March and one on 8 March, and there is one on 15 March. **THERE IS NO DATE WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE IN THIS CITY FOR A SINGLE ONE OF THEM AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE. A man decided on Sunday 1 March, on purpose, in about four minutes, not to be in that corridor on Saturday 7 March, and said so out loud in a corridor in daylight to a porter, and the porter has told nobody. There is another one on Saturday 14 March at ten o'clock at night and it will be the eighth of them in seven weeks and there is no date on it either and there is no form in this borough on which anybody could record that it happened.**

- **EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38.** Agency, ten years, four agencies in nine years, nights at a depot four to ten, a second job on Fridays he does not talk about, rents a top floor, a daughter of nine who walks to a bus stop. Has been to a counter five times in his life. **HAS A PLASTIC WALLET IN THE GLOVEBOX OF A VAN HE DOES NOT OWN WITH FOUR PIECES OF PAPER IN IT, AND HE HAS SHOWN IT TO NOBODY, AND HE HAS WORKED OUT ON HIS OWN WHAT A DATE IS AND THAT HE CAN NOW DO IT TO ANYTHING.**
- **HOWARD BRAMBLE, 61.** Fourth floor of a block of forty flats in Tideglass, on the estate with the parade of shops on the top. Nineteen months. A wife who cannot carry anything heavier than a kettle. **IS NOT M. FURNEAUX AND MUST NEVER BE CONFUSED WITH HIM. M. Furneaux is 61, resident, the rear block, Carrow Lane, wrote once on 2 January, was refused, and has not written again and is not going to before about 2018. They are two different men who are the same age in the same borough in the same winter, and that is a coincidence and not a pattern, and nobody in this borough is to be allowed to make it into one.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY WHO ASKED FOR A STATE ABOUT A PERSON ON 6 MARCH 2015.** Not named, not on any list, went out into the rain with nothing. His sister is not Bernice Ofori and nothing in that scene refers to her and nothing about her is in this account.
- **THE WRITER OF THE LETTER OF 18 FEBRUARY.** A records officer. Not named. The letter is in a tray on the second floor. Its contents are not stated in this account and may not be stated in a room.
- **THE WRITER OF THE LETTER OF 6 FEBRUARY.** Not named, not on any list. Asked whether the counter can be put on the borough's website, and said first that he was not asking for a list of people who use it and would like it recorded that he had not asked for that. **There is no website. The borough has no website.**
- **ABU BAKAYOKO, DOREEN WHITLOCK, TREVOR NUNN, EZRA MBEKI, M. FURNEAUX, Mr Hesketh, the woman of about fifty at the records counter who has been there eleven years and is NOT Nina Calder, the clerk who comes down to the vestibule at about ten and about three, the man of about fifty-six who is not a member and is not on any list, the woman of about sixty at the housing office answer book, the man of about fifty who came in on 6 March** — all carried forward from Batch 0002 and unchanged.
- **BRAM OSEI is a porter at Saint Orra west, the day bearer, and has a refused Thursday on the board in his own handwriting with *no reason I can give* under a *no reason given* in somebody else's hand, and he has asked twice that the second line not be looked at, and nobody has looked at it. He has now also said, in a corridor, that he is a man who knows something and that he does not want to be two, and he has told nobody.**

## 11. WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND MAY NOT BE RESOLVED IN BATCH 0004

The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line at the bottom of the form, the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor, Margo Doré's pump, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Selah Mora log, the tin, the brass tag, the 11:00–13:30 gap, Bram Osei's Thursday, Priya Ranganathan's card, the unverified compliance week and paragraph 4.11, the man of forty-eight, the hand that puts the strip in the drawer, the roof void at Carrow Lane, the second of the pair that did not go into the register, the third and the fourth of the four differences between the two public records, the two conditions on the Old Ward board and the initials on the back of the enamel strip, the seventh shape, the lowland's missing seat, the letter of 18 February, the card in the post slot, and the shelf. **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times; nine hundred and eighty-six is a number of Wednesdays, and it appears exactly once in Chapters 121–130, in Chapter 0130, correctly labelled and in a sentence containing neither of the other two; fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument. None of the three is a number of people and none of them may be, and they have never been in the same sentence and must not be.** The claims box is closed and empty and the sealed envelope may not be opened or written into. **The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew; no 48-minute figure may ever be printed; the forty *hours* of 3 April is the gate being open and is a different number about a different thing; and no other figure of minutes exists anywhere in this borough.** No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens — **those are Volumes 07, 09 and 15.** **A person who is not counted is not a person who is helped, and Volume 03 may not refute that sentence in a chapter.** The ninth seat is not a chair and may not be made into one, **and there is no chair of that body at all. Prue Calloway's finding of 23 April 2014 was opened once, in a room, in daylight, on 28 January 2015, and is not resolved, and MAY NOT BE OPENED A SECOND TIME; what may be argued afterwards is its consequence, and nobody may resolve it. The claim to open a door in this volume is spent.**
